# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P03b`: Cadence Hysteresis Coasting Semantics (Product Decision)

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `unresolved_product_decision`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
The statement claims 'strictly Python-oracle parity' for the cadence-hysteresis coasting path. Three classes of findings weaken that claim. (1) The 'strictly parity' wording is factually overstated in the audited region itself: the same code carries recorded post-oracle user rulings — the `legs_coast` predicate split feeding `braking` into the coast reason (native/src/drivetrain/step.cpp:1364,1458 with the caller-only ruling at native/src/drivetrain/step.hpp:242-243 and out.legs_coast=request.caller_braking at step.cpp:2411), the shaft-floor added to the rate wish (native/src/drivetrain/pedaling.cpp:191-193, documented as a 2026-11-05 amendment), the reposition edge clearing the coast latch (pedaling.cpp:107), and removal of the coast brake per the 2026-11-10 ruling (docs/agents/simulation-decisions.md:91-99; the spindle coast branch now applies zero crank torque, no damper, no brake, no hold at native/src/rider/spindle_controller.cpp:1726-1733). Parity exists only 'as amended'. (2) The single `PedalingSnapshot::coasting` bit conflates the two candidate roles the decision names — hysteresis latch (threshold selection at pedaling.cpp:135) and coast-profile-active (guard at pedaling.cpp:199-204 that decides whether to re-latch deceleration/targets) — and it is armed by EVERY coast reason, not just cadence: the reason ternary (pedaling.cpp:137-141) sets `state_.coasting=true` (line 205) for braking and no_effort entries identically. So a non-cadence entry converts into a cadence hysteresis latch on the very next tick. (3) Reachable boundary semantics the statement does not pin: during a reposition manoeuvre the snapshot reports `coasting=false` (cleared at :107) while `rider_mode` telemetry reports "coasting" (mode set at :110-123) — the reported mode and the persisted FSM bit diverge by design, i.e. the current schema already cannot express 'coasting output' vs 'cadence cutoff latched'. Numerical robustness is otherwise sound: all update inputs are finite/domain-gated (pedaling.cpp:53-61), stop_time_s>0 is enforced at config validation (native/src/configuration/validate.cpp:578-583) and required in the schema (native/src/configuration/physical_config.hpp:768), wire decode rejects non-finite via finite_real (native/src/drivetrain/state_codec.cpp:126-135), and restore is atomic at writer level (native/src/writers/drivetrain.cpp:1213-1261). Residual compliance gaps: (a) non-atomic internal mutation — with finite-but-extreme rate (e.g. 1e308) `rate_sum` overflows and `std::overflow_error` throws at pedaling.cpp:208-209 AFTER `state_.coasting=true` and the latch writes (:199-205), leaving direct PedalingPolicy callers with a half-applied latch (writer transactions mask this); (b) `excessive` is gated by `config_.enabled` (:136) so `pedaling.enabled=false` disables only the cadence arm while braking/no_effort coasting still sets the shared bit — the latch is not purely a cadence artifact even in scope; (c) backward crank rate counts via `std::abs(rate)` (:133-134, :199-204), so a backdriven crank above coast_above latches coast and the profile decays the negative spin toward zero.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
Reachable sequences against the hysteresis-as-single-bit semantics (config coast_above_rpm=120, resume_below_rpm=105, coast_cadence_tau_s=0.35, stop_time_s=0.35, enabled=true — the shipped profile values, tests/fixtures/config/viewer_physics_welded.toml:44-47): REPRO A (no_effort tick converts to cadence latch): drive EMA into the band by feeding rate ≈ 11.52 rad/s (110 rpm) with effort=30 until cadence_ema=110; then ONE tick with effort=0 → reason=no_effort (pedaling.cpp:139) → state_.coasting=true (:205), latch writes target_phase/target_rate/deceleration (:199-204). Next ticks effort=30 restored: excessive = (110 >= resume_below=105) → true → reason=cadence (:140) — the rider is forced to coast until EMA decays below 105 (≈0.35 s·ln(110/105) under tau=0.35 s, plus effort_slew_nm_s re-ramp). A momentary commanded-effort gap produced a full cadence-hysteresis latch the cadence never justified. REPRO B (caller-brake dab in band): same setup; one tick with braking=true (the legs_coast flag = caller braking only, step.cpp:1364/:2411, step.hpp:242-243) → latch set; after release, ema=110>=105 → cadence-latched coast persists — a brake dab at band cadence is silently upgraded to a cadence cutoff. REPRO C (reposition edge escapes the latch): enter coasting via a brake tap at ema=110 (bit armed by braking); issue reposition_request=true → pedaling.cpp:107 clears state_.coasting and effort, arms the goal; the reposition early-return (:110-123) bypasses the EMA update (:132-134) so the EMA freezes at 110 for the manoeuvre (up to reposition_timeout_s=3.0); on exit, state_.coasting=false → threshold=coast_above=120 → 110<120 → pedaling resumes — the reposition edge is an asymmetric escape hatch that erases a brake/no_effort-derived latch but not a genuine cadence latch (ema>=120 re-latches immediately). REPRO D (mode/bit divergence on the wire): during the manoeuvre snapshot carries coasting=false while result.rider_mode=coasting — two distinct 'coasting' signals already exist (crank_target_phase_rad presence drives support_diagnostics.coasting in legacy_controller.cpp:335 and the effort gate in rider/state_codec.cpp:2038-2040, while the hysteresis reads the bit). REPRO E (non-atomic throw): update(phase=0, rate=1e308, ..., dt=1e-3, effort=0) — all inputs pass finite gates (:53-61); deceleration=1e308/0.35 is finite; rate_sum overflows → std::overflow_error (:208-209 via validation::derived) thrown after the latch writes — direct policy callers observe coasting=true with a partially-written profile.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
Valid product scenarios the strict single-bit parity contract blocks or mis-serves: (1) Brake modulation during technical riding: the rider's brake channel (caller demand, e.g. a ride-program keyframe or viewer brake) while coasting at band cadence latches a cutoff that outlives the brake release (REPRO B) — the TOML rationale justifies hysteresis only against twice-per-rev torque-ripple flicker on the EMA ('EMA убирает дёрганье от двух пульсаций момента за оборот', tests/fixtures/config/viewer_physics_welded.toml:40-43), not against pedal/brake intent; a split contract would arm cadence_cutoff_latched only on the cadence branch and treat braking/no_effort ticks as transient coast without the resume_below hangover. (2) Momentary effort gaps: a program emitting human_torque_nm=0 for a single tick (the same shape as the research-environment's motor_control(0.,0.) rest ticks) at band cadence forces ~0.4 s+ of unwanted leg coast plus slew re-ramp (REPRO A) — physically the rider eased for an instant and should resume at will. (3) The recorded steady90 launch overshoot: crank spins to ~145-158 rpm at start (docs/agents/simulation-decisions.md:76-81), the 0.35 s EMA was kept 'as is' explicitly pending the delays task with a documented fallback to instant 120/105 cutoff (simulation-decisions.md:113-116) — the parity contract freezes the smoothed variant and blocks evaluating the fallback, which the user already reserved as a product option; a cadence_cutoff_latched bit decoupled from the coast-profile marker would let the entry semantics change (instant vs smoothed) without touching the profile/schema. (4) stop_time_s retirement: on the active spindle path the decaying target is semantically dead for physics — the coast branch zeroes crank torque and ignores both target fields (spindle_controller.cpp:1726-1733; legs rest on pedals per the 2026-11-10 ruling, simulation-decisions.md:91-99). The profile survives only as: the crank_target_phase_rad-presence coasting marker for the command gate (rider/state_codec.cpp:2038-2040) and legacy follow (legacy_controller.cpp:335-343), the phase_error/target diagnostics, and the 'stopped' evidence metric keyed on crank_tracking.target_rate_rad_s==0 (native/src/validation/evidence.cpp:270). Retiring stop_time_s is nearly free physically but needs a replacement coasting marker for those consumers — the current contract conflating 'marker' with 'profile' blocks a clean retirement. (5) The reposition manoeuvre's implicit latch-clear (REPRO C) is an unexamined semantic — under a split contract it should be decided explicitly whether arming a manoeuvre resets cadence_cutoff_latched.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even taking the statement as satisfied, the admission surface is wider than the FSM's reachable set — corrupt snapshot states restore cleanly and produce semantics the oracle cannot generate: (a) coasting=true + target_rate_rad_s≠0 + deceleration_rad_s2=0 is admitted by set_state (pedaling.cpp:20-43 only requires coasting⟹target_phase_rad engaged at :26, deceleration merely nonnegative at :24) but is unreachable — update() latches deceleration=|rate|/stop_time_s>0 strictly whenever rate≠0 (:199-204). Result: a coast profile whose target rate never decays and whose target_phase races forever, reported to rider command/telemetry and to the 'stopped' evidence metric (evidence.cpp:270) which never fires. (b) coasting=true + effort>0 admitted (effort only nonnegative, :25) though update() always writes state_.effort=0 on coast (:210) and the command-level gate forbids crank_target_phase+effort>0 (rider/state_codec.cpp:2038-2040) — the snapshot wire tolerates a combination the command wire rejects; a following tick with reason==none slews from the phantom effort. (c) coasting=false + engaged target_phase_rad and/or nonzero deceleration_rad_s2 admitted — shipped fixture tests/fixtures/state/drive_minimal.json itself carries 'coasting':false with 'deceleration_rad_s2':40.0 — stale profile fields persist across serialize/restore until the next coast entry overwrites them. (d) coasting=true + cadence_ema=nullopt admitted (finite_optional, :22) though unreachable — the EMA is always engaged before the coast branch can run (:132-134 precede :199); the restored latch then applies resume_below to a cadence that was never measured, silently reseeding cadence memory. (e) Relational checks elsewhere do not cover pedaling: validate(DriveSnapshot) (native/src/writers/drivetrain.cpp:25-85) cross-checks transmission/shift fields only; restore() delegates to the per-member set_state (drivetrain.cpp:1213-1261) so no rate/deceleration/effort/target-phase consistency is enforced anywhere on the restore path. (f) Atomicity is only writer-level: the overflow throw at pedaling.cpp:208-209 after latch writes (:199-205) leaves a half-applied state to direct PedalingPolicy users (the writer's scratch-copy transaction at drivetrain.cpp:1213-1261 masks it in production). These are wire-tolerant admissions, not parity violations — but 'sufficient' the contract is not: it constrains the transition rule while leaving the state shape under-constrained.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Revise the property text to: 'Cadence-hysteresis coasting is an engineering contract of Python-oracle parity AS AMENDED by recorded user rulings (legs_coast caller-only braking at step.cpp:1364/:2411, shaft-floor rate wish at pedaling.cpp:191-193, reposition latch-clear at pedaling.cpp:107, coast-brake removal per simulation-decisions.md:91-99); it is not a confirmed physical requirement. Pending product ruling: split PedalingSnapshot::coasting into (i) cadence_cutoff_latched — armed ONLY by the cadence branch (pedaling.cpp:140) and released when cadence_ema<resume_below_rpm; braking/no_effort produce transient coast ticks that do not arm it (fixes REPROs A-B); and (ii) coast_profile_active — an explicit marker replacing today's implicit crank_target_phase_rad-presence signal consumed by the command gate (rider/state_codec.cpp:2038-2040), legacy follow (legacy_controller.cpp:335-343), telemetry, and the stopped-coast evidence metric (evidence.cpp:270); decide explicitly whether a reposition edge clears the latch (REPRO C) and whether stop_time_s retires with a replacement marker. Independently of the ruling, tighten the wire/set_state contract: reject coasting=true without cadence_ema and with effort≠0; reject target_rate_rad_s≠0 with deceleration_rad_s2==0; reject coasting=false carrying engaged target_phase_rad or nonzero profile fields — or explicitly document the looser admission as intentional wire tolerance (tests/fixtures/state/drive_minimal.json already relies on it).'

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P03c`: Motor Assist Brake Reset, Power-Capped Run-On & Snapshot Relational Invariant

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `mixed_subclauses`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Subclause-by-subclause audit at commit 0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1. (a) BRAKE RESET: motor.cpp:59-62 routes braking==true to reset() (motor.hpp:77, state_={}) which zeroes all six AssistSnapshot fields before returning 0; verified end-to-end by test_rider_brake_channel.cpp:217-220. Two boundary gaps: (i) the finite/nonnegative input gates at motor.cpp:53-58 run BEFORE the braking check, so step(human=NaN|Inf, ..., braking=true) throws and leaves the armed hold state un-zeroed (rejected-call state preservation, but contradicts 'braking zeroes all fields' read literally for unwound calls); (ii) at writer level the braking flag only reaches step() under 'in.active && effort_' (drivetrain.cpp:862-866) — an advancing tick with braking=true/motor_braking=true but active=false never calls step(), seeds bank.assist from live (drivetrain.cpp:783), then forces only bank.assist.torque=0 (drivetrain.cpp:894-895) — the committed snapshot keeps hold_power_w/hold_torque_nm/release_hold_left_s>0, so 'braking zeroes all AssistSnapshot fields' fails for inactive ticks. Production reachability: apply_forces(false,advance,...) with brake demands exists only in the init settle (settle_contacts.cpp:60) where hold fields are zero anyway; mid-ride callers always pass active=true (step.cpp:1448-1470,1719), so this is a latent mechanism, not a live trigger, unless a restore() seeds an armed hold and an init-style inactive braking tick follows. (b) POWER-CAPPED HOLD: motor.cpp:104-108 — when release_hold_left_s>0 (checked pre-decrement, so the cap also applies on the expiring step): delivery = omega>0 ? min({hold_power_w/omega, hold_torque_nm, limit}) : 0, then torque = max(0, min({delivery, limit, cap})). min-associativity makes this identical to the stated min({hp/om, ht, ceiling, cap}) for omega>0 (limit==ceiling==limit*taper from :71/37-47; cap==request, nonnegative at :57); the omitted max(0,.) floor is dead code under validated configs since torque_curve/config torque values are nonnegative (config_validation.hpp:175-201). (c) NO-RE-ARM: arm requires 'hold_power_w<=0 && omega>0' inside 'target<state_.torque' (motor.cpp:81-86); after expiry hp>0 persists as spent marker (config_types.hpp:99-100) so no re-arm until target>=torque clears the triplet (:87-91). Holds. settle() indeed replaces assist.torque with the solved actuator force clamped to [0, requested+1e-8] (drivetrain.cpp:1049-1067) but ONLY when live_.pending_actuation is engaged — publish is skipped otherwise (:1174-1175), and the committed snapshot torque is additionally the safety-clipped/enable-gated m.delivered, not step()'s raw return (:866-895). (d) SET_STATE REJECTIONS — FALSIFIED: AssistController::set_state (motor.cpp:28-35) performs ONLY per-field validation::nonnegative (finite && >=0, validation.hpp:15-19). Every combination the statement claims is rejected is admissible: release_hold_left_s>0 with hold_power_w=0 or hold_torque_nm=0, and hold_power_w>0 with hold_torque_nm=0 all pass. No compensating relational check exists in validate(DriveSnapshot,...) (drivetrain.cpp:25-85 — topology/shift only), the state codec (state_codec.cpp:333-338), validate_tick (drivetrain.cpp:652-658), or validate_settlement (drivetrain.cpp:1024-1030).

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
REPRO 1 (subclause-4 falsification, controller level): with any validated AssistConfig, drivetrain::AssistSnapshot s{ .torque=0, .last_gain=0, .hold_power_w=0, .hold_torque_nm=0, .release_hold_left_s=0.3, .pedaling=false }; c.set_state(s) — no throw; admitted (motor.cpp:28-35). Same for {hold_power_w=5, hold_torque_nm=0, release_hold_left_s=0.3} and {hold_power_w=5, hold_torque_nm=0, release_hold_left_s=0}. Reachable via DrivetrainWriter::restore() -> a.set_state(s.assist) (drivetrain.cpp:1213-1220) and via a crafted recorded-state JSON through decode_drive policies.assist (state_codec.cpp:333-338 — fields are num()-parsed with no relational check). REPRO 2 (admitted-invalid -> dynamic effect): restore a snapshot with {torque=0..small, hold_power_w=1e9, hold_torque_nm=1e9, release_hold_left_s=0.3} — passes all guards; next advancing step with target<state_.torque skips re-arm (hp>0) and yields delivery=min(1e9/om, 1e9, limit)=limit — a corrupt restore grants a full-ceiling run-on for the whole injected window that the reachable FSM cannot produce; note this escapes even the claimed invariant because all hold fields are >0 ('consistent'). REPRO 3 (underflow re-arm): injected {hold_power_w=0, hold_torque_nm=0, release_hold_left_s=0.3} + a step with target<state_.torque and omega>0 — the injected window is discarded and the hold RE-ARMS immediately (motor.cpp:82-85), i.e., an admitted underflowing hold manufactures an arm the automaton never took. REPRO 4 (brake-not-reset): a restore()-seeded armed hold followed by an advancing tick with braking=true and active=false commits {torque=0, hp>0, ht>0, left>0} — braking did not zero the snapshot (drivetrain.cpp:862-895); step() is also never invoked so release_hold_left_s is not even decremented. REPRO 5 (throw-before-reset): step(human=NaN, ..., braking=true) throws at motor.cpp:53 before reset() — the armed hold survives a rejected braking call.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No valid product scenario is wrongly blocked — in fact the claimed set_state rejections are never exercised at all because they do not exist. Internally reachable assist states are exactly {0,0,0} (cleared, motor.cpp:87-91 and reset), {hp>0,ht>0,left>0} (armed, :83-85 — arm requires state_.torque>target>=0 and omega>0 so hp=torque*om>0 and ht=torque>0 always co-occur), {hp>0,ht>0,left=0} (spent marker; also immediate when release_hold_s=0 — config_validation.hpp:182 admits 0), plus {torque=0,hp>0,ht>0,left>0} produced by inactive ticks (drivetrain.cpp:894-895) and by settle() zero-solves (drivetrain.cpp:1063-1066). Every claimed-rejected class ({left>0,hp<=0}, {left>0,ht<=0}, {hp>0,ht<=0}) is unreachable internally, so implementing the gate would block no legitimate state — the constraint is not over-broad, merely absent. Brake-reset and the power-capped run-on are confirmed product requirements: simulation-decisions.md decision 16 (lines 179-185) — 'При торможении мотор выключается сразу' and 'момент мотора не больше того, что был в её начале, и остаётся в пределах ограничения момента и снижения у предельной скорости' — matching min(hp/om, ht, limit); 'Когда сила растёт, мотор подстраивается сразу' matches the target>=torque clear-then-filter path. RIDE.md/RECORDING.md contain no scenario requiring brake-state preservation or hold-state restoration that the invariant would exclude.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even granting subclauses 1-3, the admission boundary remains porous: (1) every combination claimed rejected is admitted (see repros) — the stated relational invariant is a true invariant of reachable states but is enforced NOWHERE (motor.cpp:28-35; drivetrain.cpp:25-85,652-658,1024-1030,1213-1220; state_codec.cpp:333-338); (2) magnitude-unbounded 'consistent' holds — {left>0,hp>0,ht>0} with arbitrary values — pass both the real gate and the claimed one, and grant ceiling-bounded amplified run-ons (delivery<=limit); only the ceiling saves it, since nothing relates hp/ht to plausible armed values or to current omega; (3) snapshot torque is unbounded above — a restored torque=1e6 arms ht=1e6 on the next target<torque step (min still caps at limit); (4) the pedaling flag admits torque/pedaling inconsistency (set_state stores pedaling verbatim; internally it is always torque>0 at motor.cpp:111) and the safety/motor_limit_nm clip already produces {torque:0,pedaling:true} snapshots on clipped ticks (drivetrain.cpp:866-895 vs motor.cpp:108-111); (5) last_gain is write-only telemetry — arbitrary admitted values are harmless; (6) a settle() that solves 0 mid-hold publishes {torque=0,hold armed} which silently cancels the run-on on the next step (target>=0==torque clears) — semantically defensible (delivered power is the hold's baseline) but an admitted state whose effect is hold abortion, not hold preservation; (7) nonfinite pending_torque from actuator_force is correctly caught by set_state's finite() inside validate_settlement (drivetrain.cpp:1026-1029 -> motor.cpp:29), but an in-range stale solve is adopted as torque; (8) on the unwind axis staging is atomic — step() runs on assist_scratch_ (drivetrain.cpp:863), throws before commit, live_ untouched — so unwind is safe; the admitted-invalid surface is exactly the set_state/restore/codec boundary plus the inactive-tick torque-forcing path.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Split P03c into an implemented part and a claimed-but-absent part. Implemented (keep, verified): (1) 'braking==true inside an executed step zeroes the AssistSnapshot and returns 0' — qualify with 'when the assist step runs (in.active && effort_); braking ticks that skip the step preserve the hold triplet while forcing torque=0 (drivetrain.cpp:862-895)' and 'a rejected (throwing) step preserves prior state'; (2) 'while release_hold_left_s>0 after the arm/clear update, delivered torque = omega>0 ? min(hold_power_w/omega, hold_torque_nm, ceiling) further clamped by max(0, min(., ceiling, request_cap)) : 0' — ceiling includes taper and max_power/omega; (3) 're-arm requires hold_power_w<=0 && omega>0 inside target<state_.torque; a spent hold (hp>0,left=0) never re-arms until target>=torque clears the triplet; settle() may rewrite state_.torque to the solved, request-bounded actuator force when a pending actuation exists, and m.delivered (safety/enable-gated) is what lands in the snapshot.' Replace the false subclause 4 with the actual emergent invariant: 'all committed snapshots satisfy release_hold_left_s>0 ==> hold_power_w>0 && hold_torque_nm>0, and hold_power_w>0 ==> hold_torque_nm>0; AssistController::set_state does NOT enforce this — it admits every fieldwise-nonnegative snapshot, so corrupt restores may inject underflowed (self-healing via re-arm or clear) or magnitude-amplified (ceiling-bounded) holds; if boundary hygiene is intended, add the relational checks to set_state and mirror them in the codec restore tests — as written the stated rejections describe code that does not exist.'

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P04a`: Shifting Snapshot & Cut-Order Invariant

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `mixed_subclauses`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Per-clause audit of the statement against native/src/drivetrain/shifting.{hpp,cpp}, policy_set.hpp:46-57, writers/drivetrain.cpp:25-85 and the wire codec state_codec.cpp:268-339. (a) '(shift_count > 0) <=> (direction != "none")' HOLDS on every enforced path: both restore gates check it (policy_set.hpp:48-51 '(shift.direction != "none") != shifted'; drivetrain.cpp:38-42 plus the shift_time_s coupling at :39), and every update() commit writes direction together with ++shift_count (shifting.cpp:101-102 stationary, :202-203 shared commit); direction is never cleared anywhere. (b) 'shift_count == 0 => cooldown_s == 0 && cut_remaining_s == 0' HOLDS: same gates ('!shifted && (cooldown_s != 0. || cut_remaining_s != 0.)'), and timers are armed only at commits (shifting.cpp:103-104, 204-205). NaN timers are caught by set_state's nonnegative/finite (shifting.cpp:17-18 via validation.hpp:10-19) even though validate() itself would pass NaN for count>0 — restore() runs both layers atomically (drivetrain.cpp:1214-1220). (c) '0 <= cut_remaining_s <= cooldown_s' — the lower bound is enforced (nonnegative in set_state, :17-18) but 'cut <= cooldown' is enforced NOWHERE — not in set_state, not in validate_state, not in drivetrain::validate. On committed trajectories it holds incidentally (config pins shift_cut_duration_s <= shift_cooldown_s at config_validation.hpp:161 'timing_band'; both timers decay together shifting.cpp:58-59; commits arm cooldown >= cut, stationary arms cut=0). A restored {count:1, direction:'up', cooldown_s:0.0, cut_remaining_s:0.3} passes every gate — the invariant is reachable-violated at runtime. (d) 'enabled => rear_teeth, from_teeth in cassette' HOLDS (contains-check at policy_set.hpp:52-56 and drivetrain.cpp:43-47, both teeth, enabled-only — disabled mode admits arbitrary teeth >=3 by design, test-pinned). (e) 'shift_count == 0 => rear_teeth == from_teeth' on reset/committed trajectories HOLDS trivially (reset sets from=rear shifting.hpp:142-143; count only leaves 0 via commits that set from=old rear). (f) 'CadenceShifter::update auto-band admission requires next.cut_remaining_s <= 1e-12' — FALSE AS STATED: the band 'admitted' predicate (shifting.cpp:63-65) checks next.cooldown_s <= 1e-12 and contains NO cut term at all; the cut_remaining_s <= 1e-12 gate exists only in the two rider-owned branches (:87 stationary, :112 adjacent request). The two forms coincide on committed trajectories only because cut <= cooldown holds there — and that ordering is itself unenforced on restore.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
REPRO A (cut > cooldown -> band commits inside an in-flight cut): PolicySnapshot s = policy.snapshot(); s.shifting.shift_count = 1; s.shifting.direction = "up"; s.shifting.rider_response = "accepted"; s.shifting.rear_teeth = 24; s.shifting.from_teeth = 28; s.shifting.cooldown_s = 0.0; s.shifting.cut_remaining_s = 0.3; policy.restore(s) — ACCEPTED: set_state passes (shifting.cpp:9-21: teeth>=3, count>=0, direction parses, response label in domain, timers nonnegative, EMAs paired), validate_state passes (count>0 <=> direction!='none'; both teeth on cassette). Then policy.shifting.update(115.,115.,.01): cooldown decays 0-><=1e-12 so 'admitted' is true (shifting.cpp:63-65), cadence 115 > target max -> upshift scan commits a SECOND shift while cut_remaining_s ~= 0.29 still runs — the claimed auto-band cut guard does not exist. Writer-level twin: same state via set_drive_state/decode_drive_snapshot needs shift_time_s engaged (drivetrain.cpp:39) and ideal_hub.ratio inside [from_ratio,to_ratio] (:63-69), both satisfiable (ratio == to_ratio); next advance tick reproduces the mid-cut commit and stage_pedal_advance's ratio sweep recomputes around the corrupt cut (:726-738). REPRO B (unbounded timers): restore cut_remaining_s = 1e6, cooldown_s = 0 -> shift_torque_factor applies the restored direction's factor ~forever (shifting.hpp:100-114), telemetry shift_active stays true (drivetrain.cpp:203), ShiftFeedback.cut_active stays true (step.cpp:1283-1285) -> LaunchPlan refuses new upshift requests (launch_plan.cpp:146 '!fb.cut_active') and the handover->blend transition freezes forever (launch_plan.cpp:212). REPRO C (relational bypass via bare set_state): policy.shifting.set_state({count:3, direction:'none'}) or ({count:0, cut_remaining_s:0.3}) installs silently — set_state checks only field domains (shifting.cpp:8-23), PolicySet::shifting is a public member (policy_set.hpp:63); snapshot() then exports a state restore() itself refuses, breaking restore o snapshot. Latent: all in-tree production callers route through restore()/validate(), only tests use bare set_state (test_cadence_shifter.cpp:86,336). REPRO D (direction/teeth-order contradiction): {count:1, direction:'up', rear_teeth:33, from_teeth:28, cut:0.2, cooldown:0.4} — automaton-unreachable (up always lands a SMALLER cog: rider prev(at) :129, band reverse-scan :152-162; stationary always lands larger :85,98-99) yet restore-admitted; the 0.2s phantom cut then applies torque_factor_smaller_cog (shifting.hpp:107-112) while the recorded gear change was to an easier cog — wrong class of effort cut, plus contradictory shift telemetry.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No valid product scenario found that the statement wrongly blocks. (1) The hold-phase stationary pre-select — LaunchPhase::hold + grade_ahead > 0 + rear_teeth < launch_teeth -> stationary_rear_teeth (intent.cpp:412-417; simulation-decisions.md:435-436 'передача переключается на месте') — deliberately bypasses 'admitted' (commits while braking and not pedaling, test-pinned test_cadence_shifter.cpp:452-458) and bypasses cooldown too (commits mid-cooldown once cut clears, :500-506) — but the statement's admission clause is scoped to the AUTO-BAND, so no conflict; the stationary path is a separate rider-owned gate not covered by the claim. (2) 'count==0 => timers==0' blocks only a 'cooldown pending without a committed shift' state — the automaton arms timers solely at commits, and shift_retry_s=0.25s lives in the launch plan (simulation-decisions.md:406), not the shifter. (3) 'direction != none <=> count>0' is load-bearing, not over-blocking: direction = last-shift record feeding the shift_direction telemetry lane and shift_torque_factor during a cut (shifting.hpp:100-114); transitively the restore gates guarantee cut>0 => direction in {up,down}, which is exactly what the factor needs. (4) 'enabled => teeth in cassette' only applies when enabled — disabled configs legitimately carry off-cassette teeth (test_native_drive_policies.cpp:290-295; test_native_state_restore.cpp:629-634 comment). (5) 'cut <= cooldown' as a candidate check matches the config-level timing_band rule (config_validation.hpp:161) — adding it cannot exclude any config-legal trajectory. (6) 'count==0 => rear==from' is scoped by the assumption to reset/committed trajectories where it is vacuously true; nothing in RIDE.md/RECORDING.md/simulation-decisions.md requires a meaningful from_teeth at count 0.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
States the current gates still admit (all reachable through the public wire restore decode_policy_snapshot/decode_drive_snapshot -> PolicySet::restore/DrivetrainWriter::restore, or bare CadenceShifter::set_state): (a) cut_remaining_s > cooldown_s — automaton-unreachable, passes every check; escalates to a band commit inside a live cut (auto-band never tests cut) and corrupts the cut-order the title claims. (b) cooldown_s > shift_cooldown_s or cut_remaining_s > shift_cut_duration_s — no upper bound exists anywhere; a 1e6s cut yields permanent torque-factor cut, permanent shift_active/cut_active, frozen launch-plan handover (launch_plan.cpp:146,212) and an indefinitely lingering ideal-hub ratio sweep (drivetrain.cpp:726-738). (c) direction vs teeth-order contradiction: 'up' with rear>from or 'down' with rear<from admitted; wrong torque-factor class during the restored cut and lying telemetry. (d) count>0 with rear_teeth==from_teeth — a recorded shift that changed nothing; every automaton commit moves the cog (selected != rear enforced :179 and rider branches are strictly +/-1 cog or strictly larger). (e) count==0 with rear!=from — admitted (test-pinned: restored rear=51/from=45 on a fresh policy, test_native_drive_policies.cpp:279-282); from_teeth is dead while cut==0 but is still serialized to the shift_from_teeth lane (drivetrain.cpp:207-208). (f) rider_response incoherent with shift_count — e.g. 'accepted' with count==0 (accepted is only ever written beside ++count); the domain check (shifting.cpp:15) ignores coherence; feeds ShiftFeedback.response consumed by LaunchPlan::shifting (launch_plan.cpp:139-152, step.cpp:1283). (g) absurd finite EMAs — any finite cadence_ema/required_ema admitted (paired-domain only, shifting.cpp:19-21; negative EMA restore test-pinned test_native_drive_policies.cpp:280-281); a huge required_ema converts a later ordinary shift into std::overflow_error via validation::derived(landing) (shifting.cpp:94-97,192) — detected downstream, not at the boundary. (h) enabled=false + teeth >=3 off-cassette — admitted by design, harmless while disabled. (i) shift_count == INT_MAX — admitted, then every subsequent commit attempt throws overflow (:91,:198) — designed, test-pinned. (j) Via bare set_state: every relational clause bypassable (count>0+direction='none', count==0+timers>0, cut>cooldown, off-cassette teeth when enabled) — the invariant lives one level above the public state-entry point and PolicySet::shifting exposes it directly.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Revised statement with the real gates and the missing bounds: '(shift_count > 0) <=> (direction in {"up","down"}); shift_count == 0 => (cooldown_s == 0 && cut_remaining_s == 0); 0 <= cut_remaining_s <= cooldown_s, with cooldown_s <= shift_cooldown_s and cut_remaining_s <= shift_cut_duration_s — all enforced as relational restore-time checks (currently only the field-level halves are); enabled => rear_teeth, from_teeth in cassette; shift_count > 0 => rear_teeth != from_teeth, and direction="up" => rear_teeth < from_teeth / "down" => rear_teeth > from_teeth (teeth order coherent with the recorded direction); shift_count == 0 => rear_teeth == from_teeth only on reset/committed trajectories — on restore from_teeth is dead state and the equality is deliberately unenforced. Admission gates, corrected: the auto-band requires enabled && pedaling && !braking && contact && next.cooldown_s <= 1e-12 && min(cadence,required) >= 0 (shifting.cpp:63-65) — the guard is COOLDOWN, not cut; the rider-owned request and stationary paths additionally require next.cut_remaining_s <= 1e-12 (:87,:112) while stationary bypasses 'admitted' entirely by design. Recommend: (1) enforce cut_remaining_s <= cooldown_s (and optionally the config-duration ceilings and direction/teeth-order coherence) in CadenceShifter::set_state or in PolicySet::validate_state/drivetrain::validate so restore cannot smuggle the one ordering the timer pair is maintained by; alternatively add next.cut_remaining_s <= 1e-12 to the band predicate itself for defense in depth; (2) either move the count/direction/idle-timer relational checks into CadenceShifter::set_state or document set_state as field-level-only, since PolicySet::shifting is a public member and bare set_state currently bypasses every relational clause of this property.'

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P04b`: Unified Torque-Cut Active Predicate

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `mixed_subclauses`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
The property is violated on its face: no symbol named shift_cut_active exists anywhere in the tree (zero matches repo-wide). The torque-cut window is instead evaluated inline at eight sites with TWO different thresholds, so the (0.0, 1e-12] split window the statement claims to eliminate is live: (a) '> 0.' sites — shift_torque_factor (native/src/drivetrain/shifting.hpp:103 'if (state.cut_remaining_s <= 0.) return 1.'), telemetry shift_active (native/src/writers/drivetrain.cpp:203), validate(DriveSnapshot) hub-ratio domain (native/src/writers/drivetrain.cpp:63), and the ideal-hub linear ratio sweep (native/src/writers/drivetrain.cpp:726), plus the exact '!= 0.' clock/count invariant (drivetrain.cpp:41 and native/src/drivetrain/policy_set.hpp:50); (b) '> 1e-12' sites — rider-request deferral (native/src/drivetrain/shifting.cpp:112), stationary-target deferral (shifting.cpp:87), and the rider-facing LaunchContext.shift.cut_active (native/src/runtime/step.cpp:1285, consumed by LaunchPlan::shifting at native/src/rider/launch_plan.cpp:146 and the handover->blend transition at launch_plan.cpp:212). The in-code documentation even asserts unification falsely: native/src/rider/launch_plan.hpp:32 comments 'cut_active = cut_remaining_s > 1e-12 (the ideal hub sweeps with it)', but the actual sweep gate is '> 0.' at drivetrain.cpp:726. Additionally the automatic band path inside CadenceShifter::update has NO cut check at all (shifting.cpp:137-180); automaton cut-gating is only implicit through 'admitted' requiring cooldown_s <= 1e-12 (shifting.cpp:63-65) and the config-level invariant shift_cut_duration_s <= shift_cooldown_s (native/src/config_validation.hpp:161, writer_config.hpp:1010, configuration/validate.cpp:638) — an ordering enforced on configs but never on restored snapshots. Boundary-value analysis: cut_remaining_s == 1e-12 exactly is 'active' for torque factor/telemetry/sweep/validate but 'inactive' for both deferral gates and rider feedback — the same committed snapshot field is read both ways in one tick.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
REPRO 1 (config boundary): ShiftingConfig{shift_cut_duration_s = 1e-13, shift_cooldown_s = 0.4} passes every validator (nonnegative at writer_config.hpp:998, cut <= cooldown at writer_config.hpp:1010). Every committed shift publishes cut_remaining_s = 1e-13 (shifting.cpp:205): that tick records shift_active = true and shift_torque_factor = e.g. 0.8 (drivetrain.cpp:203,221) and applies the reduced factor to pedaling (drivetrain.cpp:751->758), while the rider's LaunchContext.cut_active at the next tick is permanently false (step.cpp:1285: 1e-13 > 1e-12 is false) and no request is ever deferred — the recorded flag and the rider-feedback flag disagree on every shift forever. REPRO 2 (restore): a state JSON with shifting{shift_count:1, direction:'down', cooldown_s:0.0, cut_remaining_s:5e-13, rear_teeth:22, from_teeth:24, cadence_ema:x, required_ema:y} decodes (state_codec.cpp:323-332), passes CadenceShifter::set_state (shifting.cpp:8-22; validation::nonnegative at validation.hpp:15 rejects only NaN/<0) and passes validate(DriveSnapshot) (shifted=true satisfies drivetrain.cpp:38-42; cut>0 admits hub.ratio in the from/to interval at drivetrain.cpp:63-69). Until the next advancing tick, non-advancing/probe passes apply shift_torque_factor = 0.8 to the pedaling candidate (drivetrain.cpp:680->758, since the advance gate at 681 is skipped) while rider cut_active = false — physics treats the cut as live, the rider-facing predicate does not. REPRO 3 (organic float residue, no exotic config): update() stores next.cut_remaining_s = max(0, cut - dt) (shifting.cpp:59); iterated subtraction of binary-inexact dt leaves residues. Verified sequence: shift_cut_duration_s = 0.4, dt = 0.00125 (both the documented scenario values, native/src/validation/scenarios.cpp:113) leaves cut = 3.015e-15 in (0,1e-12] at the 320th tick after a shift; 0.3/0.00125 leaves 8.5e-16; 0.1/0.01 leaves 1.04e-17. On that residue tick the post-update state is committed with shift_active = true and shift_torque_factor < 1 recorded and applied — while the SAME post-update value reads 'no cut' at the deferral gate (3e-15 > 1e-12 false), so a caller-issued owns_shifting + shift_request through the rider_commands channel (step.cpp:1354-1369, overrideable via RuntimeRigAccess::set_rider_commands) commits a new shift during a recorded-active cut; and on the following tick LaunchContext.cut_active = false fires LaunchPlan's handover->blend transition (launch_plan.cpp:212) and unblocks upshift issue (launch_plan.cpp:146) while telemetry of the previous tick asserted the cut was active. REPRO 4 (admitted-invalid automaton path): restore {shift_count:1, direction:'up', cooldown_s:0.0, cut_remaining_s:0.1} — admitted because set_state checks each field independently and validate() only tests '!shifted && (cooldown!=0 || cut!=0)'. Next advancing tick: admitted passes (cooldown post-decrement stays 0 <= 1e-12) and the band, having no cut check, can select and commit a shift while cut_remaining_s = 0.1 - dt is still materially positive — a mid-cut shift the cooldown >= cut ordering was supposed to make impossible.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
The constraint's demand for a single threshold is not free: the two thresholds carry different locally-correct semantics. '> 0.' is the physically-exact 'was any cut time in force this tick' — it is the only choice self-consistent with the actually-applied shift_torque_factor and the recorded wire (a residue tick that applied factor 0.8 must record shift_active = true, otherwise the record contradicts itself); '> 1e-12' is the deliberate epsilon on the admission/feedback side that prevents float dust from deferring rider requests or stalling the launch plan's handover->blend transition (launch_plan.cpp:212 waits for 'the tick without a cut'). Unifying at '> 0.' blocks a valid scenario: during a non-advancing span (probe/settle-only steps, paused runtime) a committed residue cut never decrements, so cut_active would stay true indefinitely and the launch plan could never complete handover — the 1e-12 epsilon is load-bearing for 'cut is over' on the rider side. Unifying at '> 1e-12' instead breaks wire self-consistency (shift_active = false while shift_torque_factor = 0.8 was applied that same tick) and likely diverges the statement-level Python port that telemetry mirrors. So a blind single-predicate refactor wrongly overrides a real semantics fork; the deduplication itself is legitimate engineering but the threshold choice needs a ruling, not a mechanical unification.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even if a unified predicate existed, set_state/restore admits states no valid run can produce: (1) cooldown_s < cut_remaining_s (e.g. 0.0 vs 0.1) — the config-level invariant shift_cut_duration_s <= shift_cooldown_s (config_validation.hpp:161) is never re-checked on snapshots by set_state (shifting.cpp:17-18), validate(DriveSnapshot) (drivetrain.cpp:38-42) or PolicySet::validate_state (policy_set.hpp:46-57); this arms the band's missing cut check (REPRO 4). (2) cut_remaining_s unbounded above — any finite positive is admitted, e.g. 1e300 s; all predicates agree it is 'active' so no unified predicate catches a cut that outlasts the universe; nothing bounds it by shift_cut_duration_s. (3) cut_remaining_s > 0 with direction-specific factor semantics desynced: a snapshot with direction 'down' + arbitrary cadence_ema values (finite_optional only, shifting.cpp:19-21) applies torque_factor_larger_cog regardless of whether the recorded EMA pair is consistent with a mid-cut state; EMA presence is paired-checked (shifting.cpp:21) but EMA/cut coherence is not — shift_count = 5 with both EMAs null is admitted and re-seeds EMAs mid-'shift history' on the next update (shifting.cpp:51-53). (4) NaN/Inf are correctly rejected (nonnegative -> finite at validation.hpp:15-16), and negative cut is rejected — the admitted-invalid surface is purely the missing relational checks (cooldown >= cut, cut <= duration, EMA/history coherence) plus the undefined-threshold window itself.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Split the contract into two named, separately-justified predicates instead of pretending one already exists: (1) `[[nodiscard]] bool shift_cut_engaged(const ShiftingSnapshot&) noexcept { return s.cut_remaining_s > 0.; }` — the physically-applied window; single call-site owner of today's `> 0.` reads at shifting.hpp:103 (shift_torque_factor), drivetrain.cpp:203 (telemetry shift_active — must stay self-consistent with the factor that was actually applied), drivetrain.cpp:63 (validate ratio domain) and drivetrain.cpp:726 (hub sweep). (2) `[[nodiscard]] bool shift_cut_blocks_requests(const ShiftingSnapshot&) noexcept { return s.cut_remaining_s > 1e-12; }` — the admission/feedback epsilon; owner of shifting.cpp:87,112 and step.cpp:1285, documenting that residue-level dust neither defers a rider request nor stalls LaunchPlan handover (launch_plan.cpp:212). State explicitly in the contract that the (0, 1e-12] divergence between the two windows is intentional and that the telemetry flag reports the physically-applied window. Independently of the naming, close the restore gap the audit exposed: set_state or validate(DriveSnapshot) should enforce the relational invariants configs already guarantee — cooldown_s >= cut_remaining_s, cut_remaining_s <= shift_cut_duration_s (or rule otherwise), and cadence_ema/required_ema coherence with shift_count — otherwise REPRO 4 lets the band shift mid-cut. Fix the false comment at native/src/rider/launch_plan.hpp:32 claiming the hub sweeps with the 1e-12 predicate. Alternatively, if the product ruling is that the wire flag must follow the rider-visible window, then move telemetry/factor to > 1e-12 as well and accept one-tick `shift_torque_factor < 1` records under `shift_active = false` — that choice needs an explicit simulation-decisions entry, which is why this property is a mixed contract rather than a pure dedup.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P04c`: Launch Stationary/Upshift Protocol

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_engineering_contract`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Audited all five sub-claims against launch_plan.cpp and RiderIntent::resolve_full at commit 0502660d. (1) Mutual exclusion HOLDS at three independent layers: commands_of emits shift_request only from c.shift_up (settable only inside the accelerate switch arm, launch_plan.cpp:146-151, cleared on the same tick the phase exits at :207) and stationary_rear_teeth only when c.phase == hold (intent.cpp:412-417) — phase-disjoint by construction; validate(RiderCommands) rejects the pair plus owns/<3 violations (intent.cpp:94-105), enforced on the caller-override boundary (runtime_access.cpp:30); CadenceShifter::update throws 'rider shift conflict' if the pair ever arrives (shifting.cpp:39-40). Commands are fully overwritten each due tick (intent.cpp:568-570), so no stale field survives the hold->release boundary. (2) Bridging: commands.shift_request does NOT clear after the first physics sub-step — it is a level re-presented to the shifter every sub-step until the next intention tick (step.cpp:1354-1362). The single-commit bridge instead relies on the latch (shift_up held until fb.shift_count > request_count or 'rejected', launch_plan.cpp:139-145) plus the shifter's per-sub-step admission gate on cooldown_s and cut_remaining_s (shifting.cpp:63-65, 87-90, 112); rider_response is rewritten every sub-step (shifting.cpp:44) so the tick-level label is the last sub-step's verdict, and a deferred-after-commit window still clears via the count (test_launch_plan.cpp:186-188). This holds ONLY under the stated assumption shift_cooldown_s >= period_s, which no validation enforces (see counterexamples). Across Perception delay the design is correct: blocked_teeth = request_teeth suppresses re-requests while the delayed senses still report the pre-shift cog (launch_plan.cpp:141,147; 'idempotent across the 0.15 s perception lag', shifting.cpp:84). (3) shift_up == false on every accelerate exit: ->handover clears same-tick (:207); ->idle via !in.eligible resets State{} (:159); no other exit exists. (4) done/arm/reset all rebuild State{} wiping the full request memory including retry_tick (:71,122-127,215,221). (5) VIOLATED: LaunchPlan::restore checks retry_tick == -1 for release (:295) but does NOT check request_count/request_teeth/blocked_teeth, which hold explicitly enforces (:284-286).

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
REPRO A (clause 5, confirmed): with brake_release_s = 4*period, plan.restore(State{.phase=release, .phase_ticks=1, .launch_ticks=1, .brake=0.75, .request_count=9, .request_teeth=39, .blocked_teeth=51, .retry_tick=-1}) is ADMITTED — passes every check at launch_plan.cpp:289-296 — yet update() can never produce it: request memory is written only by shifting() (:148-151), which runs only in accelerate (:200), and entry into release via to_release preserves hold's enforced zeros (:284-286). Behavioral consequence after continuing: on accelerate entry the forged blocked_teeth=51 equals the engaged felt cog, so (p.senses.rear_teeth != n.blocked_teeth || retry) stays false with retry_tick==-1 (:146-147) and every upshift is suppressed for the whole launch (up to max_launch_s) — a wrong-gear launch from an admitted-invalid snapshot, breaching the documented contract 'Rejects every state update() cannot leave behind' (launch_plan.hpp:89-91). REPRO B (disabled plan, admitted): with plan_enabled=false, restore(State{.phase=accelerate, .shift_up=true, .request_teeth=51, .launch_ticks>=release_ticks_, .launch_ticks<max_launch_ticks_}) is admitted; update() then early-returns command() at :157 before the eligibility reset, so the 'always idle' contract (hpp:6) is permanently broken — every tick emits owns_shifting=true + shift_request=up to the shifter, phantom rider shifts driven solely by a corrupt restore. REPRO C (unenforced assumption, latent): profile with shift_cooldown_s=0.003, shift_cut_duration_s=0.002, seated_climb.period_s=0.005 — passes all validation (writer_config.hpp:997-1010 checks only cut<=cooldown and nonnegative; launch_config.cpp:169-190 checks only plan times on the period grid; nothing relates cooldown to period_s) — a commit at the first sub-step lets both cooldown and cut expire inside the ~4-sub-step request window, so the still-held request commits a SECOND upshift before the next tick clears the latch (shift_count +2 per request) — the 'single-step' bridge fails exactly as the stated assumption predicts; shipped profiles (cooldown 0.25 s vs period 0.005 s) keep it inactive. REPRO D (cross-component inconsistency): RiderIntent::restore checks the plan clocks vs delivered percepts (intent.cpp:681-683) but nothing cross-checks plan.request_count against the drivetrain's restored shift_count; a forged request_count = count+k holds the latch while the request is re-committed each cooldown (k extra cogs shifted by one field) until the count catches up — bounded only by max_launch_ticks.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No valid product scenario is wrongly blocked. The two channels occupy disjoint phase windows matching the design: stationary_rear_teeth is an absolute jump-to-cog for standstill prep that deliberately bypasses the admitted/braking gate so a reposition commits under held brakes (shifting.cpp:68-106); shift_request is the single-adjacent-cog step used only while accelerating (shifting.cpp:124-136). Retiring the request at accelerate exit loses nothing: an in-flight mechanical cut still completes while handover waits on !cut_active (launch_plan.cpp:212-216, test :309-317), and a merely-deferred request is redundant once the cadence band resumes in blend. The blocked_teeth-until-felt-cog rule costs one perception delay of pause per committed shift — required to avoid re-requesting on the stale cog during the ~0.15 s lag (Perception, perception.cpp:117-122; shifting.cpp:84). done->re-arm on standstill implements the recorded mid-ride restart decision (simulation-decisions.md item 14, test :328-342). RECORDING.md carries shift_request/owns_shifting/stationary as fields of one rider_commands channel, so the exclusivity preserves the closed recorded domain rather than constraining behavior. The property gates only corrupt/restore inputs and hostile configs, not authored rides.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Beyond the clause-5 gap, restore/update still admit states update() cannot produce: (a) release-phase request memory — the headliner (see repro A); (b) a !plan_enabled plan holding any non-idle state, which then emits live brake/weight/shift commands forever (repro B) — restore never checks plan_enabled and update() early-returns before the eligibility reset (launch_plan.cpp:157); (c) accelerate/handover/blend with phase_ticks != 0 — phase_ticks is checked only for hold/release (:283,:290-291) yet is dead in later phases, benign but outside the reachable domain; (d) request_teeth/blocked_teeth in {1,2} when !shift_up — only <0 is rejected (:268) since the >=3 floor is gated on shift_up (:270), while Perception enforces rear_teeth >= 3 (perception.cpp:44-46), benign since such values never equal a felt cog; (e) forged request_count uncorrelated with the restored shifter shift_count (repro D) — an admitted-invalid state with real multi-shift effect; (f) rise_ticks larger than any reachable elapsed tick count (no cross-bound vs launch_ticks/phase_ticks), impact capped at the surface cap by ramp (:109-114); (g) adjacent to scope: RiderIntent::restore re-commits state.intent unvalidated (intent.cpp:704) — decode bounds the posture (state_codec.cpp:2000-2003) but effort_ceiling_nm is only finite-checked (:1021-1022), so a direct-API restore can inject a negative/arbitrary ceiling emitted on non-due steps until the next tick recomputes.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Keep clauses 1-4 as stated — they hold and match the design. Replace clause 5's assertion with the reachable-domain rule and implement it: in the LaunchPhase::release branch of LaunchPlan::restore (launch_plan.cpp:289-296) add 's.request_count != 0 || s.request_teeth != 0 || s.blocked_teeth != 0' to the rejection set, mirroring hold (:284-286) but WITHOUT preload_missed (legitimately true in release after a hold timeout, :182-183) — the precise contract is 'request memory and retry_tick are exactly the zeros/-1 hold enforces, in every phase preceding accelerate'. Optionally tighten further: reject phase_ticks != 0 in accelerate/handover/blend and request_teeth/blocked_teeth in {1,2} regardless of shift_up, and reject any non-idle state when !config_.plan_enabled (or make update() reset instead of early-returning at :157 so a disabled plan self-heals). Elevate the single-step assumption from convention to contract: add 'shift_cooldown_s >= seated_climb.period_s' to the root post_init beside validate_launch_plan_context (validate.cpp:1143), or explicitly document it as a profile precondition since it is what makes the level-held request single-commit; consider a whole-snapshot check that plan.request_count never exceeds the restored drivetrain shift_count.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P05a`: Plan-Disabled Phase Quiescence

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_confirmed_requirement`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
The statement holds at three of the four lifecycle points and fails at the fourth. Construction leaves s_ = State{} (phase=idle, launch_plan.hpp:56,105); arm_start() returns false before mutating when disabled (launch_plan.cpp:74); update() returns command() without touching s_ when disabled (launch_plan.cpp:157). But LaunchPlan::restore() (launch_plan.cpp:257-311) contains NO plan_enabled check anywhere: it validates only phase-grammar, counter, cap and per-phase coherence rules and then commits s_ = s unconditionally (line 310). A fully self-consistent non-idle state is accepted on a disabled plan for every phase: hold needs only {phase=hold, brake=1.} (checks at 281-288 all pass with the other fields defaulted: phase_ticks=0 < max_hold_ticks_, launch/end_ok/blend=0, !preload_missed, request memory zero, retry_tick=-1, effort 0 <= max_cap, rise_ticks=-1); release takes {phase_ticks=0, launch_ticks=0, brake=1} when release_ticks_>0 (289-296); accelerate/handover take {brake=0, launch_ticks>=release_ticks_} (297-308); blend takes {blend_ticks<handover_ticks_} when handover_ticks_>0; done takes State{.phase=done} (277-279). The violation is not inert: command() (228-249) derives weight=1 and owns_shifting=true from s_.phase==hold with no enabled guard, and update() returns it forever because the disabled early-return at line 157 precedes the !eligible -> s_=State{} reset at 158-161, so a restored non-idle phase can never self-heal; only reset() or another restore() clears it. Reachability is production, not just unit: state.rider_intent document -> decode_intent_state (runtime.cpp:321-323; state_codec.cpp:2336-2338 decodes launch whenever the key is non-null) -> NativeRideRuntime::apply_bootstrap -> physical_->restore (runtime.cpp:484,544) -> PhysicalStep::restore -> rider_intent_.restore (step.cpp:3074) -> staged_->launch->restore (intent.cpp:693). The intent-level gates do not block it: presence only requires state.launch.has_value()==core_->launch.has_value() (intent.cpp:658), and core_->launch is engaged whenever the intent is enabled (intent.cpp:464) regardless of plan_enabled; encode_intent_state emits launch non-null for every enabled intent (intent.cpp:650, state_codec.cpp:2449-2452, capture.cpp:215). The clock joint rule (intent.cpp:680-683) only bounds launch_ticks<=delivered and hold phase_ticks<=delivered; a hold with phase_ticks=0/launch_ticks=0 passes even at last_tick_step=-1. No NaN/Inf trick is needed; plain in-range values suffice.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
Unit repro (extends the existing fixture, native/tests/reference/test_launch_plan.cpp:42-75,355-361 which tests the switch only for arm_start/update, never restore): rider::LaunchConfig t = table(); t.plan_enabled = false; rider::LaunchPlan plan(t, kPeriod); rider::LaunchPlan::State hold; hold.phase = rider::LaunchPhase::hold; hold.brake = 1.; plan.restore(hold); -> returns normally; plan.state().phase == LaunchPhase::hold violates P05a. plan.update({.percepts=p,.eligible=true}) then returns {phase=hold, brake=1., weight=1., owns_shifting=true} (launch_plan.cpp:157,232-247). End-to-end: a captured/edited state document with config.rider_launch.plan_enabled=false, seated_climb.enabled=true and state.rider_intent.launch={phase:'hold',brake:1,zeros} passes decode_launch_state (senses_codec.cpp:549-575) and RiderIntent::restore; staged_->commands becomes {front_brake=1,rear_brake=1,owns_shifting=true} via commands_of (intent.cpp:405-419,699-702), resolve_brakes merges it into final_front/final_rear so motor_braking/shift_blocked set and riding=false (step.cpp:2396-2416); every subsequent tick keeps it (update early-returns the hold command), intent.effort_ceiling_nm is overridden to plan.effort_nm=0 because plan.weight==1 (intent.cpp:553-566) so the rider never pedals; output() flags effort_limited (intent.cpp:592); balance_channel reads launch().phase==hold and reports BalanceStop::braked at standstill under motor_braking (step.cpp:2804-2809); with delayed senses grade_ahead>0 it even emits stationary_rear_teeth (intent.cpp:412-417). The bike is permanently braked, unpowered and shift-owned by a plan the config says is off — contradicting decision 1's byte-identical disabled output. done phase ({phase=done} alone) also restores on a disabled plan and stays done forever (weight 0, benign output, but phase != idle still violates the statement).

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
None found in the documented contracts. The switch is a confirmed product decision, not an implementation nicety: plan-3b decision 1 (docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-12-launch-3b-launch-plan.md:29-34) states 'при false узел всегда в idle, команда нейтральная (тормоз 0, вес 0, просьбы нет, владения переключениями нет), и выход намерений побайтно равен выходу части 3a', pinned by oracle test test_switched_off_resolver_equals_the_bare_policy; the header repeats 'Off (always idle) unless rider_launch.plan_enabled' (launch_plan.hpp:6). plan_enabled is fixed at LaunchPlan construction (config_ has no mutator; step.cpp:1033 builds the intent once from config_.launch decoded at runtime.cpp:354) so no mid-run disable path exists that could strand a legitimate non-idle phase. Honest documents never trigger the proposed reject: a capture written by a disabled plan always serializes phase=idle, and checked replay is same-environment/same-config (RECORDING.md 'Replay execution identity', lines 288-296), so a plan-enabled capture is never replayed under a plan-disabled config without editing the document — an out-of-contract input where decision 11's own rule ('rejects every state update() cannot leave behind', hpp:89-91; plan doc lines 137-142) already mandates loud rejection, since update() on a disabled plan can leave only idle. The only arguable friction is a hypothetical ablation workflow (restore a mid-launch capture with the switch flipped off): rejection would block it where a 'clamp launch to State{}' policy would proceed; no document in RIDE.md, RECORDING.md or simulation-decisions.md sanctions cross-config state transplants, so this is at most an unresolved nicety, not a wrongly-blocked valid scenario.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even with a plan_enabled guard added, restore's 'rejects every state update() cannot leave behind' contract still admits unreachable states (inert for outputs but same class of gap): (a) phase_ticks>0 in accelerate/handover/blend — update() leaves it 0 there (to_release zeroes it at launch_plan.cpp:131; only hold/release increment at 177,187), yet restore bounds it only in hold/release windows (262,283,290); {accelerate, phase_ticks=1e9, launch_ticks=release_ticks_} is admitted. (b) end_ok_ticks unbounded in handover/blend — reachable maximum is end_hold_ticks_+1 (update caps at 202); restore bounds it only in accelerate (302-304), so {handover, end_ok_ticks=INT64_MAX} is admitted. (c) launch_ticks unbounded above in handover/blend — reachable values lie in [release_ticks_, max_launch_ticks_] (transition at 205); restore checks only >=release_ticks_ (301), admitting launch_ticks=INT64_MAX; the delivered-clock joint rule (intent.cpp:681-683) catches this only when restoring through RiderIntent, not through LaunchPlan::restore itself. (d) request memory in release — hold requires request_count/request_teeth/blocked_teeth==0 (284-286) and to_release never sets them, but the release case (289-296) omits that check, admitting {release, request_count=5}. (e) update()'s own validate() does not bound senses.rear_teeth or the sign of crank_rpm (83-89), so request_teeth<3 via a 1-2-tooth felt cog and a negative cadence counting as standstill (standstill(), 117-120) are reachable — consistent that restore then admits them. For the property's own scope the disabled path is airtight once the guard exists (idle/done already require exact reset equality at 279), but the deeper sufficiency caveat: the hazard lives in consumers reading s_.phase unconditionally — command() (228-249), commands_of (intent.cpp:405-419), resolve_brakes (step.cpp:2396), balance_channel (step.cpp:2806) — with zero quiescence masking when disabled, so the invariant is the only barrier; a neutral-command-when-disabled fallback in command() would be the defense-in-depth the file lacks, and the line-157 early return ordering guarantees any non-idle disabled state, once admitted, is permanent.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Keep the statement and extend restore's per-phase grammar with the switch: '!config_.plan_enabled => s_.phase == LaunchPhase::idle across construction, arm_start(), update(), reset(), restore() and command() output'; implement as `if (!config_.plan_enabled && s.phase != LaunchPhase::idle) bad_state(launch_phase_name(s.phase), "a switched-off plan is always idle");` early in LaunchPlan::restore (before the phase switch at launch_plan.cpp:276), which rejects every non-idle phase uniformly rather than relying on per-case checks. Add a regression test mirroring test_ineligible_ticks_cancel_and_the_switch_keeps_idle that calls restore() on a disabled plan with hold, done and a coherent accelerate state and asserts rejection plus unchanged state. Optional hardening, defense-in-depth rather than contract change: have command() return the neutral command (phase=idle, weight=0, owns_shifting=false) whenever !config_.plan_enabled regardless of s_, so future writers cannot leak a phantom command; and tighten RiderIntent::restore to reject state.launch carrying a non-idle phase when the configured plan is disabled at the presence-check site (intent.cpp:658-683), keeping the failure named at the intent boundary. If an ablation replay (plan-enabled capture restored under plan-disabled config) is ever desired, decide explicitly between reject (decision-11-consistent, current proposal) and clamp-to-State{} — do not silently accept as today.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P05b`: Launch/Front-Lift/Intent/Posture Restore Sentinel & Bounds Coherence

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `mixed_subclauses`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
The statement is a mixed bundle: several subclauses match the code, several describe checks that do not exist, and the two Seated* clauses are entirely absent. VERIFIED: (a) rise_ticks uses the -1 sentinel (launch_plan.hpp:71; set to 0 on the first percept at/above effort_rise_speed_mps in ramp(), launch_plan.cpp:110-112, then ++ every eligible tick at launch_plan.cpp:164-168); idle/done pin rise_ticks=-1 indirectly via the whole-state equality at launch_plan.cpp:279. (b) FrontLiftReaction::restore effectively validates-then-commits: every check runs on the const-ref candidate and s_=s is the last statement (front_lift_reaction.cpp:208-284); RiderIntent::restore additionally stages into *staged_ and commits by a noexcept unique_ptr swap (intent.cpp:685-705). (c) RiderIntent::restore does enforce plan.launch_ticks <= delivered and hold's phase_ticks <= delivered (intent.cpp:673-683). FALSE/ABSENT: (d) 'rise_ticks < phase_ticks in hold' — no such check in restore (launch_plan.cpp:281-288 never mentions rise_ticks) AND it is not even a runtime invariant: rise_ticks counts every eligible tick once started (line 164-168) while hold's phase_ticks counts only percept ticks without external_brake (line 176-177); a hold with rise_ticks > phase_ticks is reachable. (e) 'rise_ticks < delivered in RiderIntent::restore' — never checked; the delivered bound covers launch_ticks and hold phase_ticks only. (f) 'handover/blend enforce launch_ticks <= max(release_ticks_, max_launch_ticks_)' — inverted: the accelerate/handover/blend block enforces the LOWER bound launch_ticks >= release_ticks_ (line 301: 's.launch_ticks < release_ticks_' -> reject) and only accelerate gets the upper bound launch_ticks < max_launch_ticks_ (lines 302-304); handover/blend admit launch_ticks up to INT64_MAX though runtime caps it at max_launch_ticks_ (accelerate exits at >= max, lines 205-208). (g) 'blend_ticks <= delivered' — no such check; blend_ticks is bounded only by < handover_ticks_ (line 305). (h) 'SeatedPostureProgram::restore validates duration_s>0, [0,1] load shares, finite fields' — restore is a raw field copy with zero validation (intent.cpp:260-266); the wire decoder decode_intent_program (state_codec.cpp:1053-1074) enforces only finiteness: load_samples share and delayed_load_share use optional_real with NO [0,1] bound (contrast decode_signals' front_load_share [0,1] gate at state_codec.cpp:986-990), and pulses are fixed<3> finite triples with no duration>0 check. (i) 'SeatedClimbPolicy::restore validates time_s>=0, effort_nm>=0, [0,surge_budget]' — same: raw copy at intent.cpp:388-396, finite-only at decode (state_codec.cpp:1026-1051); no sign, no [0,surge_budget_s] bound anywhere.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
Each input decodes cleanly and is committed by restore (then RiderIntent::restore for nested states): (1) LaunchPlan::restore({phase=hold, brake=1., rise_ticks=INT64_MAX, /*all else default*/}) — passes every hold check (brake==1, phase_ticks=0<max_hold_ticks_, counters zero, retry_tick=-1, rise_ticks>=-1 at launch_plan.cpp:271-288); the NEXT eligible update() unconditionally throws 'launch plan rise tick counter overflowed' (launch_plan.cpp:164-166) — a restore that dooms the following tick and a state update() cannot leave behind, violating launch_plan.hpp:89-90. (2) LaunchPlan::restore({phase=handover, launch_ticks=1LL<<40, brake=0., effort_nm within cap}) — admitted (only launch_ticks>=release_ticks_ enforced, line 301); runtime handover launch_ticks <= max_launch_ticks_ <= 2^31 — unreachable. Same for phase_ticks!=0 in accelerate/handover/blend (frozen at 0 since release->accelerate, line 193) and end_ok_ticks arbitrary in handover/blend (runtime <= end_hold_ticks_+1). (3) LaunchPlan::restore({phase=hold, brake=1.}) on a plan built with plan_enabled=false — admitted; update() then early-returns at line 157 BEFORE the !eligible reset (158-161), so command() (lines 240-246) emits brake=1, weight=1, owns_shifting=true forever; RiderIntent::tick maps it to RiderCommands{front=1,rear=1,owns_shifting} plus lean/effort override (intent.cpp:549-570) — a switched-off plan brakes the bike and can never be cleared. Accelerate+shift_up (request_teeth>=3 passes) yields permanent upshift requests from a disabled plan. (4) Reachable violation of the claimed invariant: armed hold + percepts with speed_mps>=effort_rise_speed_mps for 2 ticks (rise_ticks=1, phase_ticks=2 via ramp at line 178), then external_brake=true for 5 ticks (line 176 break: phase_ticks stays 2, rise_ticks grows to 6) — a committed hold state with rise_ticks>phase_ticks that no restore check rejects and that the statement's '< phase_ticks' bound would wrongly forbid. (5) SeatedPostureProgram::restore({pulses={{0., -1., 0.1}}}) — admitted (no validation, intent.cpp:260-266; wire path also passes fixed<3>, state_codec.cpp:1069-1072); every subsequent program.update() throws inside body_pulse 'pulse duration must be positive' (intent.cpp:213 -> 109-115) -> every RiderIntent::tick throws -> resolve_full throws every intention tick forever. Same dooming for lean_rad with |x|>0.8 (finite on wire, then validate_posture throws at intent.cpp:374 each tick) or NaN via the C++ API. (6) decode_intent_program with load_samples [[t, 1e9]] or delayed_load_share=1e9 — optional_real admits out-of-[0,1] (state_codec.cpp:1004-1010, 1067-1068); restored value feeds trim_rad_ += gain*(target - share)*dt unvalidated (intent.cpp:226-238) — trim pinned to +lean_trim_limit on bogus input. (7) SeatedClimbPolicy::restore({surge_budget_s_left=1e9}) — admitted (finite-only decode, state_codec.cpp:1048-1049); power_target_w then returns surge_power_w indefinitely (intent.cpp:304-308: 'surge_budget_s_left_ + 1e-12 >= dt' stays true) — the surge budget contract 'surge then recover' is silently replaced by unlimited surge. (8) RiderIntent::restore with state.intent.effort_ceiling_nm=-50. — nothing checks it (decode_intent_block finite-only, state_codec.cpp:1021-1022; restore assigns verbatim at intent.cpp:704); output() copies it into control.human_torque_nm (intent.cpp:581) — a negative rider effort the same codebase rejects in decode_rider_command (state_codec.cpp:2026-2027). (9) policy.time_s=-1e9 or non-multiple-of-period admitted -> ready_time window desynced (intent.cpp:338-343), samples never pop, phantom delayed_ drives inclination/effort forever. (10) FrontLiftReaction::restore saturated-branch hole: config window_ticks_=10, lag=1, decision=0, clear=0 -> percepts_cap()=3 < window_ticks_+1=11; restore(percepts_seen=3, hand_n.size()=11, crank_angle_rad.size()=11, matching total) passes line 217-218 ('hands < min(cap,window+1) || hands > window+1' is false for 11) though update() can never produce more entries than percepts.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No valid product scenario is wrongly blocked by the IMPLEMENTED checks — they deliberately test necessary conditions, not exact reachability (decision 11, docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-12-launch-3b-launch-plan.md:137-142), and committed hold/blend states round-trip bitwise (native/tests/reference/test_launch_plan.cpp:366-386). Specific checks: the delivered bounds (launch_ticks, hold phase_ticks at intent.cpp:681-683) hold with margin because release can only start on a percept tick; accelerate upper bound matches the same-tick exit at launch_ticks==max_launch_ticks_; hold phase_ticks<max_hold_ticks_ matches the to_release transition (line 181); blend bounds match the same-tick done transition (line 221) and handover_ticks_==0 unreachable-by-design. rise_ticks>=0 in hold is also correctly NOT blocked: it is reachable when delayed percepts still show pre-stop speed or arm_start is invoked on a rolling bike (arm() never checks standstill; only the idle/done auto-arm at line 173 does). The necessity problem is instead on the STATEMENT's side: had the claimed 'rise_ticks < phase_ticks in hold' bound been implemented, it would wrongly reject the reachable hold state of counterexample (4) — rise_ticks legitimately counts eligible ticks while phase_ticks counts only percept/no-external-brake ticks. Likewise 'blend_ticks <= delivered' would be a correct invariant but is unneeded since blend_ticks < handover_ticks_ <= 2^31 already caps it. The Seated* restores cannot block anything by construction (no checks).

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even granting every claim that holds, these corrupt/unreachable states are still admitted: (A) Poisoning restores — program.pulses with duration<=0 (throws every update, intent.cpp:213->114-115), program.lean_rad/policy.lean_rad outside the posture envelope or NaN via C++ API (throws via validate_posture at intent.cpp:372-374 each tick), load_samples with non-monotone/absurd time_s (stalls the front-pop loop at intent.cpp:199-203, pinning delayed_load_share), policy.time_s negative or off the period grid (desyncs ready_time at intent.cpp:338), samples_ unsorted, delayed_ engaged while no sample could be due (time_s < reaction_delay_s), surge_budget_s_left outside [0, surge_budget_s] (unbounded or dead surge). (B) Unreachable launch states — non-idle phase under plan_enabled=false that update() can never produce and can never clear (early return at launch_plan.cpp:157 beats the reset at 158-161) yet emits live brake/owns_shifting/effort/lean commands; launch_ticks>max_launch_ticks_ in handover/blend (no upper bound, only >= release_ticks_ at line 301); phase_ticks!=0 in accelerate/handover/blend; end_ok_ticks>end_hold_ticks_+1 in handover/blend; blocked_teeth in {1,2} (runtime: 0 or a real cog >=3); rise_ticks=INT64_MAX in any non-idle phase (guaranteed throw next eligible tick); rise_ticks>delivered. (C) Cross-field incoherence — RiderIntent::restore never validates state.intent (effort_ceiling_nm sign/cap, posture fields other than torso_lean_rad which update() never writes so runtime always carries defaults), never cross-checks policy.time_s against last_tick_step, never bounds blend_ticks or non-hold clocks by delivered, and skips every last_tick_step check when the intent is disabled (guarded by if(core_->perception) at intent.cpp:660). (D) Reaction gap — saturated hand_n admits lengths in (cap, window+1] when percepts_cap() < window_ticks_+1 (front_lift_reaction.cpp:217-218); crank_angle_rad monotonicity and hand_average_n-vs-hand_n consistency are unverified beyond the back-entry equality (lines 224-226) — documented as necessary-conditions scope. (E) Adjacent seam — PhysicalStep::restore applies rider_intent_.restore without checking last_tick_step <= step_ (step.cpp:3047,3074); a snapshot whose intent clock runs ahead of the restored physics step makes every future resolve() throw 'reset rider intention before rewinding' (intent.cpp:610-611). (F) Marginal — LaunchPlan ctor ticks() uses llround (launch_plan.cpp:18-19) while validate_launch_plan_context uses rint (launch_config.cpp:174); a half-integer ratio can pass the 1e-9 grid tolerance only when period_s <= ~2e-9 s, then convert differently. (G) Wire asymmetry — program load_samples share and delayed_load_share decode via optional_real with no [0,1] bound (state_codec.cpp:1004-1010, 1067-1068) while the same quantity inside IntentSignals is bounded (state_codec.cpp:986-990).

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Restate the enforced contract as: (1) rise_ticks is a -1 sentinel; it MUST be -1 in idle/done (enforced via whole-state equality, launch_plan.cpp:279) and only >= -1 elsewhere; it counts every eligible tick once started, so it may exceed hold's phase_ticks (which counts only percept ticks without external_brake) — the claimed '< phase_ticks in hold' and '< delivered' bounds do NOT exist; recommend either adding 'rise_ticks <= delivered' in RiderIntent::restore or documenting the gap; a bound like rise_ticks < INT64_MAX would also prevent the next-tick overflow throw. (2) Counter bounds actually enforced: launch_ticks >= release_ticks_ for accelerate/handover/blend plus launch_ticks < max_launch_ticks_ for accelerate only (launch_plan.cpp:297-308) — extend the upper bound to handover/blend (runtime <= max_launch_ticks_); launch_ticks <= delivered and hold phase_ticks <= delivered in RiderIntent::restore (intent.cpp:681-683) — extend the delivered bound to blend_ticks and non-hold phase_ticks if exact coherence is desired; add phase_ticks==0 for accelerate/handover/blend and end_ok_ticks<=end_hold_ticks_+1 for handover/blend if decision-11 tightness is intended. (3) LaunchPlan::restore should reject non-idle phases when config_.plan_enabled==false — a disabled plan currently admits live commands it can never clear. (4) SeatedPostureProgram::restore and SeatedClimbPolicy::restore currently perform NO validation; the claimed checks (duration_s>0, [0,1] load shares, finite fields; time_s>=0, effort_nm>=0, surge_budget_s_left in [0,surge_budget_s]) are absent on both the C++ API and the wire decoder — either implement them (recommended: finite fields, lean within the posture envelope, pulses duration>0, load/delayed shares in [0,1], samples sorted with times <= time_s, delayed_ consistency, time_s nonnegative and on the period grid, effort_nm>=0, surge budget in [0,surge_budget_s]) or restate the property as 'the wire decoder enforces finiteness only and restore trusts the decoder' to match reality. (5) FrontLiftReaction::restore: validate-then-commit holds (checks on the candidate const-ref, s_=s last); tighten the saturated hand_n bound to exactly min(percepts_seen, window_ticks_+1) to close the cap<window+1 hole. (6) RiderIntent::restore keeps the presence match and the delivered-clocks joint rule; add intent.effort_ceiling_nm>=0 (and optionally the posture envelope for the C++ API path), last_tick_step sanity when disabled, and a last_tick_step<=step cross-check at the PhysicalStep boundary.
