# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P05c`: Automatic Rider-Command Domain & Clamp Bounds

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_confirmed_requirement`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Clause-by-clause findings. (1) The effort-merge guard set holds exactly as stated: resolve_full returns the caller control untouched when !config_.enabled || !active (native/src/rider/intent.cpp:606-607), output() returns untouched when !control.rider_enabled (intent.cpp:575-576), and the human_torque_nm merge — ceiling, reaction factor, and launch-weight composition — is gated by !control.human_torque_nm.has_value() (intent.cpp:580-593). PhysicalStep::apply_forces_body mirrors the identical four-condition predicate as automatic_effort for the drivetrain ceiling/sensed argument (native/src/runtime/step.cpp:1259-1262, 1367-1368). (2) The torso_lean guard holds: control.posture is filled from core.intent.posture only when the caller's posture is absent (intent.cpp:577-578), inside the same enabled&&active&&rider_enabled envelope. (3) PARTIALLY VIOLATED BY MISATTRIBUTION: SeatedPostureProgram::update does NOT enforce min(config_.max_forward_lean_rad, *lean_limit_rad). It computes forward_limit = lean_limit_rad.value_or(config_.max_forward_lean_rad) (intent.cpp:204-205) — an explicit limit REPLACES the configured ceiling, and the project's own test proves it: config max .1 + lean_limit .4 yields lean .4 (native/tests/reference/test_seated_posture_program.cpp:660-665, 'an explicit geometric limit replaces the config ceiling'). The min() is composed by the sole production caller, PhysicalStep::apply_forces_body, which passes std::min(config_.intent.max_forward_lean_rad, *rider_control_->lean_limit_rad()) (step.cpp:1291-1294) — matching the recorded user decision in docs/agents/simulation-decisions.md (2026-11-16: the limit 'строго ограничен минимумом из анатомического предела и настройки профиля'). Effective system bound holds on the production path, but at the update/SeatedClimbPolicy::update/resolve_full API boundary a caller passing lean_limit_rad > max_forward_lean_rad receives posture intent above the configured envelope; only the ±0.8 rad validate_posture throw (intent.cpp:62-64, 374) stops it further out. (4) start_lean_rad is bounded only against the configured bounds [-max_backward, max_forward] (validate.cpp:1137-1140, launch_config.cpp:91-92, SeatedPostureProgram::seed_lean intent.cpp:143-148), never against the kinematic forward_limit; a prepared lean above the effective limit then decays toward target <= forward_limit at lean_rate_rad_s (intent.cpp:241-246) — the slew-decay hedge holds, though the code does not document it. Additionally the min_lean_rad floor can snap lean_rad_ UP to backward_limit instantaneously (intent.cpp:247-248), an un-slewed jump within the envelope. Composition-order gap outside the stated claim: RiderCommands (brake, shift ownership, stationary cog) are automatic rider commands merged unconditionally into BrakeResolution and rider_shift (step.cpp:2396-2410, 1354-1362) — they are NOT gated by rider_enabled/active/human_torque; only the launch plan's internal eligible flag (step.cpp:551; launch_plan.cpp:158-161) resets them on the next intent tick, so a mid-hold rider disable or caller torque override leaves the plan's brake=1 applied for up to period_steps_ physics steps.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
R1 (clause 3, direct): SeatedPostureProgram(config with max_forward_lean_rad=.1).update(0., .5, .1, std::nullopt, .4) returns .4 — lean intent above the configured ceiling; already asserted by test_seated_posture_program.cpp:660-665, so the named function demonstrably does not enforce the min the statement assigns to it. Not reachable through step.cpp's pre-clamped call, but reachable for any other caller of update/resolve_full (e.g., a future research path or the ABI passing an unclamped kinematic reach > config max). R2 (restore path): a state file with program.lean_rad=0.7 decodes fine (finite_real only, state_codec.cpp:1060) and restore() admits it (intent.cpp:260-266); with forward_limit=.4 the next tick emits posture .7 — transiently above forward_limit, covered by the slew-decay hedge but above the bound the clause names. With program.lean_rad=2.0 the same restore is admitted and the next policy.update throws via validate_posture (intent.cpp:374) instead of being rejected at restore — deferred, transaction-safe but loud-by-accident. R3 (instantaneous snap): any restore or qpos discontinuity that raises min_torso_lean_rad (elbow floor) above the current lean makes intent.cpp:247-248 teleport lean_rad_ to backward_limit with no slew bound — reachable in one step, not decay. R4 (brake domain): with the launch plan in hold (brake=1), a caller presenting rider_enabled=false or human_torque_nm at a non-tick step keeps final_front=final_rear=1 through resolve_brakes (step.cpp:2403-2408) until the next due tick clears commands via eligible=false — automatic rider brake force applied while the rider is explicitly disabled.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No valid product scenario is wrongly blocked. The min() composition is itself a user-confirmed product decision (docs/agents/simulation-decisions.md, 2026-11-16 entry: the runtime 'передаваемый в намерение райдера предел наклона строго ограничен минимумом из анатомического предела и настройки профиля' and bounds channel_control.posture.torso_lean_rad <= 0.55 rad); treating anatomical reach as able to raise the behavioral ceiling would defeat the decision's purpose. Caller-supplied posture/human_torque suppression is the documented manual-override path (RideControl post_init / control.py semantics). Slew decay from a seeded lean is required for prepared-lean launch starts whose table lean may exceed the kinematic reach. The instantaneous backward_limit snap is the elbow-flexion floor enforcement (decision: no backward lean past ~straight arms / 15 deg) — prompt clamping is the point, not a blocked behavior.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
The restore path admits states update() can never produce, breaking the repo's own reachable-domain restore convention that Perception/FrontLiftReaction/LaunchPlan do enforce (perception.cpp:129-163, front_lift_reaction.cpp:203-284, launch_plan.cpp:257-311; header: 'Rejects every state update() cannot leave behind'): (a) state.intent.effort_ceiling_nm is decoded with finite_real only (state_codec.cpp:1021-1022) and committed verbatim (intent.cpp:704); output() then merges it into control.human_torque_nm without re-validation (intent.cpp:581; the caller gate control_validate_for ran pre-merge at step.cpp:1246). A negative value throws later inside the drivetrain's nonnegative check (pedaling.cpp:60), an arbitrarily large positive value is silently admitted as rider torque far above max_crank_torque_nm. (b) state.intent.posture admits shape-foreign fields the program never emits — use_saddle=false, pelvis_pitch_rad!=0, pelvis_offset_m — all inside codec bounds (state_codec.cpp:1997-2012) while the policy only constructs {torso_lean_rad,use_saddle=true} (intent.cpp:371-374); output() emits the held posture verbatim on every resolve until the next tick. (c) state.program fields are unchecked: lean_rad beyond ±0.8 defers to a next-tick validate_posture throw instead of a restore rejection; pulses entries with duration<=0 defer to a body_pulse throw; delayed_load_share/load_samples outside [0,1] are admitted (effect bounded by the trim clamp only). (d) state.policy.surge_budget_s_left is unchecked — a restored 1e9 spends surge_power_w every tick indefinitely, exceeding the configured budget update() enforces via min(config.surge_budget_s,...). (e) Cross-component consistency is unchecked: PhysicalStep::restore (step.cpp:3046-3074) does not verify s.step >= s.rider_intent.last_tick_step, so a well-formed state file wedges every subsequent intent-enabled apply_forces into the 'reset rider intention before rewinding physics' throw (intent.cpp:610-611) with no recovery path short of reset.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Restate with correct enforcement attribution and the observed gaps: 'On the automatic path (PhysicalStep::apply_forces_body, native/src/runtime/step.cpp:1259-1298), effort composition (policy ceiling, climb cap, launch weight, reaction factor) is emitted only when config_.enabled && active && control.rider_enabled && !control.human_torque_nm.has_value(); torso_lean_rad is emitted only when additionally !control.posture.has_value() (intent.cpp:575-593). The caller composes the effective forward lean bound as min(config_.intent.max_forward_lean_rad, kinematic reach) at step.cpp:1291-1294; SeatedPostureProgram::update itself treats a supplied lean_limit_rad as the authoritative forward limit (replacement semantics, intent.cpp:204-205 — required for oracle parity), so the min() invariant is caller-maintained and any new resolve/update call site must re-impose it. start_lean_rad is validated against the configured bounds only (validate.cpp:1137-1140); a seeded or restored lean above the effective forward_limit is permitted to decay toward it at lean_rate_rad_s (slew decay), while the min_lean_rad floor may snap lean_rad_ upward to backward_limit instantly (intent.cpp:247-248). Out of scope of the stated guard but on the same channel: automatic RiderCommands (brake/shift) merge unconditionally and clear only at the next intent tick after eligible becomes false (launch_plan.cpp:158-161); up to one intention period of stale brake force can persist after rider_enabled flips false. Restore-domain gap: state.intent.effort_ceiling_nm, state.intent.posture (use_saddle/pelvis fields), state.program.lean_rad/pulses/load_samples and state.policy.surge_budget_s_left are admitted at restore with finite-only checks — a reachable-domain validation pass (like Perception/FrontLiftReaction/LaunchPlan::restore) is needed before the property can claim no out-of-envelope automatic commands on restored runs.'

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P05d`: Bounded Launch & Front-Lift Termination

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_engineering_contract`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Compliance analysis of the two clauses. (1) LaunchPlan: the stated bound `release_ticks_ + max(0, max_launch_ticks_ - release_ticks_) + cut_clear_ticks + handover_ticks_` is short by exactly one intention tick for every config with `max_launch_ticks_ <= release_ticks_`, because `validate_launch_plan_context` (launch_config.cpp:184-190) constrains each duration independently and never requires `max_launch_s > brake_release_s` (`brake_release_s` only `on_grid` >= 0, `max_launch_s` only `positive_on_grid` >= 1). `launch_ticks` already reaches `release_ticks_` inside `release` (launch_plan.cpp:186-194), but the exit check `launch_ticks >= max_launch_ticks_` (launch_plan.cpp:205) is evaluated only in `accelerate`, so the machine always spends one accelerate update before `handover` when the deadline has already passed. Actual ticks release->handover = `max(release_ticks_ + 1, max_launch_ticks_)`; the formula gives `max(release_ticks_, max_launch_ticks_)`. (2) The bound's epoch is unstated: `hold` has no term, and in `hold` the clock freezes whenever `!p || in.external_brake` (launch_plan.cpp:176). Percept absence is unbounded (percepts are absent until `reaction_delay_s` passes and may stall arbitrarily) and caller braking is a required wait (design doc 'who owns what': the plan must not release a brake someone else holds; test_no_percept_and_caller_brake_freeze_the_hold runs 200 frozen hold ticks). `in.eligible` stays true during a caller brake (intent.cpp:551 computes eligible without external_brake). If the bound counts from arming, it is violated by an unbounded amount under the stated assumptions; if it counts from release entry, the statement must say so explicitly (the project's own launch window is hold-entry to handover-entry, simulation-decisions.md:418). (3) FrontLiftReaction: the stated assumptions (`hand_drop_ratio < 1.0`, `rate * period_s` above ULP absorption) do not guarantee `idle`: a sustained percept stream holds `predicate` true forever (idle->deciding->acting, `acting` never exits while predicate holds, front_lift_reaction.cpp:160-173). `hand_drop_ratio < 1` only narrows the required decay rate; a geometrically decaying `hand_press_n` keeps `average < ratio * reference` with `reference > 0` for all time. The missing premise is predicate quiescence. Under quiescence (predicate absent for clear_ticks_+1 consecutive updates, then absent) `acting`->`releasing`->`idle` does hold: `step_toward` contracts each enabled output by `rate*period_s` per update and `releasing`->`idle` fires the same update both reach neutral (:180-187). (4) The `!in.eligible` 1-tick reset is conditional: `validate(in)` runs before the eligibility check (:155-160), so an ineligible tick carrying NaN percepts or `shift_count = -1` throws instead of resetting.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
REPRO 1 (LaunchPlan, off-by-one): period_s=0.01, `brake_release_s=1.0` (release_ticks_=100), `max_launch_s=1.0` (max_launch_ticks_=100) — legal config, no cross-field rule. Arm, feed standstill percepts with `human_crank_torque_nm >= release_torque_nm`, `in.eligible=true`, `cut_active=false` throughout, `handover_s=0`. Timeline from release-entry: updates 1..100 = release (brake 1->0), update 101 (accelerate): `++launch_ticks`=101 >= 100 -> handover, update 102 (handover): `!cut_active` and handover_ticks_==0 -> done. `done` is reached at update 102. Stated bound: 100 + max(0, 0) + cut_clear + 0; with the only sensible reading of cut_clear_ticks (handover updates until the transition, here 1), bound = 101 < 102 — violated by exactly one accelerate update for every config with `max_launch_ticks_ <= release_ticks_` (degenerate but admitted; shipped profiles use max_launch_s=6.0 >> brake_release_s=0.15 so it is unexercised). REPRO 2 (LaunchPlan, epoch): `arm_start` a plan (plan_enabled, eligible), then deliver `percepts=nullopt` forever, or deliver percepts with `external_brake=true` — phase stays `hold` with brake=1, weight=1, owns_shifting=1 for any number of intention ticks; never reaches `done` or `idle`. Matches test_no_percept_and_caller_brake_freeze_the_hold (test_launch_plan.cpp:129-144). Violates the literal statement since no assumption bounds percept starvation or caller braking; resolves only if the bound is declared to start at release-entry. REPRO 3 (FrontLiftReaction, sustained predicate under hand_drop_ratio < 1.0): config `table()` from test_front_lift_reaction.cpp:50-55 (hand_drop_ratio=0.7 < 1.0). After 41 loaded percepts, feed `percept(80 * pow(0.99, k), 1.0, 0.)` for all k>=0 — press decays geometrically toward 0 without reaching it, so the window average stays < 0.7 x the lagged reference and `reference > 0` holds forever; pitch_rate 1.0 > threshold 0.5 keeps the pitch term true. `predicate` holds every tick; phase reaches `acting` and stays there for all k — `idle` is never reached (the shipped test asserts exactly this perpetual `acting`, test_front_lift_reaction.cpp:76,226-232). With the config-legal `hand_drop_ratio=1.0` (validate_reaction_table admits it, front_lift_reaction.cpp:53-54, test line 381) the predicate holds under any strictly decreasing press. An oscillating predicate likewise bounces `releasing`->`acting` forever (:171-173). REPRO 4 (eligible-reset ordering): any `update({.percepts=percept with NaN field, .eligible=false})` on an armed plan throws `std::invalid_argument` before reaching the `s_ = State{}` reset (launch_plan.cpp:155-160) — the 'resets to idle in 1 tick' clause fails for malformed ineligible ticks.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No valid product scenario is wrongly blocked by the constraint as implemented; every conditional the bound enumerates traces to a confirmed decision: the hold freeze under a caller brake ('внешний тормоз и удержание от отката сильнее: план не отпускает то, что держит другой', design doc line 145; launch_plan.cpp:176), the handover wait on `!in.shift.cut_active` ('переключения переходят к автомату, но не посреди переключения: план ждёт конца среза', design doc line 108; launch_plan.cpp:212-216; the cut is a bounded mechanical interval, cut_remaining_s ~0.2 s per accepted shift, step.cpp:1285), the `!in.eligible` reset ('если задана, главнее плана', external command wins; intent.cpp:551), and the `done`->`hold` re-arm at standstill (restart after a full stop is a confirmed user decision П9 `1303e344`, simulation-decisions.md:431-438; launch_plan.cpp:171-174). Two framing consequences: (a) `done` is deliberately non-absorbing, so the liveness statement can only demand 'reaches' done, never 'settles' — a stronger 'terminal neutral' clause would wrongly criminalize the required restart; (b) the front-lift reaction is required to persist while the wheel is up and to continue through the handover ('Реакция, начатая во время старта, продолжается и после передачи управления', design doc line 136), so 'reaches idle' is only sound conditioned on the predicate going absent — the product semantics 'реакция заканчивается, когда признак пропал на заданное время' (line 134). Nothing in the constraint itself cuts off a valid scenario; the defect is that the stated assumptions do not express these required preconditions.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Reachable/admitted states and inputs outside the contract's clean domain: (1) `restore()` never checks `config_.plan_enabled` (launch_plan.cpp:257-311), so restoring a mid-launch `hold`/`accelerate` state into a switched-off plan yields a permanently stuck non-idle command — `update()` early-returns at :157 before the eligibility reset at :158, leaving `brake=1`, `weight=1`, `owns_shifting=1` forever and unreachable by any reset (loading a saved mid-launch state under a profile with the plan switched off). (2) `validate()` checks only `shift_count` and four percept doubles (:82-90); a negative `senses.rear_teeth` is admitted into `request_teeth`/`blocked_teeth` (:147-151), producing a state `restore()` itself rejects (:268 'negative cog') — the update()-reachable set and the restore()-admitted set disagree. Similarly `senses.surface_front` outside the enum passes validation then throws inside `cap()` on every non-idle update (:92-101) — loud, but the plan stalls for a corrupt-but-finite input; an out-of-enum `ShiftFeedback.response` is admitted and silently treated as non-`rejected` (:139,144). (3) `FrontLiftReaction::update(std::nullopt)` in `acting` advances `clear_ticks` (predicate forced false, :150-152) without touching `percepts_seen` (:153), so update() produces `phase=releasing` with `percepts_seen < percepts_cap()`, which `restore()` then rejects (:280 'percepts_seen != percepts_cap()') — a reachable state the restore contract ('rejects every state update() cannot leave behind', :98-104, :203-207) refuses; the codec write->read->continue loop (design doc lines 217-226) breaks for it. In production `RiderIntent` never delivers nullopt after warmup and even requires `percepts_seen == min(delivered, cap)` (intent.cpp:673-676), so this is a class-API inconsistency rather than production-reachable. (4) A finite-but-huge `crank_rpm` (e.g., 1e300) passes `validate()` (:110-114) and drives `crank_angle_total_rad` to +inf permanently (:123-124); `state()` then carries inf and `restore()` rejects it (:224-226). (5) `rise_ticks` keeps counting on every eligible update once armed — including `idle`, `done`, `handover`, `blend` (:163-168) — so it is a per-episode clock whose only exits are `!in.eligible` reset or the overflow throw (:165-167); combined with `done`->re-arm, long standstill sessions keep it marching toward the throw the assumption only defers. (6) At `done`, a standstill percept re-arms instantly (:171-174): a launch that ends while the bike still satisfies `standstill()` (e.g., `max_launch_ticks_` expires with the bike stalled on a grade) cycles `done`->`hold`->...->`done` indefinitely — reaches `done` per cycle but never settles neutral; intended retry behavior per П9, but it means the runtime can hold `brake=1`-armed launches forever at a standstill.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Revise as follows. (1) State the epoch explicitly: bound the ticks from the update that first enters `release` (or `accelerate` when `release_ticks_ == 0`) to `LaunchPhase::done`; exclude `hold`, which legitimately waits forever for percepts and defers to the caller's brake by design — and note the plan may re-arm afterward, so `done` is 'reached', not 'settled'. (2) Fix the formula to `max(release_ticks_ + 1, max_launch_ticks_) + cut_clear_ticks + handover_ticks_` where `cut_clear_ticks` is the number of updates spent in `handover` (consecutive cut-active handover updates plus the one observing `!cut_active`; 1 when the cut is already inactive) — or equivalently keep `release_ticks_ + max(0, max_launch_ticks_ - release_ticks_)` and enforce `max_launch_s > brake_release_s` in `validate_launch_plan_context` so the two coincide. (3) For FrontLiftReaction add the missing precondition: 'once the predicate is absent for `clear_ticks_ + 1` consecutive updates and stays absent, `phase` reaches `idle` within `ceil((1 - target)/(rate*period_s))` further updates'; drop or re-derive `hand_drop_ratio < 1.0` — it does not ensure quiescence (geometric press decay sustains the predicate at any ratio < 1). (4) Condition the '!in.eligible resets to idle in 1 tick' clause on `validate(in)` passing, since validation precedes the reset. (5) Same-contract-family hardening worth recording even if outside the liveness letter: have `LaunchPlan::restore` reject non-idle states when `!plan_enabled`; extend `LaunchPlan::validate` to bound `senses.rear_teeth >= 0` and `senses.surface_front`/`ShiftFeedback.response` to their enum domains so `update()` cannot leave states `restore()` rejects; relax `FrontLiftReaction::restore`'s `percepts_seen == percepts_cap()` rule for `releasing` to `>= acting_from` since percept-less ticks advance the clear countdown; consider clamping or rejecting magnitude-absurd `crank_rpm` inputs before `crank_angle_total_rad` saturates to +inf.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P06a`: Rider Unilateral Support, Spindle Anti-Windup & Zero Joint Damping

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `mixed_subclauses`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Subclause-by-subclause verdict. (a) Zero joint damping: HOLDS for generated models — no 'damping' attribute is emitted anywhere under native/src/model (linkage.cpp:49,86 emit only stiffness/range/axis; drivetrain.cpp:16 crank_spin has none; physical_topology.cpp tendons none), xml_writer.cpp emits no <default> class, so MuJoCo leaves mjModel.dof_damping all-zero; asserted by test_actuators_are_pure_motors_without_dof_damping (native/tests/reference/test_joint_strength.cpp:445-471) and per-mode MJCF comparison (native/tests/model/test_mjcf.cpp:126). Caveat: the untracked capture tools/fp32_probe/artifacts/common/model.xml carries damping 0.2–0.5 on four linkage joints and IS consumed by GPU tests/tools (native/tests/gpu/test_gpu_model_geometry.cpp:89, test_gpu_batch_runners.cpp:168, tools/colab_gpu/mjwarp_batch_sweep.py:72) — a pre-decision-26 stale artifact that literally violates 'all MJCF modes/variants' if the sweep reads consumed MJCFs. (b) Flat-pad unilateral: HOLDS — contactlaw::normal_contact returns max(0, k*delta + c*delta_dot) (native/src/contact/laws.hpp:30-37) applied per pad (native/src/writers/rider_contacts.cpp:520-529). (c) 25 N margin: HOLDS — kPedalPressMarginN=25 (spindle_controller.cpp:1353), f_min=max(25,f_base) only when !steady_state (1658-1661), press delta max(25-f_task_press,f_base)-f_passive (1783-1785). (d) >=0 press on coast/reposition: HOLDS — coast zeroes task+hold (1731-1732); leg press servo targets f_weight>=0 for every mean<=0 branch incl. reposition (1782); f_task_press clamped below at -f_rest_n=max(f_weight,0) so a negative crank task can only relieve resting press, never pull (1758-1771). (e) Anti-windup freeze: HOLDS — at_press_limit = raw_task + max(0,hold) >= crank_press_ceiling_nm gates integration (1688-1695). (f) |coast_hold_nm| <= weld_servo_limit_nm 'clamped at decode/validate': VIOLATED — no bound exists at any stated enforcement point: state_codec.cpp:2065 optional_real checks finiteness only; validate_state spindle_controller.cpp:2161-2168 checks isfinite only; commit_state:2175 assigns verbatim; equilibrium material equilibrium_cache.cpp:118 uses doubles() finite-only and :229 re-emits verbatim. Only the integrator output is bounded (1699-1703); the applied hold is clamped per-tick (1705-1709), but the stored state is not. (g) -6 N rule: HOLDS via the 'or' — scoped to ride-level acceptance: kSupportPullToleranceN=6 (runtime/status.hpp:74), rider_support_ok judges recorded history.rider_support minima (status.cpp:171-184) accumulated every published interval (accounting.cpp:760 from the per-step rider_welds channel, step.cpp:2185-2191) and exercised only by acceptance tests (test_realistic_pedelec_acceptance.cpp:255-263); the in-run PeriodAccounting gate deliberately uses the weld envelope budget (-1050/-2250 N, runtime.cpp:620-627; physical_config.hpp:484-494), matching decision 34 ('only acceptance checks pull'). (h) Documented exceptions present and scoped: solreflimit end-stop impedance (decisions 26/32), setup-only crank hold kd (decision 33).

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
REPRO A (subclause f, reachable, no test needed to show the gap): 1) Craft a rider State document {"coast_hold_nm": 250.0, ...} — 10x weld_servo_limit_nm=25.0. decode passes: state_codec.cpp:2065 optional_real requires only a finite real. 2) controller->restore(state): validate_state (spindle_controller.cpp:2161-2163) accepts any finite value; commit_state:2175 stores coast_hold_nm_=250. 3) compute(data, command{enabled=true, mean_crank_torque_nm=20}, advance=true, dt, steady_state=false, ...): at line 1688 at_press_limit = raw_task + max(0,250) >= crank_press_ceiling_nm is true whenever the press-bound ceiling is finite, so the weld servo never integrates (1691-1695 skip); hold_nm stays 250; applied_hold is clamped to min(task, ceiling-task) (1705-1709) so the applied torque stays in envelope; commit_hold writes coast_hold_nm_=250 back (1734-1735). 4) state().coast_hold_nm re-emits 250 (2122); the equilibrium material channel state_control_coast_hold_nm round-trips it through doubles() finite-only reads (equilibrium_cache.cpp:118) and verbatim writes (:229). Net effect: an out-of-envelope hold persists across unlimited capture/restore cycles and permanently freezes the weld-press servo during pedaling until a coast tick (1731) or disabled advance tick (1315/reset_activation:629) zeroes it. Boundary: hold=+25.0 and +25.000001 are treated identically — there is no bound to be exactly at. REPRO B (negative side): coast_hold_nm=-1e6 restores identically; at_press_limit uses max(0,hold)=0 so integration is allowed and the first eligible tick clamps held to -limit (1699-1703) — self-heals only when integration conditions are met; on advance=false or weld-absent ticks the corrupt value persists and re-serializes. NEGATIVE RESULT: NaN/+-Inf are rejected loudly at all three entry points (optional_real->finite_real; isfinite; doubles() nonfinite=false at npy.cpp:101) — finiteness is enforced, magnitude is not. REPRO C (subclause a, literal): tools/fp32_probe/artifacts/common/model.xml contains nonzero joint damping (main_pivot 0.5, horst_pivot 0.3, rocker_frame_pivot 0.3, yoke_pivot 0.2) and is loaded as a physics model by native/tests/gpu/test_gpu_model_geometry.cpp:89 and tools/colab_gpu/mjwarp_batch_sweep.py:72 — any runner stepping it has dof_damping[i]!=0 for 4 DOFs; it is a stale pre-decision-26 capture, not one of the declared exceptions (#26/#32/#33).

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No documented valid scenario is wrongly rejected by the press, damping, or -6 N subclauses — each is a direct transcription of a user decision: press>=0 + 25 N margin + coast-rest-at-weight + anti-windup (simulation-decisions.md decision 9, incl. 'the learned add-on coast_hold_nm_ does not grow while the demand is at the limit'), zero joint damping (decision 26), solreflimit and setup-only crank-hold exceptions (decisions 26/32/33), -6 N acceptance pull rule (decision 34: 'ride fails if any physics step shows any foot or saddle support below -6 N; speed gates deliberately skip pull'). Two scoping edges worth flagging rather than calling violations: (1) rider_support_ok throws std::invalid_argument when any of front_pedal/rear_pedal/saddle min_n is absent (status.cpp:173-180); for pedal_attachment='flat' — a supported mode (validate.cpp:795-799) — support_target returns nullopt (writers/rider_contacts.cpp:1095,1102) so no rider_welds entries exist and the helper cannot judge a flat or mixed-attachment ride at all. This is deliberate ('an acceptance ride always records all three channels', status.hpp:76-78), and every acceptance profile runs spindle/pin/connect, but it means the -6 N contract is implicitly defined only on fully-linked configurations — the same unilateral flat pads the property separates in its own premise are unjudgeable, not vacuously passing. (2) attachment_violations uses '<' for foot but '<=' for saddle (period_buffer.cpp:113 vs 117): with a welded saddle and no weld bound declared, saddle_min_normal_n defaults to 0 (runtime.cpp:626-627), so a momentarily fully-unloaded saddle reading exactly 0.0 N flags 'normal' as a model violation even though a zero saddle load is the physical boundary of 'saddle can only prop from below'; all shipped profiles set weld_saddle_min_normal_n=-2250 so it is unreachable in them. The 25 N margin is a servo demand, not an admission test — a leg unable to deliver it saturates muscle limits rather than being rejected, which is the designed outcome (decision 9).

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
(1) Finite out-of-envelope coast_hold_nm (|x|>weld_servo_limit_nm) is admitted at every state entry — state decode (state_codec.cpp:2065), restore/validate_state (spindle_controller.cpp:2161-2168), commit_state (:2175), equilibrium material (equilibrium_cache.cpp:118, :229) — persists in coast_hold_nm_, re-emits via state() (:2122), disables the weld-press servo indefinitely when positive (at_press_limit pinned true, 1688-1695), and only clears on a mode transition (coast zeroing :1731, reset_activation :629), never on a bound check. Tests themselves rely on the unvalidated injection path (test_native_spindle_controller.cpp:646-647, 717-718 commit_state with arbitrary values), confirming validate_state is not the only admission route. (2) In-run sufficiency gap by design: the PeriodAccounting attachment gate admits sustained foot pulls between -1050 N and -6 N without a single violation reason (budget foot_min_normal_n=weld_foot_min_normal_n=-1050 when declared, physical_config.hpp:484-486 + runtime.cpp:620-623; profiles set it), so a corrupt -500 N foot pull completes 'model_valid' and is only caught post-hoc by rider_support_ok — acceptable per decision 34 but means the in-run safety net does not cover the property's own -6 N band. (3) evaluate_attachments silently drops a measurement whose wrench solve fails or is unobservable (period_buffer.cpp:74-75 'continue') — no sample and no violation reason is produced on this path (the ':unobservable_attachment_wrench' string is emitted by the separate measure_samples catch, writers/rider_contacts.cpp:1238-1241). (4) WorkHistory::from_wire restores rider_support min_n with a finiteness check only (samples.cpp:605-608) — a checkpoint-restored ledger carrying a fabricated finite min_n (e.g., 0.0) is judged as compliant history; same trust level as other wire-restored state but the ledger is the property's evidence. (5) crank_press_ceiling_nm degrades to +inf whenever no leg has shares>0 with press_per_nm>1e-12 (spindle_controller.cpp:1669-1680) — near pedal dead spots the press ceiling and therefore the anti-windup freeze are structurally absent; consistent with the stated formula but means the freeze is vacuous exactly where weld measurements are least reliable.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Split P06a into per-subclause contracts. (a) 'For every model generated by native/src/model for every build_request mode/variant (including crank_joint and every rider/attachment combination), mjModel.dof_damping is identically zero' — keep it scoped to generated models and the mjModel-level assertion; explicitly exclude in-tree captured artifacts (tools/fp32_probe/artifacts/common/model.xml is a stale pre-2026-11-04 capture with nonzero linkage damping, still loaded by GPU tests; regenerate or mark it out of scope). (b) 'Flat support pads exert only compressive normal force: contactlaw::normal_contact >= 0 by construction; weld/spindle/pin equalities are bidirectional, so the unilateral invariant on pinned supports is enforced at controller-target level and measured by the -6 N ledger, not physically' — state this separation verbatim. (c) Keep the spindle target clauses as confirmed requirements (decision 9): 25 N margin when !steady_state && mean>0; pedal press >= 0 on coast/reposition via the -f_rest_n clamp and f_weight servo target; anti-windup freeze formula raw_task + max(0, coast_hold_nm_) >= crank_press_ceiling_nm. (d) Replace '|coast_hold_nm| <= weld_servo_limit_nm clamped at decode/validate' — currently false — with either the implemented invariant '|coast_hold_nm_| <= weld_servo_limit_nm for internally generated holds; applied hold is clamped per-tick to [-task, min(task, ceiling-task)]', or a required contract 'all state-entry paths (decode_rider_state, restore/validate_state, equilibrium material state_control_coast_hold_nm) reject or clamp |coast_hold_nm| > weld_servo_limit_nm' pending a code fix. (e) Keep the -6 N rule but state its true scope: 'for linked (weld/spindle/pin) supports, rider_support_ok fails the ride when recorded min_n < -6 N, judged at ride-level acceptance only; the in-run attachment gate uses the weld envelope budgets (weld_foot/saddle_min_normal_n), which deliberately tolerate deeper pulls; missing entries are a schema error, not a verdict — the rule does not apply to flat/unlinked supports'. (f) Reaffirm the exceptions list verbatim: solreflimit end-stop impedance, setup-only crank hold, and name the non-joint dampers that are allowed as separate force elements (pad/support contact damping, brake hold damper, freehub/tendon elements, controller-torque dampers leg_active_damping_nms_rad and reposition_damping_nms_rad).

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P06b`: `PedalRecoveryState` Closed Enum & Escape-Direction Integrity

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_engineering_contract`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
The statement overstates enforcement on three of its four cells. (a) decode_pedal_recovery (native/src/rider/state_codec.cpp:473-486) does enforce the stage closed set at :479-482, but reads direction only as finite_real (:483): any finite double — 0.0, 2.5, -3.7, 1e300 — is admitted at every stage including "escape"/"raise"; the claimed {-1,+1} closure exists nowhere for this field. (b) SpindleController::validate_state (native/src/rider/spindle_controller.cpp:2148-2169) inspects only allocation_memory, pd_split and coast_hold_nm; state.pedal_recovery is never touched — stage, direction, release_offset_x_m, entry names and arity are all unvalidated, so the 'enforced in both' claim is false: the stage conjunct holds at 1 of 2 named gates, the direction conjunct at 0 of 2. (c) PedalRecoveryState::stage is std::string (native/src/rider/spindle_controller.hpp:42), not a C++ enum — 'closed' is a boundary property only, and only the wire boundary has it; commit_state (spindle_controller.cpp:2183) moves the entries verbatim. In-memory restore paths that bypass the decoder are production-reachable: PhysicalStep::restore feeds StepState.rider_controller into rider_control_->restore (native/src/runtime/step.cpp:3072-3073), install_material runs decode->validate->commit (native/src/initialization/equilibrium_cache.cpp:110,154,157), and the state()->mutate->restore() pattern is used in production (native/src/validation/runtime_access.cpp:43, native/src/initialization/initial_pose.cpp:98-99) and in tests (native/tests/reference/test_native_spindle_controller.cpp:645-647,716-718).

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
Wire repro 1 (stall): take tests/fixtures/state/rider_controller.json (or an equilibrium-cache state_native_rider_controller blob, decoded at equilibrium_cache.cpp:110) and set pedal_recovery.front = {"stage":"escape","direction":0.0,"release_offset_x_m":0.0}. decode_pedal_recovery accepts it (finite); validate_state passes untouched; commit_state stores it. On a flat-pedal config (observe_recovery is gated recovery && !steady && !welded at native/src/rider/legacy_controller.cpp:308) the escape test state.direction*(sole[0]-origin[0]) > ext[0]+pad_half+radius (legacy_controller.cpp:105) becomes 0 > positive, permanently false -> eternal 'escape' stage; recovery_goal aims the sole at origin[0]+0 beneath the pedal block (:113-114) and legacy_allocation.cpp:97 holds recovering=true forever. Wire repro 2 (wrong-side escape, inside the claimed +/-1 set): {stage:"escape",direction:-1.0} after a +x release -> recovery_goal offset origin[0]-(ext+pad+radius+clearance) drives the sole into the pedal block on the wrong side — exactly the escape-direction harm in the title. Wire repro 3: direction=3.0 -> escape gate fires at 1/3 of the geometric threshold and the escape goal lands 3x too far. In-memory repro: auto s=ctl.state(); s.pedal_recovery={{"front",{.stage="bogus",.direction=NAN,.release_offset_x_m=INF}},{"rear",{}}}; ctl.restore(s) (validate at spindle_controller.cpp:2143) passes. Consequences: recovery_goal returns a raise-like goal for ANY non-'none' stage string (only 'none' yields nullopt at legacy_controller.cpp:110) so a bogus stage is silently treated as active recovery and observe_recovery can never advance it (no matching branch :103-107); NaN direction/offset produces a NaN goal -> two_link_ik require2 -> validation::finite throws std::invalid_argument mid-step inside LegacyController::targets (legacy_controller.cpp:183-194, native/src/rider/spindle_math.cpp:82,17-19); an empty or renamed entries vector -> get(c_.pedal_recovery_,"front") throws std::out_of_range on every compute (legacy_controller.cpp:54). Knock-on artifact: a committed corrupt state re-emits verbatim via encode_spindle_state (state_codec.cpp:2133-2142); a NaN direction encodes a 'nan' lexeme (real_value :316-325) that finite_real then rejects, so a snapshot the runtime accepted is not re-loadable by its own decoder.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
None found — the direction closure blocks no legitimate state. The sole producer observe_recovery only ever writes direction = release_offset_x_m < 0 ? -1. : 1. (legacy_controller.cpp:103), and both consumers use it purely as a sign multiplier (the escape gate at :105 and the escape offset at :113); magnitude is carried by release_offset_x_m. No scenario in docs/RIDE.md, docs/RECORDING.md or docs/agents/simulation-decisions.md requires a non-sign direction. If anything the statement's conditional 'whenever stage != None' is slightly loose, not strict: the producer keeps direction=1.0 at 'none' too (struct default spindle_controller.hpp:43 and the state={} reset at legacy_controller.cpp:107), so even an unconditional +/-1 rule admits the full reachable set. Scope caveat, not a blocking one: pedal_recovery is only consumed by the legacy/flat-pedal delegate — for pure spindle (non-legacy_) or welded configs the FSM never runs, so corrupt values are inert at runtime there but are still snapshotted and re-emitted onto the wire.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even if the full claimed rule existed at both gates, these corrupt states still pass: (1) release_offset_x_m is only required finite — stage 'release' with release_offset_x_m=1e9 yields recovery_goal offset origin[0]+1e9, an absurd but 'valid' goal the IK merely saturates (legacy_controller.cpp:113, spindle_math.cpp:94-95). (2) Container shape is outside the statement: an in-memory State with pedal_recovery = [] / {{"left",...}} / three entries / duplicate "front" is admitted by validate_state and then get() throws std::out_of_range every compute (legacy_controller.cpp:54,183,203,321); the wire's exact {front,rear} check (state_codec.cpp:943) does not cover in-memory restores. (3) Sign-vs-geometry coherence is not captured by +/-1 closure: {stage:'escape',direction:-1,release_offset_x_m:+0.05} is contradictory (release was +x, escape aims -x) yet satisfies the stated rule; the producer's exact invariant direction == (offset<0 ? -1 : 1) (legacy_controller.cpp:103) is checkable but unchecked. (4) Stage-graph coherence is not checked: a restore directly into 'raise'/'return' with a contradictory release_offset_x_m is admitted. (5) A committed corrupt stage is re-emitted verbatim (state_codec.cpp:2136), producing wire that the decoder itself would reject on the next restore — admitted corruption becomes a persistent self-poisoning artifact.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Restate the invariant to match the producer's reachable set and actually bind both named gates: (1) decode_pedal_recovery (state_codec.cpp:473-486): keep the stage closed-set check; strengthen direction to the closed sign set — reject unless direction == -1.0 or direction == +1.0 whenever stage != 'none' (unconditional +/-1 is equally safe since the producer holds it at 'none'); keep release_offset_x_m finite. (2) SpindleController::validate_state (spindle_controller.cpp:2148-2169): add a pedal_recovery block — entries must be exactly {'front','rear'} once each; per entry stage must be in the closed set, direction must be finite and, for stage != 'none', exactly +/-1; release_offset_x_m must be finite; optionally check the producer relation direction == (release_offset_x_m < 0 ? -1 : 1) for non-'none' stages (legacy_controller.cpp:103). (3) Prefer making the closure structural: replace std::string stage with an enum class {None,Release,Escape,Raise,Return} and direction with a +/-1 sign type so in-memory States are closed by construction rather than by two divergent boundary checks. (4) Document that for non-legacy (spindle/weld) controllers the field is wire-carried but runtime-inert, so corruption there is a snapshot-hygiene issue rather than a control-path one.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P06c`: Cohesive Two-Hand Spring-Grip Release (Product Decision)

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `unresolved_product_decision`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
All three sub-claims are violated by reachable call sequences. (1) 'Reachability and trial forces computed for both hands before deciding pair release' fails structurally: the per-side loop at native/src/writers/rider_contacts.cpp:690-749 interleaves computation and decision. s.active at :701 reads w.enabled[kGripIndex], which the side-0 iteration may have just cleared at :744 — so a side-0 unreachable event forces side-1's s.active=false before its jacobian/grip_step/trial_norm are ever computed (s.trial_norm stays 0.0, reported as the hand's trial force in diagnostics). The 'decision' therefore precedes side-1's computation entirely. (2) 'On release, qfrc/grip_xi_local/elastic_energy_j zero for both hands in the same step with old[0]+old[1] dumped' fails whenever exactly one side triggers release while the other was healthy: (a) side-0 reachable + side-1 unreachable — side-0's out += jrel^T f (:736-740) and w.grip_xi_local[0]=fresh (:745) commit BEFORE side-1's reachability is evaluated; after :744 latches enabled=false, the step commits (797-807) with nonzero grip qfrc, grip_xi_local[0]!=0, and side-0's grip_energy in elastic_energy_j; old0's stored .5k|old0|^2 is NOT dumped (it persists inside fresh0 and is dumped one step later by the else-branch :742); (b) single-side overload — release_if_overloaded is per-side against limit/2 (:719-724); the overloaded side is zeroed in-place (:726-732) but the companion still evaluates s.active with w.enabled[3] still true (latch at :750 is post-loop), so its force lands in out, its xi and energy are retained under enabled=false, and its stored energy is never dumped. (3) '!enabled[kGripIndex] => grip_xi_local == {0,0} at decode/assign boundaries' is unenforced everywhere: decode_rider_contacts_state decodes enabled (state_codec.cpp:2511) and grip_xi_local (:2539-2545) independently with no cross-field check, and assign_state (rider_contacts.hpp:354-360) is a blind noexcept move that relies entirely on decode-time validation. Additionally, w is a live RiderContactsState& aliasing state_ (signature :435; evaluate into w at 441-453), so lines 744/745/750 mutate committed state in-place BEFORE the staged commit at :797-807 — a mid-loop throw (e.g. grip_step 'grip update overflow' on side 1 after side-0 committed xi/enabled) leaves partially-mutated state; Stepper::mutate poisons only on engine::EngineFailure (stepper.hpp:161-174), so a plain runtime_error leaves an unpoisoned Stepper holding xi[0] already-advanced while last_time_s is unchanged — a retry double-applies the side-0 step. Oracle parity check: git show 3d84cf04^:src/bike_sim/sim/ride/rider_contacts.py has the identical sequential loop — the port is expression-faithful; the asymmetry is oracle behavior, and the goldens pin its diagnostic artifacts (grip.enabled=1 while enabled.grip=0 in unreachable_hand_releases_and_reports_loss) but never exercise a stretched/moving surviving hand.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
REPRO A (asymmetric unreachable release; violates clauses 1+2): rider_contacts.grip_attachment='spring', grip_pair_force_limit_n=null. Drive/restore state so enabled[3]=true, grip_xi_local={left:[eps,0,0], right:[d,0,0]} with eps!=0 (or give side-0 nonzero relative velocity), and pose the frame so hypot3(hand_gap[side=1]) > grip_release_distance_m while side-0 stays within reach (reachable boundary uses <= at :698-700, so release_distance+1ulp suffices). One compute_qfrc_into(data,dt,advance=true) call: iteration side=0 applies out += jrel^T f0 (:736-740) and stores w.grip_xi_local[0]=fresh0 (:745); iteration side=1 takes else-branch s.grip_loss=.5k|old1|^2 (:742) and latches w.enabled[3]=false (:744). Result: enabled[3]=false but out carries side-0's spring force, grip_xi_local[0]=fresh0!=0, elastic_energy_j includes side-0's energy; release bookkeeping = .5k|old1|^2 + side-0 damping loss — NOT old0+old1. Diag lies: grip.enabled=1 (:774 aggregate.enabled=active0||active1 uses pre-release flags), grip_left.enabled=1 (:757), aggregate.release_loss_j=pair_loss which includes side-0's ordinary damping loss mislabeled as release loss (:781-782), and side-1's d.trial_pair_force_n reports its post-release residual. This is exactly the golden test unreachable_hand_releases_and_reports_loss extended to a surviving hand with nonzero stretch — the current golden only exercises the zero-stretch case so the defect is latent, not pinned. REPRO B (single-side overload; violates clauses 1+2): set grip_pair_force_limit_n between 2*trial0 and 2*trial1 via asymmetric stored stretch (e.g. xi[0] large, xi[1] small). One evaluate: side=0 release_if_overloaded zeroes force/xi/energy and dumps .5k|old0|^2 (:719-732); side=1 still sees w.enabled[3]==true (:701, latch deferred to :750) so its force enters out, w.grip_xi_local[1]=fresh1, pair_energy+=e1; then :750 sets enabled=false. Result: a 'pair release' that left one hand's force applied, its xi retained (clause-3 violation produced by the writer itself), its stored energy never dumped, and diag with grip_right.overloaded=true (:760 pair flag stamped on both sides — lies about which hand overloaded), grip.release_loss_j=0 (:781-782 both active -> 0 although .5k|old0|^2+damping went to loss_step_j), aggregate.trial_pair_force_n=|f1| — the residual post-release force, not the trial that triggered release (:779 uses total_force which was zeroed at :730 for the released side). REPRO C (decode boundary; violates clause 3): snapshot JSON with enabled.grip=false and grip_xi_local.left=[0.01,0,0] — decode_rider_contacts_state (state_codec.cpp:2494+, enabled :2511, xi :2539-2545) accepts; assign_state (rider_contacts.hpp:356-360) commits. Next evaluate takes else-branch :742 and launders the phantom .5k|old|^2 into loss_step_j as if a release occurred. Reproducible without hand-editing: snapshot the writer immediately after REPRO A/B — a legitimately-produced snapshot carries enabled=false+xi!=0. REPRO D (unwind/partial mutation + huge-finite): restore grip_xi_local.right=[1e308,0,0] (finite_real admits any finite double, :148-153; fixed<3> :227-230) with enabled=true and side-0 normal: side-0 commits w.grip_xi_local[0]/w.enabled in place (:744-745), then side-1's grip_step throws runtime_error on nonfinite arithmetic (contact_math.cpp:46-71) — state_ keeps xi[0] advanced and possibly enabled=false while diagnostics/supports/loss_step_j/last_time_s are all stale (commit :797-807 never ran); mutate() does not poison (stepper.hpp:161-174) so a retry at the same timestamp double-steps side-0 and produces a different result than a clean call. Non-throw variant: enabled=false + xi=[1e308] hits else-branch :742 with NO finiteness check -> .5*k*dot(old,old)=Inf -> w.loss_step_j=Inf committed silently; same Inf via set_enabled's pending_release_loss_j += .5k|xi|^2 (:316-319). REPRO E (NaN-driven silent release, no restore needed): NaN in site_xpos/xpos makes hypot3()=NaN -> reachable=false (:698-700 NaN<=x is false) -> silent release committed (enabled=false, xi zeroed, .5k|old|^2 dumped as ordinary loss) — evaluate has no finiteness gate on engine buffers. REPRO F (order-dependence/symmetry): identical physical event (one hand rips off) produces cohesive release only when the failing hand happens to be side-0 (its latch forces side-1's active=false at :701) but the asymmetric partially-applied release when side-1 fails — outcome depends on iteration order, defeating the property's 'symmetry' claim.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No valid product scenario is wrongly blocked. grip_attachment='connect' is the production/qualified topology (simulation-decisions.md item 7, lines 48-49: hands hold the bar hinged via connect; RIDE.md records the native capability subset as spindle pedals / saddle pin / grip connects); 'spring' is explicitly non-primary and grip_pair_force_limit_n is null in every production fixture (tests/fixtures/config/resolved/*.json, pinned/spindle configs) — the overload path is exercised only by test_pair_force_limit_releases_both_hands with a 1N limit. The deleted oracle's comment states the intended semantics — 'a grip that lets go does so with both hands at once' — i.e. the property documents intent the code only half-implements; no recorded decision requires an asymmetric or one-step-delayed companion-hand release, and the single enabled['grip'] flag never offered one-hand release as a product feature. The legitimate nonzero-xi assign_state uses (test_sag.cpp:97-101, 183-189: injecting stretch/shear coordinates for equilibrium refinement) set enabled=true and are unaffected by clause 3. One sequencing caveat: clause 3 (hard decode rejection of enabled=false+xi!=0) must land together with the evaluator fix — today the evaluator legitimately produces and serializes such states (REPRO A/B), so enforcing clause 3 alone would brick restores of snapshots the current writer itself emits.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even if all three clauses held, decode/assign still admits: (a) forged ledgers — pending_release_loss_j, loss_step_j, elastic_energy_j are decoded as bare finite reals with no nonnegativity or cross-consistency check; a restored elastic_energy_j can disagree with sum(.5k|xi|^2) and the forged value persists in telemetry until the next advancing step overwrites it (:799); (b) unbounded finite xi — [1e308] passes fixed<3> and drives loss_step_j/pending_release_loss_j/stored_energy to +Inf through paths lacking finiteness checks (:742, :316-319, :814-815); (c) phantom/forged grip_anchor_local — null anchors produce a plain std::runtime_error ('initialize rider contact anchors', :441-443) on the next evaluate rather than rejection at restore, and a far-but-finite anchor silently re-targets the spring; (d) last_time_s restored in the future stalls every subsequent evaluate with 'advances only once per timestamp' (:438-440); (e) settled_welds.crank_torque_nm injected arbitrary -> forged delivered_crank_torque_nm on the next step; (f) diagnostics/probe decoded independently — restored diagnostics can claim enabled/reachable/nonzero forces for a released pair, and the advance=false probe path can publish probe_->enabled[3]=false while live state stays true; (g) telemetry inconsistency is designed in: aggregate.enabled (:774) and d.enabled (:757) report pre-release activity, so the release step reports grip.enabled=1 while state.enabled[3]=0 — golden-pinned, property-blind; (h) release_loss_j semantics mix stored-energy dumps and damping loss inconsistently (:763 active->0 hides the dump on overload-release steps; :781-782 reports pair_loss including the surviving side's damping when a hand rips off); (i) same gap class on support pads (out of P06c scope): disabled pads retain evolving shear xi; (j) NaN engine buffers drive a silent committed release (REPRO E) with no rejection.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Revise P06c into a two-phase contract plus boundary invariants. (1) Evaluator: split the spring-grip loop into a compute phase (reachable/active/trial_norm/jacobian/grip_step for BOTH sides, writing only to per-side locals, no out accumulation and no w mutation) followed by a single pair decision: if !reachable[0] || !reachable[1] || any release_if_overloaded fires, release the pair — zero both hands' out contributions (never accumulate), grip_xi_local={{},{}}, grip_energy=0 both, and loss += .5k*(|old0|^2+|old1|^2), mirroring the cohesive set_enabled path (:316-319). This removes the iteration-order dependence and makes all four side-orderings identical. (2) Atomicity: stage enabled/grip_xi_local updates in locals and commit at :797-807 with everything else — a mid-loop throw must leave state_ bitwise unchanged (matches the 'validate and stage before committing' rule in native-engineering). (3) Ledger: define release_loss_j explicitly as the stored-energy dump .5k*(|old0|^2+|old1|^2) on the release step; report it in diagnostics.grip.release_loss_j on BOTH unreachable- and overload-triggered releases (today :781-782 reports 0 on overload release and mixes damping loss on unreachable release); keep per-side release_loss_j=0 for sides that did not release, and stamp d.overloaded only on the triggering side (:760 currently stamps the pair flag on both). (4) Diagnostics truthfulness: diagnostics.grip.enabled and grip_sides[].enabled should reflect post-release w.enabled[3], or be renamed (was_active) — current goldens pin enabled=1 on the release step. (5) Boundaries: in decode_rider_contacts_state add cross-field checks — enabled.grip=false => grip_xi_local == {0,0,0} for both sides (same pattern for disabled support pads vs pad xi); bound |grip_xi_local| (e.g. <= a documented multiple of grip_release_distance_m) so .5k|xi|^2 stays finite; require pending_release_loss_j>=0, loss_step_j>=0, elastic_energy_j>=0 and elastic_energy_j ~= sum(.5k|xi|^2) within tolerance (or drop the stored field and recompute); require grip anchors non-null whenever enabled.grip=true; reject last_time_s outside the session clock domain. (6) Add a finiteness gate on engine buffers consumed by reachability (site_xpos/xpos/qvel/qacc) so a NaN pose rejects instead of silently releasing. (7) Verification: regenerate unreachable_hand_releases_and_reports_loss and pair_force_limit_releases_both_hands goldens under the pending non-primary-grip parity ruling, and add a regression where the surviving hand carries nonzero stretch AND nonzero relative velocity at the release step — the case today's goldens never exercise — plus restore-side tests for enabled=false+xi!=0 rejection and the huge-finite xi rejection.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P07a`: Observational Purity of Non-Advancing Probe Evaluation

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `mixed_subclauses`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
The statement's central mechanism does not exist. Claim 'all persistent controller and step mutations are gated on advance==true (commit_hold=advance, staged/restored named fields, no committed wheel_x_m_/rider_state_/CruiseWriter overwrites)' is falsified at multiple exact sites: (1) commit_hold is literal true, not advance — native/src/rider/spindle_controller.cpp:1953 populate_leg_torques(ps, true) with commit at :1734-1735; a coasting probe (command.mean_crank_torque_nm<=0, no crank_reposition) executes hold_nm=0 (:1731) and commits it, destroying the committed pedaling hold so the weld servo resumes from 0 — oracle-visible divergence on resume. (2) None of the named controller fields are staged/restored: command_enabled_ is written unconditionally (spindle :1294; legacy native/src/rider/legacy_controller.cpp:296); saturated_ik_ is cleared unconditionally (:1295) then repopulated by target/IK evaluation; ik_reach_limited_, last_terms_ (:1961-1972 plus :1216-1219 in finalize_effort; legacy :381-384), effort_diagnostics_ (:1197-1214), support_diagnostics_, allocation_diagnostics_, joint_torques_nm_/joint_capacity_nm_ (:2000-2008; legacy :384), pd_split_ (legacy :349,:356), sole_targets_/sole_goal_diagnostics_ are all overwritten on advance=false with no restore. allocation_memory_ is committed, not a discarded scratch: legacy_allocation.cpp:118 stages a copy but :135 writes c_.allocation_memory_=std::move(staged_memory) unconditionally, and response_allocation.cpp:59 updates memory.branch/memory.solution on any feasible solve — a probe updates the warm start. Only pedal_recovery_ is restored on success (legacy :380) and the full State is restored only on the exception path via the facade catch (spindle :1288-1291). (3) wheel_x_m_/rider_state_ are committed on every probe: step.cpp:1248-1257 sets control_tick=!advance||initializing_||is_tick(step_), so !advance forces control_tick and both members are assigned unguarded; rider_state_->road is then consumed by intent resolution at :1265-1295 on subsequent non-tick advancing intervals, so a probe between control tick k and tick k+n on graded terrain with intent enabled changes the road-grade input of a real step. (4) CruiseWriter is committed unconditionally: step.cpp:1553-1558 zeroes state_.torque_nm via set_state on every probe when has_cruise(); and when drive_mode=="ideal_speed_control" && active, cruise().compute(d, rear_in_contact, false, controller_grounded) runs — cruise.cpp:51-83 unconditionally assigns state_=next including integral_mps_s integration and engaged flips, so each probe advances the PI controller by one dt. ideal_speed_control is a decoded-admitted mode (bootstrap.cpp:273-277, capabilities.cpp:20-21) reachable via NativeRideRuntime::probe_step_inputs (runtime.cpp:816-827). (5) The explicitly required snapshot/restore of mjModel::dof_frictionloss and Stepper::posvel_fresh_ is absent: probe_step_inputs (step.cpp:2953-2971) validates then calls apply_forces(true,false,...) inside stepper_->mutate — which is a health/poison wrapper, not a transaction — and reads m->dof_frictionloss/d->ctrl post-apply at :2965-2971 to build bounds; the probe's friction bound, ctrl, qfrc_applied, and the acc-stage arena remain stained in the live model/data with posvel_fresh_ left true (consistent with the stained inputs, but not restored).

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
Reachable sequences from source (no runtime executed; read-only audit): (A) ideal_speed_control rig -> runtime->probe_step_inputs(command,0,0) -> apply_forces(active=true,advance=false) -> step.cpp:1561-1583 cruise().compute commits engaged/integral_mps_s/torque_nm (cruise.cpp:59,82); next advancing interval's PI torque differs from the no-probe timeline -> physics divergence. Additionally every probe on any has_cruise() model stomps state_.torque_nm=0 at :1553-1558. (B) spindle config, coasting: after advancing intervals build up coast_hold_nm_>0, a probe with mean_crank_torque_nm<=0 commits hold_nm=0 (spindle_controller.cpp:1731->1735 via :1953) — resuming pedaling loses the held weld-servo bias; the channel is serialized into state material at :2122. (C) any rider model: probe then PhysicalStep::state() (step.cpp:3019-3043 serializes rider_control_->state()) — the serialized last_terms_/effort_diagnostics_/allocation_memory_/saturated_ik_ now carry probe values; likewise capture_accounted_presentation reads presentation_terms() (presentation.cpp:195) which are probe-mutated if captured before the next advancing compute. (D) unwind: a probe that reaches limit_torques/finalize_effort and throws runtime_error 'allocated rider torque is outside final actuator limits' (spindle_controller.cpp:1183, called from :1999) leaves coast_hold_nm_ already committed (:1953), saturated_ik_ cleared (:1295), command_enabled_ flipped (:1294), last_terms_/allocation_diagnostics_ partially repopulated, and dof_frictionloss/posvel_fresh_ changed — the spindle path has NO equivalent of the legacy facade catch (:1288-1291), and Stepper::mutate only poisons on EngineFailure, so a std::runtime_error escapes leaving a half-mutated, still-usable step/controller. (E) probe between a control tick and a non-tick advancing interval on graded terrain with intent enabled refreshes rider_state_ (:1250-1257) so the next real step's resolve_full reads road grades for the probe pose, not the last tick's (:1265-1295). (F) legacy path probe commits allocation warm start: allocate_uncoupled stages staged_memory (legacy_allocation.cpp:118) but line :135 commits it unconditionally; allocate_response mutates c_.allocation_memory_ via select_allocation_branch :59. NaN/Inf demands are correctly rejected pre-mutation (step.cpp:2957-2963, runtime.cpp:820-823), and pending_reposition_, applied_control_, held_rider_terms_, brake latch, tire brush state, rider-contact writer state, intent Core, and drivetrain policy lanes ARE properly probe-quarantined — the violations are concentrated in controller members, cruise, coast_hold, wheel_x_m_/rider_state_, and model-buffer residuals.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
Partially over-broad as stated. The probe's declared output surface is built FROM the stained model/data buffers: probe_step_inputs reads m->dof_frictionloss for brake bounds and copies d->ctrl after apply_forces (step.cpp:2965-2971), so the residual writes are how the probe previews staged inputs — the oracle-parity tests state this explicitly ('The oracle staged apply_forces(advance=False) before reading; the probe stages the same inputs', test_native_runtime_step.cpp:256-257) and compare probe ctrl/components/bounds against oracle-recorded staged inputs (:181-220). A strict snapshot/restore of every buffer would still allow copying out results, but the wheel_x_m_/rider_state_ refresh is oracle-mirroring behavior: the !advance term in control_tick (:1248) exists precisely so the oracle's apply_forces(advance=False) recomputes the kinematic cache; gating those writes on advance would diverge native from the oracle on graded intent rides. Per RIDE.md/RECORDING.md/simulation-decisions.md, the probe/settle paths exist to preview forces for equilibrium material and sensor/fold recording without consuming steps — restoring controller diagnostics would change the serialized state material captured between probe and next compute, breaking oracle-parity goldens if the Python reference leaks the same fields. No valid scenario requires coast_hold_nm_ zeroing, cruise PI drift, or allocation_memory_ warm-start theft — those subclauses of the contract are correct and necessary; the staging-residual subclauses are contrary to confirmed oracle semantics.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even taken literally the statement admits corrupt states: (1) no failure atomicity — nothing requires a throwing probe to restore what it mutated before the throw; the spindle compute path has no try/commit boundary so mid-fold runtime_errors leave half-written members while the runtime stays unpoisoned and reusable. (2) The probe-residual window itself: dof_frictionloss/ctrl/qfrc_applied/xfrc_applied and the acc-stage arena remain probe-stained until the next apply_forces; any caller reaching public Stepper surfaces (mj_forward/mj_step/brake_torques) in between integrates contaminated inputs — the contract either forbids this only by an unwritten 'post-restore NativeRideRuntime' carve-out or admits it. (3) posvel_fresh_ covers pos/vel-stage freshness only; the flag can read true while qacc/efc_* arena corresponds to probe forces — observable via sensordata channels — so 'restored posvel_fresh_' would still admit stale-acceleration observation. (4) Probe-designated committed writes the statement does not enumerate are admitted by silence: snapshots_ (probe pair pre-first-commit), probe_query_, components_ row store, live_.probe_last, acc_inputs_+acc_inputs_valid_, commit_epoch_++ (invalidates any in-flight PreparedTick/PreparedSettlement — currently unreachable but uncontracted), component_out_, accumulator_ force rows. (5) Controller corruption via non-probe entry points (initialize(), settle paths calling apply_forces(false,advance,...), restore()) is out of scope — a settled equilibrium can carry probe-evaluated controller state into the material. (6) Non-finite posture/IK inputs deeper than the demand scalars can still throw mid-mutation; only the demand scalars are validated up front.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Split P07a into two clauses. (a) Staging-residual clause (oracle-mandated, keep as explicitly permitted residuals): probe_step_inputs may leave mjModel::dof_frictionloss, mjData::ctrl/qfrc_applied/xfrc_applied, the acc-stage arena, snapshots_/probe_query_/components_/probe_last/acc_inputs_/commit_epoch_ probe-lane writes, and the wheel_x_m_/rider_state_ kinematic-cache refresh committed, provided the probe publishes them into ProbeResult and the next apply_forces fully rewrites them; document that posvel_fresh_=true post-probe denotes freshness under probe-stained inputs and that no inter-probe engine access is permitted before the next apply_forces. (b) Persistent-state clause (genuinely required, currently violated): on advance=false, gate or stage+restore all non-diagnostic-lane persistent writes — specifically make populate_leg_torques receive commit_hold=advance (step passes through; spindle_controller.cpp:1953), gate cruise().compute and the set_state(torque_nm=0) stomp on advance for non-probe lanes (step.cpp:1553-1583), and either stage+restore or document-as-oracle-required the unconditional controller members (command_enabled_, saturated_ik_, ik_reach_limited_, last_terms_, effort/support/allocation_diagnostics_, joint_torques_nm_, joint_capacity_nm_, pd_split_, sole_targets_, sole_goal_diagnostics_, allocation_memory_) — if the oracle itself leaks them, replace 'staged/restored' with 'mutated identically to the oracle reference' and assert equivalence rather than purity. Add an exception-safety requirement: a throwing probe must leave the same persistent state as a throwing advance (legacy facade already does via :1288-1291; extend equivalent protection or a documented no-rollback contract to the spindle path). Verdict rationale: the enumerated restore/commit_hold mechanisms are absent and several unconditional commits (coast_hold_nm_, cruise PI, allocation_memory_) produce observable post-probe divergence, so compliance=violated; justification=mixed_subclauses because the staging lanes are confirmed oracle parity while the persistence lanes are a valid engineering contract.
