# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P07b`: Hill-Hold Rollback-Brake Parity on Active Probes

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_engineering_contract`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
The statement describes a mechanism that does not exist at this commit. No symbol named peek_rollback_brake_demand (or any peek variant) exists anywhere in the tree; the only evaluator is the mutating PhysicalStep::rollback_brake_demand (native/src/runtime/step.cpp:2367-2383), which writes rollback_hold_ at lines 2375, 2379 and 2381. In apply_forces_body the resolved==nullptr fallback builds resolve_brakes({.caller_front=front, .caller_rear=rear, .rollback_demand=0., ...}) unconditionally at step.cpp:1308-1316 — there is no 'active ?' gate. Therefore the parity outcome the statement asserts is concretely falsified: probe_step_inputs calls apply_forces(true,false,...,resolved=nullptr) (step.cpp:2962-2963) so every probe resolves brakes with rollback_demand=0, while advance_body computes hold=rollback_brake_demand(qvel[root_x_dofadr_], &control) (step.cpp:1694-1696) and merges it into final_front/final_rear via resolve_brakes (step.cpp:1702-1705; merge at 2403-2408). Only the trailing clause ('equilibrium settle unaffected') holds, and only vacuously — apply_forces(false,...) sees 0 with or without the change (settle_contacts.cpp:60; the advancing relax pass equilibrium.cpp:109 also passes active=false). Residual hazards that would threaten even a conforming implementation: (a) 'active' is a leaky proxy for 'probe caller' — prepare_research_startup also calls apply_forces(true,false,...) (research_startup.cpp:65); it is spared only by its t=0/completed-equilibrium preconditions (research_startup.cpp:51-52), not by the contract; (b) the header contract 'control nullopt means the apply_forces default' (step.hpp:457-458) suggests the reflex was meant to be evaluated from apply_forces with nullptr — a peek wired that way would skip the rider_enabled gate (step.cpp:2374 '(control != nullptr && !control->rider_enabled)') and report a demand where the advancing path returns 0, breaking the very parity claimed; (c) a naive 'fix' calling the mutating rollback_brake_demand on the probe path would corrupt rollback_hold_ while passing the existing no-commit probe test, because that test only checks snapshot().step==0 / mjSTATE_INTEGRATION (test_native_runtime_step.cpp:134,209) and rollback_hold_ lives in StepState (step.cpp:3027,3056), outside the integration-state snapshot; (d) the guard's speed input is never finiteness-checked before mutation — advance_body calls the mutating guard at step.cpp:1694 on raw qvel[root_x] and only downstream does stage_components validate finite(in.speed) (drivetrain.cpp:764), so a NaN/±Inf velocity silently releases or engages the latch before the interval throws.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
Reachable divergence of the exact claim 'probe_step_inputs (active) matches advance_physics': configure drive_mode 'crank_effort' or 'articulated_effort' with default pedaling rollback fields (rollback_brake=true, rollback_engage_mps=.25, rollback_release_mps=0., rollback_demand=1. — writer_config.hpp:953-959 and tests/fixtures/golden/*/wire.json), rider_enabled control (IntentControl::rider_enabled defaults true, intent.hpp:85). Ride one interval where qvel[root_x_dofadr_] < -0.25 (a backward roll on a grade, or a restored state): advance_physics latches rollback_hold_=true and returns hold=1.0 (step.cpp:1694-1696,2378-2382), merges 1.0 into final_front/final_rear, applies brake bounds at full demand (brake_.apply, step.cpp:1323) and emits telemetry rollback_brake_demand=1.0 (step.cpp:1919). In the same committed state call probe_step_inputs(control,0.,0.) (runtime.cpp:816-828 → step.cpp:2952-2963): the resolved==nullptr fallback fixes .rollback_demand=0. (step.cpp:1313), so brakes.final_* exclude the reflex, brake_.apply writes smaller dof_frictionloss bounds, out.front_brake_bound_nm/rear_brake_bound_nm (step.cpp:2967-2971) report those bounds, and the staged brake_hold component is forced to zero because cap=torque*clamp(u=0)=0 (static_brake.cpp:91,120-124) even while a latch is engaged. The very next advance_physics stages materially different force inputs — probe/advance parity fails. Boundary: speed==-engage exactly does not engage (strict '<', step.cpp:2381); held at speed==0.0 stays held (0.0 <= -0.0, step.cpp:2379) — intended standstill hold. NaN qvel[root_x]: NaN<=x and NaN<x are both false, so the mutating call clears rollback_hold_ and returns 0 — the latch dies silently; a peek would return the same 0 so instant return-parity holds, but the advancing call's hidden state write is a violation the statement does not cover. -Inf engages (-Inf < -0.25), +Inf releases — both reachable before finite(in.speed) throws at drivetrain.cpp:764.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No valid product scenario is wrongly blocked; the constraint restates the probe's own documented contract rather than adding a new restriction. probe_step_inputs is documented as surfacing 'the same ordered force fold, actuator input row, model brake bounds and staged diagnostics views an advancing interval's pre-solve assembly leaves behind' (step.hpp:310-314, step.cpp:2946-2950), and the launch-1-channels design already mandates that the probe path see the same rider_commands() as the advance (docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-11-launch-1-channels.md:580). The rollback reflex is a state function of (qvel[root_x], rollback_hold_) that the next interval always applies independent of caller demands — a probe hiding it reports a staged fold the next interval cannot reproduce. simulation-decisions.md:209 defines hill-hold as the rider clamping brakes when rolling back faster than 0.25 m/s — a reflex, not a caller choice, so excluding it from 'what the next step commands' is the anomaly. The carve-outs the statement encodes are all preserved today: equilibrium settle calls apply_forces(false,advance,...) — including the advancing relax pass (equilibrium.cpp:109, settle_contacts.cpp:60-62) — and stays reflex-free under an 'active ?' gate, which is semantically right since the settle holds a prepared equilibrium, not a ride interval. research_startup (research_startup.cpp:65, active=true) is the only other active non-advancing caller and is vacuously safe: it throws unless time==0 with a completed equilibrium (research_startup.cpp:51-52), where rollback_hold_==false and |speed|≈0 make the peek return 0. Nothing in RIDE.md or RECORDING.md specifies probe behavior, so no documented scenario is contradicted.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even with the statement fully implemented, these invalid/corrupt states remain admitted: (1) Mutation-before-validation — rollback_brake_demand writes rollback_hold_ at step.cpp:1694/2375-2381 from raw qvel[root_x_dofadr_] before any finiteness check on that channel; advance_body validates scalar_field(front/rear) and external (step.cpp:1676-1687) but not qvel, whose first check is finite(in.speed) deep inside apply_forces→stage_components (drivetrain.cpp:764). A NaN or ±Inf velocity — reachable via solver divergence counted as warnings (step.cpp:1750-1754) or a crafted restore — clears or engages the latch, then the interval throws, contradicting the 'validates every public input before any mutation' contract (step.hpp:301-303). (2) restore() commits s.rollback_hold unconditionally (step.cpp:3056; serialized at 3027); the wire schema is a bare bool, so a well-formed-but-false restore injects an engaged hill-hold whose speed history never occurred — and with release_mps=0 the hold persists at any speed <= 0 (step.cpp:2379) until forward motion, silently altering every subsequent advance. (3) The peek contract leaves the neighboring hysteresis FSM unmirrored: the static-brake latch in hold_qfrc is read-only on probes (advance=false → latching skipped, step.cpp:1334-1336, static_brake.cpp:97-118), so a probe still cannot preview a latch capture/release the next interval will commit — staged-force parity remains approximate in exactly the same class the property claims to fix. (4) ProbeResult exposes no rollback lane — out.drive is the drivetrain probe diagnostics (step.cpp:2972-2975) while rollback_brake_demand exists only on the advancing path (step.cpp:1919); peek parity is observable only through merged bounds/components and is vacuous wherever caller demands already >= rollback_demand. (5) The control==nullptr branch (step.cpp:2374, step.hpp:457-458) is production-dead — the sole caller passes &control — yet encodes a different rider_enabled semantics that a peek signature inherits unchecked; and a mutating-probe implementation of parity would go undetected by the existing no-commit test since rollback_hold_ sits outside mjSTATE_INTEGRATION (test_native_runtime_step.cpp:134).

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Restate as an implementable invariant with pinned inputs and explicit scope: (1) Add a pure evaluator mirroring the FULL guard — including the disable branch (!cfg.rollback_brake || rollback_demand<=0 || drive_mode not in {crank_effort,articulated_effort} || rider_enabled==false) — that computes next_hold = rollback_hold_ ? (speed <= -rollback_release_mps) : (speed < -rollback_engage_mps) and returns next_hold ? rollback_demand : 0. without writing rollback_hold_; note the disabled branch of the mutating call also CLEARS the latch (step.cpp:2375), a side effect the peek deliberately cannot mirror and which lazy-clears identically on the next advance. (2) Feed it the identical inputs advance_body uses: speed = qvel[root_x_dofadr_] (step.cpp:1695) and the CALLER control (caller_control, pre-intent), not the intent-resolved local — today equivalent because IntentControl::output never rewrites rider_enabled (intent.cpp:574-594), but pinning the caller control is robust; do NOT pass nullptr despite the step.hpp:457-458 'apply_forces default' comment, which would skip the rider_enabled gate and break parity. (3) Gate on the caller class, not the shared 'active' flag alone, or document that apply_forces(true,false) parity extends to every active non-advancing caller including prepare_research_startup (research_startup.cpp:65). (4) Extend the probe no-commit assertion to StepState.rollback_hold (outside mjSTATE_INTEGRATION; test_native_runtime_step.cpp:134 sees only step/integration state) so a mutating implementation is detectable. (5) Add std::isfinite(speed_mps) validation inside rollback_brake_demand before any latch write — a NaN/±Inf qvel currently mutates rollback_hold_ at step.cpp:1694 before finite(in.speed) fires at drivetrain.cpp:764, violating validate-before-mutate. (6) Scope the parity claim honestly: it covers BrakeRequest.rollback_demand → final_front/final_rear only; caller_braking/legs_coast/motor_braking stay caller/rider-owned by design (step.hpp:241-248, step.cpp:2387-2391), ProbeResult carries no rollback telemetry lane, and the static-brake latch transition remains probe-invisible (static_brake.cpp:97-118). Audit framing note: the statement was challenged both as a description of this commit and as a candidate contract — as a description it is violated (the named function and the 'active ?' wiring are absent; the probe resolves rollback_demand=0 unconditionally at step.cpp:1313 versus the advancing merge at step.cpp:1694); evaluated as a candidate contract its boundary semantics are internally consistent but inherit the specification gaps enumerated above.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P08a`: `engine::invoke` TLS Error Containment

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `mixed_subclauses`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Mechanism: invoke() (native/src/engine_call.cpp:398-434) installs a thread-local log handler (_mjPRIVATE_setTlsLogHandler, engine_abi_312.hpp:16), pushes a Frame on the TLS active_frame stack, setjmps, runs the Operation, and restores on both paths (restore_frame, :180-183). mjLOG_ERROR -> fatal_message (:112-122) copies the bounded message into the caller ErrorBuffer and longjmps to the innermost live frame; non-error levels forward to frame->forward_handler (:124-132). Nesting restores transitively (test_boundaries.cpp:166-182 proves a raw mj_step inside an op lands on the outer frame). The containment core is sound and well-tested (fatal reuse, nested frames, two threads, warning forwarding, allocation failure: test_contracts.cpp:726-799,819-924). Adversarial cracks: (1) the statement's 'enforced trivial-frame noexcept Operation' overclaims — noexcept is type-enforced (engine_call.hpp:20) but 'trivial automatic state' is a comment-only precondition (engine_call.hpp:22-23); no check exists, and the routed surface is ~25 mj_*/mjv_*/mjr_* entry points across ~15 ad-hoc op sites, far beyond the assumed mj_{loadModel*,makeData,forward,step,resetData,setConst} set (mj_loadXML+mj_addBufferVFS at builder.cpp:105-119, mj_copyModel at transmission.cpp:45-55, mj_jac/mj_jacDot/mj_contactForce at native_contacts.cpp:66-79 + rider/legacy_*.cpp, mj_sizeModel/mj_saveModel at project_config.cpp:70-87 + capture.cpp:131-147, mjv_makeScene/mjr_makeContext/mjv_updateScene/mjr_render/mjr_overlay at render_owner.cpp:124-139, mj_setState/mj_kinematics/mj_comPos/mj_camlight/mj_tendon at :98-105). (2) The unsupported_callback() scan (:401-405, :134-150) is once-per-invoke on process-global slots: a callback installed after the scan is honored by MuJoCo inside the same frame — proven for mju_user_malloc by the tests themselves, which install it INSIDE the operation and get an immediate allocation fatal (test_contracts.cpp:808-817,830-839,888-893). If pinned 3.12 dispatch honors mju_user_error at error time (the project's own scan treats it as a live channel, :145), a concurrent or in-op install routes the fatal to foreign code instead of the TLS interceptor — a genuine escape. (3) mjcb_time is deliberately absent from the scan and ungated at engine scope: step_skip_operation calls mjcb_time() inside the frame (:230,263) and test_boundaries.cpp:254-265 proves a foreign timer executes inside engine::try_step with no rejection — containment then depends on the foreign callback reporting through mju_error only. (4) Cross-thread engine work escapes the interceptor entirely: a host binding a threadpool to the exposed mjData* (stepper.hpp:177 data()) and enabling mjENBL_ISLAND sends solver work to pool threads whose TLS has no frame — a worker mju_error hits the global handler and aborts. Zero threadpool references exist in native/src — no gate. (5) Warning/info forwarding (:130-131) executes the previous foreign handler inside the marked frame by design (test_contracts.cpp:790-799) — a foreign-execution channel inside the interceptor boundary.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
R1 (cross-thread escape, reachable via bindings): host holds mjData* (Stepper::data(), stepper.hpp:177), calls mju_bindThreadPool(d, pool), sets opt.enableflags|=mjENBL_ISLAND; engine::step -> island task on pool thread raises mju_error -> worker TLS handler is nullptr -> falls to global/stock handler -> process abort, not EngineFailure. Violates 'on the thread-local interceptor stack'. Unreachable in standalone flows (no threadpool use in native/src), reachable through the mutable engine pointers the adapters expose. R2 (TOCTOU on the callback scan): thread A inside invoke()'s mj_step (engine_call.cpp:427) while thread B assigns mju_user_error=&host_hook (or mjcb_control); the entry scan already passed (:401) — the mid-flight install is honored, and if dispatch consults mju_user_error the fatal escapes the interceptor (host hook returning -> MuJoCo aborts). The in-op-install variant is deterministic: the suite itself proves ops may install callbacks the engine immediately uses (test_contracts.cpp:810-816). R3 (foreign callback inside the interceptor window at property scope): engine::try_step(model,data,error) with mjcb_time=&foreign — test_boundaries.cpp:254-265 shows it runs inside the frame unrejected; only Stepper::try_step/try_forward gates it (stepper.cpp:245,261) and PhysicalStep's direct engine::step/step_skip/step_skip_fresh calls (runtime/step.cpp:1760-1768,1153-1158) rely on mutate()'s require_unmarked_time_callback (stepper.hpp:164-174) — still a check-then-many-calls window wider than Stepper::try_step's per-call check. R4 (forwarded foreign execution inside frame): any mju_warning inside a marked engine call invokes frame->forward_handler (:130-131) — for a bindings process that is the Python-installed global handler, i.e. interpreter code runs inside the RTSan-marked region; contained only for the mju_error path. R5 (torn library internals admitted under containment): mju_error inside mj_loadXML's C++ parser (compile_operation, builder.cpp:116-119) longjmps over libmujoco's own non-trivial frames — partial parse allocations/state leak inside the engine while invoke still returns false cleanly.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No valid product scenario found that the constraint wrongly blocks. The vendored-Python timer scenario is preserved by design: mjcb_time is excluded from unsupported_callback() (engine_call.cpp:134-150) and gated by the Stepper-side provenance policy — none/native accepted, foreign classified by address and admitted only via the bindings ForeignTimeCallbackProbe (stepper.cpp:196-238, stepper.hpp:529-538). Foreign C timers still execute inside frames at engine scope (test_boundaries.cpp:254-265), so legitimate host timers are not blocked — only unmarked-unsafe ones at Stepper entry (:188-194). Warning forwarding preserves diagnostic flow to prior handlers (:130-131, test_contracts.cpp:790-799). The blanket mju_user_{error,warning,malloc,free} rejection is blunt — it rejects even ops that never allocate — but no in-product flow installs those hooks (standalone is Python-free; the pinned vendored runtime is probed, not hooked), and the fail-closed posture matches RECORDING.md:60 ('does not bypass fatal engine errors'). A host wanting its own mju_user_error diagnostic channel cannot coexist — single-owner TLS is the documented intent, not a blocked product scenario.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Containment holds yet corrupt states are admitted: (a) post-fatal mjData is only committed-prefix safe — the engine layer is deliberately stateless (test_boundaries.cpp:318-321 comment) and its check covers qpos/qvel/time/timer only (:308-316); a fatal inside an integrator or fwdConstraint mid-write leaves torn buffers that any non-mutate caller may keep using — engine::try_* callers outside Stepper::mutate (rider/writer refresh paths such as rider_contacts.cpp:1330-1343, and all raw engine:: callers) get no poison marking; only mutate()/try_step()/try_forward()/reset()/set_state set poisoned_ (stepper.hpp:164-174, stepper.cpp:240-268,580-608). (b) post-fatal mjModel mutations persist — opt.integrator=-1 in the test stays mutated; a fatal mid-mj_setConst (engine_call.cpp:297-300) can leave the model half-recomputed. (c) foreign mjcb_time admitted at engine scope can return garbage, writing corrupt d->timer values with no error at all. (d) Stepper::~Stepper calls mj_deleteData/mj_deleteModel raw, unframed (stepper.cpp:175-180) — any future error path there aborts outside the interceptor. (e) a contained fatal inside mj_loadXML leaves libmujoco-internal partial parse state/leaks (builder.cpp:116-119) — containment of control flow, not of engine-internal consistency. (f) ErrorBuffer aliased across nested frames lets the outer fatal overwrite the inner's message (benign but admitted). (g) resource-provider callbacks resolving assets under asset_root inside compile_operation (builder.cpp:127-130) are a callback surface the scan does not cover — foreign code inside the frame.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Split the invariant: (1) Containment — 'for calls whose engine work stays on the invoking thread, a MuJoCo C error (mju_error / mjLOG_ERROR) reaches the innermost live TLS frame and is reported via ErrorBuffer; it never unwinds through MuJoCo C frames' — add explicit ambient preconditions: no concurrent mutation of mjcb_*/mju_user_* globals during the framed call, no bound threadpool/island dispatch on the involved mjData, and foreign callbacks (mjcb_time, resource providers, forwarded log handlers) execute inside the frame at caller risk, contained only for the mju_error channel. (2) Preconditions — replace 'enforced trivial-frame noexcept Operation' with 'noexcept is type-enforced (engine_call.hpp:20); trivial-automatic-state is a documented, unenforced contract — each new ad-hoc op site requires review' and either enumerate the actual routed surface or state it as 'any Operation reaching engine entry points through invoke'. (3) Boundary honesty — state that containment is control-flow containment only: post-fatal mjData/mjModel and libmujoco-internal state may be torn; validity is caller-owned (Stepper poison contract, stepper.hpp:164-174) and outside this invariant; non-error log levels are forwarded to the prior handler by design (engine_call.cpp:130-131).

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P08b`: `Stepper` Fail-Closed Poisoning Boundary

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_engineering_contract`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
The mechanism exists and is exercised: try_step/try_forward latch poisoned_ on ErrorKind::fatal (native/src/stepper.cpp:248-251,264-267); mutate() catches engine::EngineFailure, sets poisoned_, rethrows (native/src/stepper.hpp:164-174 area / stepper.cpp mutate def); require_healthy() gates ~25 mutating faces incl. all rider_contacts_* mutators (native/src/stepper.cpp:760-784,792-797,807-822); reset() re-poisons via catch(...) after data_reset (stepper.cpp:304-310); set_state() snapshots all spans pre-mutation, poisons on engine::reset_data EngineFailure, and clears only after recovering writer resets succeed (stepper.cpp:581-608). ErrorKind::unsupported_callback maps to std::invalid_argument in throw_failure (native/src/engine_call.cpp:436-443) so it never latches in try_step/try_forward/mutate. Tests prove poison->refuse->recover (native/tests/reference/test_native_engine_errors.cpp:313-343,347+) and docs state the contract (docs/TESTING.md:844-848). BUT the boundary is breached in six ways: (1) reset()/set_state() are recovery valves, not poisoned entry points -- the flat listing is only true under a 'governed by poison' reading, never a 'refused while poisoned' reading; (2) unframed fatal-capable mj_* calls bypass fatal->EngineFailure conversion entirely: native/src/rider/spindle_controller.cpp:1857,1863 mj_forwardSkip (genuine mju_error paths via mj_fwdConstraint, reached inside the mutate-wrapped apply_forces via rider_control_->compute / SpindleController::compute at spindle_controller.hpp:243), spindle_controller.cpp:1813 mj_copyData, native/src/drivetrain/transmission.cpp:160-161,178-179 mj_kinematics+mj_comPos on live mjData, and native/src/writers/drivetrain.cpp:260 mj_kinematics on live data_ inside DrivetrainWriter::reset() which Stepper::reset() itself invokes (stepper.cpp:293-302) -- the recovery valve calls an unframed fatal-capable op; the project's own audit-fix plan documents this as a known gap ('Сбой MuJoCo внутри решателя ног ... превращается в EngineFailure и «отравляет» шаговик, а не завершает процесс', docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-16-audit-fixes.md Задача 3, naming spindle mj_copyData/mj_forwardSkip). A fatal there means process-level error handling, never EngineFailure, poisoned_ never engages. (3) The Stepper-level wrappers rider_contacts_set_enabled/release_all/attachment_samples/settle call require_healthy() but do NOT catch -- EngineFailure from framed refresh_kinematics* (native/src/writers/rider_contacts.cpp:334,350,900,908,1330-1343) escapes unpoisoned unless the caller wrapped the call in mutate(); covered by the documented caller-responsible seam (native/src/stepper.hpp:421-429,440-455) and all in-tree call sites wrap, but the latch depends on caller discipline. (4) kinetic_energy() invokes framed engine::mul_m (stepper.cpp:703) -- an EngineFailure would escape unpoisoned (vacuous today since mj_mulM has no fatal paths). (5) reset()'s catch(...) poisons on ANY post-data_reset exception (stepper.cpp:307-310): a deterministic invalid_argument from rider_contacts_->reset's planar-topology validation permanently bricks the owner under a 'fatal MuJoCo error' message with no engine failure, and a concurrent foreign-callback install between the require_unmarked_time_callback pre-check (stepper.cpp:285) and engine::forward (stepper.cpp:291) would let an unsupported_callback outcome poison -- a marginal TOCTOU violation of the 'never poisons' clause. (6) Read latching is inconsistent: kinetic_energy, tire_stored_energy, tire_brush_loss_step_j, tire_radial_dissipation_power_w, rider_equality_qfrc latch via require_healthy while rider_contacts_stored_energy (stepper.cpp:786-790), tire_state, suspension_components, brake_torques, resistance_components, cruise_state, total, and all buffer views stay open -- the hpp:371 blanket claim 'Runs require_healthy() like every read path' is false, and same-semantic stored-energy APIs split on opposite sides.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
R1 (boundary bypass, confirmed-reachable mechanism): stepper.mutate([&]{ stepper.model()->opt.solver = 99; }) -- the same injection test_failure_boundary uses (native/src/runtime/runtime.cpp:682, test_native_engine_errors.cpp:195) -- then a ride step reaches the spindle leg solver: mj_forwardSkip(model_, target_data_, mjSTAGE_VEL, 1) raw at native/src/rider/spindle_controller.cpp:1857,1863 -> mj_fwdConstraint mju_error('unknown solver type') with no active engine frame -> process-level termination; never EngineFailure, never poisoned_, no recovery. The identical corruption via stepper.forward() cleanly yields EngineFailure + poison + reset recovery (test_native_engine_errors.cpp:313-326). docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-16-audit-fixes.md Задача 3 confirms this is a tracked unfixed gap. Same class: Stepper::reset() -> DrivetrainWriter::reset() -> raw mj_kinematics(model_,data_) at native/src/writers/drivetrain.cpp:260 -- a fatal inside the recovery valve cannot become EngineFailure either (contrast RiderContactWriter which deliberately frames mj_kinematics/mj_comPos at rider_contacts.cpp:95-111,1330-1343 'so a fatal engine failure surfaces as engine::EngineFailure and poisons the owning Stepper via mutate()'). R2 (unwrapped-call escape): any C++ caller invoking stepper.rider_contacts_set_enabled("saddle", false) directly (not via mutate) on an owner whose seen_ pose triggers refresh_kinematics(seen_) (rider_contacts.cpp:334): a fatal there propagates EngineFailure through the require_healthy-gated wrapper (stepper.cpp:775-778) with poisoned_ still false; subsequent try_step proceeds on post-fatal state. Reachable only via caller error + a real mj_kinematics fatal -- low practical reachability, documented seam. R3 (domain-error bricking): stepper.reset() on a healthy owner where rider_contacts_->reset(d_) throws invalid_argument (planar support topology rejection, support_geometry.cpp:275-333 via rider_contacts.cpp:245) -> catch(...) at stepper.cpp:307-310 with data_reset already true -> poisoned_=true despite NO engine failure; every retry hits the same deterministic rejection -> permanently bricked while the poison message claims 'fatal MuJoCo error'. R4 (admitted corruption): stepper.data()->qpos[0] = NaN via the public mutable accessor -> try_step -> mj_checkPos emits mjWARN_BADQPOS (warning, not error) -> returns true, poisoned_ stays false, NaN integrates forward -- invalid state admitted and propagated (warning/error split is intentional but the latch sees nothing). R5 (TOCTOU on never-poisons clause): a foreign mjcb_control installed between stepper.cpp:285 (require_unmarked_time_callback) and :291 (engine::forward) inside reset() -> unsupported_callback status -> throw_failure -> invalid_argument -> catch(...) -> poisoned_=true -- an unsupported_callback outcome poisons; needs concurrent process-global callback mutation, single-threaded unreachable. R6 (literal-statement mismatch): stepper.reset() and stepper.set_state(valid...) on a poisoned owner SUCCEED and clear poisoned_ (stepper.cpp:303,608) -- they are the designed recovery valves (runtime.cpp:975-978; require_healthy's own message says 'call reset', engine::set_poisoned_error). R7: set_state recovery never forwards (posvel_fresh_=false, stepper.cpp:594) so unlatched diagnostics read reset-pose-derived values under caller qpos until a forward runs.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
The literal wording would wrongly block the documented recovery flow if reset/set_state were hard-refused: runtime.cpp:975-978 calls Stepper::reset on poisoned owners as 'the supported recovery path', the poison message itself directs 'call reset', and RIDE.md's reset-separated episodes plus RECORDING.md's replay/state-restore workflows depend on both valves succeeding while poisoned -- the carve-out is necessary and present in implementation. Unlatched reads serve post-fatal diagnostics and the crash-path accounting flush (runtime.cpp:103 flush_accounting; TESTING.md:847 'read-only state views remain accessible while poisoned'; hpp:162-163 'Read-only diagnostics remain available after a fatal engine error'). unsupported_callback non-poisoning is correct: it is an environmental/transient refusal (foreign mjcb_time/mjcb_control provenance, plugin models) retryable after the callback is removed -- poisoning would brick an owner for a process-global condition it did not cause. Residual necessity friction: the latched read set contradicts the 'read-only diagnostics remain available' contract -- a post-fatal energy audit can call rider_contacts_stored_energy() but NOT kinetic_energy(), tire_stored_energy(), tire_brush_loss_step_j(), or tire_radial_dissipation_power_w() (all require_healthy-gated), and rider_equality_qfrc is latched while it is a pure read of the efc arena -- either the latch over-reaches on reads or the docs overstate availability; the split should be pinned. No scenario was found where a mutation legitimately needs to run while poisoned (all real mutation paths are inside mutate() at step.cpp:1222-1235,1642-1665 and init/finalize wrappers), so the mutation-side closure blocks nothing valid.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even where the stated boundary holds, substantial invalid states remain admitted: (a) public model()/data() mutable pointers admit arbitrary corruption -- NaN qpos (propagates as warnings, not fatals: R4), ctrl/act tampering, opt.solver corruption that then either poisons (framed path) or kills the process (spindle path) depending on which code hits it; (b) borrowed-ref writer mutators (tire_writer().set_state/commit_prepared_state, drive().restore/reset, suspension()/resistance()/cruise() mutators, rider_contacts().assign_state/set_enabled) mutate a poisoned owner with no gate -- caller-responsible by documentation only; (c) non-EngineFailure exceptions inside mutate() leave partial writer mutations unpoisoned -- the staged-then-commit transactional discipline (e.g. TireWriter::prepare_state validates everything before prepared_states_=next at tire.cpp:563) is convention, not enforced by the boundary; (d) unframed fatal-capable calls (spindle_controller.cpp:1813,1857,1863; drivetrain.cpp:260; transmission.cpp:160-161,178-179) admit failures that never reach the latch at all; (e) set_state recovery admits stale derived state (no forward; reset-pose-derived xpos/efc readable under caller qpos); (f) public note_posvel_stale()/note_posvel_fresh() toggles admit mislabeled freshness, partially defended by acc_inputs_unchanged gating before step_skip_fresh (step.cpp:1760-1769); (g) reset()/set_state() catch(...) admits a false-positive poison class -- any non-engine exception after data_reset (domain validation, allocation failure) latches as 'fatal MuJoCo error' and can brick deterministically; (h) invalid_solver_error (engine_call.cpp:159-178) poisons on a pre-flight model-field check with zero data corruption, and persists through reset until the field is fixed via the same public model() seam; (i) TireWriterView::commit_prepared_state is noexcept (stepper.cpp:471-474) -- any future throwing commit terminates the process rather than poisoning; (j) install_writers cleanup (stepper.cpp:164-172) resets only rider_contacts_/drive_ before mj_deleteData/mj_deleteModel -- a ctor-failure dtor-order hazard if other writers' dtors dereference m_/d_.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Restate P08b with explicit scoping: 'After engine::EngineFailure propagates from a framed engine call at any Stepper boundary, poisoned_ latches: try_step/try_forward/step/forward/mutate and every require_healthy-gated mutator refuse (ErrorKind::poisoned status / std::logic_error) until a guarded reset() or valid set_state() succeeds. reset() and set_state() are recovery valves, not refused entry points: they re-poison on EngineFailure or any post-data_reset exception and clear poisoned_ only on full success (stepper.cpp:304-310,581-608; TESTING.md:844-848). ErrorKind::unsupported_callback (foreign/unmarked callback policy, plugin models) is an environmental refusal that never poisons and is retryable once the callback is removed. Mutating paths reachable through borrowed refs (model(), data(), tire_writer(), drive(), suspension(), resistance(), cruise(), rider_contacts(), rider_attachment_lstsq()) are caller-responsible seams: each must be wrapped in mutate() or proven free of EngineFailure-capable calls -- this must be stated as an obligation, not implied. Pin the read split: document exactly which observations stay open while poisoned (currently inconsistent -- kinetic_energy/tire energies/rider_equality_qfrc latch while rider_contacts_stored_energy/components/state views do not; hpp:371 and TESTING.md:744-745 overstate uniformity). All mj_* calls on Stepper-owned or scratch mjData must route through engine::invoke-framed wrappers -- raw fatal-capable calls at spindle_controller.cpp:1813,1857,1863, drivetrain.cpp:260, and transmission.cpp:160-161,178-179 currently bypass fatal->EngineFailure conversion and defeat the latch (tracked in docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-16-audit-fixes.md Задача 3); pure-arithmetic reads (mj_jac, mj_mulJacTVec, mj_name2id) may stay unframed per the documented classification.'

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P08c`: `BIKE_NONBLOCKING` Realtime Status Path

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_engineering_contract`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Audited call graph: Stepper::try_step/try_forward (native/src/stepper.cpp:240-270, declared noexcept BIKE_NONBLOCKING at stepper.hpp:152-153) gate poisoned_ and the mjcb_time address whitelist, then call engine::try_step/try_forward (engine_call.cpp:599-623), which prevalidate nplugin and opt.solver with bounded formatters (plugin_error :89-93, invalid_solver_error :159-178) and enter engine::invoke (:398-434). Within the audited functions' own code, all early-return writes are bounded char copies into the caller-owned 2KB ErrorBuffer (engine_call.hpp:15-18) — allocation-free. Three reachable challenges remain. (1) UNVETTED LOG-HANDLER FORWARDING: invoke installs log_handler (engine_call.cpp:124-132) which forwards every non-error message to frame->forward_handler — selected from the previous TLS handler or _mjPRIVATE_getGlobalLogHandler() (:410-418). unsupported_callback() (:134-150) vets mjcb_control/passive/sensor/contactfilter/act_dyn/gain/bias and mju_user_error/warning/malloc/free, but never the TLS/global log-handler slot. mju_warning inside mj_step/mj_forward is reachable during normal stepping (mjWARN_CONTACTFULL/CNSTRFULL arena overflow, BADQPOS/BADQVEL/BADQACC on nonfinite state, which is reachable through physics divergence or direct mjData* writes via the public Stepper::data() accessor stepper.hpp:177 that bypasses set_state's finite checks stepper.cpp:568-571); test_contracts.cpp:790-799 proves warnings inside invoke reach the prior handler. A foreign handler that allocates or locks executes inside the marked region. This is defensible only under the broadest exclusion in docs/TESTING.md:1333 ('any callback/FFI surface outside the marked functions'); the briefing's stated exclusion names only 'MuJoCo C mju_error/mju_warning snprintf/stdio paths', and a foreign handler is neither — it is an engine-invoked callback the precheck list missed, the same class that the mjcb_time whitelist and unsupported_callback() exist to vet. (2) FIRST-TOUCH TLS ON A FRESH THREAD: invoke reads/writes `thread_local Frame* active_frame` (engine_call.cpp:78) and calls _mjPRIVATE_setTlsLogHandler/:getGlobalLogHandler (:410,:418,:182) — private ABI whose own TLS storage lives in the pinned dylib (engine_abi_312.hpp:16-17, dependencies.lock.json:14). On the qualified macOS RTSan lane, first TLV access per thread routes through tlv_get_addr/malloc. The constructor warms this only on the construction thread via init_engine_data's forward (stepper.cpp:104-113); refresh_time_callback_policy warms only the native_time epoch (stepper.cpp:219, engine_call.cpp:491) and never enters invoke. A Stepper built on thread A and stepped on thread B therefore performs lazy TLS initialization inside the marked region on B's first call — cross-thread use is explicitly admitted (thread_local frames; test_thread_local_engine_frames test_contracts.cpp:757-777) and never exercised under RTSan (all marked calls in the suite run on the warmed thread). In-tree verification of the dylib is impossible (deps fetched outside the tree), so this is a probable-not-proven violation conditioned on dyld TLS laziness. (3) The property's own prevalidated-error list is honored: poisoned_ (:241-244), foreign mjcb_time (:245-248), nplugin!=0 (:613-616), out-of-range opt.solver (:617-620) all return through bounded status writes with no engine entry. Noted quirk: invalid_solver_error reports kind=fatal (:160) which poisons the Stepper (stepper.cpp:250-251) despite no engine call having run, while plugin_error reports unsupported_callback (:90) which does not poison — same prevalidation class, different durability.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
CE1 (foreign log handler inside marked region): install an allocating/locking TLS log handler via _mjPRIVATE_setTlsLogHandler(&host_handler) before a Stepper's realtime loop (or a global handler via _mjPRIVATE_getGlobalLogHandler-side API); construct Stepper and run try_step on a state that emits any mju_warning — e.g., write d->qpos[i]=NaN through the public data() accessor (stepper.hpp:177), or drive the model to contact/constraint arena overflow (mjWARN_CONTACTFULL/CNSTRFULL), or let a stiff interval produce nonfinite qacc (mjWARN_BADQACC). log_handler (engine_call.cpp:124-132) forwards the warning to frame->forward_handler (:414-418) = the foreign handler, which then allocates/locks inside the BIKE_NONBLOCKING region — RTSan abort, or a silent deadline miss in unmarked builds. Nothing in unsupported_callback() (:134-150) or the Stepper gates rejects this slot. CE2 (cross-thread TLS warm-up gap): construct Stepper on thread A (ctor warms active_frame and the log-handler TLS via engine::forward at stepper.cpp:109); run refresh_time_callback_policy + try_step on previously-unused thread B → B's first access to `thread_local Frame* active_frame` (engine_call.cpp:408/422) performs dyld TLV allocation inside the marked region. refresh warms only the mjcb_time epoch (stepper.cpp:219; engine_call.cpp:491) — the documented warm assumption does not cover the frame machinery. CE3 (shadow of the scope, sufficiency-side): install foreign mjcb_time, then advance the production tick — PhysicalStep::advance_body calls engine::step_skip/step (step.cpp:1760-1768), whose operations invoke mjcb_time() unguarded (engine_call.cpp:230,263) because unsupported_callback() omits mjcb_time and engine::try_step* has no whitelist — the invalid state the audited functions reject reaches the engine through the sibling entries the runtime actually uses (mitigated only by the outer mutate() gate step.cpp:1648 → stepper.hpp:165-174, an unmarked upstream check, not the marked functions).

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
The gates are conservative but justified for this product: post-T63 there is no interpreter, so every foreign callback owner is suspect; RIDE.md:230-233 records the only real foreign-mjcb_time scenario (the macOS _simulate viewer timer) as transition-era and dead with the bindings. Plugin models are rejected at load too (engine_call.cpp:567-568,577-578) and no product doc (simulation-decisions.md, RIDE.md, RECORDING.md) requires MuJoCo plugins or engine callbacks — physics is closed-model with writer-side forces. Two marginal over-rejections exist: (a) try_step/try_forward compare mjcb_time by snapshot address equality (stepper.cpp:245-247,261-263), so installing the whitelisted-safe &engine::native_time AFTER a refresh that saw nullptr is refused until the caller manually re-refreshes — step()/forward() auto-refresh (stepper.cpp:273,279) but direct try_* callers must call refresh_time_callback_policy first (documented stepper.hpp:157-159): the same always-safe function is accepted or rejected purely by snapshot timing. (b) The unsupported_callback() blanket refusal treats mju_user_malloc/mju_user_free presence as fatal to stepping even though verified-allocation-free mj_step never invokes them — a host-side allocator/profiler installed process-globally blocks every Stepper; defensible as fail-closed defense-in-depth, but it is the one gate that refuses a callback that would never fire inside the region. No product scenario is wrongly blocked; these are documented, fail-closed edges rather than contradictions of confirmed goals.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even with the statement holding exactly as written, these invalid/corrupt states remain admitted: (1) All of mjOption except solver is unvetted — opt.integrator out-of-range reaches mj_step's verbatim `default: mju_error("invalid integrator")` (engine_call.cpp:252-253) via the excluded snprintf path; opt.timestep<=0/NaN is only checked for nhistory>0 at make_data (engine_call.cpp:581-582) and afterwards silently integrates backwards/zero, admitted AND unreported; opt.enableflags/disableflags (mjENBL_FWDINV extra pass, mjENBL_ISLAND island machinery whose allocation profile is unverified for this pin) engage arbitrary engine paths. All reachable through the public non-const Stepper::model() accessor (stepper.hpp:176). (2) The foreign TLS/global log handler — the unvetted engine-invoked callback of CE1 — is admitted. (3) The audited gate does not cover the product's actual step entries: engine::try_step_skip/try_step_skip_fresh/try_forward_skip/try_implicit and the throwing engine::step/forward carry neither BIKE_NONBLOCKING nor the mjcb_time whitelist; the production tick (step.cpp:1764-1768) is protected only by the unmarked upstream mutate() callback refresh — the same invalid state enters through siblings outside the statement's scope. (4) Direct mjData* mutation via Stepper::data() bypasses every finite/width check of set_state — NaN/Inf states, inconsistent arena state, corrupt nefc — admitted into the marked region, where they surface as warnings/fatals on excluded paths. (5) Poison durability is inconsistent: a solver prevalidation failure poisons a provably-untouched engine state (kind=fatal, stepper.cpp:250-251) while equivalent prevalidation rejections (nplugin, callbacks) do not — a poisoned-but-clean state the contract cannot distinguish from real mid-step corruption. (6) Process-global callback checks are TOCTOU snapshots — a foreign callback installed between invoke's unsupported_callback() check and the engine call executes inside the region; admitted under the implicit single-threaded-callback-installation assumption. (7) Cross-thread Stepper use is admitted with the CE2 per-thread warm-up gap.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Keep the property but tighten its edges. (1) Resolve the log-handler ambiguity explicitly: either extend the prevalidation set (vet the installed TLS and global log handlers beside mjcb_time — e.g., pin/whitelist them in refresh_time_callback_policy and reject foreign handlers in unsupported_callback()), or amend the statement's exclusion list to name the warning-forwarding path, since the briefing's 'MuJoCo C snprintf/stdio' wording does not cover a foreign handler invoked through log_handler. (2) Define threading scope: either require the marked path to run only on threads that already executed engine::invoke (constructor or documented per-thread warm-up), or have refresh_time_callback_policy warm the invoke machinery itself (e.g., a framed no-op) so the 'warmed' assumption covers active_frame and _mjPRIVATE_setTlsLogHandler TLS, not just the mjcb_time epoch. (3) Scope honesty: the statement covers the status entry points; document that sibling engine entries used by the product tick (engine::step_skip*, forward_skip, implicit — step.cpp:1764-1768) are unmarked and unguarded for mjcb_time, protected only by the unmarked mutate() wrapper — or extend the gate/mark to the whole-tick entry as rtsan.hpp:27-29 already anticipates. (4) Align the exclusion wording with docs/TESTING.md:1333 ('any callback/FFI surface outside the marked functions') so the contract doesn't rely on two different exclusion lists. (5) Decide and document poison semantics for prevalidation-only failures: solver=99 poisons an untouched engine while nplugin!=0 does not — either lower solver prevalidation to a non-poisoning status or state the fail-closed intent. (6) Optionally widen prevalidation to the rest of mjOption (integrator, timestep, enableflags) now that model() hands out a mutable mjModel*.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P08d`: `ControlClock` Alignment & Hold Gate

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `holds`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_engineering_contract`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Constructor gate is airtight within its stated assumptions: !isfinite or <=0 on either duration -> invalid_argument (control.cpp:51-54); ratio non-finite or >= 0x1p63 -> invalid_argument before the float->int64 conversion (control.cpp:62-64) — the bound is correctly placed since the largest double below 2^63 is 2^63-1024, so nearbyint can never yield an out-of-range int64; steps<1 checked first, short-circuiting the product check so steps_per_period==0 can never be stored (control.cpp:65-70); held() on empty held_ throws exactly std::runtime_error (control.cpp:84-88), exercised by test_control_clock.cpp:96-107. Residual edges that do NOT violate the letter: (a) std::nearbyint honours the ambient FP rounding mode — Python's round() is unconditionally half-even, so under a foreign fesetround(FE_UPWARD) the computed steps diverges from the oracle (e.g. ratio exactly 0.5 -> steps 1 instead of 0, combined with the 1e-9 slack a 0.5*timestep period can then be admitted); (b) is_tick rejects step<0 with std::invalid_argument (control.cpp:74-76) where Python's modulo semantics would evaluate -1 % steps to nonzero -> False — divergence outside the stated property text, reachable only by direct C++ API misuse since decode validates step>=0 (runtime.cpp:265-267); (c) the 1e-9 tolerance is absolute, so 'integer multiple' is only enforced to +/-1ns: ControlClock(1e-9,1.5e-9) admits steps=2 for a genuinely non-integer period, and conversely for products >= ~4.5e6 s ulp(product) exceeds 1e-9 so an exact integer multiple can be falsely rejected — both regimes are unreachable at product scale (dt>=1e-4 s).

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
No constructor input satisfying the preconditions is wrongly admitted or rejected at reachable scales: (0,.005),(.0005,inf),(.0005,-.005),(1e-300,1e300) all throw invalid_argument (control.cpp:51-64; test_control_clock.cpp:111-115); ratio underflow to 0 (e.g. 1e-320/1e300) -> steps=0 -> steps<1 rejection; steps*timestep overflow -> inf > 1e-9 -> rejection (the NaN-comparison hole is unreachable: operands are guarded finite so the difference can only be finite or +inf). In the normal lifecycle held()-while-Empty is unreachable: step_ starts 0 (finalize.cpp:66), is_tick(0) is always true (control.cpp:77), and hold() precedes any held() read (step.cpp:1529 vs 1534). The one reachable crash repro is a corrupt restore: a StepState with step=5 (mid-period), held_control=null, initializing=false decodes cleanly — decode validates step>=0 only (runtime.cpp:265-267), restore commits unconditionally (step.cpp:3047,3051-3054) — then the first apply_forces(true,true,...) hits is_tick(5)==false and executes torques=control_clock_.held() -> std::runtime_error 'control clock holds no command yet' (step.cpp:1534, control.cpp:86): a guaranteed mid-run crash admitted at restore time rather than rejected on decode.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
None found — the constraint is a confirmed product decision, not over-reach: simulation-decisions.md:646-648 records the 2026-11-09 user decision that control period / actuator delay / sensor latency / sample period enter the profile as mandatory fields 'с проверкой кратности' (with integer-multiplicity check). All shipped configs satisfy it exactly (timestep 0.00125/control 0.005 -> 4 steps; 0.0005/0.005 -> 10; research 0.01 -> 8-20). Decimal-spelled multiples pass trivially: both TOML literals round to adjacent doubles so residual ~1e-20 << 1e-9. The same rint-based rule is enforced earlier at configuration::validate (validate.cpp:1154-1158) for control_period_s and 1159-1215 for the sibling periods, so ControlClock is a redundant-but-consistent last line for direct construction. Non-integer ratios are intentionally rejected (test_control_clock.cpp:36,92) — by design, not a wrongly blocked scenario.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
(1) steps_per_period/timestep_s/period_s are public non-const 'Python-attribute mirror' fields (control.hpp:116-118) and PhysicalStep::control_clock() hands out a mutable ControlClock& (step.hpp:387-389): physical.control_clock().steps_per_period=0 is reachable through the public API and the next is_tick evaluates step % 0 (control.cpp:77) -> integer division by zero, a hard SIGFPE/UB rather than the catchable ZeroDivisionError the Python oracle would raise under the same attribute assignment — the 'steps>=1' enforcement is construction-time only and the modulo has no divisor guard (same exposure at step.cpp:1741's (step_+1)%steps_per_period). (2) restore() admits the inconsistent triple step NOT aligned mod steps_per_period + held_control absent + initializing=false (step.cpp:3046-3054) — decodes clean, crashes on the first advancing non-tick; the wire schema (runtime.cpp:256-267) has no cross-field check that held_control must be present whenever step % steps_per_period != 0. (3) hold() stores NamedEntries<double> verbatim with no finiteness/name/duplicate gate (control.cpp:80-82) — the restore wire path is gated by decode_named_reals -> finite_real (state_codec.cpp:267-278) but the direct C++ ABI trusts caller input; NaN torques held then replayed verbatim into write() (step.cpp:1536). (4) Degenerate magnitudes admitted: period_s marginally below timestep_s (ControlClock(0.00125,0.0012499995) -> steps=1, residual 5e-10) gives a control period shorter than a physics step; gigantic steps (dt=1.0, period=1e15 -> steps=1e15) pass while making the clock effectively never re-tick.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Keep the stated constructor contract (positive finite durations; ratio finite and < 2^63; steps = nearbyint(period_s/timestep_s) >= 1; |steps*timestep_s - period_s| <= 1e-9 absolute; held() while Empty -> std::runtime_error) and extend it with: (a) an explicit invariant clause that steps_per_period remains >= 1 for the object's lifetime — make the three mirrored fields const or privately-set (mirroring the oracle's attribute names does not require mutability), since today is_tick's step % steps_per_period and step.cpp:1741's modulo have no zero-divisor guard and post-construction writes produce SIGFPE rather than a catchable error; (b) a restore-time cross-field rule: StepState must satisfy s.step % steps_per_period == 0 || s.held_control.has_value() || s.initializing, enforced in PhysicalStep::restore/decode_step_state so an inconsistent checkpoint fails at decode instead of throwing mid-run (step.cpp:3046-3054, runtime.cpp:265-267); (c) document the absolute-tolerance scope (1e-9 s, not relative) and the nearbyint dependence on the ambient FP rounding mode vs Python's unconditional half-even round() — either pin FE_TONEAREST or use an explicit round-half-even helper shared with configuration::validate's rint (validate.cpp:1155); (d) optionally state that is_tick's negative-step invalid_argument is a deliberate native strengthening over Python modulo semantics, outside the held-gate clause.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P09`: `NativeRideRuntime` Single-Owner Exclusion, Committed-Prefix Preservation & Balance/Brake/Crash Monitors

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `mixed_subclauses`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Clause-by-clause verification against the scoped sources. (a) AdvancementGuard (native/src/runtime/runtime.cpp:74-90) is a non-blocking CAS reject (std::runtime_error, no latching, no waiting) taken by every public entry point: advance() (runtime.cpp:779), probe_step_inputs() (824), request_crank_reposition() (834), snapshot()/render_frame()/model_image() (861/873/887), flush()/prepare_samples()/acknowledge_samples()/accounting_state()/recorded_columns()/recorded_intervals()/seal_episode()/release_samples()/take_pending_warning() (893-953), reset() (957), close() (995), set_finish_position() (748), test_fail_at_step() (668). close() takes the guard BEFORE closed_.store (995-996), so a close() rejected during an in-flight advance leaves closed_ false — matches the claim. Committed-prefix atomicity: committed_integration_state_/presentation_ are staged in next_* and swapped at runtime.cpp:717-718; committed_step_/time_s_/outcome_ follow at 719-721; every throw site before 717 (collect InvalidReferenceRun, test_failure_boundary EngineFailure, advance_physics, accounting_->push, get_state, capture_presentation) leaves the triple at the last completed interval; failures after it (close_period/flush InvalidReferenceRun at 727-734) refresh only the mjData-free joints_saturated overlay via capture_accounted_presentation (presentation.cpp:190-197). Weak points found: (i) the guard is not held inside advance_interval itself — NativeResearchRuntime::advance_control drives it under busy_ only (research.cpp:343,376), so advancing_ stays false during research stepping; the inner runtime is private and friend-restricted (research.hpp:183-196) so every in-tree caller also holds a guard, but the stated exclusion is a public-API guarantee, not an internal invariant; (ii) advance() validates scalar arguments before the guard (runtime.cpp:764-778) so a concurrent caller can observe invalid_argument instead of the reentrancy error — documented ordering but observable; (iii) a bad_alloc in crash_outcome() or the CrashEvent string copy inside cache_research_boundary() (runtime.cpp:721-722) can split committed_step_ (already written at 719) from committed_outcome_/the research-boundary cache — the next interval then commits one extra step past a latched crash; (iv) a second hand-rolled commit path exists in RuntimeRigAccess::external_interval (native/src/validation/runtime_access.cpp:99-104) that skips test_failure_boundary and the finish-position outcome and writes "crash:"+cause rather than crash_outcome()'s pitch-sign mapping (runtime.cpp:62-69), so a pitch_over latch commits as 'crash:pitch_over' there instead of 'crash:loop_out'/'crash:endo'; (v) recorded_intervals() returns std::span<const SamplePtr> that outlives the guard release (runtime.cpp:922-926) — a borrow escape the exclusion cannot protect; (vi) reset(): if physical_->restore() inside apply_bootstrap() throws mid-way (allocation in held_rider_terms_/signals/state copies), step_ may already be 0 while committed_step_ retains the pre-reset value — the next commit writes a regressed committed_step_ (OOM edge). (b) CrashDetector::check early-returns on a latched event (control.cpp:365) and the contact-crash set_event is gated by !event().has_value() (step.cpp:2260-2268); only restore() can rewrite event_ (set_event, step.cpp:3065), so first-cause irreversibility per episode holds. However the two latch sites use different field bases: the contact latch records interval-START t and pre-step qpos (step.cpp:1690-1691,2263-2268) while check() records post-step data->time/qpos (control.cpp:371-377) — the recorded time_s/position_m/pitch_rad differ by one dt depending on which monitor fired, and external_interval adds a third outcome-label spelling (see iv above). (c) Balance monitor: started_s latches only on a riding sample (control.cpp:300), grace_left_s recomputes every sample as max(0, started+grace-time) so it burns in wall-clock through !riding windows (301-303), a braked stop clears started_s only when !riding (299 — riding is unreachable under motor_braking by resolve_brakes' !motor_braking term, step.cpp:2409-2415), the production braked classification requires motor_braking AND |speed|<=standstill AND (launch hold phase OR spent grace) (step.cpp:2803-2809) — matching the confirmed decision (docs/agents/simulation-decisions.md:431-436, П9 2026-11-15) — and the first event latches permanently while current_event clears on !riding/in-grace/speed-recovery (control.cpp:304-308,312-317). DIVERGENCE: the statement says non-braked !riding windows 'pause the dwell timer' — the code does not pause, it CLEARS the accumulation (low_started_s.reset(); low_speed_s=0; control.cpp:305-306). A true pause retains the count; the code discards it, so after any single !riding sample a full dwell_s of contiguous low-speed riding is required again. The confirmed decision text uses the same word ('только ставит счётчик простоя на паузу', simulation-decisions.md:166), so either the property is imprecise or the code diverges from the requirement — flagged either way. Secondary: balance_channel computes grace_spent from the RESTORED/stale balance.state().grace_left_s BEFORE update() recomputes it from started_s+grace_s-time (step.cpp:2800-2807 vs control.cpp:301-303) — a one-interval stale classification input on restored state. (d) Codec is strict: exact {front,rear} keys and null-or-finite members validated before the State is produced (brake_latch_codec.cpp:22-34), and StaticBrake::restore validates both anchors then commits both (static_brake.cpp:134-143) — atomic. PhysicalStep::restore deliberately calls brake_.reset() and ignores s.brake_latch (step.cpp:3059-3061; the StepState comment at step.hpp:203-206 documents the drop); the wire field is emitted by state() (step.cpp:3037) and decoded strictly into bootstrap_step_state_ (runtime.cpp:314-316) — carried but never applied, matching the claim. Note StaticBrake::restore has NO production caller (only native/tests/reference/test_static_brake_latch.cpp exercises it) — the apply-atomicity clause is vacuous on the production path, with the bootstrap decode being the only strictness gate actually in force.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
R1 (clause c, reachable): rider below floor with grace spent — riding at speed<floor for dwell_s-epsilon, then ONE !riding sample (brief brake tap), then riding again. Under the statement's 'pause the dwell timer' semantics the retained accumulation fires after ~epsilon more low-speed; the code requires a fresh full dwell_s because update() cleared low_started_s/low_speed_s at control.cpp:305-306. A rider feather-braking rhythmically below floor can therefore ride unbalanced indefinitely without balance_lost — an observable output divergence from the stated property, and arguably from the 'паузу' wording of the confirmed decision (simulation-decisions.md:166). R2 (clause b label fork): drive a pitch-over crash through RuntimeRigAccess::external_interval (validation/runtime_access.cpp:103) — committed_outcome_ becomes 'crash:pitch_over' while NativeRideRuntime::advance's crash_outcome() maps the same latch to 'crash:loop_out' (pitch_rad<0) or 'crash:endo' — two commit implementations produce different outcome spellings for the same physical event. R3 (clause c stale grace_spent): bootstrap with balance{started_s:0, grace_left_s:0, last_time_s:0} — admitted; on the first interval balance_channel reads grace_spent=true from the stale field (step.cpp:2801-2802) even though update() would recompute ~3s, so a motor_braking standstill on that interval classifies BalanceStop::braked and clears started_s — a spurious grace re-arm the stated model excludes. R4 (clause a): bad_alloc between runtime.cpp:719 and 722 leaves committed_step_ advanced while committed_outcome_ stays unset — the next advance_interval commits one more physics interval after the crash latch; crash_outcome is retried at 721 and lands a step late. R5 (clause a): reset() with a mid-restore allocation failure inside physical_->restore (step.cpp:3046-3085 member copies) leaves step_=0 vs committed_step_=N — the next interval commits a REGRESSED committed_step_, breaking prefix monotonicity. Both R4/R5 are OOM-gated.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No confirmed product scenario is wrongly blocked; every constraint traces to a documented decision. Candidates assessed and found defensible or already sanctioned: (1) concurrent snapshot()/probe reads during advance are rejected — but the assumed 'single-owner runtime thread' (native-engineering.md:45, scratch-confinement rule) forbids such callers anyway, and a torn vector read is a worse outcome than a fast reject; (2) advance(target_step < committed_step_) is rejected — reset() is the designed rewind surface; (3) the brake-latch drop means a mid-episode captured latch can never carry into a restore — but bootstraps are t=0-only (runtime.cpp:578-587), where anchors should be null anyway, and the fresh-bite rule is an explicit decision (step.cpp:3059-3060 citing physical_runtime.py:426-428); (4) the crash latch cannot distinguish a transient handlebar graze >1N from a terminal fall — borderline modeling edge, but contact-with-terrain is terminal by the model's contract, not a blocked scenario; (5) a riderless physical config makes 'riding' permanently false (resolve_brakes step.cpp:2414 requires rider_control_) — the monitor is inert by design since there is no balance expectation without a rider; (6) the dwell-clear behavior flagged in R1 actually makes the monitor MORE permissive than the stated 'pause' — a valid slow-riding-with-brake-taps scenario escapes detection rather than being wrongly blocked.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
The t=0 bootstrap gate (runtime.cpp:578-587) validates step==0, data->time==0, empty history/model_status, monitor.strict and measurement_decimation — but performs NO consistency check on the monitor-state sections, admitting: (1) crash.event != null at t=0 — including a fabricated cause with NaN pitch_rad via the $nonfinite sentinel that vread::real() accepts (value_readers.hpp:94-106, tagged_nonfinite) — producing committed_outcome_ at step 0 (runtime.cpp:660); advance() then returns an outcome with zero intervals run, and a NaN pitch is mislabeled 'crash:endo' because NaN<0 is false (runtime.cpp:65-67); (2) balance.last_time_s>0 or filters.*.last_time_s>0 — the ctor admits them, then the first update throws 'balance monitor time must not rewind' (control.cpp:293-295) / 'timestamp moved backwards' (control.cpp:106-108), yielding a permanently bricked but 'valid' runtime; (3) balance.started_s<0 (finite optional — only finiteness is checked) — grace is pre-spent at t=0, so the 'engages on first riding sample' invariant is defeated from the start; (4) balance.event/current_event pre-seeded — a fabricated latched loss surfaces in presentation.balance_lost_at_m (presentation.cpp:144-145); (5) NaN/±Inf in every real_decoded field — balance.low_speed_s/grace_left_s/event fields, crash fields, contacts.*_load_n, contact_query/probe_query.held_load_n, energy.* counters (all go through real_field -> vread::real, which deliberately accepts the $nonfinite tag) — NaN loads are visible in the t=0 presentation (capture_presentation reads contacts_, presentation.cpp:140-141, invoked at runtime.cpp:661-662) and NaN counters poison the energy ledger; (6) contact_query.unloaded_steps as a negative int64 extends the dropout bridge beyond the configured window (control.cpp:121-130); (7) the escaped std::span<const SamplePtr> from recorded_intervals() (runtime.cpp:922-926) — a caller-held borrow that races subsequent advances after the guard releases; (8) stale restored grace_left_s feeding grace_spent one interval before recompute (step.cpp:2801-2807). None of these violate a clause of the stated property, but they are corrupt/inconsistent runtime states the admission surface allows.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Revise the statement as follows. (c) Replace 'pause the dwell timer' with the implemented transition: 'any !riding, in-grace, or floor-recovered sample CLEARS the dwell accumulation (low_started_s=nullopt, low_speed_s=0); a fresh contiguous low-speed run of dwell_s is required to fire' — and reconcile against the confirmed decision's 'паузу' wording (simulation-decisions.md:166): if retention was intended, the code at control.cpp:305-306 — not the property — is what must change. Also pin that grace_spent in balance_channel reads the serialized grace_left_s BEFORE update() recomputes it (step.cpp:2801-2807), or move the read after recompute. (b) Specify the field basis: the contact-crash latch records interval-START time/position/pitch while the pitch/handlebar fallback records post-step values; require all commit paths — including validation's external_interval (runtime_access.cpp:103) — to use the canonical crash_outcome() mapping so 'crash:pitch_over' can never be emitted where 'crash:loop_out'/'crash:endo' is required. (a) State explicitly that advance_interval itself does not take advancing_ — exclusion is supplied by each caller (research busy_, golden/validation Guard, or the public API's guard); document the bad_alloc split window between committed_step_ and committed_outcome_/boundary cache (runtime.cpp:719-722); document recorded_intervals()' span as a non-owned borrow outside guard protection; document the reset() mid-restore regression edge. (d) Note that StaticBrake::restore() has no production caller — latch-apply atomicity is test-only; the bootstrap decode is the sole strictness gate on the wire. Add a sufficiency clause to the surrounding contract: the t=0 bootstrap should reject or normalize monitor state inconsistent with t=0 (crash.event must be null; balance/filter last_time_s in {null,0}; started_s/low_started_s in {null,0}; contacts/energy sections finite-checked), or explicitly document fabricated monitor state as admitted-and-self-correcting.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P10a`: `ModelStatus` Verdict Lattice & Wire/Accounting Hardening

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `mixed_subclauses`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Clause-by-clause probe of native/src/runtime/status.cpp:186-288 and native/src/runtime/runtime.cpp:559-587. (a) The observe-time lattice holds exactly: next_numerical = energy_quality_ok(...) && !was_false evaluated only under `include_energy && find(channels,"energy")!=nullptr && !object(*energy).empty()` (status.cpp:209-214), so not_evaluated->{true,false}, true->{true,false}, false->{false} on evaluation events, with stutter on unmeasured/masked rows (include_energy=measured, accounting.cpp:692-740). Gaps: the transition set omits the stutter edges (not_evaluated->not_evaluated, true->true on unmeasured/absent/empty energy), omits ModelStatus::reset() which adds {any}->not_evaluated (status.cpp:221-227; exercised at native/tests/research/test_status.cpp:88), and omits the throw paths — object(*energy) on non-object energy, channel_violations/real_or on malformed fields, energy_quality_ok on nonfinite members or scale==0 — all propagate before the staged commit at status.cpp:215-218 (atomicity preserved). observe() also admits negative time_s (only !isfinite is rejected, status.cpp:188) which from_wire's own threshold would then reject on restore (status.cpp:273) — writer/reader asymmetry. (b) `count >= 1` is FALSE: status.cpp:259 rejects only `count < 0`; a 0 count decodes and still makes counts non-empty, so as_wire model_valid=false at status.cpp:232. (c) `unique counts keys` is NOT enforced: status.cpp:257-261 emplace_backs every entry without dedup; exact() only size-checks (samples.cpp:95-103). Uniqueness is inherited only from parse_json's duplicate-key rejection (io/json.cpp:254, json.hpp:39) — a constructed bio::Value::Object or WireObject with duplicate keys IS admitted, e.g. via a fabricated RuntimeBootstrap (the ctor explicitly contemplates fabricated bootstraps, runtime.cpp:455-468). (d) `counts.empty() == !first.has_value()` is NOT enforced: status.cpp:265-276 validates first standalone (interval_id in [0,last_interval], time_s threshold, non-empty reasons); `{counts:{}, first:{interval_id:0,time_s:0.,reasons:["x"]}, last_interval:0}` decodes, and `counts:{a:1}, first:null` decodes too. (e) `result.counts.empty() when last_interval == -1` is NOT enforced anywhere: `{counts:{a:1}, first:null, last_interval:-1}` passes from_wire AND initialize_accounting, whose only model_status gate is `last_interval != -1` -> throw (runtime.cpp:580-583). (f) `initialize_accounting requires !monitor.first_failure.has_value()` is ABSENT: runtime.cpp:576 decodes the monitor, then lines 580-587 check history.last_id/last_end, model_status.last_interval, monitor.strict==config_.strict and measurement_decimation only; the PeriodAccounting ctor (accounting.cpp:431-454) checks energy baselines/config but never the monitor. No code anywhere enforces a clean monitor at t=0.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
All wire inputs are well-formed bike_recording.json.v1 documents (parser-legal) reaching ModelStatus::from_wire/ReferenceMonitor::from_wire through initialize_accounting (runtime.cpp:572-577). R1 — `state.runtime.monitor = {"strict": <config.strict>, "first_failure": [0.0, ["fabricated:reason"]]}` at t=0: from_wire accepts (status.cpp:96-105: finite nonneg time, non-empty violations) and no later check rejects it. Consequence: every subsequent real monitor.observe() hits `first_failure.has_value()` and no-ops (status.cpp:72-75) — the genuine first reference rejection is never latched AND warning_pending stays false, so take_warning() (status.cpp:78-83) returns nullopt forever: the non-strict diagnostic WARNING promised by RIDE.md:118-120 is silently swallowed, while snapshot()/render_frame() (runtime.cpp:869,882), the HUD banner (viewer/hud.cpp:143,152), golden comparison (golden/capture.cpp:327-328) and journal model_first_failure events report the fabrication. R2 — `state.runtime.model_status = {"counts": {"rear:tire_compression": 9223372036854775807}, "first": null, "last_interval": -1, "maximum_compression_fraction": 0.15, "maximum_linkage_error_m": 0.002, "numerically_valid": "not_evaluated", "calibration_status": "parameterized_unvalidated"}`: decodes (count<0 only) and passes initialize_accounting; the first real matching violation then throws overflow_error at status.cpp:201-202 inside the staged observe (accounting.cpp:739) -> flush_local restore() -> buffered raws retained -> every close/flush retry rethrows -> advance() permanently fails; reset() aborts inside flush_accounting (runtime.cpp:969) and would re-poison from bootstrap_accounting_state_ anyway (runtime.cpp:962-963). A milder `counts:{"x":0}` similarly passes and poisons model_valid=false from interval 0 with zero counted violations, publishing the impossible-via-observe combination model_valid=false + first_model_violation=null into every sample channel (accounting.cpp:741) and episode columns, making classify_outcome report model_invalid at row 0 (acceptance.cpp:197) and stop_on_model_violation truncate the research episode (research.cpp:260-264). R3 — `numerically_valid: false` (or true) at t=0 with last_interval=-1: admitted by the domain check (status.cpp:281-285); a fabricated false feeds the was_false latch so every subsequent measured verdict is forced false for the runtime lifetime and across reset(). R4 (function-level, needs constructed WireObject or last_interval>=0 doc): `{counts:{}, first:{interval_id:0, time_s:0., reasons:["x"]}, last_interval:0}` decodes, violating the claimed counts<->first biconditional; `{counts:{"a":1,"a":2}}` decodes, violating the claimed uniqueness.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No valid product scenario is wrongly blocked by the constraints that actually exist. The enforced invariants match the only legitimate bootstrap shape (tests/fixtures/research/delayed_bootstrap/state.json: step=0, history last_id/last_end=null, model_status counts={}, first=null, last_interval=-1, numerically_valid="not_evaluated", monitor={strict:false, first_failure:null}, energy initial_energy_j set / energy_scale_j=26.03>0). The sticky-false numerically_valid semantics and the include_energy/unmeasured-row mask are confirmed requirements: simulation-decisions.md item 32 records that the unmeasured row's hybrid residual must not feed the verdict (accounting.cpp:734-740 passes include_energy=measured; status.hpp:50-54 documents the oracle's masked observe), and RIDE.md:118-125 pins strict/diagnostic first-failure semantics the monitor implements. Strictly-advancing interval_id (status.cpp:188) is required by the WorkHistory contiguity check (samples.cpp:434-437) and async-prefix check (accounting.cpp:489-493); flush_local retries whole periods from a staged copy, so no valid retry needs re-observation of an interval. The exact 7-key schema and strict/decimation agreement checks reject only fabricated or mismatched docs. The claimed-but-missing checks (count>=1, unique keys, counts<->first biconditional, counts empty at last_interval==-1, !monitor.first_failure) would likewise block only documents no real capture emits — they are needed hardening, not harmful restrictions.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even where the statement held, corrupt runtime states remain admitted: (1) monitor.first_failure latched at t=0 passes every check and permanently corrupts reference evidence — fabricated failure reported as first (snapshot/HUD/golden/journal) while the real first rejection is neither latched nor warned in diagnostic mode (status.cpp:72-83). (2) Non-empty model_status.counts with last_interval=-1 admitted at t=0: model_valid=false forever within the episode and across reset() (bootstrap_accounting_state_ is reused verbatim, runtime.cpp:962-963); a count of 0 or int64 max is admitted (claim says >=1), the latter guaranteeing an unrecoverable overflow livelock. (3) numerically_valid ∈ {true,false} admitted at t=0 with zero evaluated intervals; a fabricated false is absorbed by the was_false latch so all later measured verdicts are forced false. (4) first.reasons and counts keys are never cross-validated against each other or the channel_violations vocabulary — inconsistent evidence (first names reasons absent from counts, or counts for reasons first never saw) decodes and publishes. (5) energy baselines: only initial_energy_j presence is checked (accounting.cpp:442-443); energy_scale_j=0 with zero works is admitted and makes energy_quality_ok throw 'nonzero scale' (status.cpp:166) on the first measured close — another guaranteed livelock. (6) observe() admits negative time_s and any interval_id > last_interval without time monotonicity; the resulting state then fails its own from_wire threshold on restore. (7) The lattice claim itself is incomplete over the object's mutators: reset() restores not_evaluated from any verdict, and a false verdict is absorbing only under observe — reachable recovery edges and throw semantics are unmodeled.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Split P10a into three contracts. (1) Verdict lattice: keep the stated evaluation-event transitions {not_evaluated->{true,false}, true->{true,false}, false->false} with the evaluated-iff clause verbatim from status.cpp:209-210; add explicit stutter on non-evaluation (include_energy=false or absent/empty energy object), add the mutator edge reset(): {any}->not_evaluated (status.cpp:221-227), and state that all evaluation throw paths (malformed energy object, nonfinite members, zero scale) leave the status unchanged because staging commits only at status.cpp:215-218. (2) Wire contract — replace the claimed invariants with the enforced ones: exact 7-key object; counts values are int64 with count >= 0 (NOT >= 1 — zero counts admitted and still poison model_valid); no key-uniqueness check inside counts (uniqueness is inherited only from the JSON parser, not from from_wire); last_interval >= -1; first, when present, requires interval_id in [0,last_interval], finite nonnegative time_s and non-empty reasons — but NO counts<->first consistency and NO counts-empty-at-last_interval==-1 requirement is enforced (flag both as open hardening gaps, together with reasons/counts vocabulary cross-validation). (3) Accounting boundary — actual initialize_accounting requirements: step==0 && data->time==0, history.last_id/last_end absent, model_status.last_interval==-1, monitor.strict==config_.strict, measurement_decimation agreement (runtime.cpp:578-587) plus PeriodAccounting's initial_energy_j presence check (accounting.cpp:442-443). Record as OPEN gaps the checks the statement claims: monitor.first_failure must be null, model_status.counts must be empty, and numerically_valid must be "not_evaluated" at a t=0 bootstrap — all currently unenforced and fabricable via parser-legal documents.
