# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P10b`: `ReferenceMonitor` Episode-Scoped Warn-Once & First-Failure Latch

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_confirmed_requirement`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Core contract verified end-to-end: ReferenceMonitor::observe latches first_failure and arms warning_pending=!strict only on the first non-empty-violation call (status.cpp:68-76), take_warning emits first_failure->message() once and clears the flag (status.cpp:78-83), strict mode raises InvalidReferenceRun only AFTER the whole period commits state_ including the latch (accounting.cpp:792-794,800-812), each reset() builds a fresh PeriodAccounting from the once-decoded bootstrap monitor so warn-once re-arms per episode (runtime.cpp:576-577,652-655,962-965), the warning flag is transported exactly once by take_publication's clear (accounting.cpp:485) and never lost by apply_publication's OR-merge (accounting.cpp:495) or by detach replay (accounting.cpp:522-537), and the EngineFailure path explicitly suppresses the trailing InvalidReferenceRun so the fatal engine error propagates after committing partial-period diagnostics (runtime.cpp:801-807; research.cpp:333-336 captures it as a cleanup note). Three reachable counterexamples remain: (A) initialize_accounting validates step==0, empty history and model_status.last_interval==-1 but never requires monitor.first_failure==null, so a bootstrap carrying a fabricated first_failure is admitted (runtime.cpp:576-585 vs capture.cpp:109-126 where the legitimate emitter always writes null) — the fabricated latch makes observe() no-op on the first REAL violation, yielding warn-never plus a wrong latch for the whole episode; (B) the reset-time warning lift is single-slot: when pending_warning_ is already occupied, the outgoing owner's undelivered warning_pending is destroyed with it (runtime.cpp:970-980), silently dropping that episode's only warning despite the comment's 'cannot silently drop' guarantee; (C) EngineFailure cleanup swallows ALL exceptions via catch(...) not just InvalidReferenceRun (runtime.cpp:806), wider than the stated exception class.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
A) Fabricated latch: hand-edit state.runtime.monitor in a bootstrap directory to {"strict":false,"first_failure":[1.0,["grip.pull"]]} — read_bootstrap() accepts it because the digest check covers only model.mjb (runtime.cpp:458-460) and the t=0 invariants at runtime.cpp:578-585 omit first_failure. Effect: monitor boots with a fake latched failure; status.cpp:72's engaged-optional guard makes the first real violation a no-op — no warning_pending is ever armed (from_wire leaves it false, status.cpp:90-107) and snapshot().first_failure reports a violation that never occurred. Works in strict mode too (the latch/report is wrong even though the raise still fires). B) Warning starvation: non-strict runtime; violation in episode 1 latches warning_pending; caller never calls take_pending_warning; reset() moves it into pending_warning_ (runtime.cpp:973-974); violation in episode 2 re-arms its fresh monitor's flag; second reset() skips the lift because pending_warning_ is occupied (runtime.cpp:973) and accounting_=std::move(accounting) at runtime.cpp:980 destroys the outgoing PeriodAccounting still holding warning_pending=true — episode 2's single warning is never emitted through take_pending_warning (runtime.cpp:942-954 drains the stale episode-1 message then episode-3's). Reachable by call order alone; the GUI viewer path never calls take_pending_warning at all (cli/ride.cpp:127-150 hooks; viewer/ride.cpp:153-156 surfaces only the first_failure banner). C) Repro outline for A: compose_runtime-style bootstrap mutation on the welded_floor fixture — decode a docs tree whose state.runtime.monitor.first_failure is non-null, observe one real violating interval, assert take_pending_warning()==nullopt and first_failure unchanged (no current test covers this; test_reference_monitor.cpp:33-44 and test_native_runtime_accounting.cpp:582-602 exercise only the in-memory paths).

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No valid product scenario is wrongly blocked. Warn-once-per-episode plus the sticky first_failure latch mirror the oracle's ReferenceMonitor.accept contract cited at test_reference_monitor.cpp:1-6 and status.hpp:1; strict mode's first-violation-stops-the-run is confirmed decision #30 (docs/agents/simulation-decisions.md:275-276) and RIDE.md:118-126 ('first model violation ends the run'; diagnostic policy prints the violation 'as a WARNING' and continues); RECORDING.md:55-60 requires diagnostic continuation to retain 'sticky invalid status and the original failure facts', which the latch supplies while per-interval evidence stays complete in period_violations, attachment_violations, samples and sealed episodes (accounting.cpp:761-765,830-844; research/lifecycle.cpp:175-182 journals model_first_failure from final_snapshot.first_failure even when the raise was suppressed). Warning-per-violation-class is intentionally absent — not a blocked need since every later violation is still recorded. The EngineFailure precedence matches RECORDING.md:73-75 (engine failure may leave a physical suffix; both extents saved).

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
(1) t=0 bootstrap with monitor.first_failure non-null is admitted (runtime.cpp:576-585) even though every legitimate emitter writes null (capture.cpp:109-110,126) — it plants a fabricated latch that suppresses the real first failure's latch AND warning for the entire episode; the parallel fields history.last_id/model_status.last_interval ARE pinned to empty/-1 (runtime.cpp:580-583), so this is a missing conjunct, not a principled acceptance. (2) warning_pending is not representable on the wire: as_wire emits only {strict,first_failure} (status.cpp:85-88) and exact() rejects any extra key (status.cpp:91-92; samples.cpp:95-103), so a 'latched-but-unwarned' state silently decodes as 'already warned' — currently exploitable only via the same fabricated document. (3) from_wire bounds first_failure.time_s only as finite>=0 (status.cpp:101) — arbitrarily large or arbitrarily small values unrelated to any interval are admitted, and violation strings are unchecked beyond non-empty (status.cpp:100-103). (4) A malformed later ReferenceFailure (nonfinite/negative time_s) throws std::invalid_argument even when the latch is already engaged (status.cpp:70-71), and because WorkHistory::add applies the same check first (samples.cpp:438-441) a corrupt RawStep wedges the buffered period permanently — every flush/advance retry deterministically rethrows a non-InvalidReferenceRun error that neither strict nor diagnostic policy can publish past. (5) monitor.first_failure and model_status.first are decoded with no cross-consistency check (runtime.cpp:574-583) — an admitted document may claim a reference rejection while asserting zero observed intervals.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Keep the episode-scoped warn-once + first-failure latch and the explicit EngineFailure precedence as stated, but tighten three edges: (1) add the missing t=0 conjunct — in NativeRideRuntime::initialize_accounting require bootstrap_accounting_state_.monitor.first_failure to be disengaged whenever history/model_status prove zero accounted intervals (extend the runtime.cpp:580-583 check), since the only legitimate emitter writes null (capture.cpp:109-126); (2) make the reset-time warning transport lossless — either hold a small FIFO of pending warnings instead of the single optional pending_warning_ (runtime.cpp:970-980,942-954) or explicitly document and test that an occupied slot drops the outgoing episode's undelivered warning, contradicting the current 'cannot silently drop' comment; (3) scope the EngineFailure cleanup suppression to the documented exception class — catch (const InvalidReferenceRun&) plus a comment for why other exceptions are also swallowed, or record the swallowed failure the way research.cpp:333-336 does via cleanup notes so a non-reference accounting error during fatal cleanup is not invisible. Optionally: persist warning_pending on the monitor wire (or assert !first_failure at t=0 inside from_wire) so the wire cannot encode a latched-but-unwarned state the in-memory FSM never produces.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P10c`: `PeriodAccounting` Rollback & `AsyncCloseRunner` Recovery Semantics

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `mixed_subclauses`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Clause-by-clause: (1) AsyncWorkerLost auto-degrade HOLDS. peek() throws AWL in exactly three places — out-of-order result (async_close.cpp:42-43), worker_lost_ (:47), finished_-with-pending (:55) — and collect() catches it into detach_async() (accounting.cpp:550-552); submit() throws AWL for stopping_/finished_/failure_ (:25-26) and submit_period's catch (:565-571) routes through collect(false)->detach->flush_local. (2) AsyncPublicationError surfaces without fallback and is not caught anywhere in PeriodAccounting (collect only catches AWL, :550). It recovers only via explicit detach_async() — but that recovery is nominal for the dominant failure class (deterministic eval failure): detach replays the same raws into an identically-seeded evaluator (accounting.cpp:522-534) which throws the same error, leaving detached_at_ unadvanced and the object permanently wedged. A transient worker escape (e.g. bad_alloc inside work()'s inner try, async_close.cpp:126-131) is also branded AsyncPublicationError (worker_lost_ stays false, :46-53), denying the AWL fallback for a failure that synchronous replay could plausibly survive — and no caller of detach_async exists outside accounting.cpp internals/tests, so NativeRideRuntime exposes no recovery handle at all. (3) detach_async() replay order/no-loss/no-dup HOLDS on the success path: pending_ is FIFO in submission order, detach_pending preserves it (:78-89), detached_at_++ only after apply_publication commits (:532-533), a strict-error period is consumed+applied before the InvalidReferenceRun throw (:529-534, mirroring collect's apply-ack-throw at :545-548), and ready-but-unacked worker results are discarded+replayed deterministically. HOWEVER the 'in order' guarantee is only caller-discipline: submit_period/close_period contain no detached_ check (:555-557, :576-580), unlike collect (:541) and flush (:583). (4) The restore() clause is FALSE as literally stated: restore() neither strips synthesized keys nor copies a pristine spine — it moves the merged channel object (carrying appended 'energy','attachment_violations','model_status', effort and add_detail keys) back into raw.channels (:658-671), which the code comment itself admits (:593-603 'Restored channels may carry merged-in keys that a copy would not have produced'). RawStep::channels is observably augmented after an abort; the protected invariant survives only via set() overwrite-in-place (samples.cpp:76-83) + deterministic re-append order, yielding byte-identical retried samples.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
REPRO A (claim-4 literal mechanism violation, always true in memory): any flush_local abort after accounting.cpp:711 leaves raw.channels carrying synthesized keys. Reachable abort points that hit the donor/restore path: dot() width mismatch at :756 on a force component whose name is outside the work/external folds (never dotted at :80/:146, so it survives validation through :711 and throws at :757); WorkHistory::add 'tires'/contiguity rejection at :760 (samples.cpp:431-456); model_status.observe throw at :739; bad_alloc at :742/:752/:755. restore() moves the merged object back (:659-670) — raw.channels keeps 'energy'(:716),'attachment_violations'(:719),detail keys(:720-733),'model_status'(:741). The effective invariant (byte-identical retry) nonetheless holds: every synthesized key is rewritten at its existing position on the next attempt, so samples are identical. No in-tree consumer reads buffered raw.channels outside flush_local (verified: state_wire counts sizes only :834-836; submit_period deep-copies :562-563).
REPRO B (claim-3 ordering/loss, public-API reachable): strict monitor + async_telemetry. close_period P1(ids 0-9, attachment_errors set) submits; close_period P2(ids 10-19) submits. detach_async() -> replay P1 -> evaluator.flush_local throws InvalidReferenceRun -> strict_error captured -> take_publication -> apply_publication commits P1 -> detached_at_=1 -> throw InvalidReferenceRun (:529-534). Caller catches (strict rejection is an expected signal — runtime.cpp catches it at :706/:729/:740), pushes raws 20-29 (PeriodBuffer accepts: next_interval_ survived :33-34), calls close_period() -> submit_period -> buffer nonempty, !async_ -> flush_local() commits ids 20-29 while ids 10-19 sit in detached_. Result: permanent interval GAP; every subsequent collect()/flush() -> detach_async -> evaluator.push(id=10) fails 'period buffer requires consecutive, continuous intervals' (period_buffer.cpp:23-25) or apply_publication's prefix check (accounting.cpp:490-493) -> P2 can never publish -> 'in order without loss' VIOLATED. In-tree immunity is incidental: NativeRideRuntime::advance_interval calls collect(false) (runtime.cpp:705) before every close_period (:727-728), which drains detached_ first — the class's own contract does not enforce it.
REPRO C (claim-2 nominal recovery / wedge): deterministic worker eval poison — RawStep with constraint_snapshot mismatch (flush_local :677-680), missing 'endpoint_mass' diagnostics (:624/:725), or nonfinite component power (dot :80 -> samples.cpp:112-113). Worker dies -> peek -> AsyncPublicationError (worker_lost_=false) -> detach_async -> replay throws identical invalid_argument (not caught at :529-530) -> detached_at_ stays 0, async_ already reset (:519) -> PERMANENT wedge: every collect/flush rethrows (has_raws() true forever, state_wire().buffered_intervals nonzero forever :834-843, seal_episode unreachable). Same wedge for transient bad_alloc worker escapes misclassified as publication errors.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
The no-auto-fallback rule for AsyncPublicationError is justified only for deterministic evaluation failures (replay is bit-identical, so fallback buys nothing); it wrongly blocks one valid scenario: a TRANSIENT worker-side resource failure — bad_alloc or thread error inside work()'s inner try (async_close.cpp:126-131) — is classified as an evaluation failure (failure_ set, worker_lost_ false) and denied the AsyncWorkerLost auto-detach that would recover the ride via synchronous replay (the worker's memory is freed at detach, so a memory-pressure failure often succeeds on retry). This is material under RetentionMode::full long recordings (RECORDING.md:324-326, measured 3.11 GB RSS) where worker OOM is plausible. Compounding it: detach_async has no caller outside accounting.cpp/tests — through NativeRideRuntime::advance/flush_accounting/seal_episode (runtime.cpp:705,739; research.cpp:333,512) an AsyncPublicationError is permanently terminal with no recovery surface, so the recoverable-transient case is a guaranteed dead ride. Secondary: the pending()>=2 transport bound (accounting.cpp:558-560) forces a blocking collect(true) inside submit_period, stalling the stepping thread on worker catch-up — a keep-physics-realtime/defer-accounting scenario is blocked, but this is the documented backpressure design and arguably intended.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even where the statement holds, these invalid states are admitted: (1) Permanently wedged detached_ — a non-strictly-poisoned replay (invalid_argument from evaluator.flush_local, uncaught at accounting.cpp:529-530) retains the period forever; has_raws() (:465-467) and buffered_intervals (:834-843) perpetually report unflushable evidence; every collect/flush/seal throws identically with no drop-the-poison API. (2) Out-of-order commit — submit_period/close_period have no detached_ guard (:555-557,:576-580): after a failed detach, a full buffer commits newer intervals ahead of retained older ones, producing a permanent history gap the prefix check then keeps unhealable (Repro B). (3) AsyncCloseRunner used standalone admits unbounded submission growth — the >=2 bound lives only in PeriodAccounting::submit_period (:560); submit() (:22-35) has no queue cap. (4) ~AsyncCloseRunner -> close() silently discards all unacknowledged originals in pending_/results_ (:20,:92-99) — a loss admitted by the resource-close contract (asserted intentional by test_resource_close_never_publishes_or_drains, test_async_close.cpp:255-277). (5) submit() brands ANY failure_ — including evaluation failures — as AsyncWorkerLost 'worker is unavailable' (:25-26), mislabeling the failure class at the transport surface; peek re-derives the correct class via worker_lost_ so it self-corrects, but the intermediate signal is wrong. (6) An idle-worker infrastructure loss (work() outer catch, :133-137) with empty pending_ is invisible to peek (:39 returns {} before checking failure_) — benign, admitted. (7) Theoretical only: a throwing ConstraintSnapshot move-ctor at :752-753 would leave raw.constraint_snapshot partially-moved and unrestored (snapshot==nullptr skips :666-667) — member moves are noexcept in practice, unreachable.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Split into three precise clauses. (a) Failure classes: 'peek() reports worker-evaluation escapes as AsyncPublicationError (nested original preserved) and transport/context loss — out-of-order results, worker_lost_, finished-with-pending — as AsyncWorkerLost; collect() catches only the latter into detach_async(); AsyncPublicationError propagates and leaves the failed job queued until explicit detach_async(). submit() conservatively reports any dead worker as AsyncWorkerLost and lets collect() re-classify. Note: transient worker resource escapes (e.g. bad_alloc) are classified as AsyncPublicationError, so recovery of a survivable transient failure requires the same explicit detach path; NativeRideRuntime currently exposes no such handle, making any AsyncPublicationError terminal through the production driver.' (b) Replay: 'detach_async() stops the worker, returns every unacknowledged period in submission order (pending_ FIFO, results never double-applied since apply precedes acknowledge), and replays each into a fresh evaluator seeded from the committed prefix; a replayed period is consumed only after apply_publication commits; a strict-rejection period is applied then rethrown; a non-strict replay failure preserves that period and blocks the queue, and while detached_ is non-empty submit_period()/close_period() MUST drain or reject before committing newer intervals — close_period currently admits the out-of-order commit (accounting.cpp:555-557); add the detached_at_<detached_.size() guard that collect() (:541) and flush() (:583) already enforce.' (c) Rollback: 'flush_local donates captures only inside try and restore() moves every donated field (channels, constraint_snapshot, qpos, qvel, components) back to its raw; the restored channels object may retain merged-in synthesized keys, which the deterministic set() puts rewrite at identical positions on retry, so a retried period publishes byte-identical samples — drop the incorrect strips-keys/spine-copy mechanism description.'

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P10d`: `SensorPipeline` Cursor & Delivery-Time Contract

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `holds`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_confirmed_requirement`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Exhaustive mutation audit of SensorState in native/src/runtime/sensors.cpp: the only writes to state_.cursor/samples_attempted are the paired increments at sensors.cpp:142-143 inside push() (placed after every throwing operation per the comment at :138 'Only append may allocate; commit scalar clocks/counters afterwards'), the whole-state replaces at :86 (reset clears to {}), :88 (reset failure restore) and :120 (import). import_state rejects cursor!=samples_attempted at :91 before any commit, so the equality holds unconditionally inside every SensorPipeline — but as an enforced gate, not an emergent property of the plain SensorState struct (decode_sensor_state at research_io.cpp:142-172 and unflatten_state at test_native_research_sensors.cpp:41-78 can build unequal values; import rejects them, verified at :241-244). delivery_time: read(now) rejects now < *delivery_time with a strict < at :148-149 before any mutation; NaN/Inf/negative now rejected by nonnegative() at :10-13 via :147; a read failing the >1e-12-future-source check at :158 leaves queue and clock untouched (comment :163); equal-time re-reads are non-strictly admitted and idempotent (frozen at test_native_research_sensors.cpp:122-128). All read()-call sites in research.cpp use monotone clocks: ctor read(0) at :96 and finish_window read(committed_time_s_) at :305 — physics time never decreases, so the gate is never even stressed inside an episode. Startup: ResearchEpisode ctor requires cursor==attempted==1, last_time==0, delivery_time==0, startup.time_s==0 at :92-94 before import_state at :95; startup_sensor_state produces exactly this shape via reset()+read(0) at native/src/research/rider_random.cpp:44-47; NATIVE_POLICIES.md:28 documents 'Startup acquires exactly one row ... starts the runtime with the cursor already at one'. consume_sample pushes only when source_step==next_sensor_step (research.cpp:204-210) preserving the cursor-step alignment; a skipped acquisition throws rather than misaligning the tape. Boundary/exception audit: push() throw points (:123-126,:135,:139) all precede counter commits; reset() restores the prior episode on failure (:85-88); NaN/Inf/tampered-state inputs are rejected with state bitwise preserved (test_native_research_sensors.cpp:220-250). No reachable sequence violates any of the three subclaims — every violating input is a designed rejection.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
No invariant-breaking counterexample found; nearest misses and boundary notes: (1) Empty tape: SensorPipeline(delivery, NoiseTape{}) + reset(raw) throws 'sensor noise tape exhausted; research run is invalid' (sensors.cpp:126-127) leaving cursor==0 — frozen at test_native_research_sensors.cpp:273-276. The 'cursor==1 after reset(initial)' subclaim silently assumes tape.noise.size()>=1; unreachable in research (tape sized 1+floor(max_steps/sensor_steps)>=1 at research.cpp:159-161) but reachable via the standalone pipeline API. (2) reset(initial) alone yields cursor==1 but delivery_time==nullopt — it does NOT satisfy the startup-import shape the property conflates it with: the episode gate (research.cpp:92-94) additionally requires *delivery_time==0, *last_time==0, attempted==1 and startup->time_s==0, which only the startup_sensor_state composite (reset+read(0), rider_random.cpp:44-47) establishes; a plain reset() is rejected at research.cpp:92-93 and at test_bootstrap.cpp:723-729 a default cursor-0 SensorState is likewise rejected. (3) 1e-12 future tolerance: read() delivers a queue sample with source_time_s up to ~1e-12 after 'now' (sensors.cpp:158,154,156), so the delivered row can carry source_time_s > time_s=now within tolerance — intentional FP-parity with validate() at sensors.cpp:36, not a violation. (4) Monotonicity is non-strict: read(same now) twice returns the identical row and leaves state untouched (strict < at :148; frozen at test_native_research_sensors.cpp:122-128) — 'monotonic' must be read as non-decreasing. (5) Enforced-rejection battery confirmed: duplicate-source push (:124), read(-1)/read(delivery-eps)/read(NaN) (:147-149), tampered cursor import (:91), exhausted tape (:126) — all throw preserving state.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No documented product scenario is wrongly blocked. Episodes always re-base to t=0: NATIVE_POLICIES.md:32 'Reset regenerates sensor delivery/noise and resets the policy with the chosen seed'; RIDE.md:162 reset seals the generation; every reset call site rebuilds tape+state through startup_sensor_state (lifecycle.cpp:126-138, policy_session.cpp:114-128, environment.cpp:61-67) so the cursor==1 gate accepts all genuine resets. Replay 'does not reuse controller or sensor state' (RIDE.md:289; RECORDING.md:262 verifies recorded rows), so the monotone-read gate and the cursor==attempted import gate block nothing the product needs. A hypothetical mid-episode sensor-state restore (startup cursor>1, last_time>0, delivery_time>0) is rejected at research.cpp:92-94, but no product scenario resumes mid-episode — NativeResearchRuntime::reset() replays the recorded t=0 pair (reset_tape_/reset_startup_, research.cpp:650-653). The one hard limitation — a captured bootstrap without raw_initial cannot be reseeded (lifecycle.cpp:133-134, policy_session.cpp:121-123) — is a stated product decision in NATIVE_POLICIES.md:32, not a wrongly blocked valid scenario. Marginal: the monotone gate forbids an in-pipeline retrospective 'what was deliverable at earlier t' probe, but that evidence is retained in episode observations (research.hpp:99-101) rather than re-queried from the pipeline.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
import_state (sensors.cpp:90-121) validates structural shape, never provenance or tape-consistency; the admitted state set is strictly larger than the genuinely reachable set: (1) Queue-vs-delivery backlog — queue rows are checked only against last_time and startup->source_time_s (sensors.cpp:112-119), never against delivery_time or config_.latency_s: {cursor=2, attempted=2, dropped=0, queue=[s@0.001,s@0.002], last_time=0.002, delivery_time=0.1} on a latency=0.001 pipeline is admitted although a genuine read(0.1) would have selected the newest eligible row and popped s@0.001 (:153-164 pops only pre-selection entries; the selected front is never removed). (2) Dropped counter vs tape — samples_dropped need only satisfy <=attempted (:92-93); a state claiming drops that tape_.dropout_uniform[0..cursor) contradicts (e.g., dropped=1 with all uniforms >= dropout_probability) is admitted — counters are trusted, never cross-verified. (3) Vanished non-dropped rows — {cursor=1, attempted=1, dropped=0, queue=empty, last_time=0, delivery_time=0} is admitted, but a non-dropped push permanently leaves >=1 queued row (push :139; pop_front :164 removes at most size-1 entries), so this is unreachable; read(0) then silently delivers the zero-invalid startup instead of the measured row — a divergence from identical tape+seed that no check catches. (4) startup.time_s unchecked — any zero-invalid sample with source<=last_time passes (:103-109); startup.time_s=99 admitted at pipeline level (blocked only at the episode gate research.cpp:93); queue rows' time_s likewise need only satisfy source<=time+1e-12 via validate (:113,:34-37) — delivery restamps anyway (:160), confining the impact to state_wire() exports. (5) Forged measurements — decode_sensor_state (research_io.cpp:142-172) -> make_research -> import_state admits queue rows with arbitrary finite values and valid=true: sensor readings that never passed through the noise tape or physics, deliverable by later reads; this is the inherent trust boundary of state_dict import kept for Python parity (test_native_research_sensors.cpp:17-20), not a defect, but it is an admitted corrupt-state class.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Tighten the statement to the actually enforced contract: (a) 'cursor == samples_attempted for every state held by a SensorPipeline' — maintained atomically by push() and enforced at the boundary by import_state rejection (sensors.cpp:91); the claim applies to pipeline-owned state, not to decoded SensorState values which may be inconsistent and are rejected. (b) 'delivery_time is non-decreasing across successful read(now) calls within an episode' — equal-time re-reads are admitted and idempotent; rejected reads (non-finite or negative now, now < delivery_time, read before reset, source > now+1e-12) leave state unchanged; reset() clears delivery_time to unset and import_state installs an arbitrary validated value — both are the sanctioned re-base paths. (c) 'After reset(initial) on a non-empty tape: cursor==samples_attempted==1, delivery_time unset; the episode-importable t=0 shape is produced only by the startup_sensor_state composite (reset+read(0)): additionally attempted==1, last_time==0, delivery_time==0, startup a zero-invalid sample, and the queue retaining the measured row iff it was not dropped — the shape ResearchEpisode requires (research.cpp:92-94).' The statement should also name the enforcement caveat: import_state is a structural gate and admits shape-consistent but semantically unreachable states (queue-vs-delivery backlog, dropped-counter-vs-tape mismatch, vanished non-dropped rows, arbitrary startup.time_s), so consumers must not treat an admitted SensorState as provenance-verified.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P11`: `NativeResearchRuntime` Control-Window Staging, Retry Idempotency & Exclusive Episode Outcome Machine

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `mixed_subclauses`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Sub-claims (a) and (c) hold; sub-claim (b) is violated on canonical, tested paths. (a) delivery_pending <=> pending_transition holds: both are set together in finish_window (research.cpp:318,322) inside a nonthrowing tail (moves into pre-reserved vectors, POD/string-move assigns), cleared together only in acknowledge_control (research.cpp:401-403), and reset replaces the whole episode (research.cpp:645). delivery_pending => window holds because acknowledge clears delivery_pending before window.reset. The count chain 0 <= completed <= requested <= completed+1 holds: ++requested_count (research.cpp:195) is the last statement in begin_control and every operation after the only throwing one (e.commands.emplace_back, research.cpp:192) is a nonthrowing POD copy, so begin_control is effectively atomic. The idempotent-retry conjunct holds only for valid arguments: advance_control validates budget/target_step (research.cpp:345-348) BEFORE the delivery_pending early return (research.cpp:350), so a retry carrying an invalid budget or a stale target_step (< committed_step_) throws invalid_argument instead of returning the staged transition. (b) is violated three independent ways, detailed below. (c) holds: advance_interval commits exactly one interval (step.hpp:301-308; committed_step_ = physical_->step() at runtime.cpp:719), so the loop at research.cpp:355 runs at most window->target_step - window->start_step physics steps; yields and failures only shorten it. (d) is only implicitly satisfied: consumed prefix vs committed_step_ is recoverable only via accounting.pending_intervals (research.cpp:442 -> accounting.cpp:842), and seal_episode flushes unjudged raws into retained_ (research.cpp:512-513) without consuming them.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
C1 (terminated && truncated coexisting, canonical path): handle_failure sets e.terminated = true unconditionally (research.cpp:326), then runs consume_samples(false) (research.cpp:335), where consume_sample's energy gate (research.cpp:251-257) or model gate (research.cpp:260-263) sets e.truncated = true. This fires deterministically on EVERY strict InvalidReferenceRun inside a window: flush_local commits pending_ and only then throws strict_failure (accounting.cpp:800-812); the violating interval's sample carries non-empty attachment_violations in channels (accounting.cpp:686,719), channel_violations appends all of them (status.cpp:149-152), model_valid = counts.empty() = false (status.cpp:232), and stop_on_model_violation is pinned equal to strict at research.cpp:118-120, so the model gate at research.cpp:260 fires during the post-termination fold. Final state: terminated=true && truncated=true with reason restored to invalid_controller at research.cpp:337 - a flag partition violation plus flag/reason incoherence (truncated flag with an error-family reason). Same collision via test_fail_at_step whenever a trailing sample breaches the energy or model gate, and whenever e.truncated was already set by an earlier consumed sample before a later failure (e.g. a strict rejection reaching handle_failure after a numerical_quality truncation). C2 (done() && stop_requested, the named defect - confirmed reachable): stop() latches e.stop_requested while a window is active (research.cpp:658); handle_failure clears window and delivery_pending but never stop_requested (research.cpp:324-340). After the failure the episode has done()=true AND stop_requested=true permanently: acknowledge_control is unreachable (throws at research.cpp:400 since delivery_pending=false) and stop() no-ops on done (research.cpp:657). This is the production drain path: research::drain_active calls environment.stop() then loops advance_control (research/lifecycle.cpp:47-56); an engine failure during that drain leaves the latch set, exposed via status().stop_requested (research.cpp:443) and inside the sealed evaluation (research.cpp:467). C3 (error iff and prefix clauses): set_error_text gates only on episode_->terminated && !episode_->delivery_pending (research.cpp:672) - it does not require an error-family reason, so set_error_text("x") on a finish or crash:* episode attaches a non-empty error to a non-error reason (breaks the iff), and on a simulation_error/invalid_controller episode it replaces the mandated RuntimeError:/InvalidReferenceRun: prefix with arbitrary text (breaks the prefix clause). C4 (post-done gate mutation in normal flow): after e.done() the while loop exits, but the post-loop block (research.cpp:379-382) still calls flush_accounting + consume_samples when has_raws() is true; has_raws() counts async_->pending()!=0 (accounting.cpp:465-468), and consume_samples consumes one further sample before the done-break at research.cpp:285 - its gates still mutate e.reason/e.truncated/e.numerically_valid/e.truth, so a finish can be relabeled numerical_quality or crash:*, or gain truncated alongside terminated. Reachable under async_telemetry (wired at research/environment.cpp:35; enabled since T42) whenever in-flight publications extend past the outcome interval, since publications are one sample per committed interval (accounting.cpp:802).

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No valid product scenario is wrongly blocked. The single-outstanding-window rule (begin_control rejects when window||delivery_pending, research.cpp:176) matches the documented 'a stop drains only an already accepted research control window' (docs/RECORDING.md:72-75) and PolicySession's own queueing layer (policy_session.cpp:39,148-159), so requested <= completed+1 costs nothing. done() => !stop_requested blocks nothing: stop() on a done episode is already a no-op (research.cpp:657). The error-iff blocks no legitimate use - attaching error text to a finish/crash episode is meaningless; the fix is to tighten the set_error_text gate to the error family, not to weaken the invariant. consume_samples(false) keeping all gates active during failure cleanup is intended behavior ('folds trailing committed samples' - it feeds torque/metrics integration, verified by test failed_sample_consumption_is_not_retried in test_monitor_strictness.cpp:422-467); the defect is only that terminal flags are not protected during the fold. The step bound (c) is required by the cooperative pacing loops that drive the machine with 8ms wall budgets (cli/research.cpp:95,100,108,121). The idempotent-retry conjunct is a confirmed requirement - the staging comment at research.cpp:311-312 ('Python boxing may fail and retry') and the double-ack test (test_bootstrap.cpp:553) pin it.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
S1 (unjudged sealed suffix): post-done published samples are never consumed (consume_samples breaks at research.cpp:285 and acknowledge_through only clears <= sample_cursor, research.cpp:288-291), yet they are already in retained_ (retention is independent of pending ack, accounting.cpp:497-510) and seal_episode flushes further unjudged raws in (research.cpp:512-513). The sealed artifact therefore contains committed intervals that never faced the research gates; evaluation still reports numerically_valid=true and reason=finish over them, and recording_extents (research/evidence.cpp:109-126) journals physical/accounted/control extents but no 'judged prefix' - only accounting.pending_intervals indirectly exposes the gap. docs/RECORDING.md:74-75 intentionally saves the physical suffix, but nothing marks which retained rows were actually gate-evaluated, so a replay-eligible (complete&&eligible, evidence.cpp:109-114) episode can carry never-judged residual breaches. S2 (mid-window seal admitted inconsistently): seal_episode has no window/delivery_pending guard (research.cpp:506-514) while legacy_snapshot rejects the same state (research.cpp:520-521); evidence_snapshot likewise admits it (research.cpp:471-480). A mid-window seal produces requested_count = completed_count+1 evidence - marked downstream via the complete gate, but the boundary itself admits it. S3 (policy failure silently dropped): fail_policy no-ops on a done() episode (research.cpp:664-668); mark_policy_failure only records a cleanup note when fail_policy throws (policy_session.cpp:69-72), so a policy error raised after episode end vanishes without error, reason or note. S4 (latent invariant trap): handle_failure and the advance_control catch-all clear window/delivery_pending but never pending_transition (research.cpp:338-339,389-390); the delivery_pending <=> pending_transition biconditional survives today only because finish_window's staging tail is nonthrowing and the early return at research.cpp:350 keeps pending transitions out of the throwing region - any future throwing operation between research.cpp:318 and :322 or in the failure paths silently breaks (a) with no guard. S5 (taxonomy incoherence): ResearchTransition::valid_for_learning excludes simulation_error and policy_error but not invalid_controller (research.hpp:58-60) - vacuous today since no transition is fabricated for failed windows, but an inconsistent treatment of the error-outcome family the property itself defines. S6 (unwind edge): if handle_failure itself throws mid-cleanup (e.g. allocation inside e.error assignment, research.cpp:330), the catch-all (research.cpp:387-391) leaves terminated=true with reason possibly unset/stale and error empty - an error-outcome state outside every declared partition, reachable only under allocation failure.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Keep (a) and (c) as stated, restate (b) and (d) as enforceable code: (1) In handle_failure, either clear e.stop_requested alongside e.window/e.delivery_pending (one-line fix for the named defect) or define and document a terminal override rule; also snapshot and restore the {terminated,truncated,reason} triple around consume_samples(false) the way e.reason is already restored at research.cpp:337, or run the fold in a mode that updates metrics/tracker/torque but may not mutate terminal flags/reason. (2) Tighten set_error_text's gate to reason in {simulation_error, invalid_controller, policy_error} and either forbid overwriting an existing prefixed error or re-apply the family prefix. (3) Specify the error-prefix rule per family: RuntimeError: for simulation_error, InvalidReferenceRun: for invalid_controller, and explicitly no required prefix for policy_error (fail_policy currently attaches the raw message). (4) Extract the 'clear pending delivery' tail (pending_transition.reset + delivery_pending=false + window.reset) into one private helper used by acknowledge_control, handle_failure and the catch-all, so the delivery_pending <=> pending_transition biconditional cannot silently rot. (5) For (d), export the consumed/judged boundary explicitly (e.g. sample_cursor or consumed_until_interval_id inside status_unlocked/evaluation) and state that sealed verdict fields (numerically_valid, model_valid, reason) quantify only the consumed prefix; either give seal_episode the same window/delivery admission guard as legacy_snapshot or document the intentional divergence. (6) Clarify the idempotent-retry contract's argument domain (identical transition guaranteed only for valid arguments; validation precedes replay) and align valid_for_learning's reason exclusions with the declared error-outcome family (include invalid_controller). (7) Decide whether flag-level exclusivity !(terminated && truncated) is the real contract versus the weaker 'exactly one terminal reason'; if flag exclusivity is intended, C1/C4 require gate-order or flag-freeze protection, not just reason restoration.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P12a`: `measure_run` Whole-Boundary Protocol & Termination Bound

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_engineering_contract`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Two conjuncts, different verdicts. (A) EXACTLY-ONCE TEARDOWN — HOLDS. In measure_run (native/src/runtime/realtime_measurement.cpp:159-165): on factory() non-null, close() is invoked at line 163 (catch-path) or line 164 (success-path) — mutually exclusive, exactly one call on every exit including unwind; if close() throws inside the catch the run exception is silently replaced but the call count is still one; null factory throws at line 160 with no driver (vacuously consistent). NativeRideRuntime::close() is guard-protected and idempotent (runtime.cpp:992-999: closed_.store, member resets), and ~NativeRideRuntime() = default (runtime.cpp:1002) never re-closes, so no double-close or double-free through ~unique_ptr. Residual weaknesses, not violations: close is not RAII-guarded (a future early `return` between 163/164 would skip it silently), and close() is not noexcept — a throwing close on the success path discards the fully computed report Value. (B) TERMINATION BOUND — QUANTITATIVELY WRONG AS STATED. The claimed envelope `max_wall_clock_s + one compute_slice_s + flush/export` undercounts measure_run's real wall time on every reachable path: (1) factory() runs INSIDE measure_run (line 159) -> NativeDriver ctor -> resolve_measurement_ride + bike::app::prepare + physical_assets + make_ride (realtime_driver.cpp:36-57) — arbitrarily long setup that executes BEFORE the cap is armed (cap baseline started_ is set only at driver.start(), measured() line 43 / ctor line 57). The formula contains no setup term. (2) The overshoot past the cap is not `one compute_slice_s`: the cap is checked only at advance() ENTRY (realtime_driver.cpp:82), and runtime_->advance enforces the 8 ms budget only BETWEEN intervals (runtime.cpp:792-795). The interval straddling expiry runs to completion — one full engine::step plus apply_forces/rider-contact solve (step.cpp:1719-1784), plus, when the interval closes a period, close_period -> submit_period -> `if (async_->pending() >= 2) collect(true)` (accounting.cpp:560,576-579) which BLOCKS the caller until the async worker finishes evaluating the oldest pending period (async_close.cpp:37-57) — a whole measurement_decimation-sized evaluation, unbounded relative to 8 ms. runtime.cpp:790 also runs `if (accounting_->full()) flush_accounting()` BEFORE the budget loop, outside it. True worst case: cap + compute_slice_s + one_interval_wall(+ one blocked period evaluation + one buffered-period drain). (3) summary() (line 68) and execution_identity() (line 72) execute inside measured() but outside `flush/export`; identity() SHA-256-hashes the entire MuJoCo shared library file per run (realtime_driver.cpp:30). (4) driver->close() (line 164) runs inside measure_run after measured() returns; accounting_.reset() -> ~AsyncCloseRunner -> close() -> worker_.join() (async_close.cpp:20,98) waits out any in-flight period evaluation — a real tail term the formula omits. (5) The harness enforces nothing itself: the `while (!driver.reason())` loop (line 45) relies entirely on the driver to implement the cap; a cap-free Driver runs unbounded — the stated assumption papers over a bound that is driver-delegated, not harness-guaranteed. (6) Clock-seam edges (assumption-gated): the cap reads the injected clock_ while runtime_->advance's budget uses real steady_clock (runtime.cpp:787,793); an injected clock returning NaN is swallowed — std::max(0., NaN) = 0 (realtime_driver.cpp:82) so the cap never trips — termination then falls back to per-advance progress (>=1 interval per call since elapsed~0 < budget), still finite but outside the stated bound.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
R1 (bound overshoot): `bike-measure-realtime --duration 30` where prepare/make_ride take T_setup (uncapped; e.g. seconds) — total = T_setup + 300 + 0.008 + T_interval + flush + sha256(dylib) + export + worker-join > `300 + 0.008 + flush + export` for ANY T_setup>0 — the formula is violated by construction even on the happiest path, since factory() executes inside measure_run before the cap arms (realtime_measurement.cpp:159 vs driver.start() at line 43). R2 (slice overrun): during the run, an advance_interval closing a period while the async queue holds >=2 periods blocks in collect(true) (accounting.cpp:560) for a complete worker evaluation — so a single 'compute slice' (one advance call) can cost >> compute_slice_s=8ms; a pathological contact step adds a further unbounded engine::step time. Repro shape: benchmark run on a slow/loaded host where the worker falls behind; chunk_wall_s will record a multi-second chunk — the bound's `+ one compute_slice_s` is exceeded by that chunk's excess. R3 (clock seam): measure_run(factory, dest, clock) with an injected clock returning NaN — `std::max(0., NaN)=0` at realtime_driver.cpp:82 -> cap never trips; loop still terminates via max_steps progress (each advance() does >=1 interval) but not within any wall bound; elapsed(clock(), chunk_start) at line 51 then throws `invalid_argument` (lines 15-20) -> captured as an 'advance' failure -> report emitted (terminates, but the bound claim fails). R4 (report-loss unwind): a Driver whose close() throws — on success path line 164 drops `result` entirely (no report written though run dir was exported); on the catch path line 163 the original exception is silently replaced — caller sees only close's error. Exactly-once preserved; evidence lost. R5 (asymmetric failure surface): driver.summary()/execution_identity()/boundary()-at-38 throw OUTSIDE the loop's try (lines 37-38, 68-77) -> propagates through measure_run -> close -> rethrow -> CLI exit 2 with NOTHING written for that run, while advance/flush/export exceptions are captured into errors[] and still produce a report (lines 55,58,82).

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
The statement is descriptive, not prescriptive — it blocks nothing itself. The closest product tension: the wall cap is hardcoded — RunLimits::for_track's default max_wall_clock_s=300 is used unconditionally (realtime_driver.cpp:49-50; run_limits.hpp:25) and Options (realtime_runner.hpp:9-18) exposes no override. Valid scenarios it constrains: (a) documented workflows run `bike-measure-realtime --duration 60` on extreme tracks (docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-15-launch-branch-completion.md:227; 2026-11-12-launch-4b-crank-hold-damping.md:852) and one plan wraps the CLI in `alarm 1800` — a 30-minute outer allowance — while the internal cap silently truncates at 300 s; on a slower host or sanitizer/instrumented build (ASan/RTSan presets in docs/TESTING.md, MallocScribble legs in docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-04-test-durations.md) the documented `--duration 30 --warmup 1 --runs 3 --benchmark` gate (docs/RIDE.md:305-312) can exceed 300 s wall and produce a truncated 'measurement' instead of the requested window. (b) The constraint does not even BLOCK these — it degrades them: the capped run is still emitted as measured_ok=true and exit code 0 (see sufficiency), so a valid long measurement is shortened without an error or a knob. There is no scenario the bound wrongly forbids outright; the gap is the missing configurability of the cap it depends on, and that truncation is admitted silently rather than flagged as a failed measurement.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Holding the statement admits several states that should arguably be rejected or at least distinguished: S1 — A wall_clock_cap-truncated run is accepted as a clean measurement: terminal_ is a reason, not an error, so errors stays empty and measured_ok = errors.empty() && steps>0 && wall>0 (realtime_measurement.cpp:100) plus valid_prefix (line 111) are TRUE; build_report folds its real_time_factor into median_real_time_factor (realtime_report.cpp:76-77,94) and counts it toward sampling_protocol_satisfied (line 92); all_measured stays true -> CLI exits 0 (realtime_command.cpp:63,108). Only `limitations` strings (lines 97-99), prefix.reason='wall_clock_cap' and steady_state 'non_duration_terminal'/'incomplete_requested_prefix' flags mark it — a watchdog-truncated prefix can satisfy the throughput gate. S2 — Bound violations are unobservable in the report: max_wall_clock_s appears NOWHERE in the emitted Value (comparison_key at realtime_measurement.cpp:130-137 embeds compute_slice_s but not the cap), and nothing compares chunk_wall_s/wall_clock_s against the cap or slice — an overrun (e.g. the collect(true) stall) lands silently in chunk_latency.max. S3 — Asymmetric evidence policy: advance/flush/export errors are captured into errors[] with the completed prefix exported (failure_keeps_prefix_and_closes_owner test, test_realtime_measurement.cpp:135-140), but settings()/comparison_key (line 37, throws with no source digest — line 129), boundary() at line 38, summary() (68), execution_identity() (72) and elapsed() at lines 60/83/103/104 throw OUTSIDE any capture -> close -> rethrow -> exit 2 with zero artifacts for that run. S4 — Corrupt driver data admitted unvalidated: the cap-tripping advance call pushes a ~0s chunk and a 0-step count (lines 51-52) into the percentile statistics; a driver whose boundary().step decreases yields negative chunk_completed_steps — never validated. S5 — Reason overwrite erases the cap: if errors are nonempty, prefix.reason is overwritten to 'invalid_controller'/'simulation_error' (lines 64-67) — a capped run that also failed flush reports 'simulation_error', losing wall_clock_cap from the classified prefix. S6 — A throwing close() on the success path silently discards the computed report (no run-*.json) while the exported run directory remains — an inconsistent on-disk state. S7 — The bound is fully driver-delegated: measure_run itself has no timeout; any Driver lacking the internal cap and never setting reason() spins the loop unbounded — the property holds only because NativeDriver happens to enforce it inside its own advance() (realtime_driver.cpp:78-88), a fact the statement's assumptions obscure.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Restate with the bound scoped and the slack terms honest: (1) 'The measured loop in measured() — from driver.start() to loop exit — terminates within max_wall_clock_s + the duration of one advance() call, where one call costs at most compute_slice_s of budgeted stepping plus one in-flight advance_interval (one physics step including its period-close, which may block on a pending async period evaluation via collect(true) and a pre-loop flush_accounting drain); no per-interval wall bound exists.' (2) 'measure_run's total wall additionally includes factory()/setup (the cap arms only at driver.start()), summary(), execution_identity() (which re-hashes the MuJoCo library), export_run(), and driver->close() (which may join the async worker); each tail term is finite but unquantified — the statement bounds liveness, not total duration.' (3) 'On every exit path after factory() returns non-null, Driver::close() is invoked exactly once (success and unwind); callers must treat close() as possibly throwing — a throwing close discards the completed report and, on the unwind path, replaces the original exception.' (4) 'Termination enforcement is delegated to the Driver implementation (the wall cap lives in NativeDriver::advance, realtime_driver.cpp:82); measure_run supplies only per-call compute_slice_s budget — drivers lacking the cap are outside the bound.' Recommended code-level hardening matching the contract: record max_wall_clock_s in the report (comparison_key or a limits field) so cap context is auditable; treat reason=='wall_clock_cap' as not-measured_ok or exclude it from median/eligibility; wrap the close call in a scope guard to make exactly-once robust to future early returns.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P12b`: Benchmark Valid-Prefix Isolation & Monitor-into-Model-Status Merge

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `holds`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_confirmed_requirement`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Probed every reachable path where ReferenceMonitor::observe could record a violation that ModelStatus::counts misses. The merge is structurally total: accounting.cpp:686-710 builds one `violations` vector (attachment_errors + per-sample budget violations + effort.violations + `energy.constraint_work` on the last row), accounting.cpp:719 mirrors it verbatim into channels["attachment_violations"] via strings(violations), accounting.cpp:739 calls staged.model_status.observe which merges that channel into `counts` verbatim (status.cpp:149-152 -> 195-208; model_valid = counts.empty() at status.cpp:232), and only then does accounting.cpp:761-765 feed the same vector to staged.monitor.observe. No violation producer bypasses the channel: ordering violations (interval_id <= last_interval, status.cpp:188) and non-finite time_s throw before monitor.observe ever runs; WorkHistory::add (samples.cpp:438-441, called at accounting.cpp:760) already rejects non-finite/negative end_time_s so the monitor's own time guard (status.cpp:70-71) is unreachable defensive code. Every throw between 719 and 763 discards BOTH planes via restore() (accounting.cpp:658-672, 795-798) so no divergence is observable. Unmeasured rows (include_energy=false) still merge attachment_violations. set() overwrites in place (samples.cpp:76-83) so restored/merged-in duplicate keys cannot shadow the fresh vector. Strict mode commits staged (including poisoned counts) BEFORE throwing InvalidReferenceRun (accounting.cpp:800 vs 812); the async worker likewise commits, transports strict_error, and apply_publication lands the poisoned state before collect() rethrows (async_close.cpp:116-120; accounting.cpp:545-548); detach_async replays synchronously with the same commit-then-throw order (accounting.cpp:526-534). INT64_MAX count overflow (status.cpp:201-202) aborts the whole period on both planes. Non-strict mode changes only strict_failure/warning_pending — counts stay permanently poisoned, matching RECORDING.md:56-58 ('Diagnostic continuation retains sticky invalid status') and RIDE.md:322 ('classified invalid prefixes remain diagnostic, never calibrated valid rides').

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
No reachable live-path sequence produces a monitor-only violation — the statement holds on flush_local/collect/detach_async in strict, non-strict, async and replay modes. Closest counterexample-class findings: (1) Decode-seam repro: a bootstrap document with state.runtime.monitor.first_failure=[0.0,['grip_left.pull']] + model_status.counts={} + last_interval=-1 is admitted by initialize_accounting (runtime.cpp:574-585) — strict flag and last_interval are checked but monitor.first_failure vs counts consistency is not — yielding a live runtime where a reference-monitor violation exists while model_valid=true; only a forged/edited document reaches it since a real t=0 snapshot cannot contain first_failure, so it is an admitted-invalid input, not a live divergence. (2) Vacuous-hold repro: config TOML `pedal_min_normal_n = nan` passes nonnegative() (runtime.cpp:592-595 checks <0 only, unlike optional_real:600-607 which enforces isfinite) — NaN attachment budgets make every comparison in attachment_violations() (period_buffer.cpp:107-128) false, so BOTH planes record zero violations on objectively unverifiable intervals; the inclusion holds but is vacuous.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
None found. Non-strict (diagnostic/--benchmark) mode exists to finish the measured window or produce diagnostic goldens (RIDE.md:308-325: strict would end at the first violation — grip pull at t~5.29 s on welded/rough_uphill_savage); the merge does not defeat that purpose — the run continues, real_time_factor/measured_ok are still reported, and only the validity classification stays tainted (realtime_measurement.cpp:88,98,111: valid = model_valid && numerically_valid; valid_prefix additionally requires errors.empty()). That taint is the confirmed product intent: RECORDING.md:56-58 'Diagnostic continuation retains sticky invalid status... does not make an invalid physical result valid for learning/replay'; RIDE.md:322 'never calibrated valid rides'; simulation-decisions 'Действует' 30 (strict stops at first model violation) and rejected alternative #3 (masking the startup energy.constraint_work violation via tolerance/diagnostic mode was explicitly refused). The monitor's violation vocabulary IS the applicability vocabulary (attachment budgets, effort budgets, controller feasibility, constraint work); arguable friction — `rider_controller.infeasible` and `energy.constraint_work` are solver/ledger artifacts that could have been classified under numerically_valid instead of model_valid — but that is a deliberate product taxonomy, not a wrongly-blocked scenario.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even with the merge intact, corrupt states are admitted: (1) Decode seam — forged bootstrap monitor.first_failure + empty counts + last_interval=-1 passes runtime.cpp:574-585 (no cross-check); converse counts-nonempty+last_interval=-1+first=null also admitted since only last_interval is gated at :582. (2) NaN budgets — nonnegative() (runtime.cpp:592-595) lacks isfinite, so NaN pedal_min_normal_n/foot_mu/saddle_mu/grip_pull_per_hand_n/link_max_gap_m/weld_*_shear_n render attachment_violations checks vacuous. (3) NaN effort inputs — observe_effort_impl (accounting.cpp:325-341) never calls finite() on `active`/`speed`; NaN actuator_force or qvel makes power/speed/strength comparisons false, yielding zero violations while committing NaN rider_active_delivered_nm/rider_positive_power_w channels; account_energy reads raw.components, not actuator_force, so nothing downstream catches it. (4) NaN channel scalars — real_or (status.cpp:26-33) reads doubles without number()'s finite check: NaN penetration_m evades tire_compression (status.cpp:130-131), NaN tire normal_load_n skips material_load_range (:121-122), NaN patch normal_load_n skips catch_plane (:136-137); patch-level NaN is later caught by WorkHistory::add (samples.cpp:449) but tire-level scalars are validated nowhere. (5) Provenance defects — `energy.constraint_work` is published under the attachment_violations key (accounting.cpp:710+719); first_model_violation records interval START time_s while monitor first_failure records end_time_s — the same violation carries divergent timestamps (accounting.cpp:739 vs 762); warning_pending and period_violations do not survive as_wire/from_wire (status.cpp:85-107 vs ReferenceMonitor fields).

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Keep the merge invariant but harden its seams: (1) extend initialize_accounting (runtime.cpp:574-585) to cross-validate the restored planes — reject monitor.first_failure when model_status.counts is empty and require first.interval_id consistency across both documents; (2) add std::isfinite to nonnegative() config reads (runtime.cpp:592-595) so NaN attachment budgets are rejected at decode like optional_real already does; (3) call finite() on `active` and `speed` inside observe_effort_impl (accounting.cpp:327-329) and route channel_violations' scalar reads through number() or an isfinite-checked real_or so NaN effort/tire evidence cannot silently evade flagging; (4) publish energy.constraint_work under its own channel key (e.g. accounting_violations) or rename attachment_violations to interval_violations so the monitor/model provenance label matches content; (5) unify the first-violation timebase (both planes should record the same bound, or document start-vs-end explicitly); (6) either persist warning_pending/period_violations through as_wire/from_wire or document their deliberate non-persistence. The core claim — monitor violations ⊆ model_status.counts via the attachment_violations channel — is verified load-bearing and should be retained as an explicit invariant with a decode-side check.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P12c`: Installed-Provider Non-Rescue & Provenance Gate

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `mixed_subclauses`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Sub-clause by sub-clause: (a) 'correctly-hashed but unmapped file fails loaded_provider_images' HOLDS — the glibc impl (native/src/runtime/provider_images.cpp:133-145) requires canonical(required) to be an element of the dl_iterate_phdr-collected canonical loaded set, and throws ProviderImageMismatch otherwise; the installed fallback re-runs loaded_provider_images(installed.required_images) at native/src/runtime/provenance.cpp:55 OUTSIDE the rescue try, so an unmapped installed file propagates the mismatch. (b) 'no escaping-symlink or wrong-backend provider silently rescued' HOLDS in the helper — installed_provider.cpp:47-48 rejects a symlinked lib/ dir, :62-66 rejects a symlinked or escaping candidate, :67-68 requires a regular file, :69 pins SHA-256, :34-37 pins NUMPY_OPENBLAS ILP64/ILP64, :57-60 requires positional equality with required_images, identity_kind=file and a 64-hex digest, :72-73 rejects duplicate resolutions; provenance.cpp:48 catches ONLY ProviderImageMismatch, so invalid_argument/runtime_error/system_error propagate with no rescue. (c) 'collect_provider_image is noexcept-tolerant' is VIOLATED — provider_images.cpp:114-122 performs allocating work inside a C callback: the const char*->path conversion and std::filesystem::canonical at :118-119, and the std::filesystem::path copy inside loaded->push_back at :120. A bad_alloc unwinds through dl_iterate_phdr (:131), a glibc C frame that holds GL(dl_load_lock) across callbacks, so the unwind skips the unlock and leaves the loader lock permanently held; the next dladdr (provenance.cpp:86 in runtime_identity) or dlopen then deadlocks. No noexcept and no try/catch exist. This is a known, still-open in-repo finding: docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-16-audit-fixes.md:169 item 7.8 ('exceptions must not pass through dl_iterate_phdr; check sizeofcmds and command length before copying uuid_command'). The same item's second half is also unfixed: image_uuid (:66-76) memcpy's a 24-byte uuid_command at :72 without checking command.cmdsize >= sizeof(uuid_command), and walks header->ncmds with no sizeofcmds bound — a truncated LC_UUID or corrupt header overreads past the load-command table (reachability limited because dyld pre-validates images). Additional boundary violation of fail-closed spirit: an EMPTY required_images vector makes the glibc impl return {} without throwing (:133-146 has no emptiness guard) — a corrupted bake passes vacuously; CMake guards it only at bake time (BikeProviderIdentity.cmake:60-62,73-76).

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
1) noexcept violation (reachable, nondeterministic): on a glibc Linux root build, call runtime::provider_images() under allocation pressure; std::filesystem::canonical's internal path building or loaded->push_back at provider_images.cpp:120 throws std::bad_alloc; the exception crosses dl_iterate_phdr while glibc holds GL(dl_load_lock); the lock is never released, so a subsequent engine::identity()/runtime_identity() dladdr hangs — worse than the intended clean failure. Static evidence: no noexcept/try/catch in collect_provider_image (:114-122); plan item 7.8 (docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-16-audit-fixes.md:169) records the identical defect as open. 2) Vacuity repro (deterministic): repo-wide search shows zero in-tree callers of runtime::provider_images() and runtime::numerics_provider_policy_json() outside their own definitions (native/src/runtime/provenance.cpp:63-77) — the only other references are the configure-time probe native/cmake/provider_identity_probe.cpp:30 and provenance.hpp decls; every production identity path (native/src/research/metadata.cpp:139-152,207-228; native/src/replay/rebuild.cpp:220-224; native/src/runtime/realtime_driver.cpp:25-41; native/src/initialization/setup_owner.cpp:230-237; native/src/validation/evidence.cpp:111) uses runtime_identity()+engine::identity(), where numeric_provider is the compile-time constant blas::provider_name (engine_call.cpp:524; platform/blas_numpy_openblas.cpp:45 'scipy-openblas'; platform/blas_accelerate.cpp:29 'accelerate') — a static label, not a loaded-image check. Therefore current_provider_identity() (provenance.cpp:42-58) and the whole installed-provider rescue are unreachable in shipped binaries, and the glibc collect_provider_image branch never executes anywhere: the probe is FATAL_ERROR'd to Darwin (native/cmake/BikeProviderIdentity.cmake:155-161) and native_installed_provider_tests (native/tests/test_installed_provider.cpp) exercises only the isolated helper with fixture dirs, never loaded_provider_images. 3) Positive evidence the held clauses work: test_installed_provider.cpp:115-117 rejects a correctly-hashed same-name file outside lib/; :122-135 rejects symlinked candidate and symlinked lib dir; :86-101 proves moved-tree rescue resolves only canonical adjacent paths.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No documented/qualified product scenario is wrongly blocked. The qualified install mode is exactly the relocatable bin/+lib/ tree this gate serves (docs/development/install-modes.md:12 'Native binary archive'; docs/development/linux.md install_smoke checks $ORIGIN/../lib), and test_installed_provider.cpp:97-101 proves a fully-moved tree still resolves. The strict clauses only reject unqualified deployments: (a) a lib/ symlink (stow-, Nix-store- or homebrew-style managed layouts) hard-fails at installed_provider.cpp:47-48 even when bytes are bit-identical — deliberate hardening beyond the documented layout, defensible but the single strictest clause; (b) an executable whose parent dir is not literally 'bin' (:44) rejects FHS-style /usr/bin + /usr/lib64 or /usr/lib/<triplet> system installs — no doc qualifies those; (c) partial installed sets are rejected because libraries must cover required_images exactly (:39-40), so a mixed '.deps-plus-installed' rescue is impossible — deliberate per installed_provider.hpp:19 'complete agreement'; (d) canonical-path identity rejects the same file reached through a different mount/hardlink spelling (canonical resolves symlinks, not mounts) and a provider whose dlpi_name is relative resolves against CWD, so a relative-LD_LIBRARY_PATH launch from a foreign CWD is refused — again unqualified per install_smoke's scrubbed loader env. Order-sensitivity (:57) cannot block a valid case because policy and required list are generated together and both are compile-time constants. macOS has no rescue path at all (BIKE_ROOT_INSTALL_NUMERICS is Linux-only, CMakeLists.txt:327-338), so no Darwin scenario is affected.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even granting the statement, these invalid states pass: (1) DOMINANT — the gate is never invoked: provider_images()/numerics_provider_policy_json() have no in-tree callers (see repro 2), so a missing, swapped, or wrong-backend provider produces no runtime failure in any shipped path; RIDE.md:86-88 promises 'a provider mismatch fails configure or launch', but no launch-time provider check exists — the recorded numeric_provider is the static blas::provider_name label and mujoco_library_sha256 covers only the MuJoCo image (engine_call.cpp:508-525). (2) Subset-only membership: loaded_provider_images asserts required ⊆ loaded; loaded ⊆ required is never checked, so a co-resident wrong-backend provider (distro OpenBLAS pulled in transitively, dlopen'd plugin BLAS, LD_PRELOAD shadow) is admitted while the provenance record reports only the required subset — symbol interposition can then bind a different implementation for unqualified calls. (3) Empty required list passes vacuously (no required.empty() guard in either impl). (4) Darwin membership is name-only: macho_uuid is REPORTED but never compared to a required value, so a same-path Accelerate image with a different LC_UUID (e.g. OS updated between configure and run) is admitted at runtime even though the recorded build_id pins the configure-time UUID. (5) Hash-vs-mapped-bytes TOCTOU: installed_provider.cpp:69 hashes disk bytes at call time while membership at provenance.cpp:55 only compares canonical paths; a same-path swap inside the window (or post-load replacement where the mapped inode is stale) is admitted because mapped bytes are never hashed — narrow, needs write access to the install tree. (6) A loaded image whose dlpi_name fails canonical() is silently skipped (provider_images.cpp:117-120) — acceptable fail-closed for required entries, but a deleted-post-load provider file is invisible in the loaded set.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Split P12c into an enforced correctness clause plus an explicit wiring and exception-safety clause. (a) Keep the fail-closed discovery contract as verified: required ⊆ loaded canonical paths; rescue only on ProviderImageMismatch via exact adjacent bin/../lib files that are non-symlink regular files matching the pinned SHA-256, backend NUMPY_OPENBLAS ILP64, identity_kind=file, complete positional agreement, no duplicates; second membership check propagates. (b) Add a REACHABILITY clause naming the boundary: at least one shipped identity surface (launch validation per RIDE.md:86, recording metadata, or replay check_execution) must invoke provider_images()/numerics_provider_policy_json(); currently both are uncalled in-tree so the gate is vacuous — until wired, production provider assurance is only the static blas::provider_name label plus the MuJoCo image hash (engine_call.cpp:508-525). (c) Exception-safety clause: collect_provider_image must be noexcept (or wrap its body in try/catch(...) returning 0 on any exception) because it crosses dl_iterate_phdr while glibc holds GL(dl_load_lock); preallocate or move-construct paths, and bound the Mach-O walk with sizeofcmds plus cmdsize >= sizeof(uuid_command) before the 24-byte memcpy (plan item 7.8). (d) Fail closed on required_images.empty() at both impls — a corrupted bake must not pass vacuously (CMake's guard is bake-time only). (e) State explicitly whether loaded ⊆ required (no extra co-resident provider) is in scope, and whether Darwin must compare the loaded LC_UUID against the configure-time build_id; today both are admitted. (f) Note that the glibc branch has no exerciser at all (probe is Darwin-only per BikeProviderIdentity.cmake:155-161; tests hit only the helper), so a contract should also name the test that must exercise collect_provider_image end-to-end.
