# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P13`: `SetupOwner` Multi-Stage Bootstrap Lifecycle, Setup-Only Crank Hold & Equilibrium Cache Atomicity

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `mixed_subclauses`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
(a) HOLDS. settle() is single-shot on completion: setup_owner.cpp:252 throws when `state.sealed || state.result` is set; `state.result` is only assigned at setup_owner.cpp:279 as the last statement, so a failed settle (EquilibriumFailure, finalize or prepare_research_startup throw) leaves the owner retryable but unsealable — retry still 'completes once'. seal_bootstrap() is confined by setup_owner.cpp:302-304: `impl_->sealed` blocks a second seal, and `!impl_->result || !impl_->context->completed || impl_->context->initializing` forces the full settle() path (context->initializing is only cleared at setup_owner.cpp:277, after finalize_initial_state at :274 and prepare_research_startup at :276). capture_initialized additionally re-checks t=0, !initializing, research_accounting_valid and a nonzero initial energy (capture.cpp:158-161), so sealing genuinely requires the finalized accounting. Readers: capture_initial_state (:284-285), capture_state_file (:289-291, plus settled_state_file optional), project_initial_stations (:296-297) all gate on `impl_->result && context->completed`; research_initial_state gates on impl_->research_startup (:334-337). All remain legal post-Sealed because seal does not clear result/completed and never advances physical.step_ (step_ is only incremented at runtime/step.cpp:2259 inside advance_body). metadata() has NO gate (:311-331) and is legal even pre-settle (returns equilibrium: Value{}) — the statement does not forbid this, but it weakens 'readers are legal from SettledFinalized onward' as a boundary: one reader is unconfined. (b) HOLDS EXACTLY. crank_hold_torque (settle_contacts.cpp:24-32) is TU-local and only reachable via apply_initial_forces (:34-63), which calls require_initializing — and require_initializing enforces BOTH conjuncts `context.initializing && context.physical.step()==0` (initial_pose.cpp:16-19) — plus `rider_present() && pedal_attachment=="spindle"` (:53). Call sites (settle loop, refine residual, finalize.cpp:85, initial_state.cpp:196 via restore's guard at initial_state.cpp:49) all run with step_==0 because step_ only advances in runtime advance_body (step.cpp:2259). prepare_research_startup passes `external=std::nullopt` (research_startup.cpp:60-66), so the hold cannot fire there, and no runtime path reaches the TU-local function. (c) MOSTLY HOLDS. refine_equilibrium's place() (refine.cpp:62-84) restores the entry StepState, DriveSnapshot, tire row and rider state+probe, rewrites qpos from q0 with root_x excluded from qids (:41-44), and zeroes qvel on EVERY residual evaluation including the final place(best) (:114). restore_equilibrium_state (equilibrium_cache.cpp:251-274) stages all file validation (settle_metadata, qpos, decode_material, integration, model rows) before install_material (:266) performs the first mutation; install_material itself stages prepare_state/validate_state before drive().restore inside mutate (:149-162). Gaps: (i) place() does not restore d->qacc_warmstart, d->act, d->time, d->qfrc_applied or d->ctrl — each trial's forward() warmstarts the constraint solver from the PREVIOUS trial's qacc, so the measured residual is not a pure function of the candidate; (ii) physical.restore() calls brake_.reset() (step.cpp:3061) — the incoming brake latch is deliberately dropped, not restored (documented, but the StepState round-trip is partial); (iii) install_material's post-drive commits are only asserted no-throw by comment (:151-152) — a throw from commit_state/assign_state (e.g. bad_alloc) would leave partially committed material. (d) PARTIALLY VIOLATED. Termination bounds hold: budget loop (equilibrium.cpp:120 with budget from :76-81), refine retries capped 8x80 evals (:130), fit_sag max_evaluations=40 (sag_fit.cpp:28), write_state_file 64-attempt rename loop (state_file.cpp:68-70). No post-seal cache write exists — save_equilibrium_cache is called only at equilibrium.cpp:140 inside settle_context, which requires require_initializing. BUT the two stated verification mechanisms are absent on the cache path: (1) load_equilibrium_cache (equilibrium_cache.cpp:242) does read_state_file + settle_metadata ONLY — it never reads the stored fingerprint_sha256 member; the binding is the filename (equilibrium_cache_path :237-240 returns fingerprint(context)+'.npz'). Contrast load_equilibrium_state (:244) which requires `find(state,"fingerprint_sha256")` and compares `fingerprint(state)` (state_file.cpp:75-81) to the expected context fingerprint. (2) The cache-hit branch (equilibrium.cpp:103) takes residual verbatim from the file (`state_array(*cached,"residual_qacc").doubles().front()`) after a bare stepper.forward() — no apply_initial_forces, no acceleration_residual() re-measurement, no `residual > options.tolerance` rejection — while the explicit branch (:102) does all three and throws. The loop guards (`!cache_hit` at :120 and :133) ensure the stored value escapes every check. So the literal claim 'verifies the stored fingerprint_sha256 member and re-measures residual on cache hits like the explicit path' is false.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
REPRO 1 (claim d-ii): craft a schema-1 StateFile at equilibrium_cache_path(context) — the canonical fingerprint is a deterministic sha256 over public inputs (config/specs/masses/rider/track/start_x/options/provider_identity/model_sha256 + compiled arrays, equilibrium_cache.cpp:163-204) — containing a well-formed qpos + state_* material payload, steps=1, residual_qacc = 10*options.tolerance, and metadata.provider_identity/model_sha256 copied from a real run (both are observable). settle_metadata passes (steps>0, residual finite >=0, provider/model match, :50-58); restore_equilibrium_state installs the pose; the cache branch assigns residual=10*tolerance and cache_hit=true (equilibrium.cpp:103); the `!cache_hit` guards skip the settle loop and the EquilibriumFailure throw; settle() proceeds to capture_state + finalize + seal — a bootstrap is sealed on an unverified above-tolerance residual. The identical file passed via physics.equilibrium_state_file is REJECTED at equilibrium.cpp:102 (`restored residual exceeds tolerance`) — proving the asymmetry. REPRO 2 (claim d-i + sufficiency): place a schema-0 (legacy NPZ) file at the canonical cache name. settle_metadata skips provider_identity/model_sha256 for schema_version==0 (equilibrium_cache.cpp:54), the fingerprint_sha256 member is never read, and decode_material accepts the legacy state_drive_*_boundary/flat arrays (:84-144). Result: a file with NO identity binding at all is installed and its stored residual trusted — on the explicit path the same file would need a matching fingerprint_sha256 member (:244). REPRO 3 (sufficiency): an honest-format cache file whose stored fingerprint_sha256 member disagrees with its filename (renamed foreign file) is served — the member is never compared. REPRO 4 (sufficiency, initial_state path): a transferred PhysicalInitialState whose contract_sha256 matches but whose pose no longer equilibrates on the target (contract permits timestep/road-mesh divergence, initial_state.cpp:51-93,131) is installed and sealed — the re-measured residual is only RECORDED in initial_state_target_residual_qacc (initial_state.cpp:197), never compared to options.tolerance. Additionally context.equilibrium_options is bound only inside settle_context (equilibrium.cpp:88), which the initial_state path never calls — so settle():271's capture_state computes the stored fingerprint_sha256 under DEFAULT options (context.hpp:57), writing a mis-keyed certificate. REPRO 5 (claim c, minor): refine trials leak qacc_warmstart across evaluations — trial N's solver warmstart is trial N-1's qacc (place() never resets it), so a poorly-converging constraint solve can report a residual that depends on evaluation order, not just the candidate.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No valid product scenario is wrongly blocked. (1) settle() retry after ANY failure is legal because state.result is assigned last (setup_owner.cpp:279) — recovery from EquilibriumFailure/finalize errors is preserved. (2) All readers remain legal post-Sealed, which the research flow requires: bike-research consumes research_initial_state() and metadata() after prepare() seals (app/prepare.cpp:12-15, physical_session.cpp:4-9). (3) The single-shot rule does not block sag fitting: build_equilibrium_evaluator creates a fresh SetupOwner per candidate (sag_evaluator.cpp:9-19). (4) The crank-hold confinement matches the confirmed product decision — 'the crank is held by an elastic torque toward rider_launch.start_pedal_angle_rad while the pose is searched, and the hold is removed before the ride' (simulation-decisions.md:299-302), spindle-only (:310-314). (5) metadata() pre-settle is allowed and harmless (returns empty equilibrium). (6) If the claim's residual re-measure on cache hits were enforced, honest cache hits would pay only one apply_initial_forces+forward+residual pass — cheap; it would only reject the corrupt entries a verification contract should reject. No necessity counterexample found.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even where the statement literally holds, these invalid states are still admitted: (1) A cache file whose stored residual_qacc exceeds options.tolerance is certified and sealed — the cache branch never re-measures and never compares to tolerance (equilibrium.cpp:103 vs :102); the EquilibriumFailure throw at :133 is guarded by !cache_hit. result.steps/residual_qacc are whatever the file claims (bounded only to steps>0, finite residual>=0 by settle_metadata). (2) A schema-0 legacy NPZ at a canonical cache name bypasses ALL identity checks — provider_identity/model_sha256 are gated on schema_version==1 (equilibrium_cache.cpp:54-58) and the fingerprint member is never read on the cache path (:242). (3) A cache file whose stored fingerprint_sha256 member is absent or contradicts the filename is served — no code reads the member on this path; the explicit path's defense (:244) is absent. (4) Initial-state transfers: the re-measured target residual is informational only (initial_state.cpp:197) — a pose that fails to equilibrate under the target's (different-timestep) dynamics seals anyway; and the settled_state_file's fingerprint_sha256 is computed under unbound default equilibrium_options (equilibrium.cpp:88 never runs on this path; context.hpp:57) — a mis-keyed certificate. (5) Refine's trial datum is incomplete: d->qacc_warmstart, d->act, d->time, d->qfrc_applied, d->ctrl are not restored (refine.cpp:62-84), giving cross-trial solver coupling; brake_.reset() drops rather than restores the latch (step.cpp:3061). (6) context->completed is set inside settle_context (equilibrium.cpp:141) BEFORE finalize/research — an owner whose finalize throws carries completed=true+equilibrium set on a still-initializing context; owner readers stay gated by impl_->result so nothing leaks externally, but the context flag over-reports. (7) settle() and the settle loop write d->time/d->qvel directly outside stepper.mutate (setup_owner.cpp:267, equilibrium.cpp:122, refine.cpp:79-83) — outside the engine-failure poison boundary the codebase declares. (8) prepare_research_startup mutates d->qfrc_applied/d->ctrl/brake friction bounds and intent_signals_/rider_senses_ AFTER finalize but before seal (research_startup.cpp:61-81) — the sealed StepState/friction rows reflect the research probe (e.g. brake_.apply(m,0,0) un-brakes what finalize's initial_front/rear_brake set), not the finalized settle state.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Keep (a)-(c) with two clarifications, and rewrite (d)'s verification clause to match the actual mechanism: (a) add that metadata() is intentionally ungated (legal in any state, returning an empty equilibrium record pre-settle), and that settle() remains retryable after failure — 'completes once' refers to completion, not invocation. (c) state the restore datum precisely: every trial restores StepState (minus brake_latch, which is deliberately reset per step.cpp:3059-3061), DriveSnapshot, tire/rider material, qpos(q0 with root_x pinned) and qvel=0; d->qacc_warmstart, d->act, d->time, d->qfrc_applied and d->ctrl are NOT part of the datum and carry across trials — document this as accepted cross-trial coupling or extend the restore to the full integration state (engine::get_state mjSTATE_INTEGRATION pattern from equilibrium_cache.cpp:232/258). (d) replace the false verification claims with the true contract: 'the cache path is keyed by the canonical fingerprint FILENAME (equilibrium_cache_path); on a hit load_equilibrium_cache runs settle_metadata validation only — it does NOT verify the stored fingerprint_sha256 member and does NOT re-measure residual or compare it to tolerance; the stored certificate is trusted. The explicit equilibrium_state_file path additionally verifies the member fingerprint and re-measures + gates residual vs options.tolerance.' Then either (i) fix the code to match the stronger claim — add the member-fingerprint comparison and the apply_initial_forces+forward+acceleration_residual+tolerance check to the cache branch (equilibrium.cpp:103) — which would upgrade the property to a verified contract, or (ii) keep the code and weaken the claim while documenting the trust boundary: cache files are only as trustworthy as write access to the cache directory, and schema-0 payloads at canonical names bypass provider/model identity checks entirely (consider rejecting schema-0 on the canonical cache path). Separately: gate the initial-state transfer's re-measured residual (initial_state.cpp:197) or document it as intentionally informational, and bind context.equilibrium_options before capture_state on that path so the recorded fingerprint_sha256 is keyed to the actual request.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P14a`: Single `act` Per Accepted Control Window

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `holds`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_confirmed_requirement`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Attacked the single-act invariant along every leg named in the statement. Mechanism: Policy::act is invoked at exactly one site, native/src/research/policy_session.cpp:200, gated by !s.pending() (line 185), !paused/!done (187) and !s.staged (190). The computed command is staged at line 206 BEFORE begin_control at 219 and cleared only on (a) successful acceptance at 220, (b) reset_now at 133, (c) apply(stop) at 140. (1) Budget/target yields: advance_pending returns nullopt via environment->advance_control yields at native/src/runtime/research.cpp:356-359; e.window->control stays latched (364) and act is never re-entered — holds (asserted by tests: test_native_research_policy.cpp:60,108-111; test_lifecycle.cpp:114). (2) Staged begin_control retry: after a begin_control throw, s.staged is retained; the retry skips act (190) and re-issues begin_control with identical brakes (191-192). Holds because begin_control is transactional: e.window is assigned last at research.cpp:196, and after e.commands.emplace_back (192) every remaining statement (requested.push_back into capacity pre-reserved by reserve_next at 189, last_requested/requested_count/window assigns of all-value types) is non-throwing — so a throw can only precede any publication. (3) Mid-window failure: advance_control catch -> handle_failure (324-340) clears window+delivery_pending and terminates; session pending() becomes false but done() blocks re-act — holds. (4) Completed-window re-entry: s.completed (228-233) plus completed_acknowledged (234-237) gate; pending() (52-56) keeps begin_advance closed until collection. Holds. (5) Accessor-mediated sequences via session.environment() (mutable ref, policy_session.cpp:180): a direct environment().begin_control(C,0,0) creates an accepted window consuming ZERO session acts; session.advance_pending then completes and acks it and returns its transition (research.cpp:341-406). Literal 'at most one act per accepted window' still holds (0 <= 1), but the session collected a transition for an act it never issued. (6) Stale staged delivery: after a failed begin_control leaves s.staged, a direct environment().reset() (allowed by reset_unlocked, research.cpp:629-630, since no window is active) creates a new episode while s.staged keeps the act computed for the OLD episode/seed; the next session.begin_advance() delivers that stale act as the new episode's first window — still 1 act for that window, but the act<->observation correspondence is broken. (7) Wording edge: when a RiderBehavior is installed, begin_advance also calls rider_behavior->act at lines 202-203 — literally TWO act invocations per accepted window; the property evidently scopes 'act' to Policy::act. (8) actuator_delay_s > control_period_s (unbounded: research.cpp:111-113 only bounds delay_steps below): e.commands is never cleared at finish/acknowledge (360-363, 396-406), so within one accepted window the applied motor torque can derive from >=2 different acts' queued commands; the act-call count itself is unaffected.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
No reachable sequence produces MORE than one act per accepted control window; the literal property holds. Closest breaks (each preserves the count but violates the act<->window correspondence or FSM health): R1 (foreign window): session.environment().begin_control(C,0,0) with session idle -> e.window accepted (research.cpp:176-196); session.advance_pending() drives it, acks it and returns its transition to the session caller (policy_session.cpp:228-242) — an accepted window whose requested/applied journals record a command no act produced. R2 (stale staged act across reset): begin_advance -> act -> s.staged set (206) -> begin_control throws (e.g. allocation in commands.emplace_back, research.cpp:192); then session.environment().reset() — permitted because e.window/delivery_pending are unset (research.cpp:629-630) — creates a fresh episode while s.staged retains the old act and policy->reset is never invoked (start_policy is session-side only, policy_session.cpp:73-85); session.begin_advance() then delivers the stale act into the new episode (190-219). R3 (permanent wedge, pure session API): on a captured bootstrap with config.raw_initial == nullopt, queue session.reset("999") during a window (policy_session.cpp:265-268 accepts it — validate_seed checks format only); when the window completes, apply_queued -> apply(reset) -> reset_now throws 'cannot be reseeded' at policy_session.cpp:121-122; the deque entry is never popped (154-158 pops only after apply returns), s.completed stays set and completed_acknowledged stays true — every subsequent advance_pending rethrows at the same point, the completed transition is never returned, pending() stays true forever (52-56), and close() throws 'finish the accepted policy interval before close' (308). No session-level escape exists.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
Nothing the act-count property itself blocks is a confirmed valid scenario. The contract is load-bearing for the deterministic replay loop: replay/session.cpp:220-238 re-issues each recorded requested command and compares transitions/observations 1:1, and evidence.cpp:105-112 requires requested_count == completed_count for replay eligibility; re-acting a window would desynchronize a stateful/stochastic policy from its recorded command. Adjacent necessities the property does not cover: (a) the staged-retry leg hard-locks the original brakes (policy_session.cpp:190-192) — an operator brake change between a failed begin_control and the retry cannot take effect on that window, and there is no non-destructive 'discard staged and re-act' path: the only ways to clear s.staged are apply(stop) -> truncated 'operator_stop' (140, research.cpp:654-660) or reset -> full episode restart (133). For a wedged staged command (e.g. one that can never pass env-side validation — see S6) the session is unrecoverable except by destroying the episode. (b) A mid-window re-act for long control periods is intentionally excluded; the documented answer is a shorter control_period_s (environment.cpp:41). Both are consistent with the product docs — 'a stop drains only an already accepted research control window; it never asks a policy for an extra action' (RECORDING.md:73) and the viewer contract 'reset must seal the old episode BEFORE resetting and stop may drain only an already-issued research interval' (viewer/session.hpp:21-24) — so these are defensible engineering constraints, not wrongly blocked scenarios.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
The property holds while corrupt/invalid states remain reachable: S1 (undrainable completion): a queued reset that can never apply (reset_now throws at policy_session.cpp:121-122 for a raw_initial-less captured bootstrap) wedges the session — completed retained, pending() true forever, close() blocked (305-308); the completed transition can never be collected. S2 (at-least-once operator apply): apply_queued (238) precedes clearing s.completed (239-241); a throw inside apply — record's events.push_back (104-107) or any later reset_now/start_policy failure — leaves the deque entry un-popped and it is re-applied on the next advance_pending: double environment->stop, double full reset_now including before_reset/after_reset hooks, so ResearchRun::before_reset (lifecycle.cpp:86-91) seals an extra phantom episode receipt. S3 (silent act drop): a computed-but-unaccepted act is discarded without any journal entry when apply(stop) clears staged (140), reset_now clears it (133), or close() runs while staged (pending() ignores staged at 52-56); a stateful policy's side effects advanced but no window carries it and nothing records the gap. S4 (mutable environment() handle, policy_session.cpp:180-181): direct begin_control creates a 0-act window the session then collects as normal (R1); direct reset() preserves s.staged so a stale act lands in a new episode (R2); direct stop() truncates while staged is retained. The session publishes a handle that can violate its own invariant. S5 (misclassified failures): mark_policy_failure labels every throw inside the begin_advance try (194-208) as policy_error/terminated — including environment->snapshot() (203) throwing 'native research operation already in progress' under concurrent env access, SensorObservation::from_wire/sample_wire::required failures (195-199) on malformed env state, and rider_behavior->act failures (202-203) that are not motor-policy faults. S6 (validation divergence): begin_advance validates the merged control with hardcoded articulated_planar=true (control_validate_for(command, true), line 205) while begin_control revalidates with the real rider_present()/drive_mode() (research.cpp:181); a posture-bearing command from rider_behavior on a rider_present()==false environment passes session validation, is staged, and then is rejected at begin_control on every retry — a permanent staged wedge escapable only via episode-destroying stop/reset. S7 (stale post-failure residue): handle_failure (research.cpp:324-340) clears window/delivery_pending but leaves e.pending_transition and e.stop_requested stale — unreachable today, fragile residue. S8 (delay>period torque provenance): with actuator_delay_s > control_period_s the applied motor torque inside one accepted window derives from several different acts — allowed by the wording only if 'delivered' means 'act calls', not 'applied control'.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Keep the core clause but tighten scope and coverage: (1) define 'act' as the session-level Policy::act invocation at policy_session.cpp:200, explicitly excluding RiderBehavior::act (202-203) and wrapped base-policy calls inside TractionControlPolicy (policy.hpp:45-57). (2) Define 'delivered' as the control accepted via NativeResearchRuntime::begin_control for that window (research.cpp:172-197); clarify that delayed motor application (e.commands drain at 360-363, which can span windows when actuator_delay_s > control_period_s) is a separate provenance question. (3) Add a correspondence clause: the act delivered to an accepted window must have been computed against the observation sampled at that window's start within the same episode — this closes the stale-staged-act-after-direct-reset (R2) and foreign-window (R1) gaps admitted through session.environment(); either restrict the mutable accessor to read-only/diagnostic calls while a session owns window state, or reconcile s.staged/s.completed against env-side state on entry. (4) Add a disposition clause for unaccepted acts: dropping a staged command via stop/reset/close is currently silent (140, 133, 305-318); require an operator_events or run_metadata note when a computed act is discarded. (5) Add a queue-safety clause: operator requests that can never apply (e.g. a reseed on a raw_initial-less bootstrap, reset_now:121-122) must be rejected at request time, and apply_queued must be effectively atomic or the failure must drain the entry — otherwise a permanent wedge holds the completed transition hostage (228-242, 52-56). (6) Optionally pin that queued operator actions are applied at-least-once today and decide whether exactly-once is required.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P14b`: Throwing `apply` Consumes Command & Releases Transition

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_engineering_contract`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
PolicySession::Impl::apply_queued (native/src/research/policy_session.cpp:154-159) is `while (!queued.empty()) { apply(queued.front()); queued.pop_front(); }` with no try/catch: on any throw the command is NOT consumed. In advance_pending (policy_session.cpp:225-243), apply_queued() at :238 runs AFTER acknowledge_control() at :235 but BEFORE `s.completed.reset()` at :240, so a drain throw leaves `s.completed` engaged and `completed_acknowledged=true`; pending() at :52-56 short-circuits `if (completed) return true` -> pending() stays true forever. No `operator_apply_failed` kind exists anywhere in the tree (grep: 0 matches) and no fault state is entered. Reachable throws inside apply (policy_session.cpp:137-147): (a) reset_now :122 `throw std::invalid_argument` when config.raw_initial is null and chosen != seed_decimal — decode_research_config (native/src/runtime/research_io.cpp:179-237) never sets raw_initial/seed_decimal, so a PolicySession over make_research(read_research_bootstrap(dir)) hits it deterministically; (b) before_reset/after_reset hooks (:126, :134) — ResearchRun::before_reset throws `cannot reset a finalized research launch` (native/src/research/lifecycle.cpp:87, registered at :69); (c) start_policy -> policy->reset/metadata (:135, :73-85) — PluginPolicy::reset invokes external code and throws PolicyFailure (native/src/research/plugin_policy.cpp:76-80, 102-104); (d) environment->reset -> reset_unlocked throws (native/src/runtime/research.cpp:624->167, :629-632, :644); (e) record() (:146 -> :86-108): status() at :88 throws when env is closed, events.push_back :104 bad_alloc / reserve_next length_error (research.cpp:33). Boundary inputs cannot reach the drain (set_brakes validates at :264, reset validates seed at :266, before queueing), so all drain throws are env/policy/hook/allocation-origin. Aftermath: every subsequent advance_pending rethrows on the same front command (deterministic wedge for cases a-c); close() throws `finish the accepted policy interval before close` (:308); reset() cannot clear the queue (:267 requires !pending()); run_until/advance propagate (:246-259); begin_advance is blocked by pending() (:185). The session is unrecoverable via the public API except seal_episode() (:282-299, no pending check) and environment().close() bypass. Worse, retry is non-idempotent: reset_now re-runs environment->reset (research.cpp:644-648 replaces episode_ / bumps generation), re-fires hooks, and re-wipes the operator journal via events.clear() (:132) on every retry.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
REPRO-1 (deterministic, capture bootstrap): `auto env = runtime::make_research(runtime::read_research_bootstrap(dir)); PolicySession s(std::move(env), policy); s.begin_advance(); s.reset("1");` — request() queues because pending() is true (:148-153); `s.advance_pending()` completes the window, acknowledges it (env window/delivery_pending/pending_transition cleared at research.cpp:400-405, stop_requested consumed), then drain -> apply(reset) -> reset_now -> policy_session.cpp:121-123 throws std::invalid_argument since raw_initial is null and "1" != default seed "0". `s.pending()` remains true (:52-53); every further advance_pending/close throws identically — permanent wedge. REPRO-2 (any env, plugin policy failing second reset): `s.begin_advance(); s.reset(); s.advance_pending()` -> environment->reset succeeds (research.cpp:644-648 destroys the episode incl. requested/applied/transitions history), events.clear()/config update run (:129-133), start_policy -> policy->reset throws PolicyFailure (plugin_policy.cpp:102-104) -> mark_policy_failure -> environment->fail_policy sets terminated/policy_error (research.cpp:661-668; window already cleared so the :663 guard passes). Net invalid state: env episode terminated policy_error AND replaced by a fresh reset generation, session journals wiped, yet `s.completed` retained -> pending()==true forever; begin_advance later throws `cannot start a policy interval while paused or ended` (:187) once completed is somehow released — session/env permanently divergent. REPRO-3 (recorded run): after `run.finish()` (finalization_attempted_), `s.begin_advance(); s.reset("1"); s.advance_pending()` -> drain -> before_reset hook -> lifecycle.cpp:87 `cannot reset a finalized research launch` -> wedge. Test evidence of the gap: native/tests/research/test_lifecycle.cpp exercises queued_reset (:135-151, success), ctor-time reset failure (:184-201) and an advance_control throw via test_fail_at_step (:211-226 — `handle_failure` research.cpp:324-340 correctly clears window+delivery_pending so `!session.pending()` holds, asserting the SIBLING failure path is handled), but no test covers a throwing apply inside the drain.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
Weak. No product doc (docs/RIDE.md:247-250 `requests take effect at the external control boundary`; docs/RECORDING.md; docs/agents/simulation-decisions.md) requires retain-and-retry, and the current retention is not a coherent retry design — it is benign only for idempotent commands (pause/brakes/stop re-apply harmlessly) and actively harmful for reset (re-fires hooks, re-resets the physical runtime, re-clears events). The genuine tension: (1) consume-and-release forfeits transient-failure recovery — a bad_alloc in record()'s events.push_back (:104) would drop an otherwise-appliable operator command under P14b, where today's retention would succeed on retry; the codebase otherwise deliberately favors retry-tolerant staging (`Allocation failure here keeps the already computed command` :217-218, cursor-ack discipline in prepare_applied_since research.cpp:529-555). (2) The statement's `releases the completed transition` is ambiguous: the exception unwinds past the return, so `s.completed` can only be cleared, not delivered — and for capture_mode off/verify `keeps_history` is false (research.cpp:29-31) so finish_window never copied it to e.transitions (:314-317); a compliant drop-release would destroy the SOLE copy of the transition data (observation/truth/contact_state/validity), blocking the valid `deliver every completed transition` scenario those modes still rely on via the advance_pending return value.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even a fully compliant consume+release+journal admits: (1) half-applied reset — reset_now mutates env (episode_ replaced, generation bumped, research.cpp:642-648), config (:129-131) and session state (:132-133: events/brake_events cleared, paused/front/rear/intervention/staged/launch_metadata reset) BEFORE the throwing after_reset/start_policy; P14b leaves pending()==false with a stale un-reset policy object, a wiped journal, and possibly an env already terminated policy_error — session `healthy` per the flag but unusable. (2) Journal provenance loss — the operator_requested event recorded at request time (:150) is destroyed by events.clear() at :132 when the throwing command is reset and the throw lands after env reset, so a subsequent `operator_apply_failed` entry references a request whose record no longer exists. (3) Queue tail unspecified — commands queued behind the throwing one are stranded: after release, the next advance_pending throws `begin_advance is required` (:229) since pending() is false, so their fate (drain? drop? journal?) is undefined. (4) The remedy journal itself can throw — record() calls status() (:88) and push_back (:104); an operator_apply_failed write hit by bad_alloc or a closed env needs a noexcept fallback the statement does not specify (note_cleanup :57-68 is the existing pattern). (5) Public environment() accessor (:180-181) lets callers mutate the env around session invariants: session.environment().reset() succeeds post-ack (research.cpp:629 guard passes since ack cleared window/delivery_pending) while s.completed is still held — a stale transition from a DESTROYED episode is later delivered into the new generation; environment().close() makes pending() itself throw. (6) Reentrancy — set_reset_hooks (:302-304) callbacks may call session.advance_pending() inside the drain: the inner call observes completed+acknowledged, drains the queue, and returns the transition to the wrong frame, then the outer advance_pending returns nullopt for a completed window — corruption P14b does not address. (7) Cross-cutting: env stop_requested is already consumed at ack (research.cpp:404-405) before the drain, so a compliant consume of a later-throwing command leaves env truncated operator_stop while the journal claims the operator request failed — divergent operator intent.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Rewrite as a three-part drain contract: (1) Exception progress — apply_queued must consume the throwing command (pop before apply, or a scope-guard pop) and advance_pending must release the completed transition on throw so pending() becomes false; the released transition must be DELIVERED, not dropped — either returned on the next advance_pending call (retain it in a fault slot while clearing the pending flag), or journaled env-side for all capture modes (off/verify currently lose the sole copy since keeps_history is full|legacy only, research.cpp:29-31). (2) Diagnostics — journal `operator_apply_failed` (applied=false, values carrying error text and the command) with a noexcept fallback into cleanup_notes when record() itself fails; preserve the operator_requested/event pairing by journaling the failure BEFORE reset_now's events.clear() or by carrying the request record into the new journal. (3) Explicit decisions to pin: whether deterministic apply failures terminate the episode (recommend yes, uniformly via fail_policy with a distinct reason such as `operator_error`/`policy_error`, matching the existing start_policy precedent policy_session.cpp:69-85 -> research.cpp:661-668 rather than today's split brain where only policy-reset throws terminate); the fate of the queue tail (recommend draining remaining commands as `operator_dropped` with applied=false, or retaining them only when the fault state is cleared); a fault/session-terminal state covering partial application (env already reset + stale policy + wiped journals: begin_advance/apply reject, pending()==false, seal_episode/close remain legal); and a reentrancy guard or documented prohibition for reset hooks calling advance_pending (contrast with the already-handled advance_control failure at research.cpp:384-393/test_lifecycle.cpp:211-226).

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P14c`: Post-Terminal Reset Journaled With Success Truth

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `mixed_subclauses`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
The statement is violated on two reachable axes. (A) `applied` is computed as `!terminal_before` (native/src/research/policy_session.cpp:138,146), i.e. the PRE-action episode terminal flag, not the reset's own success. A reset applied to a terminal episode — the dominant product flow (viewer R-key after done: native/src/viewer/research.cpp:20; drain-then-reset in drain_active/run_until; direct session.reset() on a done() session) — successfully creates the successor episode yet is journaled `operator_after_terminal`/`applied=false`, not `operator_applied`/`applied=true`. The parenthetical exception names only post-terminal stop, but apply() applies the same rule to every action. (B) A reset that creates the successor but throws during post-reset unwinding journals NO apply row at all: reset_now() runs environment->reset() (policy_session.cpp:127-128), then events.clear() (:132 — erasing even the operator_requested row written by request() at :150), then after_reset() (:134) and start_policy() (:135). A throw in ResearchRun::new_episode (lifecycle.cpp:92-95, e.g. events_.push_back bad_alloc or environment_->status() throw on a poisoned runtime) or in policy->reset (plugin ABI reset returning an error, native/src/research/plugin_policy.cpp:102-104 → invoke throws; also any builtin/user Policy::reset) propagates out of apply() before record() at :146. Result: successor ResearchEpisode installed and possibly marked terminated ('policy_error' via fail_policy), journal empty — a reset that created a successor episode is journaled nowhere, contradicting 'is journaled ... in the new episode' absolutely.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
REPRO 1 (post-terminal success journaled applied=false): prepare_environment(duration .12) → PolicySession(make_research(bootstrap), policy) → ResearchRun(session, publication) → loop session.advance() until done() (truncated 'duration') → session.reset("1"). pending() is false (terminated → active=false, delivery_pending=false after acknowledge_control at research.cpp:396-406), so request() applies directly: terminal_before=true → reset_now succeeds → record("operator_after_terminal", applied=false) at policy_session.cpp:146. Session journal for episode N+1 contains {kind=operator_after_terminal, applied=false, terminal=false, processed_step=0}; statement requires operator_applied/applied=true. The queued twin: session.begin_advance(); session.reset(seed) queues operator_requested(queued=true) in episode N; advance_pending() → apply_queued (:154-159) applies the reset after the terminal transition is acknowledged — identical applied=false row in N+1. Only a mid-episode (non-terminal) reset yields applied=true — the path exercised by native/tests/research/test_lifecycle.cpp:135-151 (queued_reset, reset during a live window). REPRO 2 (successor created, reset unjournaled): install a plugin Policy whose reset callback fails on the second invocation (plugin_policy.cpp:102-104 forwards ABI errors through invoke → throw), or force bad_alloc inside ResearchRun::new_episode's event() push_back (lifecycle.cpp:81-84). Call session.reset() on any episode: environment->reset() installs the successor ResearchEpisode (:127-128), events.clear() wipes the session journal including the just-written operator_requested(reset) row (:150 → :132), episode_started may be emitted (:134 → lifecycle.cpp:94), then start_policy's policy->reset throws (:135 → :73-85, marking the new episode 'policy_error' via fail_policy, research.cpp:661-669). Exception exits apply() before record() → new episode journal has no reset row. A queued variant is worse: the reset stays front of `queued` (pop happens after apply, :156-157), so the next advance_pending() retries the whole reset — before_reset() seals the half-created successor (zero-step episode, seal_reason 'reset') and burns another episode_id; each retry can manufacture sealed zero-step episodes whose creation cause is absent from the journal. REPRO 3 (misattribution window): environment->reset() succeeds (:127-128) then `config.seed_decimal = chosen` (:129, std::string assignment) throws bad_alloc before events.clear() (:132) — successor exists, the OLD episode's unsealed journal rows survive and are stamped with the NEW episode_id at the next seal (lifecycle.cpp:171-197), plus no reset row.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
Under a literal reading, the constraint wrongly condemns the documented primary reset flow. NATIVE_POLICIES.md:96 defines the journal as 'Requested/applied/after-terminal operator actions ... a separate ordered lifecycle journal', and RECORDING.md:70 / RIDE.md:162 make 'reset seals the outgoing generation, then creates the successor' the product's reset semantics — including reset-after-terminal (viewer R after done, RIDE.md:162; headless restart). Requiring operator_applied/applied=true for every successor-creating reset flags this intended flow as a violation. The 'applied reflects the action's own success' model also conflicts with the implemented semantic applied=!terminal_before ('applied to a live episode'), under which post-terminal reset — the one action whose purpose is to run after terminal — must legitimately journal applied=false exactly as post-terminal stop does; the parenthetical should cover reset, not just stop. Note the constraint does NOT wrongly block mid-episode reset: a live-episode reset genuinely is journaled operator_applied/applied=true in the successor episode.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even where the statement holds, the journal admits corrupt/incomplete states. (1) Hybrid truth inside one row: kind/applied come from PRE-action status (:138,:146) while terminal/processed_step/time_s come from POST-action status (:88-89,:105-106) — a post-terminal reset row reads {kind='operator_after_terminal', terminal=false, processed_step=0, time_s=0}, an 'after_terminal' row stamped non-terminal; the briefing's 'post-state truth' assumption is not what the code records. (2) Successor episode with no creation cause: the unwind window above leaves a journaled episode_started and possibly a 'policy_error' termination but no reset request/apply row — the journal cannot attribute the episode's existence or its seed. (3) Retried queued resets can seal phantom zero-step episodes (one per retry) with no operator rows. (4) Sync reset's operator_requested row is erased by events.clear() (:132) — preserved only because ResearchRun's before_reset seal (lifecycle.cpp:86-91,141-200) snapshots session operator_events mid-call; without a ResearchRun the apply row has no matching request row — an applied action nobody requested. (5) record() early-returns for capture modes other than full/legacy (:87) — a successful reset is unjournaled; the statement silently assumes capture scope. (6) DB projection maps applied→applied_step (research_publication.cpp:34-35): post-terminal reset emits applied_step=NULL and a mid-episode reset emits applied_step=0 — neither names the episode/step the reset terminated (that truth lives only in the outgoing episode's operator_requested + episode_ended(reason)). (7) journal.emit ordering puts episode_started (wall time t1) before the reset apply row (t2>t1) that caused it — causal inversion in the received_wall_s-sorted journal (lifecycle.cpp:183-185). (8) environment-only ResearchRun::reset (lifecycle.cpp:110-140) creates a successor with no operator row at all — outside PolicySession scope but shows the property does not cover all reset paths. (9) database_validate.cpp:157 checks only event identity/clock fields; kind/terminal consistency is unconstrained, so a misattributed or hybrid row passes validation.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Revise to match the implemented and documented semantics: 'A reset apply row is journaled in the SUCCESSOR episode's operator journal (post events.clear(), stamped with the new episode_id at next seal); its kind/applied are computed from the PRE-action episode status — operator_applied/applied=true when applied to a live episode, operator_after_terminal/applied=false when applied to a terminal episode (post-terminal actions, reset included, follow the same after-terminal convention as stop; applied denotes “applied to a live episode”, not the action's own success). The operator_requested row remains in the outgoing episode's journal. A reset that throws during reset_now — including after environment->reset() already installed the successor (after_reset hook or policy reset failure) — journals no apply row; this successor-without-reset-row window should either be closed (journaled best-effort before unwinding continues, or a reset_failed row) or explicitly accepted as a documented journal gap.'

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P14d`: `PlaybackClock` Wiring Status (Dead-Code Disposition)

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_engineering_contract`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
The statement is a disjunction and NEITHER disjunct holds at commit 0502660d. (a) Wired: PlaybackClock (declared native/src/research/policy_session.hpp:58-76, defined native/src/research/lifecycle.cpp:263-292, compiled into production bike_app via native/cmake/NativePhysicsResearch.cmake:48) has ZERO production call sites — a tree-wide grep finds it only in tests and docs. The actual research-viewer pacing path is viewer::Pacer: constructed at native/src/viewer/ride.cpp:29, polled at ride.cpp:104, debited at ride.cpp:121; the research hook native/src/viewer/research.cpp:11-15 feeds Pacer's target into PolicySession::advance_pending. The headless research loop (native/src/cli/research.cpp:114-124) and PolicySession::run_until (policy_session.cpp:250-260) pace nothing at all. (b) Marked parity-dead: there is no annotation, comment, or marker anywhere near policy_session.hpp:58 or lifecycle.cpp:263; the class sits unmarked in the public research header. Worse, docs/NATIVE_POLICIES.md:94 affirmatively describes PlaybackClock as a LIVE component ('PlaybackClock converts wall time to completed-physics-step debt with pause/reset rebasing and scales 1, 2, 4 and 8; the debt cap does not shorten a physical control window') — attributing to dead code the exact behavior production viewer::Pacer performs (native/src/viewer/pacer.cpp:34-52). The documentation does not merely fail to mark it dead; it asserts the opposite.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
Repro 1 (dead code): `grep -rn PlaybackClock native/src` returns only the declaration (policy_session.hpp:58) and definition (lifecycle.cpp:263) — no call site exists; every reachable pacing sequence goes through viewer::Pacer. Repro 2 (doc contradiction): docs/NATIVE_POLICIES.md:94 names PlaybackClock as the wall-to-step-debt converter, yet the only code path that converts wall time to step debt in production is Pacer::poll/completed (pacer.cpp:34-58) driven from ride.cpp:104/121. Repro 3 (divergent duplicate, reachable if ever wired): PlaybackClock caps accumulated debt at max(.05*scale, timestep_s) (lifecycle.cpp:286) — a bounded 50 ms catch-up window that silently DISCARDS debt under sustained lag — while Pacer keeps unbounded debt and only caps each poll's offer at 1e6 steps (pacer.cpp:41,49-51). Under a 1 s compute stall at 1x, PlaybackClock would drop ~950 ms of step debt; Pacer repays all of it. Repro 4 (backward wall time): PlaybackClock clamps elapsed = max(0, now - wall_s_) (lifecycle.cpp:284), tolerating a regressing clock; Pacer throws std::invalid_argument (pacer.cpp:37). Repro 5 (step regression): after a viewer reset, Pacer is re-based on generation change (ride.cpp:97,136); a naive PlaybackClock wiring that forgets rebase hits 'completed step moved backwards; rebase after reset' (lifecycle.cpp:281) at runtime — a crash path the property's 'replacing or unifying' clause does not address.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No documented product scenario requires a second pacing implementation, so the constraint blocks nothing real: RIDE.md:223-228 defines the production playback-debt contract and viewer::Pacer satisfies it; RIDE.md:238 states 'bike-research remains headless and has no playback-scale switch' (confirmed at cli/research.cpp:114-124), and PolicySession::run_until deliberately runs flat-out (policy_session.cpp:250-260). However, the statement's first disjunct is defective as a remedy: 'replacing or unifying with viewer::Pacer' without requiring semantic reconciliation would import PlaybackClock's 50 ms*scale debt cap (lifecycle.cpp:286), which discards catch-up work — in tension with simulation-decisions.md:266-267 (decision 28: the viewer window runs 'no slower than real time and without skipping steps'), the very guarantee Pacer's unbounded debt preserves. The property also assumes the class is the right unit of disposition while its own 'seven-test suite' bundles a test of a different unit: test_speed_keys_do_not_reuse_physical_controls (test_playback_clock.cpp:57-66) exercises production viewer::speed_for_action/action_for_key (input.cpp:7-42), not PlaybackClock — a viewer-input contract that must survive regardless of the class's fate.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
The disjunction admits several invalid states. (1) 'Explicitly marked parity-dead' admits a code comment while docs/NATIVE_POLICIES.md:94 continues to present PlaybackClock as the component that paces research sessions — doc-code contradiction fully admitted. (2) 'Replacing or unifying' admits per-mode wiring: research viewer on PlaybackClock (bounded 50 ms catch-up) while physical/replay/inspection viewers stay on Pacer (unbounded debt) — identical F6/F7/F8 speed keys (input.cpp:18-20,34) producing divergent catch-up behavior across modes. (3) 'Unifying' admits a hybrid where backward-clock handling silently changes (Pacer throws, PlaybackClock clamps to zero) or where the debt-cap semantics regress decision-28 pacing. (4) The final clause is internally incoherent for the delete path: 'dropped from evidence expectations' plus 'its seven-test suite remains the behavioral contract either way' — a deleted class cannot keep a compiling suite; relocation is unaddressed. (5) The property undercounts the real coverage: beyond the seven test_playback_clock.cpp functions (Phase3Ports.cmake:39-40), an eighth consumer exists in clock_contract (tests/research/test_lifecycle.cpp:91-106), so 'the seven-test suite' is an incomplete behavioral-contract inventory. (6) Even with a parity-dead mark, the class remains compiled and exported from production bike_app — dead code shipped in the binary is admitted.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Rewrite as an enforceable disposition: 'PlaybackClock is currently unwired dead code retained from the Python parity port. Before evidence sign-off it must be either (a) deleted, with its pacing-debt tests folded into the viewer::Pacer suite and test_speed_keys_do_not_reuse_physical_controls relocated to the viewer-input suite, or (b) adopted as the single pacing engine used by viewer::run for ALL modes (physical, research, checked_replay, inspection), after explicitly reconciling its max(.05*scale, dt) debt cap, double-typed clock with backward-time clamp, int-typed scale domain, and rebase-on-step-regression requirement against viewer::Pacer semantics, the RIDE.md playback-debt contract, and simulation-decisions decision 28. While it remains dead, the declaration must carry an explicit parity-dead annotation and docs/NATIVE_POLICIES.md:94 must be corrected to attribute live pacing to viewer::Pacer. The behavioral-contract inventory is eight test sites (test_playback_clock.cpp's seven functions plus tests/research/test_lifecycle.cpp clock_contract), not seven.'

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P15`: `ResearchRun` Multi-Episode Finalization, `PluginPolicy` ABI v1 RAII Order, `TractionControl` & `PhysicalLiveCsv` Schema Safety

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `mixed_subclauses`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
(a) Core mechanism is implemented and tested: the PolicySession owner installs reset hooks (lifecycle.cpp:69) and Impl::reset_now calls before_reset (seal) at policy_session.cpp:126 strictly before environment->reset at :127-128 and after_reset (new_episode) at :134; before_reset rejects post-finalization resets outside draining_ (lifecycle.cpp:87); finish() short-circuits on path_ (:203) and retries failed publications against already-committed receipts (seal early-return :144-145), all covered by test_lifecycle.cpp:124-133,168-169. HOWEVER the literal claim 'seals outgoing episodes before EVERY plant reset' fails on both owner paths: NativeResearchRuntime exposes public reset()/reset(tape,...) (research.hpp:167-171) with no hook seam, so the environment-owner ctor (lifecycle.cpp:71-76) cannot intercept a direct environment_->reset(), and on the session path the public non-const PolicySession::environment() accessor (policy_session.hpp:44) admits session.environment().reset() bypassing the installed hooks, Impl::reset_now, and the operator journal. Under the stated assumptions the environment is borrowed and outlives the run, so this is reachable: the outgoing episode is discarded unsealed, episode_id_ is not advanced, and subsequent events are tagged to the pre-reset id. Also: (i) the post-finalization rejection is deferred to apply-time for queued resets — PolicySession::reset silently queues (policy_session.cpp:148-153) and the throw inside reset_now leaves the queued entry un-popped (apply_queued :155-158), permanently wedging the session (pending() stays true via s.completed :52-56,239-242; close() then throws :305-308); (ii) finish() computes final_reason_ via environment_->status() OUTSIDE the try (:223-231), so a finish first attempted after environment close throws unrecorded, latches finalization_attempted_, and can never finalize; (iii) finish(new_primary,stop) retries silently drop the new args (first-latch :205-216); (iv) OperatorInterrupted is thrown after path_ is set (:245,255-256) — success reported as throw on first call (documented/tested, test_lifecycle.cpp:233-236). (b) HOLDS: instance_ is declared after library_ (plugin_policy.cpp:130-131) so ~Instance always precedes ~Library/dlclose; Instance::close() exchanges value->nullptr BEFORE destroy (:63-66) giving at-most-once even on destroy throw, and member unwinding destroys an instance leaked by a failing create (invoke throw :80, null check :99, hash-mismatch :100) strictly before dlclose. (c) Holds semantically on defaults: traction_control.cpp:70-90 and builtin_policies.cpp:67-88 carry identical constants (policy.hpp:37-41), identical op order (elapsed=max(0,dt) before the valid gate, slip, ceiling=max(0,demand?torque:v[7]) without base, cap trigger/cut/recover/clamp/release, standstill hold/accumulate/clear); normalized through policy_control_from_abi (policy.cpp:46-72) outputs match including rider_enabled (intent.hpp:85 default true, host preset plugin_policy.cpp:112). Caveats: the builtin emits RideControl not BikeControlV1, so 'bit-identical BikeControlV1' is only realizable post-conversion; and a domain asymmetry — builtin's observation.validate() rejects source_time_s<0 and >time_s+1e-12 (sensors.cpp:34-37) while the example only checks isfinite (traction_control.cpp:57), so the plugin emits controls on inputs the builtin rejects. -ffp-contract=off is build-enforced (recipe numerical_flags) and demanded of the standalone build by the example's header comment. (d) PARTIALLY VIOLATED: emit() locks columns_ from the first emitted row regardless of producer (preview_csv.cpp:73-79,96); write_marker() (:116-120) emits {generation,event}, so a marker-first call fixes a two-column schema and every later write() silently emits only generation/event — all telemetry keys skipped by the locked-column lookup (:83-86). None of the three alternatives holds: no std::invalid_argument (require_open :63-66 throws std::runtime_error only for closed/failed), schema is not deferred to the first write(), and no ordering contract is documented (preview_csv.hpp:12-13; NATIVE_POLICIES.md:117 'stable first-row columns' describes schema stability, not the marker-ordering hazard).

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
C1 (d): PhysicalLiveCsv csv(s); csv.write_marker("run ended; viewer_closed; t=0.000000s"); csv.write(0.,1,{{"time_s",0.},{"x",5.}}); csv.close(); emits 'generation,event\r\n,run ended; viewer_closed; t=0.000000s\r\n1,\r\n' — columns_ locked to {generation,event} by the marker row (preview_csv.cpp:73-79,116-120); the time_s and x telemetry fields are silently discarded by the locked-column lookup (:83-86) with no error. Existing tests only cover marker-after-write (test_lifecycle.cpp:173-181, test_frontend_windows.cpp:103-110). C2 (a): ResearchRun run(*env, publication); advance intervals; env->reset() (public, research.hpp:167-171) — plant resets with no seal and no new_episode because the env-owner ctor installs no hooks (lifecycle.cpp:71-76); run.finish() then publishes only the post-reset episode and attributes post-reset events to the stale episode_id. Same hole via session.environment().reset() (policy_session.hpp:44) which also bypasses Impl::reset_now's operator-journal record. In-product callers route through launch.reset()/session.reset() (cli/research.cpp:107-110, viewer/research.cpp:20), so the gap is embedder-reachable, not product-reached. C3 (a): session.begin_advance(); session.reset() (queued while pending, policy_session.cpp:148-153); run.finish(/*primary=*/{}, /*stop=*/false) sets finalization_attempted_; next session.advance_pending() completes the window then apply_queued->apply(reset)->reset_now->before_reset throws 'cannot reset a finalized research launch' (lifecycle.cpp:87) — queued.pop_front() never executes (policy_session.cpp:155-158), s.completed stays engaged, pending() stays true, every subsequent advance_pending rethrows and session.close() throws 'finish the accepted policy interval before close' (:305-308): permanently wedged session; equally reachable post-finish via begin_advance()+a stale queued reset. C4 (a): env->close() then run.finish() — environment_->status() at lifecycle.cpp:225 throws outside the try block, latched finalization_attempted_, path_ never set, failure absent from failures_.secondary (:246-254): sealed evidence permanently unpublishable. C5 (c): SensorObservation{time_s=1.,source_time_s=2.}: builtin validate() throws 'cannot come from the future' (sensors.cpp:36-37 via policy.cpp:17) while the plugin accepts (traction_control.cpp:57) and emits a control — trajectory domains diverge on this input class (and on source_time_s<0).

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
(d): the statement's first-listed fix — std::invalid_argument for marker-before-write ordering — would wrongly block the legitimate marker-only trailer: a caller whose cadence never becomes due (duration < interval_s, or a run ending before the first due row) can still validly emit write_marker("run ended; ...") as the terminal artifact; the product path guarantees >=1 write (cli/ride.cpp:160 before :161) but the class is a public standalone sink. Only alternatives (ii) deferred-schema or (iii) documented ordering preserve it — the preferred fix as written is the harmful one. (a): the post-finalization reset ban is the point of receipt immutability (RECORDING.md:70 'reset seals the outgoing generation', NATIVE_POLICIES.md:109, RIDE.md:244), but it also permanently bans resets after a FAILED finish (publish threw — receipts exist and are retryable, yet a 'reseed and continue' scenario is barred forever) and after finish() on non-persistent runs (publication_==nullptr — no evidence to protect, resets rejected anyway at lifecycle.cpp:87 via episode_started bookkeeping); mild over-breadth, defensible under 'finalized launch' semantics. (b): nothing valid is blocked — the contract is pure safety. (c): 'on default parameters' correctly scopes out the builtin's configuration knobs and base-policy composition; nothing valid is wrongly constrained, though the BikeControlV1 phrasing is under-inclusive since the builtin cannot emit that type.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
(a) Admitted wedge: a queued reset rejected post-finalization is never dequeued — the session is left permanently pending() and unclosable with the poisoned entry retried on every advance (policy_session.cpp:154-158,225-243). Admitted post-finalization activity: begin_advance/advance_pending are unguarded against finalization_attempted_, so the plant keeps running and the operator journal (impl_->events) grows unconsumed by any later seal. Admitted multi-owner hazard: a second ResearchRun on the same session overwrites the reset hooks (policy_session.cpp:302-304), silently detaching the first owner's seal/new_episode accounting; ~ResearchRun of either then clears hooks for both. Admitted dead-end: finish() first attempted on a closed environment latches finalization_attempted_ and leaves sealed receipts permanently unpublishable, with the status() throw unrecorded (lifecycle.cpp:223-231). Narrow admitted corruption: a bad_alloc mid-commit in seal() leaves a moved-from prepared_ receipt that a retry commits as a partial episode (lifecycle.cpp:190-199). (b): no invalid state found — ordering and at-most-once hold on all paths. (c): the plugin admits a wider input domain than the builtin (negative or future source_time_s) — divergent behavior admitted under the 'identical trajectories' claim. (d): caller rows can overwrite the reserved generation/event columns via sample_wire::set merging (preview_csv.cpp:109-110, samples.cpp:76-83) — spoofed-schema rows admitted; and the marker-first silent telemetry drop itself is an admitted corrupt-but-well-formed CSV artifact.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
(a) Narrow 'before every plant reset' to 'before every reset issued through ResearchRun::reset or the owning PolicySession command path', and either document that direct NativeResearchRuntime::reset / session.environment().reset() bypasses recording, or close the gap (friend-only reset, or a reset hook on NativeResearchRuntime mirroring PolicySession::set_reset_hooks). Reject reset requests at request-time once finalization_attempted_ (PolicySession::reset should throw or drop the queued entry), or at minimum pop the queue head before applying so a deferred rejection cannot wedge the session. Record the finish() failure even when it occurs before the try block, and state explicitly that retrying finish(primary,stop) discards new arguments. (b) Keep as written. (c) Restate as: plugin and builtin produce identical runtime::RideControl trajectories on default parameters and host-validated inputs, with plugin output normalized through policy_control_from_abi; either align the plugin's source_time_s validation with SensorObservation::validate (finite, >=0, <=time_s+1e-12) or document the ABI's wider accepted domain. (d) Drop the enforced-invalid_argument option (it blocks the valid marker-only trailer); require instead that the schema lock be owned exclusively by the first write() — write_marker() before any write() must emit the marker row without fixing telemetry columns (emit against the pending schema, or document marker-before-write semantics explicitly), so no telemetry row is ever silently narrowed.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P16`: `RunCapture`, `ResearchRecording` & DuckDB Atomic Sealed-Container Publication and Post-Seal Immutability

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `mixed_subclauses`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
(a) ATOMICITY/OVERWRITE — mostly holds. The commit protocol is correctly ordered: private mkdtemp staging under output_root (native/src/recording/publish.cpp:43), UNSEALED marker first (:47), DuckDB built in a second private temp dir and hard-linked only after checkpoint+close (native/src/recording/database.cpp temp staging + platform::link_new), payloads fsynced+hashed (publish.cpp:57-68), sealed-manifest.json written (:71), staging synced, destination created exclusively with EEXIST retry (:73-79), payloads hard-linked with inode/device identity + post-link rehash (:80-84), destination+root fsynced (:85), manifest.json linked LAST (:92). All creates are exclusive: open_exclusive O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW (native/src/platform/files.cpp:111-112), create_directory fails on any existing object incl. symlinks (:189), link_new never overwrites (:199), exports use open_exclusive (native/src/recording/export.cpp:77), golden saves use create_directory+write_exclusive (native/src/golden/episode.cpp:361-371). Fork+SIGKILL tests confirm zero sealed manifests at checkpoint/link_run.duckdb/manifest_link (native/tests/recording/test_publish.cpp:88-96). HOWEVER the blanket 'no publication/export overwrites an existing path' is falsified: viz::detail::write_text (native/src/viz/plot.cpp:185-199) opens O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_NOFOLLOW WITHOUT O_EXCL and ftruncates — the code states 'Plot exports intentionally replace an ordinary destination'. Reachable post-seal via native/src/cli/ride.cpp:174-175 and via `bike-ride --html <sealed_dir>` rerun (ride.cpp:193). Confined to fixed sidecar names (extension whitelist plot.cpp:119-123; leaf .html enforcement native/src/viz/ride.cpp:114); leaf symlinks stay safe via O_NOFOLLOW, so manifest-covered payloads cannot be reached — but an existing analyst artifact inside a sealed container IS overwritten. (b) HASH PROTECTION — holds for covered payloads, overclaims for manifest.json. run.duckdb+summary.md are hashed into the manifest (publish.cpp:66-68), reader enforces exact key sets and per-payload hashes (native/src/recording/reader.cpp:36-53), opens the DB read-only with an opened-file hash pin (:59-60) and rehashes after validation (:78-79); payloads are never rewritten post-link. BUT manifest.json itself carries no hash (self-reference is impossible): a self-consistent rewrite downgrading report_status complete->failed (failed status requires file_sha256 == exactly {run.duckdb}, reader.cpp:45-46) is accepted, silently shrinking the covered set; a stray unlisted summary.md beside a failed-status manifest is never checked. Sidecar confinement holds (all post-seal writes land in publication->directory, ride.cpp:171-175) but 'exclusively' is violated for *.png/ride.html (replace-capable writer above); preview.csv is O_EXCL (ride.cpp:171). (c) RETRY FRESHNESS — VIOLATED on the property's own terms. The implementation chose the latch: capture_ is staged once inside `if (!capture_)` (native/src/recording/research_publication.cpp:18-45) and every later call runs finalize(*capture_) at :47, ignoring the fresh receipts/events spans entirely. Neither ratifying condition holds: (i) no contract pins identical-event-set retries — native/src/research/lifecycle.hpp:40 promises exceptions 'leave receipts available for retry', docs/RECORDING.md documents retry freshness only at destination level; (ii) finish() does NOT stop appending operator_stop_deferred once finalization_attempted_ — the event is emitted unconditionally whenever the per-call InterruptShield caught a signal (native/src/research/lifecycle.cpp:223 fresh shield each call; :240-244 event emission), and ResearchRun::event has no finalization guard (:78-85). The retained RunCapture cannot grow (require_collecting gate, native/src/recording/capture.cpp:92), seal() early-returns existing receipts on retry (lifecycle.cpp:144-145), so post-attempt evidence can never be staged — the sealed run.duckdb permanently diverges from events_. (d) ORPHAN episode_started — holds. episode_started is emitted only after the prior seal (ride.cpp:147 physical path; lifecycle.cpp:92-95 new_episode runs seal-then-emit; :68 initial ctor emit); the journal enforces global (episode_id,event_id) monotonicity (capture.cpp:96-99) plus prefix continuity/dedup (:109-110); a stranded episode row fails publication wholesale at 'events: orphan episode' (native/src/recording/database_validate.cpp:154); the research adapter additionally filters events per receipt (research_publication.cpp:37). No reachable ordering lets an orphan event enter an earlier completed episode's journal.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
R1 [(c) latch drops late evidence — primary violation]: construct ResearchRun over a PolicySession with make_research_publication(metadata, catalog, root, options). Inject failure via FinalizeOptions.before_stage throwing at stage 'manifest_link' (the exact fault-injection seam exercised by test_publish.cpp:47). finish() attempt 1 stages capture_ (research_publication.cpp:45), publish throws, finalize latches publication_failed (finalize.cpp:19). Between attempts, append evidence through either reachable channel: (i) call the public ResearchRun::event() (lifecycle.cpp:78-85 — no finalization guard), or (ii) raise a second SIGINT during attempt 2 so the fresh InterruptShield defers it and lifecycle.cpp:241 emits a NEW 'operator_stop_deferred' into events_. Retry finish(): ResearchRecording::publish hits `if (!capture_)` = false, runs finalize(*capture_) on the stale staged data; the sealed run.duckdb contains only attempt-1 events. events_ now contains an event that can NEVER reach the sealed container (capture_ is frozen; require_collecting at capture.cpp:92 rejects appends in publication_failed/published states; the latch is never reset). The sealed recording permanently misrepresents the launch journal, and the deferred-interrupt journaling guarantee in docs/RECORDING.md (a second Ctrl-C during finalization 'is deferred and journaled') fails exactly on the retry path. Amplifier: publish() silently ACCEPTS different receipts/events spans on retry — same sealed output, no error; test_lifecycle.cpp:124-133 exercises retry only with identical args through a mock Publication, so the divergence is invisible in the suite. R2 [(a)/(b) sidecar overwrite]: after `bike-ride --record --out R` seals, run `bike-ride --html R` twice (or any write_artifact on a colliding *.png name): the second write opens O_CREAT|O_NOFOLLOW + ftruncate + rewrite (plot.cpp:185-199), overwriting an existing path inside the sealed container — literal breach of (a)'s absolute no-overwrite and of (b)'s 'created exclusively' under the O_EXCL reading. R3 [(b) manifest downgrade]: rewrite a sealed dir's manifest.json to report_status='failed' with report_error and file_sha256 covering only run.duckdb (matching its unchanged hash): open_recording accepts it (reader.cpp:36-53 checks structure and hash consistency, not manifest provenance); the covered-payload set silently shrinks and summary.md exits hash protection. R4 [(d) attempted, fails safe — reported for completeness]: a caller-injected episode_started for a never-sealed episode_id is either silently dropped by the adapter filter (research_publication.cpp:37 — silent data loss, see S2) or, in the direct RunCapture path, kills publication wholesale at database_validate.cpp:154. Earlier completed episodes are never poisoned; (d) holds.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
N1: `bike-ride --html <dir>` (native/src/cli/ride.cpp:193) is a legitimate post-hoc regeneration flow — it writes ride.html into an existing directory, including a previously sealed one (documented analyst-sidecar pattern in docs/RECORDING.md), and replace semantics are deliberate (native/src/viz/plot.cpp:188-189 'Plot exports intentionally replace an ordinary destination'). If (b)'s 'exclusively' is read as exclusive-create, the second --html run (or any sidecar refresh) is wrongly blocked; as implemented it replaces, meaning either the property forbids a valid product scenario or the writers violate the property — an unresolved tension needing an explicit ratification either way. N2: export_data's table whitelist (native/src/recording/export.cpp:69-72) rejects 'unsupported explicit recording export' for tables like episodes/physics/runs/coordinate_catalog/assets/integration_states/recording_meta — an analyst wanting CSV/JSONL of those documented tables must detour through `bike-recording sql` + JSON. Deliberate subset, but it does block a plausible valid export request. N3: the frozen RunCapture after publication_failed (require_collecting, native/src/recording/capture.cpp:92) forbids appending a late marker event (e.g. 'finalization_failed') between retry attempts — consistent with an identical-event-set contract, but it also makes the (c) divergence unrepairable at the RunCapture layer; the only remedy is a fresh capture the implementation never builds. N4: staging lives inside output_root (native/src/recording/publish.cpp:42-43), requiring same-filesystem hard links and a writable output root; a tmpfs-stage/publish-elsewhere topology is unsupported — acknowledged by the property's own assumptions, noted as the operative limit rather than a wrongful block. N5: per-episode event ids must be 0-based contiguous (native/src/recording/database_validate.cpp:173 consecutive zero=true) — an externally numbered event stream is rejected wholesale rather than renumbered; only synthetic callers hit this since journals always emit from 0.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
S1: manifest.json is the trust anchor yet unhashed — a self-consistent rewrite downgrading report_status to 'failed' and shrinking file_sha256 to {run.duckdb} passes all reader checks (native/src/recording/reader.cpp:36-53); a failed-status container carrying a stray unlisted summary.md is likewise admitted (absence of unlisted payloads is never verified). S2: orphan operator events are silently dropped by the research adapter's episode_id filter (native/src/recording/research_publication.cpp:37): malformed events_ input seals a recording silently missing rows — the DB-level 'events: orphan episode' check (database_validate.cpp:154) can never see them because they never reach the DB. S3: no kind-uniqueness checks — duplicate episode_started/episode_ended for one episode are journaled and admitted (capture.cpp event() validates only non-empty kind; the validator enforces id consecutiveness, not marker uniqueness) yielding a validly-sealed semantically-wrong journal. S4: retained unsealed evidence is directly readable — on pre-manifest failure the catch retains BOTH the staging dir AND the destination dir containing a linked run.duckdb (native/src/recording/publish.cpp:93-96 'owned evidence retained'): a valid DuckDB payload readable outside the seal contract in a normal-looking run directory; .run-recording-unsealed-* dirs accumulate on aborts. Zero SEALED recordings holds, but raw sealed-grade payload sits on disk. S5: ResearchRun::event has no finalization guard (native/src/research/lifecycle.cpp:78-85) and seal() early-returns on retry (:144-145), so the in-memory journal stays mutable post-seal/post-publish and post-attempt session events are never re-pulled — the structural root of the (c) divergence. S6: received_wall_s monotonicity is never validated across or within episodes; applied_step is always copied equal to processed_step when applied (research_publication.cpp:34-35) so the applied column never carries a genuinely later application step — schema-valid, informationally degenerate. S7: finalize on a disabled capture returns state=published with an empty directory (native/src/recording/finalize.cpp:5-7) — a 'published' result naming no openable artifact; downstream code treating Publication.directory as openable trips on it. S8 (cosmetic): a post-seal operator_stop_deferred is journaled BEFORE the episode_ended tail event (research_publication.cpp:39-43 tail deferral) despite occurring after the end marker, and its wall time becomes seal_wall feeding derived-event timestamps (:38,:42).

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Rewrite P16 as: (a) keep the commit-point guarantee verbatim — 'any crash/failure before the manifest link_new leaves zero reader-recognizable sealed recordings' — but scope the no-overwrite clause precisely: no destination or manifest-covered payload is ever adopted/overwritten; sidecar writers may touch only the fixed names preview.csv, *.png, ride.html inside the published directory. Decide and ratify ONE sidecar policy: either all post-seal sidecar writes are O_EXCL on first creation (requires making viz::detail::write_text accept an exclusive-create flag for recording-directory targets, native/src/viz/plot.cpp:185-199), or replace-permitted refresh is documented for those exact names in docs/RECORDING.md (matching `bike-ride --html` regeneration, native/src/cli/ride.cpp:193). (b) state manifest.json's integrity model honestly: it is schema+relational (exact keys, report-status-conditional payload sets, run_id/row-count/asset cross-checks against the hash-pinned DB), NOT hash-covered; add a reader rule rejecting unlisted payload files inside a sealed dir (catches stray summary.md beside report_status=failed) or explicitly accept the manifest as the unhashed trust anchor. (c) choose one alternative and enforce it: PREFERRED — re-stage: in ResearchRecording::publish, reset capture_ when finalize throws (`capture_.reset()` in the catch) so every unpublished publish() re-adapts the CURRENT receipts/events, preserving wholesale-discard atomicity and honoring late evidence including a retry-window operator_stop_deferred (native/src/research/lifecycle.cpp:240-244); ALTERNATIVE — ratify the latch: document that retries MUST pass identical spans, make publish() verify receipt/event identity (count+episode_ids+event ids) and throw on divergence instead of silently ignoring args (native/src/recording/research_publication.cpp:47), and gate ResearchRun::event plus the deferred-signal emission behind !finalization_attempted_ (lifecycle.cpp:78-85, :240-244) so the journal can never diverge from the staged capture. (d) keep, and optionally add a capture-level tripwire rejecting RunCapture::event calls whose episode_id exceeds the highest added episode, plus kind-uniqueness enforcement for singleton markers (episode_started/episode_ended/model_first_failure) to catch malformed journals at emission time rather than only at publish validation.
