# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P17a`: Deferred-SIGINT Shield Mechanics & Scope Registry

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_engineering_contract`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Probed the shield mechanics at native/src/platform/signals.{hpp,cpp} and the honoring seam at native/src/recording/interrupt.cpp, plus every production scope (cli/ride.cpp, cli/research.cpp, cli/settle.cpp, cli/replay.cpp, cli/batch.cpp, batch/sweep.cpp, research/lifecycle.cpp). The core mechanism — counting sig_atomic_t handler, atomic g_current_target, 16-slot registry, previous_target/previous_action LIFO restore, off-main flag-holder degrade — is implemented as stated and pinned by tests (tests/platform/test_primitives.cpp:329-388). Both named caveats are literally accurate: out-of-order destruction dangles g_current_target (signals.cpp:81 stores the previous scope's &deferred_; signals.cpp:103 writes it back even after that scope died, and also restores the counting handler), and kShieldSlots=16 makes scope #17 a silent flag-holder (signals.cpp:70-78,98). However the protocol is only half-held in three reachable ways: (a) the honor-half is dropped on the physical-ride path — ride.cpp installs one run-wide shield at :67 but polls deferred() only inside the run loop (:96,:130); a SIGINT arriving during the trailing seal (ride.cpp:156) or recording::finalize (ride.cpp:166) is counted then silently discarded, exiting 0/1/2 with no operator_stop_deferred event and no exit-130, contradicting RECORDING.md:336-338 ('deferred behind the write, journaled as operator_stop_deferred, then honored'); (b) sa_flags=0 (signals.cpp:85) installs without SA_RESTART, so a delivered SIGINT still returns EINTR to interruptible syscalls inside the critical section — first-party loops retry EINTR (files.cpp:144,215; process.cpp:314; duckdb.cpp:507) but the heavy write path inside the DuckDB library is not auditable and on an interruptible fsync the first Ctrl-C can still fail the write the shield exists to protect; (c) the 'stack-allocated main-thread' preconditions are purely conventional — the public ctor admits heap/static/member storage and the dtor never re-verifies the owning thread (signals.cpp:97-107 vs ctor's on_main_thread() gate at :69), so the caveated corruption and a cross-thread sigaction swap (violating signals.hpp:16-18's own 'handler swaps reserved for the owning thread') are reachable through ordinary compilable C++.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
1) ride-path swallowed deferral: `bike-ride --record --headless --duration 30`; let the loop exit via end_of_track/duration, then deliver SIGINT during the multi-second publish window at ride.cpp:166 (RECORDING.md:325 measures ~193 MB run.duckdb writes). Handler increments interrupt.deferred_ (signals.cpp:47) but no code reads deferred() after line 167 — the function returns 0 (ride.cpp:183-186 checks only reason/model_valid). Outcome: write protected (good) but the interrupt is neither journaled nor honored — silent success. Compare settle.cpp:46/48/59, research.cpp:193/203, replay.cpp:117, batch.cpp:78, sweep.cpp:71, all of which honor the count at scope end; ride is the sole outlier. 2) finalize_guarded drop-on-throw: interrupt.cpp:9-13 — if finalize() throws (disk full, injected via FinalizeOptions::before_stage, which tests already use at tests/recording/test_publish.cpp:99 and tests/reference/test_native_recording_lifecycle.cpp:611), the deferred count is discarded with no diagnostic; research/lifecycle.cpp:248 journals the same situation ('operator interrupt deferred during research publication'), so the in-scope function is strictly weaker than the sibling path. Note finalize_guarded has zero production callers in native/src — the canonical shield+honor wrapper is test-only, and production hand-rolls the honoring half (which is how ride.cpp lost it). 3) Non-LIFO dangle (the named caveat, confirmed): `auto a=std::make_unique<InterruptShield>(); auto b=std::make_unique<InterruptShield>(); a.reset(); b.reset();` — b's slot.previous_target=&a->deferred_ (dead) is restored to g_current_target (signals.cpp:103) and b's previous_action (the counting handler) is reinstalled (signals.cpp:104): process ends with the counting handler live pointing at freed stack; every later SIGINT writes through a dangling pointer (UB) and is silently swallowed — Ctrl-C permanently dead. 4) Cross-thread destruction: `auto s=std::make_shared<InterruptShield>();` on main, last reset() on a worker — dtor runs sigaction+g_current_target mutation off the owning thread (signals.cpp:97-107 has no on_main_thread check) — violates signals.hpp:16-18. 5) static/namespace-scope shield: `static InterruptShield g;` installs at static-init on main, lives for process duration, counter never polled — SIGINT muted for the whole program lifetime, admitted with no diagnostic. 6) Registry >=17: 17 nested main-thread scopes — scope 17 gets slot_=-1 (signals.cpp:78); SIGINTs during its section still land on scope 16's counter, so a nested finalize_guarded at depth 17 would see deferred()==0, return its Publication normally, and the OperatorInterrupted then fires at the outer scope AFTER the inner result was already consumed — attribution shift, shielding intact only by accident of the enclosing scope. 7) EINTR hole: `kill -INT <pid>` during a slow fsync in publish -> EINTR in a non-retrying syscall inside the section — the shield defers termination but not syscall failure (the Python source was immune via PEP 475 auto-retry; the port diverges). 8) Theoretical: deferred_ sig_atomic_t wraps past INT_MAX (signals.cpp:47,109) — signed overflow UB; deferred()==0 after exactly 2^32 hits. Unreachable in practice.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No valid product scenario is wrongly blocked. Deepest real nesting observed is 2 (cli/research.cpp:142 outer scope + research/lifecycle.cpp:223 finish() scope; ride.cpp would similarly nest 2 if finalize_guarded were used), so the 16-slot cap never binds in product flows — >=17 is test-lab territory only. Off-main shields are intentionally flag-holders (signals.hpp:14-18) and no current scenario needs worker-thread signal ownership: all subprocess work goes through posix_spawn'd exec'd children (platform/process.cpp), and exec resets caught dispositions to SIG_DFL so children are unaffected by an active parent shield (the spawn-inheritance hazard is void — POSIX exec clears handlers). The one arguable block — unlimited SIGINTs during finalize are counted but never escalate, so a wedged finalize (e.g., fsync on a dead NFS mount) cannot be force-aborted by Ctrl-C and needs SIGKILL from another terminal — is the designed contract itself (RECORDING.md:336-338 documents deferral as intended) and mirrors the Python source, so it is the requirement, not a wrongly-blocked scenario.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even granting stack-allocated main-thread scopes, the statement admits these invalid/corrupt states: (1) a delivered-then-dropped interrupt — ride.cpp post-loop deferral exits 0/1/2 silently (operator intent lost, no journal record); (2) an interrupted-then-failed critical section — no SA_RESTART (signals.cpp:85) lets EINTR break the write the shield protects; (3) a permanently muted SIGINT — a namespace-scope `static InterruptShield` installs at static-init and its counter is never honored; (4) a dangling process-wide signal target — non-LIFO destruction (admitted by the caveat) leaves the counting handler installed pointing at a dead stack frame, silently absorbing all later SIGINTs and writing through a dangling pointer; (5) cross-thread signal-state mutation — dtor has no on_main_thread check (signals.cpp:97-107), so an installed shield deleted off-main swaps the process disposition off the owning thread; (6) a thrown finalize discards the deferred interrupt without a diagnostic (interrupt.cpp:9-13, weaker than lifecycle.cpp:248); (7) a foreign SIGINT handler installed between ctor and dtor is clobbered by the stale previous_action restore (handler-chain convention, same family as LIFO); (8) on any non-Apple/non-Linux build on_main_thread() returns false (signals.cpp:57) and shielding silently vanishes; (9) the counter overflows signed sig_atomic_t at ~2^31 hits (theoretical). Also structural: finalize_guarded — the in-scope honoring seam — is dead production code, so the property's claim rests on per-callsite hand-rolled honoring that has already diverged (ride).

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Tighten to: 'Deferred-SIGINT shielding holds for stack-allocated scopes constructed and destroyed on the main thread in strict LIFO order, provided each scope honors deferred() at every exit path (success AND exception); the following are unenforced conventions, not checked invariants: LIFO destruction (violation leaves the counting handler installed with a dangling g_current_target and permanently muted SIGINT), stack allocation (heap/static/member storage and cross-thread destruction are compilable today — the dtor lacks an on_main_thread check at signals.cpp:97-107), >=17 nested scopes degrade to flag-holders whose own deferred() under-reports hits attributed to the enclosing installed scope, and foreign handler interposition mid-scope is clobbered on restore.' Additionally: (1) record the honoring gap — ride.cpp never re-checks deferred() after its trailing seal/finalize (fix: check at ride.cpp:~168/exit path like settle.cpp:59, or document intentional drop); (2) record the EINTR exposure — sa_flags=0 (signals.cpp:85) means a delivered SIGINT can still fail interruptible syscalls inside the section; either set SA_RESTART to match the Python source's PEP-475 immunity or document that first-party loops retry EINTR but library-internal syscalls are unguarded; (3) document that finalize() throwing inside finalize_guarded drops the deferred count without a diagnostic (interrupt.cpp:9-13); (4) document that finalize_guarded has no production callers — the honoring half is hand-rolled per CLI and must be verified per callsite; (5) note the platform tail — on_main_thread() returns false outside Apple/Linux (signals.cpp:57), where no shielding exists at all.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P17b`: Exit-130 on Deferred Interrupt at Seal/Publication Boundaries

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_confirmed_requirement`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
The statement is falsified on its headline clause. ride.cpp physical_run installs InterruptShield at :67 but consults interrupt.deferred() only inside the run loop (terminal() at :96, hooks.close_requested at :130). After the loop exits, the entire seal/publication/artifact section — seal(reason)+retry :156, preview finalization :157-164, recording::finalize :166, preview.csv/PNG/HTML artifact writes :171-176, runtime->close() :179, summary prints :181-182 — executes under the still-live shield, yet the exit decision at :183-186 checks only `reason`. Any SIGINT deferred in that window leaves reason=end_of_track/duration_reached/viewer_closed/etc. → exit 0/1/2, never 130. std::rethrow_exception(failure) at :180 additionally precedes the :183 check, so deferred+failure → exit 1/2 via dispatch (application.cpp:27-30). recording/interrupt.cpp:10-12 explicitly documents that ride 'already shields seal+finalize together and calls finalize directly' — i.e. the frontend owns honoring deferred() after the section, and never does. Secondary holes exist in the 'matching' frontends: (a) research.cpp — a SIGINT landing inside ResearchRun::finish's NESTED shield (lifecycle.cpp:223) increments only the inner counter; when seal/event/publish then throws with empty failures_.primary (lifecycle.cpp:246-253 rethrows the section failure), run_research's catch sees failure≠OperatorInterrupted, interrupted=false, outer deferred()==0 → exit 3 (research.cpp:193), not 130; (b) settle.cpp:51-52 — write_state_file failure returns 4 without consulting deferred() (SIGINT deferred during that artifact write + write failure → 4); (c) replay.cpp:119-121 — the catch returns 1 unconditionally, so SIGINT deferred during session.report()/session.close()/std::cout (:113-116) or during inspect() internals (:20,:29) followed by any exception → 1; (d) batch.cpp — no local catch around batch::execute (:73), the options.completed artifact/log writes (:60-67), results_file.close() (:75) or summarize (:76): a deferred SIGINT plus any throw there propagates to dispatch → 1/2, deferred dropped. Boundary notes: for --record rides the exposed window is large (run.duckdb + manifest publish plus PNG/HTML renders), so the gap is reachable in ordinary use, not only under adversarial timing; the note() seam means tests can hit it deterministically.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
Primary (mainline, no failure needed): `bike-ride --headless --record --out DIR`. Let the run end via terminal()→end_of_track (ride.cpp:97) or duration_reached (:98). Deliver SIGINT any time after the final terminal() evaluation while seal (ride.cpp:156), recording::finalize (:166), or viz::write_artifact/plot_physical_ride_html (:171-176) runs — equivalently, inject interrupt.note() during those sections. The shield (:67) counts it; publication completes; :183 reason=='end_of_track'≠'operator_stop'; :184-185 model_valid passes; :186 returns 0. Deferred SIGINT during seal/publication → exit 0 (property requires 130). Variant A: same delivery plus seal throws twice (unwritable --out) → failure set, :180 rethrow → dispatch catch → exit 1 (or 2 if invalid_argument). Variant B (research): run completes cleanly → SIGINT lands inside ResearchRun::finish's inner shield (lifecycle.cpp:223) → publication_->publish throws (I/O error or the InterruptingPublication test double pattern) → failures_.primary is empty (first call passed {} at research.cpp:129), so lifecycle.cpp:253 rethrows the publish error → research.cpp:185 rethrow hits catch(...) not OperatorInterrupted → interrupted stays false → :193 outer interrupt.deferred()==0 → exit 3. Variant C (settle): SIGINT during write_state_file (settle.cpp:50) → write fails → :52 return 4. Variant D (replay): SIGINT deferred during the advance loop, then session.report()/session.close() throws (:113-115) → catch :119 → exit 1. No CLI-level test exercises physical_run's exit code under a deferred post-loop SIGINT; lifecycle tests cover only ResearchRun::finish and finalize_guarded.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
None found. The constraint is the documented design: signals.hpp:4-11 defines seal+finalize as the critical section whose deferred count 'the owning thread honors AFTER the section completes'; RECORDING.md:336-338 documents a second SIGINT during finalization is 'deferred behind the write, journaled as operator_stop_deferred, then honored'; finalize_guarded's comment (recording/interrupt.cpp:10-12) assumes the ride frontend performs the honoring itself; batch children already emit exit_code=130 on cancellation (process_pool.cpp:56); test_native_recording_lifecycle.cpp:630-659 pins 'publishes then stops'. Nothing in simulation-decisions.md, RIDE.md or RECORDING.md requires a non-130 exit after a deferred SIGINT. Uniform 130 is also the POSIX convention for SIGINT-terminated processes. No valid product scenario is blocked; the only debatable case (SIGINT arriving after a fully successful run still yields 130 in settle/replay/batch) is intentional and consistent, not a product violation.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even where exit 130 is produced, the property admits documented-contract violations and traceless states: (1) the physical ride path never journals operator_stop_deferred — ride.cpp's seal lambda (:102-116) emits only derived_events, while RECORDING.md:180 states ordinary physical runs journal operator_stop_deferred and lifecycle.cpp:241 does so for research; a compliant exit code still admits a sealed recording missing the documented event, and a run where the operator pressed Ctrl-C during publication is indistinguishable from an uninterrupted one (no journal event, no summary flag, no stderr note). (2) The statement's phrasing ('deferred() or folded into reason ahead of the exit decision') admits implementations that check only on success paths — settle.cpp:52, replay.cpp:119-121 and batch.cpp's uncaught execute path all satisfy a literal success-path reading while still exiting 4/1/2 with a nonzero deferred count. (3) The property says nothing about WHICH shield counts the deferral: research.cpp's nested-shield attribution (lifecycle.cpp:223 vs outer interrupt) means a SIGINT inside finish is invisible to the frontend's deferred() check on the failure path — a literal reading centered on `interrupt.deferred()` cannot even express the gap. (4) settle exits 130 discarding a successfully computed pose with no recorded reason; batch exit 130 leaves a valid partial batch_results.jsonl (acceptable, and the only frontend that reports the flag in its summary, batch.cpp:76-77); replay --view's PlaybackSession ctor (:20) precedes its shield (:21), so SIGINT there is process death rather than deferral (consistent, but outside the contract the statement implies).

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Restate as: 'Every CLI frontend exits 130 whenever a SIGINT is deferred by any InterruptShield covering the process at delivery — including shields nested inside seal/finalize helpers such as ResearchRun::finish — on EVERY exit path reachable after the deferral (success, seal/publication/artifact failure, and unrelated exception alike). The exit decision must consult interrupt.deferred(), or an equivalent folded flag that also captures deferrals surfaced as OperatorInterrupted/notes from nested shields, immediately before returning and BEFORE rethrowing any captured failure.' Concretely: (1) ride.cpp physical_run must re-check interrupt.deferred() after the seal/finalize/artifact section — before the rethrow at :180 and the reason mapping at :183-186 — and journal operator_stop_deferred when a deferral is observed mid-seal (RECORDING.md:180); (2) research.cpp must treat a deferral observed inside ResearchRun::finish as an interrupt at the :193 decision even when finish fails — e.g. have finish throw OperatorInterrupted unconditionally on shield.deferred(), or propagate its inner deferred count to the caller; (3) settle.cpp:52, replay.cpp:119-121, and batch_command's execute/callback/post-execute throws must yield 130 whenever deferred()≠0; (4) add a CLI-level test injecting note()/raise(SIGINT) during the ride seal/finalize window asserting exit 130 and the operator_stop_deferred journal event.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P17c`: `operator_stop_deferred` Journaling Boundary

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_confirmed_requirement`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
The boundary holds only on the research leg and fails on the physical frontend that the property names. (a) Research path: ResearchRun::finish installs a fresh inner InterruptShield at native/src/research/lifecycle.cpp:223; seal() runs at :239, then `if (shield.deferred())` at :240-241 appends `operator_stop_deferred` into events_ BEFORE publication_->publish(episodes_, events_) is invoked at :245 — so a SIGINT deferred during annotate_failure/launch_interrupted-event/seal is journaled (verified end-to-end by SealInterruptPolicy in native/tests/reference/test_research_recording_lifecycle.cpp:215-246). A SIGINT arriving inside publish() misses the :240 check and is caught only at :255-256 → OperatorInterrupted, not journaled (verified via InterruptingPublication at :97-114, 250-275). Clauses 1-2 hold there. (b) Physical path (physical_run, native/src/cli/ride.cpp): the single InterruptShield `interrupt` (ride.cpp:67) covers the WHOLE run — prepare, sim loop, seal, finalize, plotting, close — not just a seal+finalize section. interrupt.deferred() is read only at terminal() (ride.cpp:96) and hooks.close_requested (ride.cpp:130), i.e. during the simulation loop; both merely set reason="operator_stop". After the loop, seal(reason) (ride.cpp:156, journal still open: emit_all runs inside it at :113) and recording::finalize (ride.cpp:166 — plain finalize, NOT finalize_guarded) run with no deferred() check anywhere. grep confirms the ONLY production `operator_stop_deferred` emission in native/src is lifecycle.cpp:241 — reachable solely via run_research (ride.cpp:211, `--research`/bike-research). The physical_headless/physical_viewer journal can therefore never contain the kind RECORDING.md:177-181 explicitly lists for ordinary physical runs (`episode_start`… `operator_stop_deferred`) and that RECORDING.md:317-320/336-338 claims was live-verified ('a second SIGINT during finalization is deferred behind the write, journaled as operator_stop_deferred, then honored'). The second disjunct of clause 3 also fails: RECORDING.md does not scope the kind to research journals — it assigns it to physical runs. (c) Honoring is also absent on the physical leg: a deferred interrupt inside publish is counted on `interrupt` and silently swallowed — no OperatorInterrupted (finalize_guarded, the helper that throws it at interrupt.cpp:13, has zero production callers — only tests), and no exit-code correction (ride.cpp:183 checks only reason=="operator_stop"; every other frontend returns `interrupt.deferred()?130`: cli/settle.cpp:46,48,59; cli/replay.cpp:28,79,117; cli/batch.cpp:78; cli/research.cpp:193,203).

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
REPRO-1 (missing journal + silently swallowed interrupt, physical headless): `bike-ride --record --headless --duration N` where the loop ends via duration_reached (terminal() at ride.cpp:98, not :96). seal() (ride.cpp:156) runs seal_episode()/physical_summary/derived_events/add_episode/emit_all — the journal is still open (RunCapture state collecting until finalize sets it `publishing` at finalize.cpp:11). A SIGINT arriving during seal() — or during recording::finalize (ride.cpp:166), whose DuckDB write/hash/link stages (publish.cpp:48-92) take real wall time — is counted by `interrupt` (ride.cpp:67) but never observed: no `operator_stop_deferred` is emitted anywhere in the function, and no deferred() check runs after :130. Result: the published run.duckdb `events` table lacks `operator_stop_deferred` (violating RECORDING.md:180,336-338), no OperatorInterrupted is raised, and the process exits 0 (ride.cpp:186, reason==duration_reached) — the operator's Ctrl-C is dropped entirely. REPRO-2 (physical viewer/second Ctrl-C): first SIGINT during the sim → terminal() sees deferred()≥1 → reason=operator_stop → exit loop → second SIGINT during seal()/finalize()/preview write (ride.cpp:157-177) → deferred()=2 → still no journal event; exit 130 only because the FIRST interrupt set reason — the second is unrecorded and unhonored as a distinct fact (RECORDING.md:319-320 claims it is journaled). REPRO-3 (research, drain-window leak): in finish(stop=true), drain_active (lifecycle.cpp:217-222) executes BEFORE the inner shield is installed at :223; a SIGINT deferred there lands on the OUTER shield (research.cpp:142), so inner shield.deferred()==0 → publish commits with no `operator_stop_deferred` even though the signal was deferred before the commit point — honored only as exit 130 at research.cpp:203. REPRO-4 (misattributed audit fact): the journaled event hardcodes deferred_during="publication" (lifecycle.cpp:241) even when the signal actually arrived during seal()/annotate_failure — the row claims a deferral window that did not occur. REPRO-5 (test-suite gap): no test delivers SIGINT through cli::ride_command; the physical-journal assertions emit `operator_stop_deferred` from test code itself (test_native_recording_lifecycle.cpp:579,645,951) — production emission is faked, not verified; the comment at :574-577 ('the native ride frontend emits the same kind through research::ResearchRun::finish') is true only for --research, and the file header's claim (:16-17) that the tests mirror 'the same shielded seal+finalize composition as cli/ride.cpp' is false (ride.cpp shields the whole run and calls plain finalize).

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No valid scenario is wrongly blocked: the constraint restates the documented product contract (RECORDING.md:177-183 assigns `operator_stop_deferred` to ordinary physical journals; :336-338 specifies deferred-behind-write → journaled → honored; NATIVE_POLICIES.md:109 states 'Finalization defers SIGINT through the publication boundary and then honors the interruption'). Non-recording runs (capture.enabled()==false, no EpisodeJournal exists) are outside scope, and capture-off paths in ride.cpp:104-114 branch before any journal is used. One drafting ambiguity worth flagging: read literally, 'the physical frontend emits operator_stop_deferred when interrupt.deferred() is observed while the EpisodeJournal is still open' would also force journaling the FIRST honored interrupt (terminal() at ride.cpp:96 observes deferred() while the journal is open), whereas RECORDING.md:319-320 scopes the kind to 'a second interrupt … deferred behind the write'. A faithful port needs a baseline/nested shield to distinguish the two; the current single shield makes deferred()≥1 for the rest of the run after the first Ctrl-C. That ambiguity does not rescue the implementation (it emits nothing under either reading), but the contract should disambiguate it.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even where the boundary holds (research), invalid/inaccurate states are admitted: (1) finish() retry duplicates the marker — seal() dedupes episodes (:144-145) but the deferred-check at :240-244 runs on every non-idempotent attempt, so a second SIGINT during a retried seal appends a second `operator_stop_deferred` row; no dedupe on kind. (2) The journaled payload is wrong about the window: deferred_during is hardcoded "publication" (:241) for interrupts that arrived during seal/annotate/drain — an audit-log accuracy defect inside an audit-log property. (3) The event emission itself is best-effort: event() is wrapped in catch(...) at :241-243 ('Observability cannot break the shielded publish'), so a failed emit silently produces a journal missing the event while the rest of the contract holds. (4) received_wall_s is stamped at emission time (event(), lifecycle.cpp:84) — after seal — not at signal receipt, so the journal records a late receipt time. (5) Schema admits forged content: InputEvent::validate (capture.cpp:63-69) requires only a nonempty kind and an object `values`; a hand-emitted operator_stop_deferred with deferred_during="bogus" or arbitrary applied_step passes validation — the kind's payload contract is enforced by no one. (6) Physical-side inconsistency admitted: a published recording can carry summary reason=operator_stop with zero deferred events — no seal/finalize invariant relates summary reason to journal content, so RECORDING.md's claimed vocabulary is unenforced. (7) The honoring primitive is dead code: recording::finalize_guarded (interrupt.cpp:6-14) exists solely for callers without their own shield, but no production caller exists — any future frontend forgetting to check deferred() post-finalize inherits the swallow bug silently.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Pick one side of the OR and implement it; today neither holds. Option A (match RECORDING.md): port _journal_deferred into physical_run — record a deferred-count baseline (or nest a fresh InterruptShield) around the seal+finalize section; after seal(reason) at ride.cpp:156 and before recording::finalize at :166, emit journal->emit("operator_stop_deferred", wall(), step) when the section-deferred delta is positive; then honor post-publish by calling recording::finalize_guarded (or re-checking deferred() after finalize) and treating a nonzero count as operator interrupt → exit 130. Also make the post-finalize exit path consult interrupt.deferred() so a seal/finalize-time Ctrl-C on a duration_reached/end_of_track run cannot exit 0. Option B (scope the doc): amend RECORDING.md:177-181 to drop `operator_stop_deferred` (and fix `episode_start`→`episode_started`, which native physical also never emits — ride.cpp:119,147 emit `episode_started`) from the physical kind list, and reword :317-320/:336-338 to name research journals (bike-research / bike-ride --research) only. Whichever option: (i) set deferred_during at lifecycle.cpp:241 from the actual stage ("seal" vs "publication") rather than hardcoding "publication"; (ii) dedupe the marker across finish() retries; (iii) decide whether a SIGINT during drain_active (before the inner shield at :223) should be journaled — currently it is deferred pre-commit yet never journaled; (iv) add an end-to-end test that delivers SIGINT through cli::ride_command's seal/finalize instead of the current test-emitted journal.emit fakes (test_native_recording_lifecycle.cpp:579,645,951), and fix the misleading comments at :16-17 and :574-577.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P18`: `ReplaySession` Checked-Replay Certificate Soundness, Mismatch Halt & Command-0 Verified-Frame Rollback

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_engineering_contract`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Clause (b) is concretely violated on the command-0 failure path: `ReplaySession::Impl` member-init `verified(frame)` (native/src/replay/session.cpp:95) copies `frame` while it is still the default-constructed `runtime::RenderFrame{}` — the real validated t=0 `environment->render_frame()` is assigned to `frame` only later in the ctor body at :102 and is never copied into `verified`. `verified` receives its first real boundary only from `capture_verified()` (:139), invoked after a successful non-final command (:239) or at the end of `finish()` (:182). So a mismatch raised while `command == 0` executes `p.frame = p.verified` (:251) and exposes an EMPTY frame — `model_sha256 == ""`, `state.integration_state` empty, `view`/`model_status` empty, `presentation` zeroed (`model_fields` are six empty vectors) — not 'the validated t = 0 environment->render_frame()' the statement promises. The failure is masked in the C++ suites because tests assert only `snapshot().step == 0 && generation > 0` (native/tests/replay/test_session.cpp:165; native/tests/reference/test_replay_session.cpp:183-189), which the default frame satisfies coincidentally. A secondary over-rollback exists on `finish()` failure: after the last command's transition/observation/applied/rider_commands evidence all matched and `command` hit `requested.size()` (:238-239), `finish()` runs endgame checks without capturing the post-last-command boundary; a `finish()`-time failure (e.g. final-state mismatch) rolls back to `verified` = the boundary captured after command N-2, one verified boundary earlier than the last verified one. Clause (b)'s halt holds in-try: `p.mismatch` is set for every exception from the try block (:241-251) and subsequent `advance()` throws before touching the environment (:217); `restart()` is the only escape and re-runs the complete ctor battery. However, `advance()` argument-validation rejections (`wall_budget`, `target_step`, :215-216) propagate outside the try and do NOT halt the session — a strict reading of 'any mismatch or failure' is narrower in code. Clause (a) is sound on the iff-chain: `check_execution` runs at construction (:96) and inside `finish()` (:168) before the certificate is minted (:173-182); every recorded `requested` row is compared once per command (step exact at :224, `time_s`/`control`/brakes via the `last_requested()` wire echo at :230), every `transitions[i]`/observations[i+1] entry compared (:234-235), `applied`/`rider_commands` journals cursor-drained with completeness enforced at :143-145, `mjSTATE_INTEGRATION` checked at :100 (initial) and :167 (final) under the asymmetric `1e-9 + 1e-9*|expected|` rule (:55-67), and `executable_sha256` is re-hashed live at :169-170 because `platform::build_identity()` re-hashes the on-disk image on every call (native/src/platform/provenance.cpp:117) — likewise `engine::identity()` re-hashes the loaded MuJoCo library file per query (native/src/engine_call.cpp:523). The 'complete recorded-evidence battery' phrase overreaches, though: several recorded `summary`/`status` fields are never fed to the comparator (sufficiency gap below). Clause (c) holds inside the reachable envelope — non-viewer loop catches advance() exceptions and prints `session.diagnostics()` to stdout then exits 1 (cli/replay.cpp:109-118), interrupt exits 130, user-close exits 2, and `REPLAY ERROR:` is reserved for exceptions escaping the inner try (:119-121); viewer-mode mismatch sets `session.error()` so `hooks.terminal` halts stepping. But the command-0 bug leaks into (c): the empty rollback frame's `model_sha256 == ""` trips `frame.model_sha256 != renderer->model_sha256()` in the viewer loop (native/src/viewer/ride.cpp:127-130), `hooks.model_image` is unset in `replay_command`, and the run aborts with `REPLAY ERROR: viewer received a new model without an owned image supplier` — no diagnostics JSON is ever printed for a genuine advance() mismatch.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
REPRO 1 (verified-frame rollback content): take a contract-3 or legacy-schema-2 episode; tamper `transitions[0]` (flip `numerically_valid`, or bump `physics_steps` as native/tests/replay/test_session.cpp:155-166 and native/tests/reference/test_replay_session.cpp:172-192 already do). `ReplaySession session(dir,0)` succeeds (initial state/observation verified). `session.advance()` throws at the `transition 0` compare (session.cpp:234). Now inspect `session.render_frame()` / `session.snapshot()`: `model_sha256` is the empty string (a real frame carries `model_image_.sha256`, native/src/runtime/runtime.cpp:883), `state.integration_state` is `[]` rather than the recorded `initial_state` vector that `require_state` validated at :100, and `presentation` is all zeros. The statement requires the validated t=0 `environment->render_frame()`; the implementation returns a default `RenderFrame{}` because `verified` was copy-initialized before `frame` was assigned (declaration order `frame, verified` at :81; init `verified(frame)` at :95 vs. body assignment at :102). Root cause confirmed by grep: `verified` is assigned only at :139 via `capture_verified()`, first reachable at :239 after command 0 has already completed. REPRO 2 (viewer-mode masking): `bike-replay --viewer` on the same tampered recording: `hooks.advance` swallows the mismatch (:97-100), `hooks.frame()` returns the default rollback frame, `checked_rollback` is true (ride.cpp:118), then `frame.model_sha256 != renderer->model_sha256()` (:127) fires because the default sha is empty; `hooks.model_image` is unset, so the viewer throws `viewer received a new model without an owned image supplier`, escapes `run_replay`, and lands in the outer catch -> `REPLAY ERROR:` on stderr + exit 1 with no diagnostics JSON — violating the statement's per-advance-failure diagnostics guarantee. REPRO 3 (over-rollback on finish() failure): tamper `states.npz`/`integration_states` `final` row; advance past all commands — after the last command's evidence matched, `finish()` throws at `final integration state` (:167) and rollback restores `verified` = the frame captured after command N-2, although the post-last-command boundary's transition/observation/applied evidence had all verified — the 'last verified RenderFrame' was never captured (:239 sends final commands to `finish()` instead of `capture_verified()`). REPRO 4 (tolerance edge): `physics_steps`/`step`/`next_cursor` integer leaves compare under `max(1e-9, 1e-9*max(|a|,|e|))` (session.cpp:43-44) — at magnitudes >1e9 a ±1 step drift would pass; bounded in practice by `kMaxExactStep = 2^52` (native/src/runtime/research.cpp:70) and exact `count()`/step-alignment checks at input.cpp:190-197 and session.cpp:224, so theoretical only. REPRO 5 (non-halting rejection): `session.advance(-1.)` or `advance({}, target < frame.step)` throws `std::invalid_argument` (session.cpp:215-216) and the session remains fully live — subsequent valid `advance()` calls continue and can still mint a certificate; 'halts permanently' thus excludes argument-validation failures, which the statement does not carve out.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No wrongful blocking found. Every exclusion maps to a documented product decision: (1) `replay_eligible` gating of failed/incomplete/error prefixes (input.cpp:112-113, 129-133) is RIDE.md § Checked replay's 'not eligible to claim completed successful replay' and RECORDING.md's analyzable-but-not-replayable contract; (2) the `not_finished` reason normalization (session.cpp:146-147) and the `operator_stop` reproduction via `environment->stop()` (:149-152) deliberately keep prefix replays and operator-interrupted recordings replayable — consistent with 'A saved not_finished prefix is valid replay input'; (3) `check_execution`'s rejection of `replay_contract_version == 1` and of missing `execution_identity` (rebuild.cpp:216-218) is the documented same-environment-only identity gate (RECORDING.md § Replay execution identity across platforms) — a cross-platform recording is rejected by design, not wrongly; (4) `inert_rider` throwing on non-default removed seated-rider knobs (rebuild.cpp:80-87) is the recorded decision that those recipes were never emitted by a shipped config; (5) older containers missing `channel_rider_commands` are admitted with the compare skipped (input.cpp:164-166, session.cpp:125-126), matching 'older containers predate it and skip the compare'; (6) recordings requiring `rider_command_recording == program_inputs` (rebuild.cpp:173-175) follow the recorded rider-program input contract. The constraint is strict but calibrated; simulation-decisions.md and RIDE.md record no valid scenario it wrongly excludes.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even granting the statement, a tampered recording can still earn `passed == true` when fields outside the compared battery are corrupted: `finish()` compares only `field(summary,'outcome')` (with normalized reason), `field(current,'metrics')`, the four research keys {terminated,truncated,numerically_valid,max_energy_residual_ratio}, conditional `model_status`, conditional `episode_metrics`, and the final state (session.cpp:146-181). It never compares `input.summary`'s `first_failure`, `energy` (accounting energy ledger), `component_work_j`, `airtime_threshold_s`, `duration_s`, `rider_support`, `equilibrium`, `recording_extents`-adjacent status facts, the `crash`/`balance_event` objects' detail fields (time/position/pitch/cause — only the `crashed`/`balance_lost` booleans inside `outcome` are compared), `status`'s `accounting`, `torque_delivered_nms`/`torque_requested_nms`/`demand_integral_nms`, `max_shock_stroke_m`/`max_fork_travel_m`, `samples_attempted`/`samples_dropped`, `noise_cursor`, `recorded_rows`, `requested_count`/`completed_count`, `applied_base`, `cleanup_notes`, `stop_requested`, or `research.error` (a non-null recorded error is rejected at load, but a runtime-produced error text on an outcome-matching run would go uncompared). `run_metadata.operator_intervention` is *injected into* the evidence rather than compared (session.cpp:156-158) — correct for determinism, but it means recorded metadata is trusted, not verified. A recording rewritten under a preserved manifest is impossible for both loaders (legacy `file_sha256` binds file bytes, input.cpp:144-149; container `file_sha256.run.duckdb` + `row_counts` + `run_id` bind the DB, recording/reader.cpp:53-79), so the manifest-equality guard in `restart()` (session.cpp:259) is cryptographically sound — but within a *consistent* tampered bundle (hashes recomputed), the unverified fields above sail through and the certificate still claims the complete battery. Additional admitted-invalid edges: (1) the command-0 rollback exposes a never-verified default frame while `diagnostics()` reports `last_verified_step = 0` — a coherent-looking lie about what was verified; (2) a `finish()`-time exception leaves `environment` stopped/mutated mid-verification while `frame()` shows the pre-last-command boundary — internally consistent but one verified boundary stale; (3) the exposed `frame()`/`snapshot()` on a budget yield (session.cpp:233) is an unverified mid-command render — by design and generation-signaled, but a caller reading `snapshot()` during yields sees unverified physics; (4) `IncompatibleExecution` raised inside `finish()` is recorded in `mismatch` as generic status `mismatch` in `diagnostics()` (the `incompatible-execution` status override exists only in `check()`, session.cpp:271), so CLI consumers lose the distinction; (5) a recorded `Number` lexeme that overflows finite double (`1e999`) reaches `io::as_double` inside `compare_tree` and throws plain `invalid_argument` rather than `MismatchFailure`, so `diagnostics().first_mismatch_field` is null for a genuine evidence mismatch.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
1) Clause (b): require `verified` to be seeded with the validated initial boundary — after `frame = environment->render_frame()` at session.cpp:102, assign `verified = frame` (or call `capture_verified()`), so a command-0 failure rolls back to the real validated t=0 frame whose `model_sha256`/`integration_state`/`presentation` match `model_image()` and `initial_state`. State explicitly that the rollback target is the last `capture_verified()` boundary, and either capture the post-last-command boundary before `finish()`'s endgame checks or document that a `finish()` failure presents the pre-last-command frame by design. 2) Clause (b): carve out argument-validation — 'mismatch or failure' should be defined as exceptions raised inside the verification step; `std::invalid_argument` for `wall_budget_s`/`target_step` at session.cpp:215-216 is caller-contract rejection and does not enter `FailedHalted`. 3) Clause (a): replace 'the complete recorded-evidence battery' with an enumerated battery (the fields actually compared), or extend `finish()` to also compare `first_failure`, `energy`, `crash`/`balance_event` detail and the status counters, so 'complete' is literally true; state that `run_metadata` is injected for determinism, not verified; note that per-physics-step `physics`/`forces`/`channel_*` rows are out of scope (control-boundary granularity) since the statement already implies that scope. 4) Clause (c): after fixing (b), the viewer-mode command-0 path uniformly yields the held verified frame + diagnostics JSON + exit 1; document that a rollback frame with an unexpected `model_sha256` currently degrades to `REPLAY ERROR` without diagnostics (resolved by the seed fix), and that `diagnostics()` distinguishes mid-run `IncompatibleExecution` as status `incompatible-execution` symmetrically with `check()`.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P19`: Viewer Replica Isolation, Monotone `(generation, step)` Presentation, Pacing & GPU Destruction Order

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_confirmed_requirement`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Probed all four clauses against source. (a) RenderOwner::apply (render_owner.cpp:68-110) is verbatim kinematic-only: the engine::invoke lambda at :98-105 issues exactly mj_setState(mjSTATE_INTEGRATION)+mj_kinematics+mj_comPos+mj_camlight+mj_tendon on its own replica mjData; grep over native/src/viewer shows no mj_forward/mj_step/sensor/qfrc anywhere in the TU. It writes only the six declared mutable model arrays (render_fields.hpp:14-21: dof_frictionloss, site_pos, tendon_stiffness/damping/lengthspring/range; copied at :92-93), enforces non-decreasing lexicographic (generation,step) at :73-76 and model_sha256 identity at :72; every validation throw (:72-88) precedes the staged frame copy (:90) and all replica mutation — confirmed by tests/viewer/test_render.cpp:75-88 (rejection leaves last accepted replica intact). (b) ride.cpp:31-32 declares GlfwContext before the RenderOwner unique_ptr, so unwind destroys the owner first; explicit renderer->close() at :166 runs before member teardown on normal return; close() (render_owner.cpp:165-171) frees mjrContext/mjvScene while the window's GL context is still current; Impl::~Impl (glfw_context.cpp:57-60) refcounted glfwDestroyWindow/glfwTerminate. (c) Pacer dt is ctor-fixed and never mutated (pacer.cpp:11,19-21); set_scale routes through speed_for_action which throws outside {1,2,4,8} (input.cpp:33-42); offered=min(raw_steps,1e6) (pacer.cpp:49-51); debt=0+rebase_pending on pause/scale/reset (:23-33) and re-zeroed at :40-41; terminal_drawn (ride.cpp:138) forces request_frame before the draw block (:142-148) and the close_on_terminal break (:159). (d) legend latched per generation (ride.cpp:150-157), first_failure dedup'd per message and reset per generation (:135,153-156), reset clears braking/pause and re-arms stopped/terminal_drawn (:60-72), callers seal before reset (cli/ride.cpp:147, research.cpp:20). Found four boundary defects (below) that sit around, not inside, the stated gates, plus one genuine (d) violation.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
1) Signed-overflow UB before the gate: ride.cpp:107 `room = INT64_MAX - before.step` — `before` comes from `hooks.frame()` refreshed unvalidated at :95-99/:68/:124; RenderOwner::apply (which would reject step<0 at render_owner.cpp:73) runs only at :143, after the arithmetic. A custom SessionHooks::frame returning state.step=-1 on a request_frame refresh reaches UB at :107-108 in the same iteration (needs terminal=false, unpaused, steps>0). All shipped producers emit step>=0 (capture_frame snapshot.cpp:11 throws on negative; playback.cpp:223 uses a size_t index), so this is reachable via the public viewer::run(SessionHooks) API, not the shipped CLIs. 2) Unchecked null returns in the replica ctor: engine::load_model_image returns nullptr (not a throw) for image.size()>INT_MAX (engine_call.cpp:564-566) — a sha-verified >2GiB mjb passes render_owner.cpp:45-46, loads nullptr at :49, then `p.model->ngeom` at :56 is a null deref; engine::make_data likewise returns nullptr (engine_call.cpp:586,594) → later mj_setState(m,nullptr,...) UB at :100. playback.cpp:185-187 and :194-195 null-check the same calls — RenderOwner does not. 3) (d)-clause violation — missed user close + skipped drain: a GLFW close request processed inside glfwWaitEventsTimeout at ride.cpp:160 (window.wait(.005)) is only observed by the `while(window.open())` head at :47, which exits WITHOUT setting result.closed_by_user (only :48/:59/:89 set it) and WITHOUT calling stop() (:162). Every paused/terminal/zero-offer iteration ends in that wait, so clicking the window close button while paused reliably lands in the blind spot: RunResult misreports a user-initiated close and hooks.stop — the research pending-interval drain (research.cpp:17) — is silently skipped (cli/ride.cpp:148-151 only papers over `reason` for the ride path). 4) Mutation-before-admission on the engine-failure path: the six model-field writes at render_owner.cpp:92-93 execute before the fallible engine::invoke at :98-105; on mj failure the replica fields carry an unadmitted payload while p.frame keeps the old cursor — masked only because `poisoned` (:38,:106) makes every later observation throw. 5) Equal-cursor rewrite admitted: apply((g,s)) then apply((g,s)) with a different integration_state payload is admitted silently (non-decreasing, :73-76) — the gate orders cursors, not content. 6) Same-generation time regression admitted: the gate covers (generation,step) only; a frame with step+1 but smaller time_s passes apply; only AchievedRate::update (ride.cpp:141) rejects it afterwards, and it is skipped by design in Mode::inspection. 7) close() frees GL objects under whatever context happens to be current (render_owner.cpp:169 calls mjr_freeContext/mjv_freeScene with no glfwMakeContextCurrent); correct inside run()'s single-window flow but an undocumented currency precondition for any future multi-window caller. 8) terminal_drawn's 'one full render pass' degenerates on a 0x0 framebuffer — draw() early-returns (render_owner.cpp:114) yet close_on_terminal still exits.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No valid documented scenario is wrongly blocked. The {1,2,4,8} scale ladder and the fail-closed rejection of other --time-scale values are stated product requirements (docs/RIDE.md:94-97). Fixed physics dt is itself the requirement: 'Playback never edits the integration timestep' and 1x-vs-8x byte-identical outcomes (docs/RIDE.md:98-104) — the pacer scales only the offer count, never dt, and docs acknowledge achieved RTF << requested scale. Backward seeking in inspection is unblocked by design: every seek/speed/reset/loop bumps `generation` (cli/replay.cpp:39-42,57,67,71,63), satisfying the monotone gate — the same contract ReplaySession uses for verified-boundary rollback (session.cpp:247-251) and restart (session.cpp:256-258), and NativeRideRuntime uses for reset (runtime.cpp:956-961). A fresh model is not wrongly blocked either: hooks.model_image exists precisely for replacement (session.hpp:39-40) and clock_generation monotonicity (ride.cpp:132-136) is required of producers, which honor it (session.cpp:258 carries generation+1 across restart). The integration_state[0]==time_s exact check is consistent with the enforced mjSTATE_INTEGRATION signature (playback.cpp:132, replay/input.cpp:123). Bounded-input aborts (4096-event queue, glfw_context.cpp:53-55; wait<=0.1s, :151; <=16384px window, :66) are fail-closed resource bounds, not scenario blocks. duration_s overshoot is <1 timestep (ride.cpp:110-114).

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
The gate validates cursor order, model identity, shape, and finiteness — not physical content. Still admitted even if the statement holds: (i) equal (generation,step) re-emission with a different payload silently rewrites the presented frame (render_owner.cpp:73-76); producers never do this today (render_frame copies under a guard, runtime.cpp:872-884; playback frames are immutable), so it is a producer-discipline reliance. (ii) Finite-but-physically-inconsistent integration_state and model_fields (e.g., negative tendon stiffness, off-limit qpos, unnormalized quaternions) pass all checks and are rendered — presentation, not certification, by design. (iii) A negative-step frame is not 'admitted' but causes UB at ride.cpp:107 before the gate ever sees it — an admitted-invalid class upstream of admission. (iv) Same-generation time_s regression passes apply (gate covers only the cursor); non-inspection modes catch it downstream at AchievedRate (ride.cpp:141), inspection mode deliberately admits it. (v) Unvalidated frame.state.view/latest_sample/outcome/first_failure flow into the HUD — readers are defensive (hud.cpp:23-36) but malformed wire data can produce arbitrary console/overlay output and large staged copies. (vi) A >2GiB sha-verified image crashes the ctor instead of throwing (finding 2). (vii) A window-close event during glfwWaitEventsTimeout is admitted as an exit but with wrong journal attribution and no drain (finding 3).

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Keep clauses (a)-(c) as written — they hold verbatim. Revise to close the four boundary gaps: (1) extend the monotone contract into the driver: validate `frame.state.generation>=0 && frame.state.step>=0` on every hooks.frame() result inside run() (ride.cpp:68,95-99,124) before it reaches the arithmetic at :107 — or expose the same admission predicate run()-side so the cursor is validated once, upstream of use. (2) Journal semantics: treat any `window.open()->false` loop exit as user-initiated — set result.closed_by_user and invoke stop() on that path too (e.g., check `!window.open()` again after window.wait at ride.cpp:160 or at loop exit), since glfwWaitEventsTimeout processes close requests the :89 check cannot see. (3) Null-check engine::load_model_image/engine::make_data in the RenderOwner ctor (render_owner.cpp:49-50) exactly as playback.cpp:185-187,194-195 do, so an oversize/OOM load throws rather than dereferences null. (4) State the GL-context currency precondition of RenderOwner::close() (or make the owning context current before mjr_freeContext/mjv_freeScene) — today correctness is an emergent property of run()'s single-window discipline. (5) Decide and document whether equal (generation,step) re-admission is a tolerated cursor-rewrite or should require strictly increasing cursors outside resets; producers currently rely on generation bumps for every rewind (session.cpp:250), so tightening to 'reject equal cursor with different payload' is feasible. (6) Optional: stage the six model-field writes after the engine invoke or record them inside `staged` so an unadmitted payload never lands in the replica even on the (already masked) poison path.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P20a`: Batch Worker-Pool & Subprocess Group Lifecycle

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `mixed_subclauses`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
The statement's three clauses are not all true of the code. Clause 1 (reaped child can never re-enter ~ChildOwner via released-at-reap) is violated: the only disarm point is `child.released = true` at native/src/platform/process.cpp:386, AFTER the drain loop exits, whereas `reaped = true` is set at :311 inside the loop. Between reap and :386 several throwing paths remain live whenever a pipe fd is still open (`reaped && open_fds > 0` does not break at :318): `fail_errno` on poll error (:347), `fail_errno` on read error (:358), and `std::bad_alloc` from `end.sink->append` (:369). Any of these unwinds into ~ChildOwner (:252-257) with released==false, so the dtor runs `killpg(id, SIGKILL)` + blocking `waitpid` on an ALREADY REAPED pid: waitpid returns ECHILD instantly (benign), but killpg targets pgid==pid which, once the group is empty, is a recyclable process-group id — a TOCTOU SIGKILL of an unrelated recycled group. Clause 3 (macOS add_chdir guards on MIN_REQUIRED or a runtime check) is violated by inspection: :116 guards `#if __MAC_OS_X_VERSION_MAX_ALLOWED >= 260000` — the SDK ceiling, not `__MAC_OS_X_VERSION_MIN_REQUIRED` (deployment target), and there is no `__builtin_available`/weak-import runtime check anywhere in add_chdir (:114-132). Clause 2 (grace->SIGKILL ordering) holds for the intended path (:296-301 SIGINT then deadline-clamp, :304-307 SIGKILL at expiry) but two boundary defects: (a) the SIGINT at :297 is not gated on `!killed`, so when timeout_s fired first the group receives SIGINT AFTER SIGKILL — the strict ordering is interleave-dependent; (b) `validate_run_inputs` (:164-172) only requires finite/>0, so timeout_s=1e300 or cancellation_grace_s=1e300 pass validation and then overflow `duration_cast<Clock::duration>` (:273-275, :299-300) — UB per [time.duration.cast]; on real toolchains the wrapped deadline can land in the past, turning a 'never' timeout into immediate SIGKILL or collapsing the grace window so SIGKILL follows SIGINT within the same loop iteration (ordering nominally preserved, grace not honored).

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
1) Reaped-child dtor re-entry: `platform::run({'/bin/sh','-c','setsid cat & exit 0'}, cwd)` — sh exits, waitpid reaps at :311, but the out-of-group `cat` still holds the inherited pipe write-ends so open_fds>0; a subsequent read/poll error or bad_alloc in `result.out.append` throws -> ~ChildOwner killpg(stale pgid, SIGKILL). The simpler trigger is `ProcessOptions{max_output_bytes}` pressure plus an OOM — hard to force, but poll/read failures on the fds are reachable (e.g. POLLNVAL-adjacent errors, EIO). 2) macOS portability: build with `-isysroot MacOSX26.sdk -mmacosx-version-min=14.0`; MAX_ALLOWED>=260000 selects `posix_spawn_file_actions_addchdir` (:118), a POSIX.1-2024 symbol absent on macOS<26 runtimes -> first platform::run aborts on lazy bind failure. Correct guard is MIN_REQUIRED or `if (__builtin_available(macOS 26.0, *))` with `_np` fallback. 3) Duration overflow: `ProcessOptions{.timeout_s=1e300}` passes :164-167; `Clock::now() + duration_cast<Clock::duration>(duration<double>(1e300))` is UB and typically wraps negative -> deadline < now -> SIGKILL on the first loop pass, killing a healthy child instantly. 4) Ordering inversion: `ProcessOptions{.timeout_s=0.05}` on a >50ms child fires SIGKILL at :305; a stop_token requested at 60ms still sends SIGINT at :297 — SIGINT delivered after SIGKILL.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
The ordering requirement itself blocks no documented scenario, but the mechanism's parameters are unreachable from the product layer: batch::execute builds `ProcessOptions` internally and sets only `.cancellation` (process_pool.cpp:58); ExecuteOptions (plan.hpp:29-34) exposes neither `timeout_s` nor `cancellation_grace_s`. RECORDING.md:316-320 requires a Ctrl-C'd child to publish sealed evidence — children do install InterruptShield and defer SIGINT during finalize (cli/research.cpp:142, research/lifecycle.cpp:223, recording/interrupt.cpp:8) — yet the parent's grace is hard-wired to 5 s (process.hpp:39). RECORDING.md:325-326 documents a recording-on run with ~193 MB run.duckdb / 3.11 GB RSS finalization; sealing that can exceed 5 s, and :336 concedes SIGKILL before finalization loses the in-RAM recording. So the property's own 'allow final-only recording publication, then kill/reap' (process.hpp:37) is under-covered for the documented large-recording sweep scenario, with no batch-level knob to extend it. Additionally the hard-coded SIGINT offers no SIGTERM alternative and no per-job timeout: a worker child that ignores SIGINT AND survives the SIGKILL-adjacent group escape (below), or simply hangs with no cancellation requested, blocks execute() forever.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even granting the statement, these invalid states are admitted: (a) Group-escape defeats both group-kill and EOF-boundedness — a child doing `setsid`/`setpgid` while inheriting the pipe write-ends leaves the group; killpg can't reach it, so after the leader is reaped `open_fds` never reaches 0 and run() blocks past timeout_s AND past the SIGINT->SIGKILL cycle indefinitely, contradicting the header's 'cancellation kills and reaps the whole group, never a leftover grandchild tree' (process.hpp:5-6) and 'a grandchild holding a pipe open cannot outlive the budget' (process.cpp:302-303); the drain loop has no post-reap EOF cap. (b) Recycled-pgid kill window: killpg at :297/:305/:365 and in ~ChildOwner (:254) is unguarded whenever the group might be empty — admitted TOCTOU against an innocent recycled group. (c) exit_code conflation: 130 means 'cancelled before spawn' (:189), 'cancelled before worker launch' (process_pool.cpp:56), and genuine SIGINT death — indistinguishable to result_record/summarize; the non-WIFEXITED/non-WIFSIGNALED fallback collapses stopped/continued statuses into 255 (:397), colliding with a real `exit 255`. (d) ~ChildOwner's blocking `waitpid` (:256) on a D-state child ignores SIGKILL until kernel dispatch — a noexcept dtor can hang inside unwinding. (e) spawn_mutex serializes only platform::run callers (:191-195); a concurrent system()/library-internal spawn during pipe()->fcntl() still leaks a non-CLOEXEC fd and starves EOF; `process_environment()` (:134-142) reads global environ racily vs setenv. (f) Non-glibc Linux (musl) falls to the final `#else` (:129-131) unconditionally using the POSIX.1-2024 name — build-time-only failure, but the portability matrix is silently narrower than stated. (g) Pool relay_thread (process_pool.cpp:48) forwards a jthread stop that is never requested — dead cancellation path; harmless but it means worker threads' own tokens can never cancel the pool.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Restate as three separate clauses with code-faithful invariants: (1) Child-reap safety: '~ChildOwner never signals an empty process group: the armed flag is cleared at the reap site (process.cpp:311), and every killpg site (loop :297/:305/:365 and dtor :254) is either guarded by the reap state or documented as an accepted recycled-pgid TOCTOU; the dtor skips waitpid when the leader is already reaped.' Optionally add a post-reap drain bound (close parent read-ends N seconds after reap) so out-of-group fd-holders cannot hang run(). (2) Cancellation ordering: 'On first stop_request, killpg(SIGINT); SIGKILL is sent no earlier than cancellation_grace_s later and is suppressed once the child is reaped; once killed==true no SIGINT is sent. timeout_s and cancellation_grace_s must be finite and <= Clock::duration::max() expressed in seconds (validate at :164-172); ExecuteOptions shall expose both so batch callers can cover recording finalization.' (3) Portability: 'add_chdir selects the POSIX.1-2024 spelling only under a deployment-target guard (__MAC_OS_X_VERSION_MIN_REQUIRED >= 260000 / __GLIBC_PREREQ(2,40)) or a runtime __builtin_available(macOS 26, *) check with the _np fallback; SDK version alone never selects a newer runtime symbol.'

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P20b`: CLI Cross-Mode Validation & Jobs Grammar

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_engineering_contract`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
All three conjuncts fail on reachable inputs. (1) Validation parity: sweep.cpp:45 is `(void)cli::parse_arguments("bike-research", views);` — the parse result is discarded and `cli::validate_research_arguments` (declared cli/application.hpp:17, defined cli/configuration.cpp:172, invoked by batch.cpp:34 as `if(!parsed.help)validate_research_arguments(parsed)`) is never called, and nothing in sweep.cpp or plan.hpp:48 documents reliance on per-child validation (the only comments, sweep.cpp:53-54, are about output ownership). Semantic errors that parse fine but validate_reject (configuration.cpp:173-190: `--demand`+`--demand-file`, `--posture` without articulated_planar, nonpositive `--duration`/`--control-period`/`--sensor-period`/`--energy-tolerance`, negative `--motor-torque`/`--human-torque`/`--motor-limit`, bad `--record-decimation`, `strict`+`--diagnostic-model-limits`, malformed `--seed`) are caught only inside each spawned child (research.cpp:207 -> configuration.cpp:196 -> exit 2), i.e. after create_directories at sweep.cpp:56 and pool dispatch — output tree, per-seed logs and a failure sweep.json are produced where bike-batch exits 2 before any output exists. (2) Jobs grammar: sweep.cpp:35 `const auto count = std::max<std::int64_t>(1, args.integer("jobs"));` is literally the prohibited `std::max(1,jobs)` clamp — `--jobs 0` and `--jobs -2` both reach `create_directories` (sweep.cpp:56) and run one worker, whereas batch.cpp:22-24 throws `--jobs must be positive` before even reading --spec (test_batch.cpp:244-260 pins exit 2 with no output dir). Only `>UINT_MAX` (sweep.cpp:36) and non-integer tokens (arguments.cpp:84-95 via integer_token:39-50) are rejected. (3) Honest worker count: sweep.cpp:69 records `integer(args.integer("jobs"))` — the *requested* value, not the clamped `count` actually used — so a rejected-in-spirit input writes `"jobs": -2` or `"jobs": 0` while one worker ran; batch reports the effective count (batch.cpp:71-77 `workers=`, `summarize(..., static_cast<unsigned>(count))`).

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
1) `bike-research-sweep --out R --seeds 1 --jobs 0 -- --duration 1` → sweep.cpp:35 clamps to 1 → R created at :56 → real episodes run → exit 0; expected per statement and per test_batch.cpp:244-260: exit 2, no R. Same for `--jobs -2` and `--jobs=0` (grammar at sweep.cpp:28-31 accepts both spellings). Even `--dry-run` admits the invalid flag: `--jobs 0 --dry-run` prints commands and exits 0 (sweep.cpp:49-52), while batch rejects --jobs before the dry-run branch (batch.cpp:22-24 vs :37). 2) `bike-research-sweep --out R --seeds 1-3 -- --demand x --demand-file y` → parse_arguments succeeds (arguments.cpp:125-178), no validate call at sweep.cpp:45 → R created → each child exits 2 via resolve_research (configuration.cpp:174,196; research.cpp:208) → sweep exit 1, but with a populated output tree and sweep.json, versus bike-batch's pre-dispatch 'job <id>: choose one of --demand and --demand-file' exit 2. Identical divergence for `-- --motor-torque -1` (configuration.cpp:189-190), `-- --duration -3` (:180), `-- --control-period 0` (:183-184), `-- --check-policy strict --diagnostic-model-limits` (:173), `-- --seed notdecimal` (:178). 3) `bike-research-sweep --out R --seeds 1 --jobs -3` then read R/sweep.json: field "jobs" = -3 (sweep.cpp:69) though exactly one worker ran — dishonest worker count. 4) Admitted-invalid bonus: `bike-research-sweep --out R --seeds 9-1` or `--seeds=` → parse_seeds returns {} (plan.cpp:178-209, reversed range loop never enters; empty parts skipped) → execute returns empty results (process_pool.cpp:33-34) → all_of on empty range → exit 0, R created, sweep.json `{"jobs":N,"runs":[]}` — a silent green zero-job sweep, contradicting the repo's own 'Nothing selected is an error, never a green zero-unit sweep' convention (native/tests/tooling/tooling_sweep_engine.cmake:120).

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
None found. No doc (docs/RIDE.md, docs/RECORDING.md, docs/native-workflow.md:41-45, simulation-decisions.md) assigns a meaning to `--jobs 0` or negative counts; the in-code default is already hardware_concurrency (sweep.cpp:17), so 0-as-auto is not needed, and the usage string itself (`[--jobs N]`) implies a positive count. Requiring `validate_research_arguments` parity cannot block any valid scenario: every argument set that passes it is definitionally launchable, and batch already enforces the identical predicate on the identical final argv (batch.cpp:31-36, including the `!parsed.help` carve-out). The only arguably-blocked behavior is `--dry-run` previewing a not-yet-valid passthrough — but batch establishes the same validate-before-dry-run order, so parity does not introduce a new restriction class. Recording the effective `count` in sweep.json blocks nothing; requested-vs-used diverge only on already-invalid inputs.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even with all three clauses enforced, these remain admitted: (a) Empty seed selection — `--seeds ''`, `--seeds ','`, `--seeds 9-1` — yields zero jobs, creates the output root, writes `{"runs":[]}` and exits 0 (plan.cpp:193-209, process_pool.cpp:34, sweep.cpp:55-71); no `seeds.empty()` guard exists anywhere, so a meaningless green sweep is still possible. (b) Value-domain errors outside validate's reach: nonexistent `--track-file`/`--demand-file`/`--gen-spec`/`--physics-config` paths, or JSON-schema-invalid `--demand` payloads, still fail only per-child — batch has the identical residual (configuration.cpp:200, :244 stat nothing during validation), so parity does not make sweep fully fail-closed for file-domain inputs. (c) `--jobs` values in (INT64 range, ≤ UINT_MAX] — e.g. 4294967295 — are accepted (sweep.cpp:36 only bounds above) and silently capped by `min(workers, jobs.size())` at process_pool.cpp:77, so the recorded/requested worker count can grossly overstate the pool that ran even for 'valid' input. (d) Unbounded seed-width: `parse_seeds` accepts arbitrary-length decimal strings (plan.cpp:185-186 normalize only digits); a 300-digit seed produces job.id 'seed-<300 digits>' whose filename exceeds NAME_MAX and fails only inside the child at spawn time. (e) Passthrough may contain `--out`/`--seed`/`--help`: later-appended tokens silently override (parse last-wins, arguments.cpp:104-105) — batch instead rejects a spec 'out' key outright (plan.cpp:169) — so a user-supplied --out is silently shadowed rather than diagnosed. None of these are blocked by the statement as written.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
In sweep.cpp: (a) replace the clamp at :35 with the batch predicate — `const auto count = args.integer("jobs"); if (count < 1 || std::cmp_greater(count, std::numeric_limits<unsigned>::max())) throw cli::ArgumentError("--jobs must be positive");` — so rejection precedes create_directories and matches batch.cpp:22-24/test_batch.cpp:244-260 (exit 2, no output). (b) Change :45 to mirror batch.cpp:34 — `const auto parsed = cli::parse_arguments("bike-research", views); if (!parsed.help) cli::validate_research_arguments(parsed);` wrapped in a `job <id>` error prefix — keeping the `!parsed.help` carve-out so a `-- --help` passthrough still previews instead of erroring; this makes the fail-closed boundary identical across modes rather than relying on undocumented per-child validation. (c) Record the effective count at :69 (`integer(count)`, matching batch's workers=count at :71-77) or rename the field to `requested_jobs` to make the echoed semantics explicit. (d) Add `if (seeds.empty()) throw cli::ArgumentError("--seeds selects no runs");` after :34 to close the green zero-job sweep admitted via `--seeds ''`/`9-1`. (e) Add a sweep-side test mirroring nonpositive_worker_count_is_rejected_before_output (test_batch.cpp:246-261) and a semantic-rejection case (`--demand`+`--demand-file`) asserting exit 2 with no output root created.
