# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P20c`: Evidence Fingerprint & Source-Immutability Gate Scope

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_engineering_contract`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Clause 1 (fingerprint scope) fails on BOTH disjuncts of the statement. `source_fingerprint` (native/src/validation/evidence.cpp:91-108) walks only `root/"native/src"` selecting extensions {.cpp,.hpp,.h,.cmake,CMakeLists.txt} (:95-96). Under the walked root there are ZERO .cmake/CMakeLists.txt files (`find native/src -name '*.cmake' -o -name CMakeLists.txt` = 0), so the CMake arm of the contract is vacuous at the only root ever passed (scenarios.cpp:133, benchmark.cpp:236,259,270,280, experiments.cpp:137,262,274, drivetrain.cpp:107). Meanwhile the root omits every real CMake input: native/cmake/*.cmake (22 files incl. BikeNumerics.cmake which configure_file()s a product header at :573-575), native/tests/CMakeLists.txt, root CMakeLists.txt, CMakePresets.json. Under the walked root itself, tracked and compiled-but-unfingerprinted C++ source exists: native/src/recording/catalog_data.inc (#include catalog.cpp:13 — the telemetry catalog schema compiled into the evidence product), native/src/cli/argument_contracts.inc (#include arguments.cpp:13 — the validator's own CLI contract), native/src/gpu/cuda_kernels.cu (#include cuda_kernels_host.cpp:8 and metal_batch_runner.mm:6; built into bike_gpu_kernels at CMakeLists.txt:657-658), native/src/gpu/metal_batch_runner.mm (OBJECT lib CMakeLists.txt:711-718), and native/src/platform/numerics_symbols.hpp.in (configure_file template consumed by blas_numpy_openblas.cpp:30, lapack_accelerate.cpp:28, blas_accelerate.cpp:24, lapack_numpy_openblas.cpp:27 — the numerics-adapter layer environment_contract claims to identify). So neither '.inc/templates included' nor 'tracked C++/CMake source files' describes the implementation. Clause 2 (lattice-wide vs narrowed gating): the narrowed reading holds only for the four report types that declare the flag, and even there benchmark_suite's primary `passed` verdict excludes `unchanged`; several pass-verdict report types declare no flag at all (contact_resolution experiments.cpp:274→279-281, drive_suspension_comparison drivetrain.cpp:107→121, run_replay/rider_replay scenarios.cpp:133→136-139, rider_factor_matrix scenarios.cpp:145-153, reference_matrix experiments.cpp:262-268, automatic_coast scenarios.cpp:155-157), escaping the narrowed scope entirely. Clause 3 (two-tier explicit): the three fields are emitted (benchmark.cpp:249-250) but no scope text ties `unchanged` to the gates (contrast environment_contract's `scope` string evidence.cpp:131 and `version_scope` benchmark.cpp:239); `mechanics_gate_passed` additionally has silent preconditions — `complete_suite` AND `dts=={0.0005,0.00025,0.000125}` (benchmark.cpp:248) — with no reason field.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
A) Mid-run `.inc` edit: during `bike-validate run benchmarks` (39 cases x 3 dts) edit native/src/recording/catalog_data.inc or native/src/cli/argument_contracts.inc — both are #included into product TUs, tracked in git — evidence.cpp:96 ignores `.inc`, so source_unchanged_during_run stays true and passed/mechanics_gate_passed/release_gate_passed can all be true over changed tracked C++ source. B) Template edit: edit native/src/platform/numerics_symbols.hpp.in mid-run → changes the generated numerics_symbols.hpp baked into BLAS/LAPACK adapter TUs (blas_numpy_openblas.cpp:30, lapack_accelerate.cpp:28) — the numerics provider layer — invisible to the runtime fingerprint. Note the build-time manifest DOES hash .in (CMakeLists.txt:193), so runtime and baked fingerprints diverge in coverage. C) Build-logic edit: edit native/cmake/BikeNumerics.cmake, Checks.cmake, root CMakeLists.txt or CMakePresets.json mid-run — all outside the walked root — invisible. D) Stale binary: checkout/revert source without rebuilding, then run — `source_sha256` records the LIVE tree (evidence.cpp:106) while the binary executes old code; nothing compares the report fingerprint to the baked `build_identity::native_source_sha256` (CMakeLists.txt:284, runtime/provenance.cpp:93-94) — `source_unchanged_during_run:true` over evidence the binary does not implement; environment_contract (evidence.cpp:124-131) emits executable_sha256 but no source-identity match. E) Flag-less pass-verdict reports: `bike-validate run contact_resolution|drive_suspension_rig|rider_replay|rider_factor_matrix|reference_release` — edit a fingerprinted .cpp mid-run; `passed`/`completed_requested_duration`/`baseline_resumed`/`causal_replay_resolved` still true; `source_sha256` is start-of-run only; no source_unchanged_during_run field exists (experiments.cpp:274,279-281; drivetrain.cpp:107,121; scenarios.cpp:133-139,145-153). F) CLI acceptance keys off the non-gated tier: case_benchmarks → report_result default key "passed" (registry.cpp:80,193) → `accepted:true`, exit 0 (validate_main.cpp:50) with source_unchanged_during_run:false. G) Edit-and-revert between the start (benchmark.cpp:236) and end (:247) fingerprints → unchanged:true over a run whose middle executed on modified inputs — the point-in-time hash admits a contamination window by construction. H) write_release_manifest copies `source_sha256` verbatim from the reference_matrix report recorded at run start (experiments.cpp:262; acceptance.cpp:183-184) into the hash-verified manifest without re-verifying the end tree.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No valid product scenario is wrongly blocked. The fingerprint is content-based, not git-clean — a dirty worktree with uncommitted edits still produces unchanged=true provided the tree is stable during the run, so ordinary dev/CI workflows are unaffected. Partial/custom benchmark runs legitimately earn `passed` without the mechanics/release gates (the two-tier exists precisely so exploratory non-canonical runs can report criteria evidence without claiming release provenance). The only arguable friction: mechanics_gate_passed hard-pins the canonical grid dts=={0.0005,0.00025,0.000125} plus complete_suite (benchmark.cpp:248), so a deliberate alternate-grid convergence verification can never earn the mechanics gate — but that gate is defined as the canonical matrix verdict, a designed restriction rather than a wrongly blocked scenario (the same run still gets `passed` and full case evidence). Runs with a bad --root fail loudly via the is_directory/empty-set throws (evidence.cpp:92,99) rather than silently passing — no necessity violation.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even where the flag exists, invalid states are admitted: (1) `.inc`/`.in`/`.cu`/`.mm` edits and all of native/cmake, native/tests/CMakeLists.txt, root CMakeLists.txt, CMakePresets.json are invisible — including the validator's own CLI contract (argument_contracts.inc) and the telemetry catalog schema (catalog_data.inc): the evidence schema itself is unfingerprinted. (2) Stale-binary evidence: the live-tree fingerprint is never bound to the compiled image's baked native_source_sha256 or to a verified executable_sha256; environment_contract's locked_environment_verified (evidence.cpp:124-131) covers only the dependency lock and loaded MuJoCo image, so release_gate_passed (benchmark.cpp:250) admits evidence from a binary that does not contain the recorded source. (3) Pass-verdict reports that never declare the flag (contact_resolution `passed`, drive_suspension `passed`+status, rider_replay `completed_requested_duration`, rider_factor_matrix `baseline_resumed`/`causal_replay_resolved`, automatic_coast evidence) are accepted verdicts blind to mid-run source edits. (4) benchmark_suite's `passed` — the field run_case/CLI `accepted` and exit code key on — is fail-open to source edits by design but with no declared scope text, so downstream consumers reading `passed` alone get a non-fail-closed verdict; `mechanics_gate_passed` also hides `complete_suite`+exact-dts preconditions (benchmark.cpp:248) yielding unexplained false. (5) edit-and-revert between the two point-in-time hashes leaves unchanged=true over contaminated evidence. (6) The .cmake/CMakeLists.txt filter arms are vacuous — zero such files exist under native/src — so the description 'native source SHA-256' (registry.cpp:76) implies CMake coverage the implementation never exercises.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Make the contract self-describing and coverage-complete: (a) declare the walked root and extension set verbatim in the report (a `source_scope`/`fingerprint_scope` string alongside `source_sha256`, the way environment_contract emits `scope` at evidence.cpp:131), e.g. 'tracked inputs under native/{src,cmake,tests} plus root CMakeLists.txt and CMakePresets.json matching {.cpp,.hpp,.h,.inc,.in,.cu,.mm,.cmake,CMakeLists.txt}' — extending source_fingerprint to include .inc/.in/.cu/.mm and to walk `native/` (or repo root) so the CMake arms are not vacuous; alternatively narrow the stated contract explicitly to 'compiled C++ TUs only, excluding included-data .inc, configured .in templates, GPU .cu/.mm and all CMake' if that residue is accepted by design. (b) Emit the baked build_identity::native_source_sha256 in environment_contract and require compiled-source identity to match the live fingerprint for any gate-tier verdict, closing the stale-binary hole; reconcile the two manifest coverages (.in is build-hashed but not run-hashed). (c) For every report type that emits a `passed`-family verdict, either include `unchanged` in `passed` or emit explicit tier fields plus a `pass_scope` string stating `passed` excludes provenance — apply this to benchmark_suite (rename the non-gated tier `criteria_passed` or gate `passed`), contact_resolution, drive_suspension_comparison, run_replay/rider_factor_matrix/automatic_coast, reference_matrix/release_manifest, and surface mechanics_gate_passed's complete_suite/canonical-dts preconditions as named fields with a failure-reason term. Until then, treat the statement's '.inc/templates included' and 'tracked C++/CMake source files' readings as unmet and the narrowed gating scope as under-declared.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P21a`: `Sha256` Single-Finish Protocol

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_engineering_contract`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
The statement claims a single-finish protocol exists via one of two disjuncts; NEITHER is present in the code. (a) No latch: native/src/io/hash.hpp:25-30 declares only state_, block_, total_, pending_ — no finalized_ member — and update() (native/src/io/hash.cpp:13-27) and finish() (:29-48) contain no guard. (b) No documented contract: the header comment (hash.hpp:1-6) explains only WHY the streaming type exists ('file-hash consumers ... share the identical compression code'); a tree-wide search for 'single-use|single use|finalized' finds no Sha256 contract text, and no doc under docs/ states misuse-is-undefined. So misuse is not 'undefined by contract' — it is DEFINED by the implementation as silent deterministic corruption: finish() mutates block_/pending_/state_ and leaves pending_ in [0,56] (never >56, so block_[pending_++]=0x80 stays in bounds), meaning every post-finish call is memory-safe but semantically garbage. Tail-invariant nuance: 'no silent second finish() result reuse' is narrowly satisfied — the second call does NOT reuse the first result; it computes a NEW corrupt digest (state absorbs an extra padding block). The property's mechanism claim is false while its narrow tail invariant happens to hold, and all 9 in-tree construction sites (engine_call.cpp:371, platform/files.cpp:209, recording/duckdb.cpp:502, validation/evidence.cpp:100, initialization/state_file.cpp:80, setup_owner.cpp:110, equilibrium_cache.cpp:191, hash.cpp:114, tests/io/test_json.cpp:870) are function-local, finish exactly once, then discard — misuse is latent, unreachable from current product code.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
Compiled native/src/io/hash.cpp standalone (clang++, -std=c++20) and exercised misuse sequences. Legal path verified: Sha256('abc').finish() == ba7816bf8f01cfea414140de5dae2223b00361a396177a9cb410ff61f20015ad (FIPS vector). Violations demonstrated: (1) h.update('abc'); h.finish() -> ba7816bf...; second h.finish() -> 67a16d5b9f7f2bcb22d52d910e974ea219118700d8d19496d42de2557e6f39b2 — a well-formed 32-byte CORRUPT digest with zero diagnostic, indistinguishable from a valid digest downstream; third finish -> e4e0abdd... (yet another value). (2) finish(); update('d'); finish() -> e60d96f3aed01c51f38587d4dad2b5040922b58e2482fc06a87faaf747c00aaf, NOT sha256('abcd')=88d4266f... — update() after finish() silently continues hashing into a state that already absorbed padding (total_ keeps counting at hash.cpp:14 while block_ retains the padded final block). (3) Boundary len==55 mod 64: leftover pending_==56, second finish() writes 0x80 over the length-field MSB (hash.cpp:31) and takes the pending_>56 branch -> two garbage blocks -> c3004634... (4) Boundary len==56 mod 64: leftover pending_==0, second finish compresses [0x80][zeros][original bit_count] -> c79330a7.... No OOB or crash in any misuse path; the failure is silent semantic corruption — the worst mode for an integrity/identity primitive (hash.hpp:1-4 calls it 'the owned integrity/content-identity primitive').

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
None found. No product scenario in simulation-decisions.md, RIDE.md, or RECORDING.md requires double-finish, mid-stream digest readout, or update-after-finish. Every reachable call sequence is construct -> update* -> exactly one finish() -> discard; recording digest design docs (docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-03-gpu-shared-s0-goldens.md:592) even chose fresh-object-per-block (block_ = io::Sha256{}) over re-finishing. The copy idiom (copy the Sha256 mid-stream, finish both copies — a legitimate forked-stream use since the class is trivially copyable) is NOT a second finish on one object and would remain legal under either disjunct. A finalized_ latch or documented single-use contract would block zero valid behavior; the constraint is under-enforced, not over-blocking.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even if the statement held, the class admits states outside its meaningful domain: (1) A post-finish Sha256 remains a first-class value — storable, copyable, passable to update()/finish() — so 'invalid state' is representable rather than excluded by construction (no typestate/[[nodiscard]]-on-update design, no consuming finish()). (2) The latch would not certify input correctness: engine_call.cpp:367-383 and platform/files.cpp:207-222 hash file bytes with no post-read re-stat (TOCTOU — a file swapped mid-read yields a clean digest of inconsistent bytes; only recording/duckdb.cpp:511-514 re-fstats). (3) noexcept+unconditional return means enforcement can only be terminate/assert or a poisoned return — a silent 'poisoned digest' would be worse than today's garbage; nothing guards total_ overflow semantics beyond spec-conforming mod-2^64 wrap (hash.cpp:30). (4) Hashing unintended bytes (uninitialized buffers, wrong span length — all update() sites slice spans manually, e.g. files.cpp:219-220) produces equally clean digests; single-finish says nothing about byte provenance. (5) Prefix-forgery via copy: copying the object pre-finish and finishing the copy yields a valid digest of a prefix — admitted by design, and indistinguishable to consumers from a full-stream digest.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Rewrite as: 'Sha256 is a single-use streaming primitive: update() may be called zero or more times, then finish() exactly once; any update() or second finish() after the first finish() is a contract violation and its result is undefined.' Enforce at near-zero cost by documenting that sentence on the class in native/src/io/hash.hpp (it already describes why the type exists — add the contract) AND adding 'bool finalized_ = false;' plus assert(!finalized_) at the top of update() and finish(), setting finalized_ = true in finish() before returning (assert fits noexcept: debug/asan builds diagnose, release builds keep today's codegen and ABI). This converts silent deterministic garbage — currently indistinguishable from a valid digest to integrity consumers (engine-image identity engine_call.cpp:366-392, recording-inode check duckdb.cpp:497-517, fingerprints state_file.cpp:75-82) — into a debug-time diagnosis, while preserving the verified FIPS vectors and every existing call site unchanged.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P21b`: Lossless IO Decoders & FD Ownership Boundary

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `mixed_subclauses`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Challenged all five clauses with reachable inputs: (1) JSON SAX pushdown — exercised RapidJSON kParseNumbersAsStringsFlag lexeme retention (json.cpp:1814-1826, SaxHandler json.cpp:185-290), enforce_finite ordering (json.cpp:1381-1403 vs parse_recording_json json.cpp:1831-1836), duplicate-key rejection (json.cpp:247-257,1521), NUL/BOM/surrogate rejection (json.cpp:1794-1808,242-245), decimal/date/time/timedelta/bytes tag folds (json.cpp:1414-1531) and escaped-object recursion (json.cpp:1504-1529). (2) base64 — diffed b64_decode_strict (json.cpp:339-397) case-by-case against fetched CPython 3.13.0 binascii_a2b_base64_impl: accept/output sets verified EQUIVALENT, but the json.cpp:320-328 comment misattributes the padding_started/goto-done shape to 3.14 — CPython 3.13.0 already has it — and several error-message selections diverge from actual 3.13 ('A=BB' -> port 'number of data characters' vs 3.13 'Discontinuous padding'; 'AA===' -> port 'Excess padding' vs 3.13 'Excess data after padding'). (3) NPY — header literal grammar (npy.cpp:75-86), dtype/shape/product bounds (npy.cpp:30-40,89-94,119-134), Fortran indexing (npy.cpp:50-57), bool canonicality and string domains. (4) NPZ — EOCD scan/comment bound (npz.cpp:99-112), ZIP64 locator+member extras (npz.cpp:106-110,126-129), central/local consistency (npz.cpp:132-135,143-144), data-descriptor flags (npz.cpp:120), and the DEFLATE bit/Huffman engine vs zlib inftrees semantics (npz.cpp:25-89). (5) FileHandle — ctor/dtor/move-assign/release/close (files.cpp:70-106) and write_exclusive unwind (files.cpp:177-185). Result: one concrete losslessness violation (decimal mathematical-digit value mapping); the FD exactly-once claim holds; remaining items are admission-set asymmetries vs the stated oracles, not crashes or double-closes.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
VIOLATION — decimal canonicalization corrupts 40 of the 50 mathematical Unicode digits. kNdRanges merges the five contiguous 10-digit blocks into one 50-codepoint range {0x1d7ce,0x1d7ff} (json.cpp:475), which is correct for membership in is_nd_digit (json.cpp:480-494) but wrong for value mapping: decimal_digits computes 'value = cp - lo' (json.cpp:566-573), so every codepoint past the first 10-block yields 10..49, emitting characters ':'..'a' instead of '0'..'9'. Reachable via parse_recording_json on {"$bike_recording":"decimal","value":"<U+1D7D9 MATHEMATICAL DOUBLE-STRUCK DIGIT ONE>"}: is_nd_digit accepts (it is a Unicode Nd digit that CPython 3.13's Decimal accepts, Decimal('1')), then produces canonical ';' instead of '1' — a silently wrong Tagged{decimal} that subsequently fails emit-side validation (json.cpp:1620-1623), so the decode is neither lossless nor round-trippable. Value corruption is worse via the exponent path: 'value':'1e<U+1D7D9>' -> exp_digits=';' -> explicit_exp = (';'-48) = 11 (json.cpp:709-716) -> canonical '1E+11' where Python yields '1E+1' (~1e11 vs 10 — silent numeric corruption, no error). 'value':'NaN<U+1D7D9>' -> 'NaN;' vs Python 'NaN1' (json.cpp:662-675). Only the first block U+1D7CE..U+1D7D7 (mathematical bold digits) maps correctly; tests cover Arabic-Indic only (test_json.cpp:430-432), so this edge is unexercised. NO OTHER confirmed compliance violations found: RapidJSON NumberTooBig rejection of positive exponents >~308 (reader.h:1666-1672 of pinned commit 24b5e7a8, applies under NumbersAsStrings) is explicitly a stated bound (json.hpp:33-38) and required by RECORDING.md:170 ('unbounded numeric overflow are rejected'); base64 strict accept set equals CPython 3.13.0; FileHandle closes exactly once across normal/throw paths (files.cpp:72-106,177-185); partial mutation is unobservable because the SAX tree is local and discarded on failure.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No confirmed product scenario is wrongly blocked, but four accept-domain restrictions are narrower than the stated NumPy/CPython oracles: (a) npz.cpp:93-94 member_name rejects '..' ANYWHERE, so a legal np.savez member 'a..b.npy' that np.load reads is rejected — a defensible anti-traversal bound but over-strict vs the oracle for third-party NPZ input (decode of legacy replay states.npz at legacy.cpp:167). (b) npy.cpp:110-116 strings() forces 'S' payload bytes through valid-UTF-8 checking — NumPy '|S' is arbitrary byte storage; a valid foreign .npy holding non-UTF-8 byte strings is rejected where np.load returns bytes (interior NULs are admitted, only trailing NULs are canonicalized — consistent). (c) Consumer-side (not decoder) hardening: replay/legacy.cpp:171,174 call doubles() with the finite default, so a legacy recording whose recorded initial/final state diverged to NaN/Inf is unreadable even by the portable '--view' unverified-playback path (RECORDING.md:301-305) although the decoder offers doubles(true) — a valid failed-run evidence scenario (RECORDING.md:73-75,114-121 retains diverged runs) is hard-blocked. (d) npy.cpp:91 rejects any single dim >128MiB and npy.cpp:83 caps shape at 16 dims — both narrower than NumPy (32 dims; dims are unconstrained when product is 0), marginal since payloads are 128MiB-bounded anyway. The documented RapidJSON positive-exponent rejection (json.hpp:34-38) is a confirmed REQUIREMENT (RECORDING.md:170), not a wrongly-blocked scenario.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Several invalid states remain admitted under the held clauses: (1) sync_directory (files.cpp:163-175) omits both O_NOFOLLOW and the fstat opened-object validation used everywhere else in the boundary contract (files.cpp:40-53,108-132) — it follows a swapped symlink and fsyncs a non-directory object silently; publish.cpp:85,100 calls it on controlled dirs, but the primitive itself admits a wrong-object-type state. (2) parse_npz over-accepts vs the zlib oracle: nd>30 distance trees admitted (npz.cpp:59 checks nd>32; zlib inftrees rejects ndist>30), and Huffman ctor (npz.cpp:36-41) only rejects oversubscription — incomplete code-length/literal/distance trees that zlib rejects are decoded successfully for their assigned symbols. (3) npz.cpp:120 allows general-purpose flag bit 3 (data descriptor); the 12/16-byte descriptor after the compressed data is left as an unverified gap — extents check (npz.cpp:144) catches overlap, not coverage, so arbitrary bytes between member end and central directory are admitted. (4) NpyArray::validate() (npy.cpp:94) enforces size only: a '|b1' payload byte != {0,1} is admitted and, through doubles()/integers() (npy.cpp:97-107), yields e.g. 2.0/2 where NumPy astype canonicalizes to 1.0/1 — only booleans() rejects it (npy.cpp:109), an inconsistent canonicality boundary. (5) Header::decimal accepts leading-zero shape dims '(007,)' that Python literal_eval rejects (npy.cpp:23-27,83). (6) read_numpy_bytes (npy.cpp:143-149) detects mid-read truncation/growth only; a fully-serviced content swap inside the st_size window is undetectable — file_sha256 likewise (files.cpp:207-223); publish's post-link rehash (publish.cpp:83) is the only mitigation. (7) emit_tagged checks only the base64 alphabet for bytes tag lexemes, so a constructed Tagged{bytes,'A==B'} serializes to output that cannot re-decode (json.cpp:1613-1619, documented). (8) decimal_from_number_lexeme's isinf branch (json.cpp:868-869) is unreachable via parse_recording_json because enforce_finite runs first (json.cpp:1831-1836) — test-pinned consistent (test_json.cpp:334), but the dead branch is an admitted-ordering artifact.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Revise to: 'Within the declared bounds (kMaxJsonBytes/kMaxDepth; kMaxNumpyBytes/kMaxNumpyMembers; the documented RapidJSON positive-exponent and NPY-header limits), parse_json/parse_recording_json preserve number lexemes verbatim and reproduce the CPython 3.13 accept sets for base64 strict, ISO date/time/datetime and Decimal lexemes with canonical value mapping — including per-block digit values across merged Nd ranges; the NPZ reader accepts the pickle-free NumPy/ZIP32/ZIP64-subset domain and may admit a superset of zlib-strict DEFLATE streams (incomplete Huffman tables, nd in (30,32], unverified data-descriptor gaps) provided decoded output is exact for all assigned symbols; NpyArray::validate() canonicalizes or rejects non-{0,1} '|b1' payloads uniformly across doubles()/integers()/booleans(); read_numpy_bytes/file_sha256 provide stat-bounded single-descriptor reads with best-effort change detection (not full TOCTOU exclusion); RAII FileHandle performs at most one close() per owned descriptor including move-assignment and all unwind paths, and write_exclusive's exclusive-create→write→fsync→explicit-close boundary surfaces descriptor close errors. Malformed inputs reject without observable partial mutation of caller-visible state; decoder admission vs canonicality policy (bool payloads, member-name '..', '..'/'S' UTF-8 domains, sync_directory opened-object validation) is pinned explicitly.' Concretely: split the merged 0x1d7ce-0x1d7ff range into the five 10-digit blocks (or store an explicit per-block base) at json.cpp:475 so decimal_digits emits '0'..'9'; add S_ISDIR/O_NOFOLLOW (or an explicit exception comment) to sync_directory at files.cpp:163-175; decide and document zlib-strictness policy for Huffman completeness/ndist>30 at npz.cpp:36-59.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P21c`: `SeedSequence`/`NumpyRng` Spawn Counter Integrity

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_confirmed_requirement`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
The primary clause is NOT implemented. SeedSequence::spawn (native/src/random/seed_sequence.cpp:243-257) contains no overflow check: the loop bound `n_children_spawned_ + n_children` (lines 246-247) is evaluated in uint32 and wraps silently, and the only throws in the TU are std::invalid_argument (lines 58-60, 137, 148, 153) — no std::overflow_error exists anywhere in seed_sequence.cpp. The oracle it claims statement-for-statement parity with (numpy_compat.hpp:1-10 pins NumPy 2.5.2) DOES throw at this boundary: upstream bit_generator.pyx spawn() is `cdef uint32_t i; for i in range(self.n_children_spawned, self.n_children_spawned + n_children)`, and Cython's conversion of the range bound to uint32_t raises OverflowError when counter+n_children >= 2^32, before any child is constructed and before `self.n_children_spawned += n_children` commits. In the port the wrap is masked only incidentally: children.reserve(n_children) (line 245) must allocate ~72*n bytes, so wrap-inducing inputs (n_children >= 2^32 - counter) throw std::bad_alloc/std::length_error on strict-allocation platforms — the wrong exception class vs both the stated contract and the oracle. On an overcommit allocator (vm.overcommit_memory=1 Linux, a platform this project supports via tools/linux/container.sh) the ~310 GB reservation can succeed on virtual memory alone: the wrapped bound is then < counter, the loop runs ZERO iterations, spawn() returns an EMPTY vector, and `n_children_spawned_ += n_children` (line 255) wraps the counter DOWN (e.g. 2 -> 1). Subsequent spawn() calls then re-issue already-used spawn_key indices, producing bitwise-duplicate child streams — precisely the dishonest-counter/duplicate-stream failure the property forbids. The second clause holds: NumpyRng is move-only via unique_ptr<Impl> (numpy_compat.hpp:158-161, 199) so no two generators can alias one stream position; detail::next32 buffers the high half-word while next64 never touches the pending half (numpy_compat.cpp:119-136), matching pcg64.c; seed_bitgen clears has_uint32/uinteger (lines 102-103); the (0,)=noise/(1,)=dropout child split is verified bitwise against the oracle fixture (noise_tape.cpp:45-48; test_seeded.cpp:750-775). Caveat: every method on a moved-from NumpyRng dereferences a null impl_ (numpy_compat.cpp:167+) — reachable UB with no guard.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
R1 (oracle-mapped boundary): `rnd::SeedSequence s(1234); s.spawn(2); s.spawn(0xFFFFFFFEu);` — counter+n_children = 2^32, so the oracle raises OverflowError => std::overflow_error is the specified behavior; actual: children.reserve(0xFFFFFFFE) attempts ~309 GB => std::bad_alloc on macOS/strict Linux, or under overcommit returns {} and wraps counter to 0. R2 (silent corruption on overcommit): `s.spawn(2); s.spawn(0xFFFFFFFFu);` — bound wraps 2+0xFFFFFFFF mod 2^32 = 1 < counter=2, loop runs zero iterations, returns empty vector, `n_children_spawned_` wraps 2 -> 1 (seed_sequence.cpp:246-247, 255); then `s.spawn(1)` issues spawn_key (1,) which the first spawn(2) already issued — both key-(1,) children yield identical generate_state_u64(4)/NumpyRng streams (duplicate deterministic streams, the exact harm). R3 (error-class violation): for every wrap-adjacent input the thrown type is std::bad_alloc/std::length_error from vector::reserve, never the specified std::overflow_error; there is no code path that can produce overflow_error. R4 (coverage): no test exercises the boundary — test_seeded.cpp covers counters <= 4 (lines 320-441) plus spawn(0) (lines 821-825); the guard cannot be regression-tested because it does not exist. Note: reaching counter ~2^32 through honest spawns requires ~310 GB of live children, so R2 additionally needs an overcommit allocator (or a VM-happy operator new) — on strict-alloc macOS the reachable outcome is the bad_alloc wrong-class of R1/R3 rather than silent corruption.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
Nothing valid is blocked. Every product caller uses fixed small counts: SensorNoiseSource's spawned_streams does SeedSequence(seed).spawn(2) (noise_tape.cpp:45-48) and make_sensor_tape does seed.spawn(2) (research/rider_random.cpp:23-25); terrain/rider paths never spawn at all (they construct NumpyRng directly — terrain/road.cpp:57, terrain/generator.cpp:73, terrain/alpine.cpp:97). No scenario in simulation-decisions.md, RIDE.md or RECORDING.md approaches 2^32 children; the counter cannot even reach the boundary honestly because each spawn commits ~72 B + two heap vectors per child. The uint32 parameter further blocks an oracle quirk the other direction: upstream accepts spawn(-k) (Python int) — range(c, c-k) is empty, then counter += -k decrements and later calls REISSUE keys — the port's unsigned signature prevents that counter regression, a correct strictening rather than a wrongful block. If the guard were added it would reject only inputs the oracle itself rejects (bound >= 2^32), so necessity holds cleanly.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Invalid states admitted even if the statement held: (a) moved-from NumpyRng — unique_ptr impl_ is null after move (numpy_compat.hpp:158-161) and every draw path dereferences it unchecked (numpy_compat.cpp:167-198); SensorNoiseSource holds two movable NumpyRng members (hpp:249-250), so a moved-from source's draw() is reachable UB. (b) SeedSequence is implicitly copyable (hpp:62-99): `SeedSequence b = a;` then a.spawn(1) and b.spawn(1) issue the same spawn_key => duplicate streams; oracle-consistent via pickling but admitted aliasing the statement ignores. (c) children.reserve(n_children) (seed_sequence.cpp:245) commits n_children*sizeof(SeedSequence) upfront: spawn(~1e8) is a domain-valid call pinning ~7-8 GB plus per-child heap traffic — a reachable liveness hole the arithmetic guard does not bound. (d) generate_state_u64 computes n_words*2U in size_t (seed_sequence.cpp:234) — same wrap class as the spawn bound, masked only by the same allocation limit. (e) spawn_key depth is unbounded: each spawn appends a word (line 249) and each child ctor re-runs assembled_entropy+mix_entropy over the whole key (lines 161-200) — linear growth per depth with no cap. (f) random::NoiseTape is a public aggregate (hpp:223-228); validate() is opt-in — only build_noise_tape (noise_tape.cpp:133) and the decode path (runtime/research_io.cpp:138) call it, so post-build mutation into NaN rows / dropout >= 1 / shape skew is admitted unchallenged (the dataclass __post_init__ ran unconditionally in the source). (g) No n_children_spawned restore seam: the oracle ctor accepts n_children_spawned= for serialized reconstruction; the port omits it (hpp:62-67), so any future recording path that serializes a SeedSequence cannot represent a nonzero counter — relevant to the challenge's restore axis.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Make the guard real and ordered ahead of allocation: at the top of SeedSequence::spawn, before children.reserve, add `if (n_children > std::numeric_limits<std::uint32_t>::max() - n_children_spawned_) throw std::overflow_error("spawned too many children");` — the oracle boundary is counter+n_children >= 2^32 (the Cython range-bound conversion failing), which still permits counter+n_children == 0xFFFFFFFF (last issued index 0xFFFFFFFE), so the check must compare in 64-bit or by subtraction, never against the wrapped uint32 sum. Keep the post-loop commit `n_children_spawned_ += n_children` (line 255) so any exception — overflow_error, bad_alloc mid-loop — leaves the counter unchanged; spawn(0) remains legal and counter-neutral. Restated contract: 'spawn(n_children) throws std::overflow_error — the same class uniform() uses for the oracle's OverflowError (numpy_compat.cpp:194-195) — when n_children_spawned_ + n_children exceeds UINT32_MAX, checked before any allocation; on success the counter advances by exactly n_children after all children are constructed; on any throw the counter and the issued-key set are unchanged.' Separately, the sufficiency gaps worth folding into the same contract family: document or guard that draws on a moved-from NumpyRng are UB (null impl_), consider dropping the up-front reserve or bounding n_children to cap the liveness surface, and if a recording path ever serializes SeedSequence, add the oracle's n_children_spawned reconstruction parameter.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P21d`: `slsqp`/Least-Squares/Linear-Feasibility Status Soundness

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_engineering_contract`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Core invariant holds structurally: both slsqp converged exits gate on violation <= ftol at the returned iterate (native/src/numerics/slsqp.cpp:178-179 checks norm(qp.step)<=ftol && current_violation<=ftol; :217-218 checks |new_objective-objective|<=ftol && violation(next)<=ftol, with 'next' evaluated at the accepted candidate that becomes result.x). The violation gate is unconditional, so it also covers restoration iterations. However the stated assumption 'success exits conditioned on qp.solved' is mechanically false: neither exit tests qp.solved. After the restoration block (:159-172) overwrites qp.step with the feasibility-restoration least-squares step (:169), control flows into the same two converged gates — so a restoration iteration can in principle mint converged=true,status=0, contradicting the in-code contract comment at :160-162 ('It can yield a diagnostic iterate, never an infeasibility certificate or an optimizer success flag'). Reachability is the mitigating factor: the KKT-residual trigger (:120-122, residual > 1e-8*(1+|rhs|)) needs a linearization inconsistency of order >~1e-8, which bounds violation below by the same order — with default ftol=1e-12 the violation gate then cannot also pass, so that trigger cannot produce a false converged. The paths that remain structurally open are (a) first-block KKT inconsistency from an indefinitely-drifted BFGS hessian (the Powell guard at :208-213 bounds only s^T H s, not the spectrum) making qp.solved=false at a near-feasible iterate, and (b) active-set bound exhaustion (8*(m+n+1) iterations, :105) via add/remove cycling; then a small restoration step plus violation<=ftol satisfies :178. Both are exotic and no deterministic public-API repro was constructed — flagged as structurally reachable, not reproduced. Separately, the documented status set is incomplete: several mid-solve failures escape as std::invalid_argument rather than status=6 — component() (:21), d::validate(jac) on a nonfinite/misshaped extra jacobian (:64), the jac-shape check (:65), and d::least_squares' finite-matrix validation on a BFGS-overflowed hessian (dense.hpp:126,131) — because the catches at :154,:157,:170 only handle std::runtime_error. Same failure class, two outcomes: nonfinite extra.evaluate -> runtime_error -> status=6 (:43), nonfinite extra.jacobian -> invalid_argument escapes slsqp entirely (allocate() at rider/allocation.cpp:102 has no catch; linear_feasibility's catch at linear_feasibility.cpp:88 is also runtime_error-only, so the three-valued pipeline has a fourth, throwing, outcome). least_squares: the residual-width latch is sound (least_squares.cpp:75-77 latches first nonempty width; empty or changed width throws invalid_argument, pinned by test_sag.cpp:260). Status contract is scipy-faithful but has two warts: the stop callback is consulted AFTER a positive status latches (:341 then :346), so a stop returning true demotes an achieved status 2/3/4 to -2 (converged=false) — conservative but divergent from the reference ordering and it discards a real certificate; and status=1 (gtol on |J^T f . v|inf, :320-321) fires at any first-order point including a cost maximum — converged=true with arbitrarily large residual, reachable deterministically (see counterexamples). linear_feasibility: the three-valued pipeline holds in the sound direction — proven_infeasible is certified by outward-rounded interval arithmetic (minimum() rounds every product/sum toward -inf at :22-27, yielding a valid lower bound; bound tightening adds a +2 ulp margin at :67/:71 covering the unrounded divide at :64), and feasible requires an allocation_residual<=primal_tolerance witness (:79-86). BUT 'feasible' is relaxed: inequality_rhs is pre-biased by +inequality_slack at :40, so a 'verified linear witness' satisfies g.x <= h + slack + primal_tolerance — a nominally infeasible system violating h within that stack returns feasible with a witness that does not satisfy the queried bounds; reason='verified linear witness' overclaims. Also :87 returns witness=result.x (an unverified point) on status=unknown.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
No reproduced converged-with-violation>ftol case exists — both exits check the gate. Reachable contract deviations, by decreasing concreteness: (1) Deterministic least_squares status=1 at a cost MAXIMUM: residual f(x)=(x^2-1, 1), initial x={0.5}, bounds (-3,3): at x=0, J^T f = 2x(x^2-1) = 0 -> optimality=0 < gtol at least_squares.cpp:321 -> status=1, converged=true with cost=2 (a local max of cost); the iterate may even start there. Contract is scipy-faithful but 'converged' is not a minimum certificate; callers re-gate (sag_fit.cpp:32 rechecks magnitude; validation/drivetrain.cpp:51 ignores converged and rechecks closure). (2) Deterministic -2 demotion: any problem where the tolerance exit latches status 2/3/4 at :341 on the same iteration the caller's stop returns true -> :346 overwrites to -2, converged=false despite achieved ftol/xtol. (3) Structurally reachable slsqp restoration-minted converged: indefinite-BFGS KKT inconsistency or 8(m+n+1) active-set cycling -> qp.solved=false at a near-feasible iterate -> restoration step norm<=ftol && violation<=ftol -> :179 returns converged=true,status=0, violating the :160-162 comment 'never an optimizer success flag' and the audit assumption that success is conditioned on qp.solved; no deterministic public repro constructed (needs hessian spectral drift or cycling), source-supported only. (4) Exception-vs-status asymmetry (deterministic through the public API): AllocationProblem with an ExtraConstraint whose .jacobian callback returns a NaN entry or wrong shape -> slsqp throws std::invalid_argument (dense.hpp:31 via :64, or :65) escaping the runtime_error catches at :154/:157, while an NaN-returning .evaluate instead maps to status=6 (:43,:155). (5) linear_feasibility slack-admitted feasible (deterministic): single row g=[1], h=[0], x in [-1,1], default options -> system g.x<=0 is feasible at x<=0; adversarial variant g=[1], h=[-5e-8] (true feasible set x<=-5e-8 is empty-intersected only beyond bounds — use bounds [0,1] so g.x<= -5e-8 is INFEASIBLE) with default inequality_slack=1e-7: p.inequality_rhs becomes ~5e-8-1e-7<0 still negative... the clean repro is bounds [0,1], g=[1], h=[-4e-8]: truly infeasible, but slacked rhs ~+6e-8>0 -> the clamped witness/target path or slsqp can certify feasible for a system violating h by 4e-8; exact repro needs |h|<slack with a boundary the propagation cannot refute — the slack+tol stack (:40,:80) is the mechanism, source-supported. (6) least_squares interior() midpoint emits +-inf: a bound_stride stride rounding overshoot (select(), :222-230,:261-264) lands x+p.stride one ulp above a finite upper bound with lo=-inf -> interior() :64 sets x[i]=.5*(-inf)+.5*hi=-inf; if the user residual returns finite values at +-inf, the point can be accepted (actual>0 at :336) and the next jacobian() throws invalid_argument ('bounds have no representable difference interval', :111-112) — a solver-internal numerical artifact escaping as an input-domain exception rather than a non-converged status.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No valid product scenario is wrongly blocked. The strict 'converged only when violation<=ftol' rule is not consumed as a product decision anywhere: allocate() (rider/allocation.cpp:99-115) ignores SlsqpResult.converged/.status entirely and re-verifies via allocation_residual<=1e-7 with a 4-restart loop; linear_feasibility re-verifies slsqp output via valid() (:79-86) and never reads .converged; response_allocation.cpp:192,213 consumes only the sound direction via linear_region_infeasible (proven_infeasible), where the outward-rounded certificate is conservative — the inequality_slack relaxation only shrinks the infeasibility claim set (fewer pruned constraint modes = more QP work, never a wrong prune). The single product consumer of LeastSquaresResult.converged is sag_fit.cpp:32, which additionally requires |residual|inf<=0.5 — a converged flag on a bad point cannot bless output. docs/agents/simulation-decisions.md, RIDE.md and RECORDING.md contain no requirement for a relaxed convergence claim (the retired muscle-effort SLSQP note at simulation-decisions.md:737 is about a formula replacement, and the 12960-check native_rider_allocation gate at :875 asserts residual quality, not solver flags). ftol/slack/primal_tolerance are caller-visible knobs, so legitimate looseness is expressible without violating the invariant.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
States admitted even while the invariant holds: (a) slsqp status=0 'optimum' is not an optimality certificate — the :217 exit needs only |dObjective|<=ftol and violation<=ftol, with no step-size, stationarity or KKT check, so a large accepted step landing on a feasible point whose |x-target|^2 coincidentally matches the current one (e.g. a reflection of x about target) declares 'optimum' at a non-stationary point; :178 is stronger (step<=ftol) but still ignores multipliers/sign of the Lagrangian. (b) converged=true is structurally reachable on restoration iterations (success not conditioned on qp.solved) — the code comment at :160-162 claims the opposite. (c) least_squares status=1 converged at a local maximum/saddle of cost (first-order test only). (d) linear_feasibility status=feasible admits witnesses violating the queried inequalities by up to inequality_slack+primal_tolerance (:40,:80) — a nominally infeasible system can be reported feasible and even produce a 'witness'; and status=unknown carries a non-verified witness=result.x (:87). (e) Documented-status gaps: mid-solve failures escape as std::invalid_argument instead of status=6 (slsqp :21,:64,:65; d::least_squares dense.hpp:131 via nonfinite hessian) or FeasibilityStatus::unknown (linear_feasibility's runtime_error-only catch at :88) — the caller sees an exception where the contract promises a status. (f) least_squares can accept an +-inf iterate produced by interior()'s one-sided midpoint fallback (:64) if the residual stays finite, then throw mid-solve rather than return a status. (g) A status 2/3/4 convergence certificate can be silently demoted to -2 by the stop callback (:346).

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Restate the property to match the enforceable mechanism and close the documented gaps: (1) slsqp: 'converged=true/status=0 only when the constraint violation at the returned iterate <= ftol (L1 sum over equality |residual| and inequality shortfall), on BOTH exit gates (step-norm+violation and objective-delta+violation); success exits MUST additionally require qp.solved so restoration iterations can never mint status=0 — either add 'if(qp.solved && ...)' to both gates or document that a restoration-step acceptance may declare convergence provided violation<=ftol' (currently the :160-162 comment overclaims 'never an optimizer success flag'). (2) Map all mid-solve numerical failures to the documented status set: catch std::exception (not only std::runtime_error) around constraints()/quadratic()/restoration, or route invalid_argument from component()/d::validate(jac)/jac-shape/d::least_squares to status=6; document that callback-thrown non-runtime exceptions propagate by design. (3) least_squares: keep the width latch contract (first nonempty residual width is authoritative; mismatch/zero -> invalid_argument) and scipy status map, but do not let the stop callback demote an already-latched status 2/3/4 — evaluate stop only when status==0, or return status in {2,3,4} with a separate stopped flag; document that status=1 certifies a first-order point, not a cost minimum. (4) linear_feasibility: keep the outward-rounded proven_infeasible certificate (sound), but document that status=feasible certifies the slack-relaxed system g.x<=h+inequality_slack with residuals <=primal_tolerance — i.e. 'feasible' is relaxed-feasibility, the witness may violate the queried bounds by slack+tol; return witness={} on unknown (or rename the field to 'candidate'); catch std::exception in the slsqp fallback so every non-certificate path yields unknown, preserving the three-valued guarantee.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P22`: Configuration Mode Gates, Integer-Tick Quantization, Two-Phase Model Builders & Terrain/Kinematics Invariants

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `mixed_subclauses`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Subclause (a) mostly verifies: Element name uniqueness enforced at xml_writer.cpp:139; ComponentRegistry duplicate-ID rejection at mass.cpp:446-450; XmlBodyElement write-once inertial guard at mass.cpp:392-394 (fresh-carrier inertial() at xml_writer.cpp:114-119 genuinely bypasses it, as the property itself concedes); the 15-entry kComponentFields list (mass.cpp:253-293) and bijective expected==actual seal with part uniqueness, geom coverage and wheel-inertial checks at xml_writer.cpp:143-176 hold — BUT the seal runs at builder.cpp:63, BEFORE finish_physical_topology (builder.cpp:67-68) which adds drive_shaft and a literal 0.3 kg motor_rotor inertial (physical_topology.cpp:47-65) and rewrites crank/shaft/wheel inertials post-seal, so 'bijective sealing' covers only the registered domain, not total model mass. JointEnvelope pose-binding holds: loaded envelopes carry NaN neutral (validate.cpp:1983, 2211), joint_q_range/anatomical_angle throw via validate_joint_envelope on non-finite neutral (spindle_math.cpp:513-545), with_pose_neutral_angles binds + range-checks (rider_geometry.cpp:730-741), detail::post_init requires finite neutral (validate.cpp:1002-1005). Subclause (b) verifies only on the happy path: apply_preset commits current_preset (suspension_setup.cpp:1022) and config (1023) and updates fork clicks (1024-1025) BEFORE shock clicks (1026-1028); shock_damper.set_clicks routes through throwing damper_validate (suspension_setup.cpp:863; 'HBO bounds must be ordered' ~174-176), so the call is non-atomic — an exception leaves preset/config/fork updated and shock stale, inside the apply_preset scope the property claims is synchronized. All four members are public (suspension_setup.hpp:366-369) and current_preset defaults to base regardless of a custom ctor config (suspension_setup.cpp:1005-1018). Subclause (c-1): brake ordering verified (validate.cpp:666-678); the 1e-9 integer-tick grid verified for control_period_s (1150-1158), monitor windows, pedaling reposition windows (1195-1200), research control/sample/actuator fields (1205-1224), release_hold/shift cooldown+shift cut/stop_time/balance dwell+grace (1231-1249); tau>=2*timestep floors verified (assist profile-resolved tau 1254-1263, coast/cadence taus 1264-1272, pneumatic rate_relaxation 1273-1283, closure 1293-1300); intent-period grid checks at 876-903 and unconditional launch/reaction context checks at 1143-1149 — BUT 'every physics-clock deadline' fails: equilibrium_refine_after_s/equilibrium_refine_period_s are cycle-counted deadlines (equilibrium.cpp:25-29, 91, 128) admitted with scalar-positive checks only (validate.cpp:1285-1288) and silently round_even-rounded — a documented §12 carve-out (simulation-decisions.md:652) but a literal violation; and the research experiment boundary re-quantizes the same fields with a DIFFERENT tolerance (1e-8 ticks + std::round, research/configuration.cpp:133-143) so the domain is not enforced identically at the runtime boundary. Subclause (c-2) verifies as documented: decode_drive re-checks the identical brake domain (writer_config.hpp:1090-1107), drivetrain::validate omits the whole brake block (config_validation.hpp:207-220), the omission is explicitly documented (config_types.hpp:116-120) and defended downstream by the unconditionally constructed StaticBrake (static_brake.cpp:19-42; runtime.cpp:389-397, 428-430). Subclause (d) verifies with caveats: stroke-table memoization is content-correct under solver immutability (solver.cpp:299-313, lazy build cached in mutable member solver.hpp:275-277) but writes shared mutable state inside a const method — a latent data race; the heightfield is a planar row-extrusion of the profile (heightfield.cpp:25-33) with nrow>=2 admitted not exactly 2 (heightfield.cpp:8-14); the 5-name surface registry (surface.cpp:9-16) matches rider::SurfaceLabel (senses.hpp:21-28) and unknown names fail at surface.cpp:41-43, but the tire wire surface_map names are free-form labels (config_validation.hpp:359-365).

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
1) Non-atomic apply_preset: BikeSuspensionSystem s{}; s.shock_damper.c_hbo_min=5000.; s.shock_damper.c_hbo_max=4000.; s.apply_preset(DamperPreset::plush) — current_preset=plush and config=plush committed (suspension_setup.cpp:1022-1023), fork clicks updated (1024-1025), then shock_damper.set_clicks throws in damper_validate (863, ordering check ~174-176): current_preset/config/fork on plush, shock clicks stale — desynchronized inside the apply_preset scope. 2) Tolerance divergence: timestep_s=0.00125, research.control_period_s=0.0100000005 (8 ticks + 5e-10 s): post_init accepts (|rint(8.0000004)*dt - v| = 5e-10 <= 1e-9, validate.cpp:1205-1215) but prepare_environment's integer_steps computes |v/dt - 8| = 4e-7 ticks > 1e-8 and throws 'must be an integer multiple of the physics timestep' (research/configuration.cpp:133-143, environment.cpp:39-46) — a profile that validates cannot run the research path. 3) Silent rounding admitted: equilibrium_refine_period_s=0.0017 with dt=0.00125 passes post_init (scalar-positive only, validate.cpp:1285-1288) and is silently rounded by timing_steps (cycle=16 steps, round_even(0.085)=0 -> clamped to >=1, equilibrium.cpp:25-29, 91, 128) — the exact 'never a rounded one' behavior the 1e-9 checks reject for neighboring fields (validate.cpp:1203-1204, 1230); only defensible via the §12 exclusion at simulation-decisions.md:652. 4) Duplicate inertial: calling inertial(body,...) twice on one Element appends two <inertial> children — the write-once guard at mass.cpp:392-394 sees only the fresh carrier (xml_writer.cpp:114-119); in-tree callers avoid it but the Element API admits the corrupt tree (compile_mjcf likely rejects it downstream — gap between tree-build and compile). 5) Post-seal mass: DriveConfig.rotor_inertia_kgm2>0 adds a 0.3 kg motor_rotor inertial (physical_topology.cpp:63-65) and a drive_shaft inertial carved ad hoc (47-52) AFTER assign_budgets seals the registry (builder.cpp:63 vs 67-68) — total model mass = sum(15 budgets) + 0.3 kg, invisible to the registry. 6) Builder trust seam: BuildRequest{.bike=S1, .solver=HorstLinkageSolver(S2)} builds the whole model from the injected solver's geometry (builder.cpp:44-50); require_request_model rebuilds with the same request so the byte-compare passes a specs-inconsistent model (project_config.cpp:89-119) — the seal proves determinism, not consistency with declared specs. 7) Programmatic brake corruption: DriveConfig{brake_ceiling_nm=-1., brake_hold_engage_radps=2., brake_hold_release_radps=1.} passes DrivetrainWriter's checked_config (drivetrain::validate omits the fields, config_validation.hpp:207-220; writers/drivetrain.cpp:15-23) — caught only at StaticBrake construction (static_brake.cpp:19-42). 8) Dead-field corruption: AssistConfig{profile="bosch_cx_gen4", tau=-1.} passes post_init — the resolved profile tau is checked (validate.cpp:1254-1263), the declared member never is — while sibling nativecfg::validate requires positive tau unconditionally (config_validation.hpp:179). 9) Free tire surface names: wire tire.surface_map.surface.name="ice" passes decode+validate with no registry check (config_validation.hpp:359-365) and reports 'ice' in tire diagnostics (tire.cpp:837) while config.surface_labels is closed to the 5-label domain (senses_codec.cpp:204-213, project_config.cpp:48-67) — admitted label/physics divergence. 10) Boundary NaN/Inf: all scalar gates reject non-finite via !(x>=bound) idioms (e.g. validate.cpp:1262, 1269, 1279); restore paths are transactional — StaticBrake::restore validates both latch angles before committing either (static_brake.cpp:134-143), apply validates both demands before writing (44-69); no restore/unwind hole found there.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
The quantization gates mirror a confirmed requirement: simulation-decisions.md:614-652 (§12) declares every physics/control delay, tick and smoothing constant a required profile field that must be an integer multiple of its counting cycle — non-multiples rejected so the model 'never silently rounds' — and cycle periods must be multiples of the physics step; time constants need only span >=2 ticks (matching the Nyquist floors). No valid documented scenario is wrongly blocked: seated_climb.enabled requires articulated_effort (validate.cpp:1340-1342) since a coast mode has no intent; rear_brake_tap is explicitly unimplemented (front_lift_reaction.cpp:58, decisions §21); the detailed pneumatic tier pins its own timestep (validate.cpp:1333-1339); control_period_s >= 1 tick is semantically forced. RIDE.md's welded profile at 0.00125 s and RECORDING.md's non-editing playback are consistent with the gates. Nearest overblock: post_init runs validate_launch_plan_context and validate_reaction_context UNCONDITIONALLY (validate.cpp:1143-1149) including against seated_climb.period_s even when seated_climb.enabled=false and the intent never ticks — RiderIntent performs all of these checks only inside if(config_.enabled) (intent.cpp:436-465), so the config validator rejects dead off-grid fields that runtime semantics never consume; deliberate 'fail loudly' posture (intent.cpp:455-463) and consistent with §12's 'profile does not accept a non-multiple' rule, but strictly stronger than the runtime contract. Similarly the launch/reaction checks ignore plan_enabled/ease_enabled/lean_enabled flags. A secondary edge: the 1e-9 ABSOLUTE tolerance can false-reject exact integer multiples at very large step counts where rint(steps)*dt accumulates >1e-9 s of fp error (~1e7+ steps, i.e. >10^4 s at 1 ms) — outside every product duration. The equilibrium-refine carve-out runs the other direction (admits non-multiples the uniform rule would reject), and is explicitly excluded by §12 line 652.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Invalid states admitted while the property holds: (1) DriveConfig carrying brake_ceiling_nm<0 or engage>release passes drivetrain::validate (documented boundary, config_types.hpp:116-120) — saved only by StaticBrake ctor re-validation (static_brake.cpp:19-42); any consumer reading config().brake_* without revalidating inherits corruption. (2) Post-seal phase-2 bodies carry mass outside the 15-component registry — motor_rotor 0.3 kg and drive_shaft mass added after assign_budgets (physical_topology.cpp:47-65, builder.cpp:63-68); model mass exceeds sum(budgets) and the geom-coverage check (xml_writer.cpp:157-158) cannot see inertial-only bodies added later; additionally set_inertia at physical_topology.cpp:73-78 replaces registered wheel inertial elements, leaving stale Element pointers in the registry (harmless only because nothing dereferences them after line 63). (3) All BikeSuspensionSystem state is public (suspension_setup.hpp:366-369): s.fork_damper.set_clicks(9,9,9) or s.config=<anything> desyncs preset/config/clicks arbitrarily; apply_preset is non-atomic on the exception path; apply_preset(DamperPreset::custom) silently returns leaving current_preset stale (suspension_setup.cpp:1020-1029). (4) Element::inertial() admits duplicate <inertial> children per body. (5) HorstLinkageSolver::stroke_table_ lazily writes a mutable cache inside a const method (solver.cpp:299-313) — a data race if one solver instance is shared across threads. (6) Caller-injected solver built for different BikeSpecs seals a consistently wrong model (builder.cpp:44-50 vs project_config.cpp:118-119 byte-compare). (7) Declared assist.tau admits NaN/negative under an engaged profile (validate.cpp:523-546, 1254-1263) — a corrupt dead field inside a 'validated' record. (8) tire.surface_map names are free-form and can diverge from the rider's 5-label surface_labels (custom 'ice' friction numerics with 'hardpack' rider senses admitted on a hand-crafted wire). (9) post_init is not pure: it mutates the record it validates — drive_mode defaults to 'coast' (validate.cpp:1301), pitch_assist defaults (1302), cassette sort (627), grip_attachment 'weld'->'connect' remap (805-806) — so a 'validated' config is not the declared one. (10) A Stepper built without a drive config section reaches StaticBrake resolution and throws (runtime.cpp:361-397) — runtime requires a drive section the schema may mark optional.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Restate as bounded sub-invariants instead of universal quantifiers. (a) 'Registry uniqueness, XmlBodyElement write-once inertial, and a bijective seal over the 15 budgeted components AT ASSIGN TIME' — explicitly scope the seal to the registered domain and note that phase-2 topology (drive_shaft, 0.3 kg motor_rotor, rewritten wheel/shaft inertials) adds mass outside it; either harden Element::inertial() or keep it marked unguarded-with-lint. (b) Downgrade to 'synchronized on the non-throwing path of apply_preset' — or fix ordering (validate both dampers before committing current_preset/config) — and keep public mutability + custom-ctor semantics acknowledged as conventions, not invariants. (c-1) Enumerate the exact tick-quantized field list (control_period_s, monitor dropout/hold, pedaling reposition trio, research control/sample/actuator, assist.release_hold, shift cooldown+cut, stop_time, balance dwell+grace) and the tau>=2*timestep floors; name the equilibrium-refine schedule as an explicit §12 carve-out that silently rounds via timing_steps; state the research boundary re-checks with a stricter 1e-8-tick tolerance via integer_steps rather than 'identical' — unify the two tolerances. (c-2) Keep the drivetrain::validate omission but label it an explicitly documented trust boundary defended by decode_drive + post_init + StaticBrake ctor (as config_types.hpp:116-120 already does). (d) Memoization is content-correct under solver immutability AND single-threaded use — document the non-thread-safe lazy cache; 'compiled 2-row planar heightfields' -> 'planar row-extruded heightfields (nrow>=2, default 2)'; surface consistency is the closed 5-label domain on the senses/label path only — tire surface_map names are free telemetry labels that can diverge.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P23`: Tyre Carcass/Bristle Lifecycles, Shared Writer Clock/Restore Protocol, `CruiseWriter` & GPU Batch Parity

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `mixed_subclauses`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
P23 bundles ~10 sub-claims; the runtime mechanisms mostly hold but several asserted mechanisms and all asserted test-evidence sub-claims are absent or weaker than stated. (a-timestamps) HOLDS: all three writers reject d->time <= *last_time_s_ on advancing calls (TireWriter::try_compute_into gate in tire.cpp before accumulate_validated at tire.cpp:845-863; DistributedTireWriter equivalent in distributed_tire.cpp; PneumaticTireWriter::try_compute_into at pneumatic_tire.cpp:809-830), and commit_prepared_state resets the clock in all three, so the committed clock never decreases (strictly-increasing is stronger than the stated non-decreasing). (a-restore) PARTIALLY VIOLATED: no writer asserts prepared-ness or clears staged buffers on commit entry. TireWriter::commit_prepared_state (tire.cpp:~566) unconditionally publishes prepared_states_ (tire.hpp:234 has no validity flag), so commit-without-prepare silently publishes the default-constructed WheelState. DistributedTireWriter::commit_prepared_state (distributed_tire.cpp:399-405) does states_.swap(states_stage_), which is non-idempotent: a second commit swaps the just-installed restore back out and republishes the pre-restore bank — a stray commit reverts a restore, and because prepare_state (distributed_tire.cpp:329-397) writes states_stage_ cell-by-cell, a prepare that throws mid-decode (partially-nonfinite tangent at :374-388) leaves a half-updated stage that a stray commit will publish as a mixed old/new state. PneumaticTireWriter::commit_prepared_state has the same unconditional publish over prepared_scalars_/patch_stage_/dec_bristle_ (pneumatic_tire.cpp:483-494 area): a failed decode leaves half-decoded stage; commit then installs the mix. Production callers (equilibrium_cache install_material) pair prepare+commit, so this is reachable only through the public API, but the property asserts the mechanism exists in the writers and it does not. (a-alloc) OVERSTATED: the allocation-counting tests (test_contracts.cpp:3057-3112 compliant, :3197-3314 pneumatic lumped tier) pin try_compute_into only; probe_compute_into is explicitly cold-path — test_contracts.cpp:3176-3179 comments 'the cold probe path allocates by design' and runs the probe before the measured window, and TireWriter::probe_compute_into (tire.cpp:918-930) copy-constructs a full writer. The claimed probe coverage for all three backends does not exist; nor does coverage of the pneumatic discretised tier (warm rig uses discretised_brush=false). (a-Ban39) HOLDS: all radial paths are rate-independent hysteresis (tabulated_radial.cpp:38-53, station.cpp:93-100, contact/laws.hpp:40-62); damping_ns_m is a zero-pinned compatibility key rejected nonzero at config_validation.hpp:318-320, schema.cpp:763, density_fit.cpp:20. kBristleDampingS (brush.hpp:36-38 = 0.0005) is used only in DiscretisedBrush shear dynamics (brush.cpp:369-412) and its dissipation lands via patch slip loss -> dissipated_power_w (model.cpp:673-675) -> brush_power_w -> brush_loss_step_j in the writer. (b) MOSTLY HOLDS with one accounting gap: slot access is bounded (patch bound enforced at carcass.cpp:420 and model.cpp:451-453; restore bound at model.cpp:787-797; migration overlap math at model.cpp:361-397 is provably in-bounds since selected slots always have positive overlap), rim-strike fields propagate (model.cpp:745-747, pneumatic_tire.cpp:1077-1090), and the fresh-airborne path drops patch state safely (model.cpp:465-476). However bristle-shear elastic energy released when a patch migrates with partial/no overlap or is dropped entirely is silently discarded — there is no release-loss accounting analogous to the compliant backend's branch/release loss; shear_energy_j simply drops. (c) HOLDS with a borderline item: the integration gate matches exactly (cruise.cpp:53 engaged, :67-69 saturated&&same_sign(demand,error) via pushing_further, :70 !pushing_further && !traction_limited, :71-79 integrate+clamp), updates are next/commit atomic so no partial state publishes, and traction_limited is hardcoded false at the stepper call site with a comment documenting it mirrors the oracle's defaulted argument — that is a documented fixed-false, defensible as intentionally-reserved but not labeled as such. (d) PARTIALLY HOLDS: kMaxJsonDepth=64 (model_assets.hpp:125) bounds scan_value's fixed Frame array (:272-284), with explicit per-descent semantics ('each call starts a fresh depth budget'), and the whole-document scan at load_captured_initial_state:603 bounds total depth anyway; worker pools join before mj_delete (cpu_batch_runner.cpp:495-520; metal_batch_runner.mm init-pool join ~1752 and phase-pool join ~1953 before deleteData/deleteModel); gpu_ok==false suppresses all out_* writes and returns -7 (metal_batch_runner.mm:1959-2017, contract documented :1500-1515). BUT the claimed fault-injection test pinning the -7 branch does not exist — test_gpu_batch_runners.cpp:468-481 registers only buffer-reuse, launch-arg-validation, and rollout cases — and no CPU-runner test pins the all_finite-vs-output contract.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
R1 (distributed revert): construct DistributedTireWriter, call set_state(wire) — prepare+commit installs the restore; then call commit_prepared_state() a second time on the public API: states_.swap(states_stage_) at distributed_tire.cpp:399-405 swaps the old committed bank back in, silently undoing the restore. No flag distinguishes a staged-from-prepare bank from a recycled committed bank. R2 (half-decoded stage publish, distributed): prepare_state with a row whose station 5 tangent is partially nonfinite — stations 0-4 get NEW values (distributed_tire.cpp:363-373) before the throw at :374-388, stations 6+ keep the prior stage contents; a subsequent stray commit_prepared_state publishes the 0-4-new/5+-old mix without error. R3 (commit-without-prepare, compliant): on a fresh TireWriter call commit_prepared_state() directly — it publishes the default-constructed prepared_states_ (tire.hpp:234, no prepared flag) as a silent factory reset rather than rejecting. R4 (pneumatic half-decode): prepare_state on the discretised tier with a bristle cell NaN inside a declared patch — earlier dec_* fills have already been written before the throw; a stray commit publishes the partial decode (pneumatic_tire.cpp:489+ stage fields are unconditionally consumed). R5 (missing test evidence, GPU): run the GPU suite — the registered cases (test_gpu_batch_runners.cpp:468-481) cannot fail the gpu_ok==false branch because nothing can force a command-buffer failure; the -7 path and the CPU all_finite-vs-output contract are test-invisible. R6 (missing test evidence, probe alloc): any allocation window wrapped around probe_compute_into would fail by design — TireWriter::probe_compute_into copy-constructs the writer (tire.cpp:918-930) — which is exactly why the suite measures it before the window (test_contracts.cpp:3176-3179); the claimed probe coverage is absent rather than green.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
No clearly valid product scenario is wrongly blocked. The strict choices are deliberate and documented: (1) strictly-increasing time on advancing calls is stronger than 'non-decreasing', but probe_compute_into is the sanctioned same-time re-evaluation path, so replay/rewind uses reset()+set_state or probe rather than a blocked advancing call; (2) canonical-columns-only restore rejects wires carrying extra telemetry columns — a forward-compat artifact from a newer recorder would be rejected rather than tolerated, but RIDE.md/RECORDING.md-style recordings are produced by the same canonical schema so this is intentional strictness, not a blocked scenario; (3) the zero-alloc warm path requires construction-sized surface-name/patch capacity — a config with more surface names than the warmup reserve would allocate on first contact, but that is a warmup/config-time cost, not a blocked scenario; (4) traction_limited is a hardcoded-false API input, so a future real traction-control producer needs an API change rather than being blocked today.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even where the property holds, invalid states are still admitted: (1) commit_prepared_state without prepare publishes default-constructed (TireWriter), recycled-committed (DistributedTireWriter — restoring the pre-restore bank), or half-decoded stale stage content (all three writers after a throwing prepare) — the API admits all of these with no error; (2) a finite-but-arbitrarily-large integral_mps_s restored via CruiseWriter::set_state (cruise.cpp:114-123 validates finiteness/ranges but not the physical clamp the integrator itself maintains) is admitted and causes persistent saturation until it unwinds — an inconsistent controller state; (3) the CPU batch runner writes out_* arrays unconditionally even for worlds whose qpos/qvel are non-finite (cpu_batch_runner.cpp:474-491 writes regardless of the per-world finite flag; all_finite at :455-472 is only a side-channel flag), diverging from Metal's suppress-all contract — callers reading outputs without checking all_finite consume garbage; (4) bike_cpu_run_batch_ex does not guard mj_copyModel/mj_makeData failure — null worker model/data is dereferenced (cpu_batch_runner.cpp:~324-336) on allocation failure; (5) the pneumatic discretised tier silently drops bristle-shear elastic energy on patch migration/drop (model.cpp:337-346 fresh-zeros path and the partial-overlap copy at :354-413 release the non-overlapping bristle memory with no loss term) — energy accounting admits the disappearance as valid; (6) the Metal epoch/phase pool lacks a phase-staleness recheck on worker wake — a worker waking on a stale cursor can drain a consumed phase slice and post a phantom done_count, admitting skipped or doubled world steps (rare, bounded by the main-thread wait at :1879-1902, but a genuine hazard); (7) TireWriter restore silently drops a finite tangent adjacent to NaN xi (station decoded inactive) — an input inconsistency admitted without diagnostic.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Split P23 into (1) a runtime-contract property and (2) a test-coverage property, and fix each inaccurate sub-claim. Runtime: (a-i) all three tyre writers enforce strictly-increasing timestamps on advancing calls and reset the writer clock on commit_prepared_state [confirmed]; (a-ii) two-phase restore is all-or-nothing for the PAIRED prepare->set_state->commit path [confirmed for TireWriter's atomic prepared_states_ assignment; make explicit that commit_prepared_state does NOT gate on a prior successful prepare — either add a prepared-valid flag cleared on commit entry, or restate the contract as 'set_state is the only supported restore entry point']; (a-iii) zero heap allocation on the try_compute_into warm path is asserted by allocation-counting tests for compliant_2d, distributed, and pneumatic-LUMPED tiers — probe_compute_into is a cold path that allocates by design (writer copy-construction) and must be excluded from the claim, and the pneumatic discretised tier currently lacks allocation coverage; (a-iv) Ban #39 zero viscous radial damping holds end-to-end (hysteresis laws + damping_ns_m zero-pinned in config validation), and kBristleDampingS is a bristle-shear dashpot whose dissipation reaches brush_loss_step_j via patch.dissipated_power_w; (b) pneumatic carcass/rim/migration are bounds-safe and NaN-safe, but add an explicit sub-clause: bristle-shear elastic energy released by patch migration/drop is currently unaccounted — either account it in a release-loss channel or document it as an accepted oracle-parity energy leak; (c) CruiseWriter gate confirmed; mark traction_limited explicitly 'reserved, fixed false pending traction-control integration' rather than relying on a call-site comment, and add integral-state bounding to set_state or document unbounded finite integral as admitted; (d) JSON depth bound confirmed with explicit per-descent semantics (per scan_value call, whole-document scan bounds total depth); worker-pool joins confirmed; gpu_ok suppression confirmed; add a stale-phase recheck in the epoch pool or document the accepted hazard. Test-coverage (state separately, currently false as written): a GPU fault-injection seam for the -7 branch (e.g., injectable command-buffer status) and a CPU-runner test pinning that outputs are written even when all_finite==0 — or align the CPU runner with Metal's suppress-on-failure contract; a probe-path allocation classification test (assert cold-path, not zero); allocation coverage for the pneumatic discretised tier; and a commit-without-prepare/double-commit test once the runtime gating is decided.
