# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P24a`: Qualification Sweep Verdict Semantics & Summary Publication

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_engineering_contract`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Three clauses audited. (1) Exit-0-iff-verdict holds literally for tidy/cppcheck/frontends/odr (native/src/tools/check_frontends.cpp:644-651 verdict, :680/:717-719 status-gated return), headers (check_frontends.cpp:1176-1182, :1212-1243), scripts (check_frontends.cpp:1369-1373, :1386-1391) and context (check_contracts.cpp:1434-1465; 'rejected'/'not-applicable' count as pass verdicts, 'not-applicable' being unreachable post-validation). It FAILS for diagnostic-controls: the exit is gated on `!failures.empty()` — the list of controls whose status != "passed" — and never consults tool_health_failed or the recorded summary.status (check_contracts.cpp:447-470). The two `tool --version` probes (check_contracts.cpp:175-176) set tool_health_failed without failing any control, so a run can exit 0 while writing "status":"failed","tool_health":"failed" into diagnostic-controls.json. finding_status=='none' constant holds for both kinds (initialized check_contracts.cpp:127/:539, never updated). (2) The summary contract is overstated: neither disjunct holds. 'Every summary written before returning' fails on reachable paths — missing build dir returns 1 with no summary for run_sweep/headers/diagnostic-controls/context (check_frontends.cpp:365-369, :915-919; check_contracts.cpp:110-115, :521-525) while run_scripts DOES publish one for the identical error and even creates the missing build dir (check_frontends.cpp:1274-1276, :1309); malformed BIKE_CHECK_JOBS throws CheckError out of check_jobs with no summary (check_pool.cpp:18-29, invoked at check_frontends.cpp:578 and :1132); any exception inside a for_each_index worker is rethrown after join and escapes the sweep to main's catch -> exit 1 with no summary (check_pool.cpp:44-57; e.g. gcc_context_args sysroot-mismatch CheckError at check_context.cpp:1545-1547, replace_compile_source at :1506, tool_argv/tidy_command/temporary_database failures). 'stage+rename crash-atomicity' fails outright: write_summary is a plain ofstream-trunc write to the final path (check_reports.cpp:297-313); only the excluded contracts kind uses write_summary_atomic (check_reports.cpp:315-334, check_contracts.cpp:2678,2682). A kill mid-write leaves a truncated <kind>.json; a kill before the final write leaves the previous run's summary as stale evidence. (3) bt::run with a no-op body returns 1 'FAIL test body ran zero checks' (native/tests/support/fixture_test.cpp:57-75) — holds; every shipped main propagates the code either directly (`return bt::run(...)`) or via `failures == 0 ? 0 : 1` (e.g. native/tests/runtime/test_encoding.cpp:778-791).

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
REPRO A (exit 0 while verdict failed): run `--kind diagnostic-controls` with CLANG_TIDY set to a shim `#!/bin/sh
case "$1" in --version) exit 1;; *) exec /usr/bin/clang-tidy "$@";; esac`. resolve_tool accepts the env override (native/src/tools/check_context.cpp:1396-1410); version_record records an unhealthy `tidy --version` (check_contracts.cpp:164,175-176 -> check_reports.cpp:894-909); all six controls still pass because compile_control/first_party_use_after_move invoke the shim with real argv which forwards fine (check_contracts.cpp:187-241, :288-353). Result: `failures` empty -> `return 0` at check_contracts.cpp:470 while the summary written at :435 records "tool_health":"failed","status":"failed" (:429-433). tools/run_native_checks.sh:76 treats rc==0 as pass and line 81 prints 'all produced sweeps passed'. Equivalent variant: a context compiler.path shim that fails only `--version` hits `!record_health_ok(compiler_version)` (:163,175). REPRO B (no summary on setup error): `bike-native-checks --kind tidy --build /nonexistent` exits 1 printing only 'native sweep failed: build directory does not exist' (check_frontends.cpp:365-369) — nothing under native_check_summaries/; the identical invocation with `--kind scripts` writes scripts.json containing the failure (check_frontends.cpp:1274-1276,:1306-1311 via write_summary's create_directories at check_reports.cpp:299-303). REPRO C (no summary on mid-sweep error): `BIKE_CHECK_JOBS=abc bike-native-checks --kind tidy --build <valid>` -> check_jobs throws CheckError (check_pool.cpp:21-25) at check_frontends.cpp:578, escapes run_sweep to checks_main.cpp:250-255 -> exit 1, no summary. REPRO D (mid-sweep exception): a compile entry whose argv selects a sysroot differing from the recorded context makes gcc_context_args throw inside a worker (check_context.cpp:1541-1548), rethrown by for_each_index (check_pool.cpp:44-57) -> exit 1, no summary. REPRO E (crash-atomicity): `write_summary` trunc-writes native_check_summaries/<kind>.json in place (check_reports.cpp:297-313) — SIGKILL during the write leaves a truncated file; SIGKILL between setup and final write leaves a STALE previous-run 'passed' summary.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
None found. The property constrains only verification tooling (gate verdict semantics and evidence publication), not simulation/product behavior — no clause in docs/agents/simulation-decisions.md, docs/RIDE.md or docs/RECORDING.md requires a sweep to exit 0 on findings, write non-atomically, or pass an empty selection. Legitimate non-blocking scenarios are already admitted: only warning:/error:/fatal-error diagnostics set finding_status=blocking (check_reports.cpp:509-544, 598-612), informational output does not; negative controls use 'rejected' as their pass verdict (check_contracts.cpp:687,700,826); empty selections fail by design and callers already avoid them (tools/check_change.sh:43-50 skips an empty --only-sources list; tools/check_scripts.sh:16 exits 1 on an empty script set). bt::run's zero-checks failure is precisely the anti-vacuous-pass contract the contract-test harness needs (fixture_test.cpp:39-46).

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even where the stated rules literally hold, invalid states are admitted: (a) the diagnostic-controls gap itself — a summary recording "status":"failed" coexists with exit 0 and a green gate journal (check_contracts.cpp:447-470 vs tools/run_native_checks.sh:76-81). (b) headers sweep ignores --version probe health: the compiler version record is captured only for the tool_version field (check_frontends.cpp:1036-1040), unlike run_sweep which folds it into tool_health_failed (:508,:527) — a compiler that cannot identify itself still passes the headers kind. (c) tool_inspection_error is recorded but never gates the verdict: inspect_sweep_tool returns false (no health impact) on ANY inspection CheckError, including unrecognized tool families (check_frontends.cpp:336-359); a silent stub exiting 0 cleanly passes the sweep — the shipped b29_positive fixture does exactly this with a mock printing 'mock analysis tool 1' (native/tests/tooling/tooling_checks.cmake:989-996 -> EXPECT_EXIT 0 with a recorded inspection error). (d) stale/torn evidence: non-atomic writes plus no-summary exit paths mean a crashed or failed run can leave the previous 'passed' summary or a truncated JSON in place; consumers cannot distinguish freshness without run_id inspection. (e) evidence drop: the unhealthy tool --version record appended to summary.records (check_frontends.cpp:519-524) is overwritten at :644/:552, so a tool_health=failed summary lacks the record that caused the failure (deliberate Python parity). (f) bt::run's scoreboard counts require() calls made inside fixture helpers (bt::fixture/text/read_f64/write_text, fixture_test.cpp:85-146), so a body performing only fixture I/O with zero semantic assertions exits 0 — weaker than the 'cannot pass vacuously' intent at :39-41. (g) cosmetic: a child that legitimately exits 137 with elapsed >= timeout-0.5 is labeled timed_out (check_reports.cpp:852-854).

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
1) diagnostic-controls: gate the exit on the recorded verdict like the other seven kinds — replace the `!failures.empty()` predicate at check_contracts.cpp:447-470 with `if (!failures.empty() || tool_health_failed) ... return 1;` (or `status != "passed"`), so tool_health==failed can never exit 0. 2) Summary publication: pick one honest contract — either promote write_summary_atomic (stage+rename, check_reports.cpp:315-334) to every kind, or restate as 'a failed summary is published on every handled error path after the build-dir check; a missing build dir is stderr-only; a killed run may leave a stale or truncated summary' — and route the check_jobs/for_each_index error paths (check_frontends.cpp:578,:1132; check_pool.cpp:57) through the same failed-summary write the setup catch already performs. 3) headers: fold the version-record health into tool_health_failed to match run_sweep (check_frontends.cpp:1036-1040 vs :527). 4) Decide whether tool_inspection_error gates the verdict (currently record-only at check_frontends.cpp:341-343) or document the admission explicitly. 5) bt::run clause holds as stated; if vacuous-pass protection is intended, count only direct bt::require invocations rather than helper-internal ones.

# Stage 4 (`R2`) Adversarial Audit Report — `P24b`: Container/`link_deps` Lifecycle & Harness Self-Test Wiring

- **Inspected Commit:** `0502660dd14bd1021b3da90a61e20dcb1d5805a1`
- **Adversarial Compliance Verdict (`R2`):** `partially_violated`
- **Adversarial Justification Verdict (`R2`):** `relevant_engineering_contract`

## 1. Compliance Challenge
Clause-by-clause. (1) link_deps `.complete` lifecycle: sequentially the mechanism matches the statement — `[[ ! -f $target/.complete ]]` gates acquisition, `rm -rf` wipes an incomplete target, `acquire_deps` fills a mktemp sibling stage, `mv` publishes by rename, `.complete` is written last (tools/link_deps.sh:46-65), `.deps` is linked only afterwards (68-80). But the gate is *existence-only*: line 65 writes the full 64-char lock sha into `.complete`, yet nothing ever reads that content — a stale, foreign, or hand-planted `.complete` satisfies the gate and suppresses re-verification forever. The TOCTOU between the `-f` test (line 46) and `rm -rf` (line 49) lets a concurrent run delete a target that became complete in between; a second concurrent `mv stage target` (line 62) NESTS the stage inside the published target as `target/.stage.XXXX` (verified BSD/GNU mv semantics) and `.complete` is still written — a '.complete'd target carrying undeclared junk. An empty `.deps/_archives` makes the unquoted glob `cp -p -- "$link"/_archives/*` (line 55) fail with a literal `*` operand → link_deps dies on every run until `.deps` is removed manually. (2) container.sh pull rollback: the whole driver is dead-coded by the PLAN-CHANGE GUARD (container.sh:19-24 — unconditional `exit 3`), so the rollback claim is vacuous today by the property's own conditioning. The conditioning is UNMET: test_container_pull.sh is referenced by nothing except itself — not in run_tests.sh, run_native_checks.sh, any CMakeLists/CTest registration, ci_presets.sh or mise.toml; the `scripts` sweep (check_frontends.cpp:1246-1330 replicating check_scripts.sh:14-17) only SHELLCHECK-lints it, never executes it. It is also not marked disabled-with-reason: the file carries no annotation, and linux.md documents the lane deferral without naming the test. Run today it fails loudly at its first positive case (guard exit 3 → 'legitimate golden directory rejected', test_container_pull.sh:83-87) — a fail-loud orphan, not a fail-silent one, but not 'wired or disabled-with-reason' as the statement requires. In the dormant code itself there is a residual publish TOCTOU: for an absent destination the recheck `[[ ! -e "$destination" ]]` (container.sh:233) and `mv -- ready/<base> "$(dirname)/"` (line 242) admit a regular file materializing between them — rename silently replaces it (verified), no backup exists for pull_existed==0, pull reports success and the interloper content is lost; the LATE_DEST fixture only covers the directory case. `docker cp` (container.sh:192) bypasses `require_container_owner` (used only via `in_container` at :180) — per-operand ownership TOCTOU. The `*/build*/*` source_path exclusion (container.sh:74) matches ANY path component beginning with 'build' (builders, building, buildspec), not just generated build trees — silently dropped by sync, loudly rejected by pull. (3) Harness self-tests: allocation fault-injection is wired and exercised (bike_test_allocation_faults OBJECT lib → native_contract_tests + config_writer, native/tests/CMakeLists.txt:69-74; arm/fail_at sweeps in test_contracts.cpp:2015-2124,4160-4229, test_front_lift_reaction.cpp:717-730, test_writer_config.cpp:263-310, plus spawned --load/--make-data/--late-data-allocation-failure probes via reference.native_engine_errors — `slow` label). Publication retry is wired and exercised (recording_publish registered via native/tests/recording/CMakeLists.txt:14-33; fault_matrix injects failures at 24 publication stages then retries and re-finalizes idempotently, test_publish.cpp:40-58; crash_and_signal_contract fork/SIGKILL/SIGINT cases :88-103; plus 'disarmed retry' checks in test_contracts.cpp:2085-2124). `bt::run` no-op: the zero-checks fail-closed guard exists (fixture_test.cpp:68-72) and ~50 executables route through bt::run (wired), but NO registered test asserts the zero-checks branch itself — `native_contract_tests --require-failure` (test_contracts.cpp:4308-4316, exercised by reference.native_loader.* via test_native_loader_contracts.cmake:10-19) only proves `require` throws, and that probe targets native_contract_tests' OWN require/run_case, which lacks the scoreboard entirely: a contract case running zero require()s prints PASS (test_contracts.cpp:4286-4295). So the named 'bt::run no-op' self-test is at best partially exercised.

## 2. Concrete Counterexamples / Repros
C1a (complete-target wipe + silent dangling .deps): two checkouts share BIKE_DEPS_CACHE. Run B evaluates `[[ ! -f $target/.complete ]]` (link_deps.sh:46) while run A is mid-acquisition; B is descheduled; A publishes `mv stage→target` (:62) and writes `.complete` (:65); B resumes at `rm -rf "$target"` (:49) and deletes the completed target; A then `ln -s "$target" .deps` (:79) exits 0 leaving `.deps` pointing at a nonexistent directory — 'consumable' reported on a wiped target, exactly the invalid state the marker exists to prevent. C1b (nested publish): both A and B see `.complete` absent, both acquire; A `mv stageA→target`; B `mv stageB→target` — target exists as a directory → BSD/GNU mv nests `stageB` inside it (verified on this host) producing `$target/.stage.XXXXXXXX` garbage under a written `.complete`; both runs print 'acquired'. C1c (stale marker): `echo deadbeef > ~/.cache/bike-deps/darwin-arm64-<sha16>/.complete` after corrupting a member header → `mise run deps` sees `-f` true (link_deps.sh:46), skips all verification, links `.deps` — the corrupt tree is 'consumable'; failure only surfaces later at configure via BikeDependencyLock.cmake sha gates (:247-251), far from the cause. C1d: `mkdir -p .deps/_archives` (empty) → `cp -p -- .deps/_archives/* stage/_archives/` fails on the literal glob (link_deps.sh:55) → link_deps aborts every run. C2a: `bash tools/linux/test_container_pull.sh` today → container.sh exits 3 (guard :23-24) → first legitimate pull rejected (test_container_pull.sh:83-87) — orphan test is red-and-unwired, contradicting 'wired or disabled-with-reason'. C2b (file-in-gap): pull_existed==0 path — `[[ ! -e destination ]]` passes (container.sh:233); a regular file appears at `$repo_root/<rel>` before `mv ready/<base> "$(dirname)/"` (:242) → rename replaces it atomically; pull_installed=1, no backup, pull exits 0, interloper lost. Only the directory variant is tested (PULL_TEST_LATE_DEST). C3: `native_contract_tests` `run_case` (test_contracts.cpp:4286-4295) — a case function that performs zero require()s returns 0 → 'PASS <name>' — vacuous pass admitted; bt::run's scoreboard (fixture_test.cpp:42-72) is not used by this harness and its zero-checks branch is asserted nowhere.

## 3. Necessity Challenge (Blocked Valid Scenarios)
Mild. The `.complete` existence-gate forces a full ~300 MB reacquisition whenever the marker is lost — conservative but intended. The `bt::run` zero-checks rule (fixture_test.cpp:68-72) blocks a legitimate 'must-not-crash' smoke test that deliberately asserts nothing — it must grow one token `bt::require` (defensible friction; vacuous tests are the real hazard). `source_path`'s `build*` patterns (container.sh:74: `build|build*/*|*/build|*/build*/*`) match any component STARTING with 'build' — a future legitimate `tests/building/` fixture dir or `docs/build-notes/` would be silently skipped by `sync` (not an error — absent files in the container) and loudly rejected by `pull`; nothing tracked matches today, so it is a latent, not current, block. link_deps silently replaces ANY pre-existing `.deps` symlink (`[[ -L $link ]]` → `rm` → `ln -s`, :68-79): a user deliberately pointing `.deps` at a custom dependency root is overridden without consent — the sanctioned alternative (-DBIKE_DEPENDENCY_ROOT, BikeDependencyLock.cmake:55-59) exists, so severity is low. Empty-`_archives` abort (:53-56) blocks recovery of a pruned `.deps`, but configure requires the archives anyway (BikeDependencyLock.cmake:222-231) so the `.deps` was already invalid — unhelpful diagnostic, not a wrongly blocked scenario. The plan-change guard blocks even harmless `down`/`exec` calls — intended. No scenario in simulation-decisions.md, RIDE.md, or RECORDING.md is wrongly blocked: these are developer-tooling, not product, constraints.

## 4. Sufficiency Challenge (Admitted Invalid Scenarios)
Even granting the statement: (a) a `.complete`d target whose contents were corrupted after publication (bit-rot, user edit, rsync) is unconditionally 'consumable' — link_deps never re-verifies content or the `.complete` payload; only the downstream CMake sha256 gates catch it, so 'consumable' ≠ 'correct'. (b) `.deps` linked to a target wiped by the concurrent-run race — the 'success' run ends with a dangling symlink. (c) `.stage.XXXX` or an adopted `.deps` nested inside a published target rides along under `.complete` permanently — never garbage-collected, nor are orphaned `.stage.*` siblings and old `<platform>-<oldhash>` targets (unbounded cache growth). (d) `.deps.replaced-$(date +%Y%m%d%H%M%S)` collides on two backups within one second → `mv` nests the second `.deps` inside the first backup (:75-76) — cosmetic but admitted. (e) native_contract_tests `run_case` passes a zero-require case vacuously (no scoreboard). (f) container pull: file-in-gap silent clobber; `docker cp` ownership TOCTOU (:180→:192) and container-side symlink dereference between the in-container check and `docker cp` (docker cp resolves links — a swapped-in symlink arrives as a regular file the staged pull_tree cannot identify). (g) rollback `rm -rf destination && mv backup` — rm success + mv failure → `rollback_failed` → exit 2 with destination already deleted (documented 'not crash-atomic', linux.md:121-123). (h) tools/colab_gpu/build_cuda_writers.py compiles to the predictable world-writable `/tmp/libbike_cuda_writers.so` (:15,169-181) and `ctypes.CDLL`s it in place — no staging/rename/verification; a swapped or concurrently-truncated .so is loaded (multi-user/shared-GPU box only; single-tenant Colab mitigates). test_model_assets.py hardcodes SCRATCH_ROOT=/tmp/bike_orch/E (:37). (i) golden.cpp `Node::write` (:390-427) writes .json and .bin non-atomically in record mode — a crash leaves a torn sidecar/payload pair that the next Node ctor rejects loudly (manual repair, acceptable). (j) sync silently skips any tracked file under a `build*` component (see necessity) — a tracked-but-unsynced file is an admitted inconsistent source tree.

## 5. Proposed Contract Revision
Restate as three independently verifiable clauses and close the listed holes. (1) link_deps: serialize acquisition per target — take `flock` on `$target.lock` (or the cache root) before the `-f .complete` test and hold it through publish, so the check→`rm -rf` window and the nested-mv publish cannot interleave; make `.complete` meaningful by requiring its content to equal the current lock sha256 (mismatch or unreadable → wipe+reacquire), turning the marker into a staleness self-heal rather than an existence token; guard the `_archives` glob with nullglob or a count check so empty dirs don't abort; publish with `mv -T`/`renameat2(RENAME_NOREPLACE)` semantics or an explicit `[[ ! -e $target ]]` recheck under the lock; sweep `.stage.*` orphans and optionally expire non-current `<platform>-<hash>` targets. (2) container.sh: keep the property's conditioning explicit — while the PLAN-CHANGE GUARD stands, annotate test_container_pull.sh itself 'disabled-with-reason: linux lane deferred (guard at container.sh:19-24)' AND register it in the gate inventory as a skipped/linux-labeled test so 'orphan' is machine-visible; on lane resumption wire it into a `linux`/`slow` CTest label or run_tests stage. In the dormant code: close the absent-destination publish window with no-replace rename (or re-`lstat` between `[[ ! -e ]]` and `mv` and die on any appeared object, file or symlink), route `docker cp` through an owner-rechecking helper, and narrow `*/build*/*` to the actual generated-tree names (or enumerate exclusions from .gitignore). (3) Harness: add a subprocess self-test asserting `bt::run` on a zero-require body exits nonzero with 'ran zero checks', and give `native_contract_tests`' `run_case` the same scoreboard (count requires per case; fail on zero). Keep the allocation fault-injection sweeps and publication retry/crash matrix as the exercised evidence — they are already wired (recording_publish + native_contract_tests + native_engine_errors `slow` probes); only the no-op leg needs its own assertion.
